# Implementation history

What building webdroid taught: failure mechanisms, the measurements behind current designs, and experiments that
were rejected or reverted, with the measured reason. Check here before retrying an idea. [PLAN.md](../PLAN.md)
tracks status and open work, [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) the working rules, [architecture.md](architecture.md) the
current design and [performance.md](performance.md) how to measure. Results describe the revisions and paths
observed, not a guarantee for the current tree.

This version was compacted on 2026-10-13. Findings about deleted components were kept only where the lesson
transfers ([Removed designs](#removed-designs)). The full earlier text, with per-run evidence, is at
`git show ef6d85ca:docs/implementation-history.md`; source comments naming an AGENTS investigation refer to it.
The original APK audit and per-app handoff are at `git show 24cade3f^:<path>`.

**Adding a finding:** put it under the topic a reader would look in, as `- **Title** (date). Finding.`, with the
numbers, identifiers and commits that support it. When a later change supersedes an entry, rewrite it to the
current state and keep the measured reason for any rejected alternative. Record mechanisms, not run logs.

## Evidence and measurement

### Noise, host load and false regressions

- **The quick gate fails the same way on its base under 5 jobs** (2026-10-22, M4 Pro, branch with burning, linking ahead and early frames against `98707f3d` in its own worktree and platform build, alternating). Flappy Bird's first GMS process (pid 1003, no ticks) stopped answering diagnostics in every run of both; Doodle Jump's GMS Workers did so in 3 of 3 base runs (one wedging its main loop for 506 s) and 1 of 4 branch runs; FitNotes' settles timed out at `1-weight`/`1-reps` or `4-save`/`custom-graph` in 3 of 4 branch and 2 of 3 base runs, and Komikku's in 1 of 4 and 1 of 3. Run one at a time (`--jobs 1`), the four flows passed on the branch. The Mihon flows need `apks/mihon-v0.20.4.apk`, absent from this checkout.
- **Two quick flows at once, on the host GPU** (2026-10-22, M4 Pro, `3ac65ce1`). Against the same server, the starting harness at 5 jobs passed 2 of 5 flows in 153 s (808 s CPU) and this branch 1 of 5 under SwiftShader; on ANGLE's Metal backend 5 jobs passed 3 of 5 (a Doodle Jump wedge and one Calculator M launch crash: `IllegalAccessException: Class q4.d can not access a member of class q4.g` from an `AtomicReferenceFieldUpdater` made in `q4.g`'s initializer, a caller class read from the wrong frame: see Code delivery, **A build module over code compiled on demand**), 3 jobs 4 of 5 (a GMS Worker's diagnostics at Flappy Bird's splash) and 2 jobs 5 of 5 twice, in 45 s at 234 s CPU. SwiftShader rasterizes on the CPU: serial FitNotes 25 → 14 s on the GPU, Calculator M 9 → 6 s, visual diffs unchanged (3.06%, 1.18%). The quick gate defaults to 2 jobs and macOS harnesses to the GPU (`WEBDROID_GL=swiftshader` for software GL); `lat` keeps SwiftShader unless `--gpu`, as its baselines were measured.
- **Host load manufactures regressions** (2026-09-30..10-05). Parallel suites and live runs multiplied TikTok wedges and mid-transition settles, on the starting commit too. A TikTok "regression" (8/23 vs 1/17 wedges) was 3/8 vs 3/8 interleaved; Komikku's step after "Get started" failed 2 of 14 parallel runs against 0 of 24 before a logging change, with matching step times. doodlejump `05 back` times out ~1 run in 5 at any commit under load; a single-run bisect blamed an unrelated commit.
- **Noise floors** (2026-10-01..05). At load 30–58 (five agents, 14 cores) every latency number was noise, and samples taken while another checkout runs browser tests establish nothing. On an idle M4 Pro, 10–30 ms gated-tap differences are noise. jbench within one commit: b64java 1.00–1.13, mix 1.34–1.45 ms.
- **Live content is a variable** (2026-10-03). YouTube's intermittent video-open failure was a sponsored card (up to 1,600 px) under the fixed tap at y=1350; relay and QUIC were exonerated (matching packet counts, same failure with UDP blocked). Logging every datagram slowed boot past the 18 s bound.
- **Ticks are not frame rates** (2026-10-02). A Mihon sample that missed its setup target reported advancing ticks with no presentations. Perf windows that are hidden, stalled, input-incomplete, looper-unresponsive or present nothing are marked invalid, and missing setup targets fail.
- **Passing controls are not causal closure** (2026-10-05..07). For FitNotes' `RenderNode` crash, three timing-perturbing changes passed 39/39 without catching the stale handle; only a registration-generation trace found it (see [Rendering and text](#rendering-and-text)). Observer and Promise instrumentation shift scheduling, so they can expose or hide races.
- **Contaminated range** (2026-09-30). `git commit -am` swept a probe tracing every main-queue job into `28fd6e26`; e2e timings up to `19534e7c` are inflated.

### `lat` and response observation

- **Pixel observer cost; off-thread sampling rejected** (2026-10-08/09). The whole-image `sig` (draw, downsample, synchronous readback on the main thread) took 98.3 ms self in a 300 ms FitNotes tap window. A Worker prototype made responses 714–810 ms: analyzed frames lag the latest presentation during animation, so the join accepts only after animation ends, and accepting older pixels would weaken the gate. GPU-backed synchronous sampling kept all 117 nonuniform predicates but only nine exact hashes (max mean-channel diff 0.00464). Neither integrated.
- **Retained-frame readback** (2026-10-09/10). Under SwiftShader even a cropped readback, `VideoFrame.copyTo` or a fenced WebGL PBO read blocked up to 264 ms, so `lat` crops candidate frames on the GPU as they present and reads them back once presentation pauses. Taps then presented ~20 frames instead of 2–4; earlier tap numbers are not comparable.
- **What a tap time means** (2026-10-07..10). Scenarios observe the intended semantic response and owned app pixels (`06bfe74e`); a first changed bitmap is a proxy. The console's per-tap compiler figure stopped at the first changed frame (1.8 ms) where the intended response had 15.4 ms compile and 10.9 ms module work. Independently rounded child/root clocks gave false ordering (`e4cb5239`). A dialog dismissal presents no app frame, so `lat` times it against the next unrelated one (Komikku "OK": 2.5–2.9 s, state matched in 33 ms).
- **Compile accounting overlaps** (2026-10-07..09). Compiler spans (`b668afe3`) exclude main linker/planning and module parsing, and elapsed time is not thread CPU. Compile, module and sampled figures overlap; compilation counters include recompiles. Per-step `perfReset` erases cumulative counters: zero root AOT loads in a step does not mean none loaded.
- **`lat` launch conditions** (2026-10-10/12). `lat` boots from `?apk=` at load, so gains measured from `wd.boot()` on a pre-loaded page (spare zygote, warm) do not show in it.
- **Harness-only costs** (2026-09-27..10-03). Playwright's `Runtime.enable` makes `new Error()` capture detailed stacks (~20 vs ~4 µs): Snapseed's ~1,140 reflective `NoSuchFieldException`s per tool open cost ~65 ms only under the harness.
- **Scroll window limits** (2026-10-08/09). Presentations/s include static intervals and are not dropped-frame counts. Swiping every 500 ms instead of 1,000 ms cut idle time. ABBA pairs drift with run order.
- **Private drivers drift** (2026-10-05..09). One passed untranslated Flow actions and sent no input; a scroll driver and corpus each dropped a query setting and silently ran another configuration. Drivers share `executionFlow`/`actOf` and `webdroidUrl()`.
- **Throttling reaches only the page** (2026-10-13). Chrome refuses `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` on a Worker session ("Operation is only supported for pages, not workers"), and a page's rate does not slow its Workers. In a busy-loop probe the Worker loop took 1,081 ms at rate 1 and 1,097 ms at rate 4, while the page loop took 177 and 728 ms. `lat --throttle` therefore slows only the page thread, and figures "at 4× throttle" for app processes in Workers measure unthrottled app code. The refused call also made `--throttle` with `--cpuprofile` drop every Worker profile. `lat --ecores` replaced it (below).
- **A slower device on the efficiency cores** (2026-10-14). macOS `taskpolicy -c background` confines a process and its children to the efficiency cores at low priority, which slows every thread alike: the busy-loop probe went 169 → 1,044 ms in the page and in Workers. `lat --ecores` launches Chrome that way. Cold `--gpu` runs: Calculator M launched in 15.6 s and its first taps (`7`, `plus`) presented no response within the window, then `equals` 94, `clear` 184 and `8` 79 ms; FitNotes launched in 10.3 s with taps of 39–827 ms (`start` slowest) and scrolled at 16.5 presentations/s (p95 101 ms); Komikku launched in 49.4 s, `next` 828, `more` 1,189 and `settings` 705 ms, `start` and `appearance` presented nothing in the window, and scrolling reached 16.4 presentations/s. The floor on a slow device is the cold first tier, not the frame path.
- **One filesystem's space, in bionic's layouts** (2026-10-14). Native statfs/statvfs reported a fixed 4 GiB filesystem with 2 GiB free while Java's StatFs reported the browser estimate (a fresh Chrome profile gets a 10 GiB quota; a TikTok launch uses about 215 MB). Both now read the estimate. The old fills were also mislaid: ARM32 statfs is the kernel's statfs64, with 64-bit counts from offset 8, and the 4-byte writes put `f_bfree` in `f_blocks`' high word; A64 statvfs is eleven 8-byte longs, written as 4-byte fields. `stp` translates its value as a guest pointer, so stat times and other C longs stored with it could become a high plane's address on A64; those now use `stLong`. TikTok's intermittent "Manage your storage" dialog (two of three warm preparations on one day, then none in five matched runs, with server-delivered text) was not caused by native statfs (cause below).
- **TikTok's "Manage your storage" was a failed StatFs** (2026-10-14). Its check (`CleanCacheDialog`, run when `last_check_storage_time` allows) shows the dialog when `new StatFs(getExternalCacheDir().getPath())` reports under `diskFreeSpaceThreshold` (440 MB unless the server sets `disk_space_threshold`), and StatFs failing counts as -1 bytes. `Os.statvfs` and `setxattr` looked a path up only in the calling process's VFS, while an app's private and external app directories belong to another realm, so `statvfs` threw ENOENT for `/data/data/<pkg>`, its `files` and `/sdcard/Android/data/<pkg>/cache`. They now ask the owning realm as `stat` did. The check runs once per fresh profile, when the app first reaches its dialog manager, which is why it looked intermittent. In matched `e2e --full --test tiktok` runs (alternating, fresh profiles), both runs without the fix showed the dialog at step 03 after `X.06b6.LJIIJJI`'s StatFs got ENOENT; both with it ran all 13 steps without it, `statvfs` answering. With StatFs answering, a cold TikTok launch also stores `files/ugcommon` resources it skipped before. Free space itself was never short: headless Chrome reports a quota of usage plus ~10 GB.
- **Batched MOVEs (`?input=batched`)** (2026-10-13, 5 alternating runs per mode, page throttled 4×, app unthrottled). Komikku `scroll-appearance`: 59.9 vs 59.2 fps, p99 24.8 vs 33.6 ms, frames over 25 ms 2 vs 4 (medians). FitNotes `scroll-legs`: 59.4 vs 60.0 fps, 1 vs 0 over 25 ms, with `doFrame` p95 1.2 vs 0.8 ms. Input-to-dispatch rose from 0.1 to ~3.5 ms by design. This is no clear win: immediate dispatch stays, and the batched path was removed (cbd04094; restore it from 116c1c53).
- **First response frame** (2026-10-13). `lat` now gates on the first presented frame after `pointerup` that changes a tile (8 px) the settled frame also changed, outside the tapped node's bounds plus 72 px and outside tiles that changed while pressed; the settled state is reported beside it. The Worker sends ≤128 px crops of the response regions with each presentation, and its timeline ring (`src/timeline.ts`, read with `wd.foreground()?.timeline`) attributes each window: vsyncs, doFrame, draw, composite, present and loop idle/wake. `--throttle N` applies CDP CPU throttling to the page and every Worker; `bundles=0` keeps bundled app builds out of cold runs. The fitnotes scroll started at y=1690, outside the exercise dialog (about 522–1617), so its 41 fps measured drags that scrolled nothing; the Chest list it now drags fits on screen (3 presentations/s), so that step does not measure pacing either.
- **Crashed pages and dead app processes** (2026-10-13). A warm YouTube run waited forever on an app that had died in `handleBindApplication`, and a crashed renderer (ChatGPT warm, one TikTok cold launch, the page and its Workers sharing one renderer) left `Runtime.evaluate` pending. `lat` now listens for `Inspector.targetCrashed`, bounds the partial read and the close at 10 s, and fails a launch once the app's process has a crash record (`wd.workerProcesses()`). A scenario's launch `ms` widens the launch window (ChatGPT and TikTok need 60 s cold). The renderer crashes are not yet explained; memory is the likely cause.
- **Repeat visits and the heavy frame** (2026-10-19). On a repeat Komikku visit (`lat --repeat N`) Appearance's UP is handled within ~2 ms, the next vsync starts one 22–37 ms `doFrame` (recomposition and layout; its draw takes 0.4 ms), and the screen answers two short animation frames later. A tap time adds that vsync phase and the two waits, so its medians move ±10 ms on unchanged code, while the longest `doFrame` (`frame`) moves 2–7 ms (Appearance 35.1–38.2 ms, Settings 16.2–22.9 ms over three runs of one build); codegen comparisons read `frame`. By the third visit the engine runs most of that frame optimized, but code quality is not most of it: under `WEBDROID_JS_FLAGS=--liftoff-only` the Appearance frame of visits 3–7 was 45.1 ms (median) against 36.7 by default, and `--no-liftoff` 39.4 with TurboFan compiles in the taps.
- **Komikku `next` races the dialog's aftermath** (2026-10-14). On the profiled build `next` failed 3 of 8 warm runs at 180–256 ms, the base commit included. In a failing run no press ripple drew after `pointerdown`, the looper already held a message about 690 ms out, and after `pointerup` no Java ran for 186 ms before the next screen drew; passing runs had nothing scheduled. With 3 s instead of 1.5 s after "OK", 6 of 6 passed (22–36 ms). A later series (2026-10-14, 3 of 7 warm runs slow at 125–240 ms) split cleanly: fast runs drew a frame for every vsync after DOWN (the press ripple), slow ones ticked `doFrame` each vsync (0.2–1 ms) without drawing from DOWN until the response. A probe on `nSyncAndDrawFrame` showed the app never asked to draw in that gap (first call at +188 ms, the response), and no thread ran Java (190 of 240 ms with none). Input arrived with `uptimeMillis` event times and was handled in 1 ms, and vsync carries `uptimeMillis` as its frame time. The app waits on its own clock, as a delayed animation would; the cause inside the app is not identified and the scenario is unchanged. With the install-time build (2026-10-17), whose reopened launches run less on-demand work, the race grew: 7 of 10 warm runs failed or were slow (68–199 ms, or "did not change outside the pressed view"), against 0 of 3 for the same code without the build. In failing runs the app's background threads were still running when "Next" was pressed (a CPU profile of the 4.5 s before it: 200 ms creating its database, each statement a round trip to the page, and 200 ms in Coil's animated-image decoder), and the looper held a message due 120–690 ms out. Turning off the warm Worker or the platform prefetch passed 3 of 3 and 2 of 2, but without the prefetch 3 of 5 still failed. `lat`'s quiet test (`idle`) now also waits for no thread but the main one to run Java and for no main-looper message to be due within a second; with it 5 of 5 warm runs passed at 21–29 ms, and in the matched runs after, every `next` took 11–50 ms.

- **Raw Chrome waited on the macOS keychain** (2026-10-25). `lat` (full Chrome over raw CDP, where Playwright's launches use its headless shell) hung in `Page.navigate` to the dev server at the starting commit, renderer and DevTools unresponsive: the first cookie use waited on a keychain prompt nobody can answer. It now passes `--use-mock-keychain` as Playwright does. lat also takes the harnesses' Chrome flags now (`launchOptions`: no font hinting, LCD text, partial raster or Skia runtime opts), keeping SwiftShader unless `--gpu`; unmatched runs under host load 6-9 put calculatorm, fitnotes, mihon and komikku taps within the old harness's spread, and its steps run as e2e actions with expected states as e2e state conditions (`within` keeps Calculator's display texts apart from its keys).

### e2e capture, settling and determinism

- **Diagnostics wait for process starts** (2026-10-23). At 2 jobs the quick gate failed Doodle Jump's timed `launch` checkpoint (`wait.sample(3000)`) in about 1 lane of 3, on its base too: a second GMS process and `com.android.vending`, started by the app's binds, answered `Worker is not ready: application` through `workerRows`' 2 s of re-reads. Past that capture they took 0.7, 2.4 and 14.5 s more to start (Flappy Bird's splash 0.7–2.4 s); after the 14.5 s start Doodle Jump's main loop wedged from `title` on, as the billing lock inversion (Doodle Jump) does when billing binds late, though that run was not kept. `workerRows` then waited before every read while the broker held a process starting (`wd.processStarting`, which asks no Worker), up to 30 s, and six lanes passed 5/5 in 46–51 s. With a process still starting reported as `starting` (below), only a final read waits so (lat's `processesAfter`): at a step that wait held the flow between steps for as long as any process started.
- **One guest clock**. Animation, draw and scroller time read `performance.now` while `ValueAnimator`/`SystemClock` read guest time; unifying on `uptimeMillis` made FitNotes runs identical. The controlled clock still advances to due timers while `drive` waits on real work, so virtual time per step depends on host speed (CalculatorM's ripple varied up to 17/255).
- **Pacing a controlled clock's frames** (2026-10-22, `7d262a8e`). A settle slept 25 ms after every stepped frame and spaced pixel samples by real time, so a 2 s virtual animation cost ~4 s (FitNotes' quick flow 40.6 s). Frames that advance the clock now wait only for their presentation, and samples are 50 ms apart: ~25 s, same diff. Without the wait, frames at a frozen clock flooded SwiftShader (900% CPU; FitNotes 399 s with 16 failures); 250 ms virtual steps instead of 64 caught home-final on its loading spinner (13.9% against 3.06%).
- **Rejected: a controlled clock for `--full`** (2026-10-22). Capturing FitNotes and Calculator M on the controlled clock took `--full --jobs 1` from 88 to 34 s, but FitNotes' mean content diff rose 7.92 → 11.95%: the goldens are frames after real seconds, and transients that only time ends stayed on screen (each save's "[PERSONAL RECORD]" banner over the tabs, 2 → 19%; the welcome dialog mid-transition, 9 → 79%). The reference's dwell (next entry) recovers most of it.
- **A controlled clock passes the reference's dwell** (2026-10-23). The recorder stores each step's dwell, the real time from the end of its action to its capture, and a controlled settle advances at least that far (`max(horizon + window, dwell)`). On the same goldens at `--jobs 1`, FitNotes scores 10.71% without the dwell and 8.89% with it (welcome-ok 78.6 → 9.3%) in ~23 s, against 6.64% in 75.8 s on the real clock; Calculator M is unchanged at 12.09%. The rest is the saves: each set's "[PERSONAL RECORD]" banner still covers the tabs (19.6% against 2.2%) and saving the new exercise differs 32.0% against 12.7%, so what dismisses them follows a time the controlled clock did not pass (the next entry). A recording at animation scale 1 scored the same (8.83%), so the goldens stay at scale 0.
- **A controlled dwell at the app's pace** (2026-10-24, `0bf021ed`). `advanceBy` ran the dwell's timers in one jump. A clock-port tick is acknowledged before the Worker's looper runs what it woke, and on a controlled clock a frame comes from the host's refresh only once guest time has passed, so a window drew its first frame after the dwell. FitNotes' list then armed its scrollbar fade (`initialAwakenScrollBars`, 300 ms × 4) at the dwell's end and was captured opaque, and the personal-record banner outlived the reference's. The dwell now waits for each instant's main-thread work (a `job:` or `queued` busy key), steps a frame every 64 ms while one is asked for and only then moves the clock: saves 19.7 → 2.1%, new-save 35.0 → 12.7%, cat-biceps 17.4 → 12.7%. Alone, alternating against the jump, FitNotes took 23.0 and 22.9 s against 23.4 s (2.93% against 5.84%).
- **A compared state step settles past its state** (2026-10-24, `dbf02081`). The recorder waits for a state step's condition and then settles by the pixel rule; webdroid captured the first presentation once the text was in the tree, which caught Mihon's onboarding mid fade-in in two of three runs (launch 19 → 45–49%). A scored state step now settles as a quiescent one does once its condition holds; motion and smoke captures keep the presentation.
- **Controlled-clock traps** (2026-10-03..12). A real 60 ms tap hold passed the 400 ms long-press deadline on a controlled clock; UP now follows DOWN by 60 guest ms. Delayed messages (AbsListView's click 64 ms after UP) never ran unless the clock kept running through the pixel sampling window. Pinning `Date.now` froze `currentTimeMillis` and splash conditions; guest wall clock is start plus guest time. A settle that advanced the clock once left a later Calculator ripple requesting a frame every vsync (3 of 5 runs timed out); the probe now advances 64 ms per sample while a vsync is scheduled, up to 2 s. Non-live flows run offline because Mihon's update dialog covered every screen.
- **Rejected: a controlled clock for the Compose flows** (2026-10-23). Komikku and Mihon wait for state, and a state wait on a controlled clock advances to the next deadline only up to its virtual bound: with that bound at 0 they never advanced, and advancing only while the app's queues were idle starved launch, which keeps timers due while it builds (Komikku 294 s; mihon-onboarding failed its launch). They stay on the real clock.
- **Goldens at the flow's geometry** (2026-10-23). The recorder sets the device to a flow's `screen`, else the default 1080x2160@480. FitNotes, Mihon and Harmonic declared none, though their positional taps were measured at 720x1280@320, the geometry of their goldens, so a re-recording ran them on another layout. They now declare it, and a run whose geometry differs from its goldens' is not scored. The reference AVD is shared: another checkout's app took the foreground mid-recording (its frames landed in FitNotes' steps, and a tap anchor went missing), and once a second emulator attaches, adb without `ANDROID_SERIAL` refuses. Check every recorded frame, and restore the display (`wm size`/`density`) after recording.
- **A launch's state, on both sides** (2026-10-23). Mihon's launch steps waited for any 2–3 presentations, which early window frames give before the app draws, so webdroid captured black (content 100%). They now wait for their screen's text, and the recorder waits for a `state` wait's condition in its UI dump (`stateMatches` in `lib/settle.ts`, which the probe serialises) before it settles. The device still captured Mihon's splash: Android 12's SplashScreen keeps its starting window up to 5 s when onboarding never marks the app ready, while the content tree uiautomator dumps already holds the text, and the static splash met the pixel rule. The recorder now settles only once `dumpsys window visible` lists no "Splash Screen" window (~50 ms a query): mihon-onboarding 31.2 → 15.3%, mihon-app 36.4 → 32.8% (its launch 100 → 41%). Recording also starts from home, so Calculator M's Back shows the launcher rather than the app recorded before it.
- **A Worker still starting is not a failed diagnostic** (2026-10-23, `90f4af86`). Doodle Jump's intermittent launch failure ("diagnostics failure", no message) was the harness reading the Play Store process it binds to late, which became ready 5.5–7 s after boot on a loaded host; one 2 s retry later the step failed. A not-ready snapshot is now reported as starting, and only a process still starting when the flow ends fails: seven serial runs (18.5–19.2 s) and two gates passed.
- **A launch has a launch's budget** (2026-10-23, `ddae55ce`). A step's action ran within its wait's real bound, so Resy's launch (a 10 s state wait) failed whenever its first boot, which installs the app and builds its code (7 s alone), took longer beside another flow. A boot now has 60 s and the wait that follows keeps its own bound.
- **A corpus launch beside the gate** (2026-10-23). `check --corpus` failed Among Us (no frame within 12 s, boot 5.0 s): alone it draws in 8.5–9.8 s on Chrome 151 and 156 alike, and an earlier gate passed it in 11.7 s, but here it shared the corpus browser with Amazon beside the flows. The gate's corpus launches now wait up to 30 s for their first frame; the wait ends at the frame.
- **Synthetic swipe timestamps** (2026-10-05, `daf308dd`). Delayed timers stamped a synthetic swipe's catch-up positions as if the finger accelerated, so Compose flung Mihon's settings list and a checkpoint intermittently missed; `src/main.ts` now passes the scheduled sample uptime to `src/shell/device.ts`. A forced 100 ms stall reproduced it; one passing baseline had not.
- **Pixel settling pitfalls** (2026-10-04..07). An overscroll stretch draws no new pixels, so a settle there let a tap land in the scroll. Repeated `-1` sentinel hashes once passed the stability rule, reporting `pixels` with zero presentations; the probe now requests a redraw until a sample exists and accepts only a nonnegative stable hash.
- **Mirrored frames queued behind a held-up page** (2026-10-15). A process Worker mirrored every presented frame to the probe as a full-screen ImageBitmap (~10.5 MB at 1080x2424). With the page thread blocked for 20 s, as an 18 s readback blocked it in a failing CalculatorM step, 622 copies queued; that is the renderer's growth per redraw before "Target crashed". The page now acknowledges each mirrored frame, and past three untaken the latest frame waits: 20 copies across the same stall, and the full ~30/s once the page is free.
- **A booting process is work under way** (2026-10-15). A settle that ended while CalculatorM's GMS process (pid 1003) was still loading packages failed the launch step's diagnostics; frames spinning at a frozen clock had kept such settles presenting long enough to hide it. The probe now waits while the broker reports a Worker that has not served its endpoint.
- **Probe readbacks starved the broker** (2026-10-12). Under SwiftShader a full-screen `getImageData` waits for the GPU process's backlog (0.3–1.1 s per Komikku sample, up to 6.5 s), and the broker answers app IPC on the page thread, so the app Worker idled. Readback beyond 150 ms is now limited to a fifth of the page thread: Komikku `09 manga` 7–21 s → 1.7–1.9 s; Calculator's pixel-settled steps slowed to 2–4 s.
- **Stable pixels are not success** (2026-10-05). A FitNotes pass used a coordinate fallback and ended on a spinner; a later failure reproduced on control: home was still loading when Start New Workout fell back to its point, so taps landed one dialog behind. Required text actions now wait for their target, coordinate typing for an enabled editable target, and virtual horizons let a fling end before the next input (`AbsListView.onTouchDown` ends a fling instead of selecting). Either fix alone failed.
- **Flows must require observable state** (2026-10-07). Reddit's One Tap sheet sometimes arrived after launch, so an unconditional Back closed Reddit; live waits can now require a state. TikTok's `liveSample(90_000)` was cut by a 50,000 ms outer bound; the watchdog now adds a live wait's dwell.
- **Reading process diagnostics** (2026-10-05). Capture checks each Worker's copied snapshot (`worker:<pid>`) against the broker row's PID, process and package, so a misrouted one fails. Root-only draw counters cannot see a displayed Worker; `wd.displayCanvases()` lists every shown surface. Aborted captures once lost all steps and reported zero; completed steps are now kept.
- **Capture pitfalls** (2026-10-08). Page-level Playwright routing missed service-worker fetches, so a "candidate" Resy run executed the original bundle: patch at context level and verify with runtime markers. Resy's API uses the TCP/TLS relay, so an empty HTTP observer is not an empty API result. `locateText` searches the combined window tree, so a hit does not prove the view under a dialog got the tap.
- **Mihon launch checkpoint is black** (2026-10-09). The launch wait admits any three presentations, so the PNG is black while the UI tree holds the library (since 2026-10-23 it waits for the library's text; "A launch's state, on both sides"). Warm FitNotes runs lack PID 1002 at the pre-input snapshot, so diagnostics cover post-tap paths only.

### Profiling and attribution

- **Profiler pitfalls** (2026-10-03..09). A V8 CPU profile's first sample carries the gap since `Profiler.start` (`(program)` inflated to 15%); a forced full GC before a window deoptimized ~360 functions; inspector start (20–89 ms) admits extra compilation and does not align with HR time, so profiles show ancestry, not warm-window percentages.
- **Multi-target attribution** (2026-10-05/07). Bracket profiler start in every target with clock markers, stop all concurrently and clip to the common window: sequential start/stop made the app crossing delta (55,559) exceed the step's own (39,326). Keep harness work (`uiautomator.walkView`, page pixel hashing) separate: FitNotes' conservative boundary bound was 96.57%, largely the harness's `getImageData`. `WebAssembly.Module.imports` omits enabled js-string builtins; count from binary sections.
- **Measure in V8, not Bun**. JSC ran a loop 52% slower where V8 was unchanged and names no Wasm frames, so caller-class lookups fail; the toolchain has run on node since 2026-10-16 (Node toolchain). A posted yield-channel turn holds node's process open and an idle channel lets it exit; `--cpu-prof` writes nothing without an absolute `--cpu-prof-dir` and a process not ended by `process.exit`.
- **Stalls the timeline cannot see** (2026-10-13). Three readings of the same Komikku stall were wrong before a trace with V8's GC categories named it: a hidden process skipping vsync (its `Visible` marks never changed), a lost yield-channel wake (the main strand resumed only after an unrelated compile-worker message), and a slow page (a `fileAccess` answer took 84 ms). The Worker was in one engine task all along. A silent stretch with neither rAF nor the fallback timer means the thread itself was held: check GC and compile tasks in a trace before suspecting the scheduler. `lat --json` taps now carry the page's own timeline and long tasks (`page`), and the Worker marks served calls (`Serve`/`Served`) and visibility changes.
- **Idle waits and memory**. Bucketing a `lat --cpuprofile` by 100 ms (`scripts/lat/profsum.ts`) found the serial zygote phase, which now overlaps the APK download. A CDP heap snapshot aggregated by retainer path found per-realm CanvasKit, jar and framework-res copies; `performance.memory.usedJSHeapSize` omits Wasm memory. In OOM messages "8 MiB" is `allocSlow`'s heap-check window and "512 MiB free" is fixed text.

### Probes, differentials and debug hooks

- **Differential probes need large seeds**. API-33 probes found 0/1,000 differences in seven API recipes and 0/100 graphics differences on two small seeds, but 1,000-case graphics seeds had 58 and 74 (matrix/clip, glyph coverage, Porter-Duff edges). Its fixes were renderer edge cases (inverse fills, source-in coverage, hairlines, clip bounds); the API and UI modes are credited with none. The device fuzzer was removed on 2026-10-22: it needed a reference AVD, a signed probe APK and `adb reverse` per run, had not run since 2026-10-01, and those 132 graphics differences were never minimized.
- **Lifecycle differential through EventLog** (2026-10-04). Diffing each step's `wm_on_*_called` tags (30021-30065: event, activity, reason) from the AVD's `logcat -b events` against the page's `[I/EventLog]` found a never-sent TopResumedActivityChangeItem, a no-op `recreate` and a splash finish race. An AVD replay that diverges from the flow is not evidence.
- **Device and headless probes**. Build with d8 (`androidTool("d8")`), run `CLASSPATH=/data/local/tmp/x.dex app_process / Main` after `ActivityThread.systemMain()` (`su 2000` for shell-uid answers) and diff against the probe run headless; charset tables and UTF-32 behaviour came from this. Headless, only `zygoteInit()` (after `rt.linkNatives()`) routes `System.out` to logcat; ICU needs `mountPlatformData`, Typeface `loadSystemFonts`. `textcmp` waits for the bound application, not `bootMs`.
- **Debug hooks**. Set before boot (`page.addInitScript`): `__wdNativeTrace`/`__wdNativeStep`, `__wdWatch` (registers at pcs), `__wdSlowMs`, `__wdGlProbe` (`getError` after each GLES call). `wd.threads()` snapshots strands by Java thread with wait state and owner (`wasm:<strand>`) without calling Java; it shows owner cycles, not lock objects.
- **Reading a failed run**. `newErrors` is de-duplicated, so temporary `console.error` probes show only in `browserErrors`. A blank Flutter surface says nothing alone: diff the page logcat between two paths (Lichess).

### Benchmarks, sweeps and conformance

- **Kotlin-shaped workloads against ART** (2026-10-14, plan 0.6). G1 gained nine `kt` cases (final getter chains, a lone interface implementation, generic lambdas, parameter null checks, IntArray slot tables, field updaters, try/finally, a continuation state machine, stackless cancellation), and `scripts/wasm/g1art.ts` runs the same cut methods with `app_process` on the reference AVD. Steady state in Chrome against ART: getter chains 9.3×, field updaters 7.5×, null checks 5.2×, slot tables 4.8×, the lone implementation 4.4×, lambdas 2.4×, the state machine 2.3×, cancellation 1.6×, try/finally 0.5×; geometric mean 3.2×. Catches are not where the gap is (plan 3.5's typed catches). Unsafe already compiles to a per-field accessor, and an updater's offset is a runtime value, so constant-offset Unsafe (3.5's other half) would not reach updaters. For plan 3.6, a node `--trace-wasm-inlining` run of `ktGetters` shows V8 inlining all three getters (and a small helper per call) into the loop's second-tier copy, so same-module direct calls would add nothing; what remains per iteration is the casts, guards and loop fuel around the inlined loads. Skipping every cast (V8's unsafe `--experimental-wasm-assume-ref-cast-succeeds`) bounds what precise reference types could remove: node steady state 1.61× on the getter chain, 1.0–1.26× on the rest (1.17× geomean), and skipping null checks as well added nothing. Warm Komikku taps were unchanged with every cast skipped (sums of tap medians 333 vs 330 ms over three alternating `--gpu` pairs, scroll p95 17.2 vs 17.3 ms), so precise types have no tap to shorten. Plan 3.4's `acmp` on typed operands is two of those casts in an inlined helper. Its per-literal String globals do not fit the module builder: a defined global's index follows the imported ones, and units import globals (literals, statics) while they compile, so each later import would move it; a literal load stays a table read in a function V8 inlines. (Since 2026-10-15 code names a defined global before the imports are known; see "Inline literal tests".) The AVD's ART runs on the same host, virtualized.
- **G1 (`scripts/wasm/g1.ts`) must use the production loader layout** (2026-10-08). The harness flattened platform and app DEX into one Program, making library classes application-owned (production: `new Program(appDexes, platform)`); flattened results do not stand for production. The fix moved ArrayList steady 0.126→0.083 ms (Node) but linkedList 0.036→0.271 ms. G1 fails on incomplete samples or input drift; first-call time includes async entry creation. Shipping AOT keeps the suspending import ABI, so harnesses using it need promising entries.
- **Sweeps validate, not execute** (2026-10-05/07). TikTok's sweep exhausted V8's ~4 GiB Wasm code space because the ordinary World instantiated runtime helpers for discarded units; validating in the compiler Worker's `compileOnly` World (`cacae7a3`, `06230811`) cut peak RSS 13.8 GB (Bun) → 4.35 GB (V8). A zero-method report (split XAPKs once read only top-level DEX) never passes.
- **A benchmark is not conformance** (2026-10-23, `2da19ccb`). luni-util's ScannerParseLargeFileBenchmarkTest pushes 192 MB through a Scanner: 364 s, a quarter of all conformance class time, measuring Scanner and regex throughput that the other Scanner tests do not leave uncovered. It is excluded.
- **Rejected: sharding guava by test name** (2026-10-23). Building TestsForMapsInJavaUtil's suite takes 57 s of its 259 s (an empty filter), mostly 1.75M `NoSuchMethodException`s from JUnit 3's `getTestConstructor` probing each no-arg tester for a `(String)` constructor, so every shard would pay it again. Cheaper exceptions (see Caller stacks) shortened the class instead.
- **The corpus waits for idle, not a fixed time** (2026-10-23, `692f3642`). After its first frame a launch waited 1.5 s, while a Google Play services process was usually still starting, so most rows had no diagnostics. It now waits, up to `--observe` (5 s), until no process is starting and every main queue has stayed empty for 500 ms: at 3 jobs every app that drew but two games reports them.
- **Conformance runs need a frozen tree** (2026-09-30). The runner spawns a child per test class that imports `src/`, so edits mid-run contaminate it: run from a detached worktree at a committed snapshot, and do not start two suites at once in a fresh worktree (the first builds sql.js).
- **Conformance false failures and accounting** (2026-09-30..10-07). Test names embedding `Instant.now()` or the date (`TCKInstant`, ojluni-time's TestZoneTextPrinterParser) report "passing tests no longer ran" yet pass alone. TestTextParserWithLocale's non-ISO `chronoLocalDate` names (`Japanese Reiwa 8-10-16`) did so on every day but the baseline's (2026-10-16); `clockFree` now names a Japanese, Minguo, Thai Buddhist or Hijrah date within a day of now, converted by `Intl`, `<now>` after its chronology. TestZoneTextPrinterParser (86 s alone) exceeds the 240 s class timeout beside other suites. A class setup failure losing baseline-passing names fails coverage even if both tiers lose them. The runner adapts TestNG 7.4's array providers to Android's `GenericArrayTypeImpl` without changing bytecode. `OutputStreamTesterTest`/`WriterTesterTest` hide private `suite()` methods: a known 124-case gap. An empty result buffer does not mean nothing ran: Guava Maps hit 600 s after ≥237,568 tests because suite construction took 445 s.

### Test-runner and tooling traps

- **Slow or misleading tests** (2026-10-02..12). BigInteger `shiftRight(Integer.MIN_VALUE)` takes 8 s: AOSP allocates a 256 MB magnitude per operand. `customdiff` takes ART's answers for byte-array-view atomics, which JDK 22+ rejects.
- **One World at a time in a harness realm** (2026-10-22, `cb347f74`). `lib.ts current()` makes each entry install its World as the realm's Java. jbench makes a World per workload; a Cleaner of an earlier one, run as its objects were collected, entered Java through that wrapper while the next World's startup waited in `getpwuid`, whose `StructPasswd` then came from the old World ("a native returned a host value of no Java class"). A harness now closes each World it is done with (`World.close`), after which its Cleaners no longer run: jbench 1 of 5 → 8 of 8 passing. A first fix that let only the latest World clean, decided inside the harness's entry wrapper, also stopped Cleaners of Worlds still in use.
- **Suites at once** (2026-10-22). Serial `wasm all` took 150 s (threads 36, fuzz 27, sweep 23, jbench 11, conform-quick 9). Its 23 processes (six fuzz seeds, entry and entrytier, hostclass alone and over the platform build) now run half the cores at once, longest first by `~/.cache/webdroid/wasm-times.json`, with the platform build made once beside them: 20–25 s, 5.7 GB peak RSS. javac, d8 and the Ref fixture's JDK answers are cached under `~/.cache/webdroid/java` by the tool, its options and its inputs' contents (a missing classpath entry counts as missing), and an entry unused for a month is pruned: loaders 9.9 → 3.9 s, fuzz 28 → 7.5 s warm. Other JDK runs are not cached, since some time threads or read the directories their arguments name, and the thread cases' JDK run keeps its 120 s timeout. The sweep's worker threads (8 alone) each sweep their share of every target, loading the platform once each: 7.4–8.7 s and ~70 s CPU alone, against 12–18 s and ~120 s with a worker per target and shard. Parts that run jobs of their own (sweep, conform quick) took 8 and 7 more beside `all`'s 7; given half of its jobs through `WEBDROID_JOBS`, `all` took 16.5–18.5 s and ~180 s CPU against 20.3–21.5 s and ~206 s, alternating.
- **One pool for the gate** (2026-10-23, `54266f6b`). `check` ran its lanes one after another; it now runs every task (the platform build when stale, the typecheck, three test shards, each part of `wasm all`, each flow) in one pool that admits a task while the cores and gigabytes it declares are free, largest time × memory first, and stops at the first failure: ~83 s and 909 CPU-s with the quick flows, and 90–103 s (900–963 CPU-s) once the four flows with goldens score every step, on a host other sessions loaded (load average 6–25). The flows are the longest chain: memory admits two browsers (4.5 GB each of 13.2), and the two that start beside the heaviest wasm parts take about twice their time alone (FitNotes 45–52 s against 24 s; Komikku 46 s against 27 s, and 28–29 s when it starts at 36 s). The order follows each task's last time, so a flow slowed at the start tends to start first again. Rejected: room for three browsers (120 s; FitNotes 74 s, Mihon 66 s), the other tasks at nice 10 (91, 95 and 97 s against 90 and 95 s, alternating) and a dev server already warm (FitNotes 50 s, Mihon 39 s), so the first two launches contend with each other.
- **The gate within 90 s** (2026-10-24). Until about 55 s into a gate the host had about 80 MB free and compressed 100–400k pages a second, and CPU idled 1–7% until 60 s and 14–25% after; mihon-app took 41–46 s and Komikku 42–44 s from t=0 against 23.6 and 25.9 s alone. Three changes: scoring took less than half the time (`ba7d8bb6`: pngjs chose among five row filters for each side-by-side, and pixelmatch ran a second pass for a mask the overlay holds; FitNotes 5.7 → 2.7 s, every number unchanged). Each flow's browser sees 8 cores (`37a3b601`, `WEBDROID_CORES`: `Emulation.setHardwareConcurrencyOverride` on the page and on each worker it starts, auto-attached and held at its start): a launch had sized its compile shards by 14 cores, 7 a browser beside the 4 the pool gives it; alternating gates took 104 and 85 s against 106 and 92 s, the flows' summed time 7–10 s lower. The pool ranks by memory, then time (`2ce6b524`): ranked by time × memory, the second and third unit-test shards waited behind the small flows for a browser's memory and ended 2–10 s after the last flow in four of four gates; replayed over those gates' durations, 89.0 → 84.9, 101.5 → 97.8 and 91.3 → 88.9 s. With all three, three gates took 89, 83 and 84 s, ending on a flow. Rejected: the other tasks at utility QoS (`taskpolicy -c utility`). The flows that start at t=0 sped up (mihon-app 32–35 s against 41–44), but the unit shards took 43–45 s, one ending near 118 s, and two wall-time tests failed: `app.browser`'s animated GIF, sampled 150 ms after `start()` on 100 ms frames, so a slow draw lands on the next loop, and `webview-provider`'s fragment navigation (a Java exception with no message). Neither has failed in an ordinary gate.
- **Abandoned Worlds' parked threads** (2026-10-22/23). A process per class paid ~0.8 s of imports and startup (TCKClock: 0.4 s imports, 0.4 s `startProcess`, 1 s running). A long-lived worker with a fresh World per class ran 10 classes in 13.4 s against 20.5 s, but kept every class's World: workers reached 1.7 GB (3 GB with a collection between classes), and classes slowed as the heap grew (full ojluni-time 180 s at 4 jobs against the base's 127 s). Two retainers: Tiering's retire wait, a 5 s host-loop timer whose closure held the Tiering (cancelled when the World closes), and V8 (Node 24), which roots a JSPI stack suspended in a Suspending import until it resumes, with everything its frames reach; a pool's worker threads left in `LockSupport.park` (three after a TestNG class) never resume. `World.close` ends every wait nothing else would (parks, monitor waits and entries, class-initialization waits), and a resumed wait of a closed World throws a host error, which Java's catch clauses (the Java tag only) let through to the strand's entry; the thread runs no more Java. With conformworker closing each World, the heap after a class stays ~60 MB over 27 classes a worker: full ojluni-time 53 s at 7 jobs, without the 1.5 GB recycling that preceded it (57 s).
- **Waits that do not wait** (2026-10-05/06). Playwright `waitForFunction` returns at once for an async predicate: poll from the host. Bun's `rejects.toThrow` hung on port-transported rejections. Bare-path `bun test` picked up ignored recovery-checkout copies: pass `./tests`. `corpus` exits 0 with a crashed row.
- **Fixture bundle corruption** (2026-10-02..08). Intermittent browser SyntaxErrors under concurrent runs were corrupted Bun HTTP bodies of the ~7.97 MB fixture bundle (a repeated 65,536-byte block, backward shifts, a truncated tail); the fixture now materializes the build once before serving nine pages. Bun's trigger is unknown; other one-off failures did not reproduce. The same corruption still reaches the platform jars, which are served from memory: about 1 full `bun run test` in 6 fails 1–9 `app.browser` tests with `ZipException: End Of Central Directory signature not found` (2026-10-14). An instrumented failure had framework.jar (43,302,820 bytes) and a 372,077-byte jar each arrive at full length, with mid-archive bytes at the tail. Isolated stress against Bun and against 12 headless Chromium pages, with and without prior range reads, returned 0/48 bad copies, so only the loaded in-process fixture server shows it.
- **Fixtures that pass only over a small build** (2026-10-23, `5ecfe442`, `a494c074`). The AOT suites built a four-jar platform per run; over the build the flows load, persist's sharded fixture took every class after the platform's, the host classes among them, whose patches land in platform modules, so which shard held them decided whether "another shard's code loaded" held. The fixture now builds its own classes alone, and `wasm all --aot <build>` runs the AOT parts over a given build (the gate passes its own). A oneway `IServiceConnection.connected` runs detached, so under load a test read its connections before delivery (`356f66eb`).
- **Thread-ordering fixtures** (2026-10-05). A queued-monitor order `20,1,…,19` came from a fixture recording invocation, not acquisition, order. Independent host entries on one Java main thread are same-thread class-init re-entries (IntegerCache seen half-initialized); use real `Thread.start/join`.
- **Proxy and server traps** (2026-10-08..13). A timing proxy must keep HEAD headers (a dropped content-length broke APK ZIP loading) and forward WebSockets, or timings describe offline runs. A long-running dev server keeps the old imported format version, so after a bump the AOT manifest 404s and runs lack persisted code. The platform key kept naming `oracle.ts` after its rename, so for a stretch of commits pages compiled the platform on demand.

## Compiler

### Symbolic sites

- **First-execution resolution** (2026-10-07, `e722bae5`). App symbolic references resolve at first execution through the initiating Java loader; a compiled opcode adapter replaces the initial table thunk. Batching sites before their owners resolved produced 43 modules; batches take only resolved owners and never extend a sealed batch.
- **Sites emit from operand metadata** (2026-10-09). Site metadata gives each input's DEX register and ABI kind; after loader resolution the linker decodes only the executed instruction and emits the field/call/cast operation, instead of preparing and typing the whole method per site. Warm FitNotes exercise list 239.2/215.5 → 193.5/192.5 ms; calendar taps mixed.
- **Bounded reuse of operations** (2026-10-09). Operand identity (aliases, ABI, opcode, reference, caller class) is nearly emitted-code identity (of Calculator's 43,550, 461 repeats differed only by an `init` import omitted after initialization). The World keeps 2,048 recent functions under that key; a hit installs one without compiling (except links with invalidation dependencies or module-owned state). Calculator first-digit modules 1,993 → 1,447; latency mixed. Since the static default namespace, symbolic sites barely occur (Mihon 18), so rewriting each site in place does not pay.
- **Stable helper names** (2026-10-08). Resolved helpers are named `symbolic:operation` (no `MethodInfo`; stack recovery skips them), so identical bytes reuse engine code with no runtime cache. 12,000 helpers: 695.79 → 352.29 ms, RSS growth 481.5 → 116.5 MB.
- **Rejected: helper batching and caches** (2026-10-08). In TikTok, 11,446 of 45,073 eligible helpers were byte-exact duplicates. Not integrated: whole-method batching (more heap, blank YouTube home); 4-op basic-block batching (FitNotes instances 32,738 → 31,550, heap +0.6 MB, no compile gain); inline resolution via shared imports and a mutable slot (no compile gain, Resy final heap 1,059 vs 844 MB); an exact-export cache (instances 16,220 → 15,473, keys retained 1.4–5.8 MB, no app gain). Reuse needs a sound identity boundary and measured retained memory.

### Loops, chunks and stack depth

- **Outlined loop chunks** (2026-10-05..06, `9abe6438`). Loops and dispatch cycles re-enter outlined functions with typed activation state and preserved fuel, so long activations reach optimized code; code grew ~17%. Each irreducible/handler cycle is one chunk. Tier 2 keeps loops inline unless one ran 64 chunks in one entry (`World.longLoops`): outlining them copied state per entry and shrank V8's inlining budget (inline: Node steady state 1.25× → 1.73× of the former JS tier). Rejected: moving an inline tier-2 loop into a chunk at a fuel tick (large methods stopped inlining), direct calls to final targets (neutral), callee-sized compile estimates (worse steady state), a JSPI yield fast path (a suspend costs ~55 ns).
- **Irreducible cycles** (2026-10-05). Rare (8 of 219,874 framework methods). Region strong components include handler edges from instructions not marked `NO_THROW`; in cyclic ones, non-forward edge targets are outlined so every cross-region cycle re-enters a compiled function.
- **Fuel on back edges** (2026-09-29..10-05). Fuel covers every back edge, including arithmetic loops, mutable bounds and overflowing increments (a finite-loop exemption was unsafe). A rotated loop's back edge can target a higher pc, so checks go on retreating edges, not `target <= pc`. A compiler stack overflow no longer installs a permanent missing-method body.
- **Chunk depth and frames** (2026-10-05). Entry reserved the largest nested chunk stack for the whole activation (Mihon SOE in Compose layout); a chunk charges dispatcher+chunk only while active, restoring caller depth on return and throw. Chunks declared the whole method's locals per recursion level (Komikku SOE → SIGKILL in recursive `reportGroup`); logical locals get compact native indices, while state struct fields keep method indices.
- **Physical local reuse** (2026-10-05). ChatGPT hit `$throw_soe` at 912 Wasm frames: MC allocated one Wasm local per web, and depth accounting charges physical frame size. `allocateWebs()` shares storage across disjoint conservative liveness intervals of one physical type: ChatGPT `ug0.invoke` 329 → 79 locals; skipping it reproduced SOE at 917 frames. Rejected: inflating Java depth.

### Calls, guards and CHA

- **Hot-call guard selection** (2026-10-08). Over its two-guard limit the planner dropped all guards, so hot iterators or `Integer.hashCode` among five `hashCode` targets were never copied. It now filters by the 64-execution copy threshold and inlinability, then keeps the top two. Steady listIter 0.158 → 0.084 ms (Node), linkedList 0.230 → 0.090 (Chrome).
- **Planner work** (2026-10-07..08, `be97a845`). Planning decodes prepared instructions once (845 → 169), skips the implementation-set walk while the allocated-class set has not grown (148,500 → 14,850 classes) and shares one CHA answer between direct and guarded planning (678 → 340 queries); work counts, not time. CHA reachability walks the owner's subtree filtered by receiver, not the receiver's subtree per owner.
- **CHA assumptions in `install` builds** (2026-10-10). In platform and app builds a CHA target never changed the emitted call (guards cover only same-module targets), yet app overrides such as `ArrayList.size()I` invalidated 3,623 bodies per warm Mihon launch. No longer recorded (3 invalidations; Mihon first Dark tap 427–435 → 89–97 ms); final classes/methods record none (`eb7f97e8`). Limit: ART's link-time rejection of subclassing final classes or overriding final methods is not enforced.
- **Late overrides** (2026-10-07). A late interface implementation did not invalidate a slot assumed after symbolic dispatch; prepared identities now carry guards the override invalidates. Raw helpers decline unowned CHA devirtualization; absence-dependent helpers own negative slots and rebuild when classes arrive.
- **Direct calls in precompiled code** (2026-10-11, 2026-10-19). The first attempt crashed Mihon: a host-implemented method (`HostClasses.overlay`) keeps its bytecode body, which direct callers reached; without host classes it matched an unchanged rebuild within noise. Build code now calls a method of its own module directly unless the host takes its slot (`hostTakeover`, which the build applies as a process's World does; `83e95ab3`). Each unit's header lists the methods whose code holds another's (`held`: its direct calls and the bodies it runs in place), and `Assumptions.undoCode` undoes the holders with the callee, transitively; an undone CHA decision leaves them, since the decision is the code's own. Of the platform build's 416,130 statically bound calls 168,181 became direct. V8 inlines and optimizes only calls within a module. Komikku's repeat Appearance `frame` (median of visits 3+) went 38.2/35.1/37.8 → 32.9/32.1/33.5 ms in a first series of alternating runs, but 37.1 → 36.3 ms (means of three) in a second, within noise; with final binding and trivial inlining (below) it is kept for the calls those bind.
- **Final binding and trivial inlining** (2026-10-19, `a7135512`, `2d5600ad`). The 10-13 rejection's soundness gap was a spec implementing an inherited method of a final class (`HttpsHandler.openConnection`), reached only through a vtable repair. A call of a final class or method now binds statically unless a spec class between the call's class and the target's implements its name and descriptor, or one the Program lacks does (`hostKeys`, by spec class, which the build ships in its index, since only a build sees the specs a split bundle registers late; a World without a build binds none). Keying by name and descriptor alone, as first built, left about 4% fewer of a build's calls bound. A statically bound call of an empty method, a field accessor or a static check that returns at once unless its first argument is null runs in place (`inline.ts`); a null receiver throws at the call, and a null first argument of a static method takes the call. The index also lists the slots the host takes (`overridden`), so code compiled at run time inlines none of them. Komikku: 186,621 of 259,810 virtual sites bind statically, and 35,837 of 622,849 invoke sites are trivial (16,734 empty methods, 16,663 of them the platform's, 15,600 getters, 2,995 checks and 508 setters). Over three alternating rounds of the four builds (`--gpu --repeat 6`, means of each run's median `frame` of visits 3–7), repeat Appearance took 37.1 ms with none of the three, 36.3 with direct calls, 35.6 with final binding as well and 32.3 with inlining too; every round of the last was below every round of the others. Settings was within noise (20.9 against 20.8). First visits moved with it: `start` 78/73 → 58/60 ms and `more` 55/71 → 43/53 ms in the rounds whose build files the server had read before. This supersedes the 10-14 and 10-15 estimates below, which priced a saved call at 6 ns of Liftoff time; the measured gain is larger.
- **Straight-line bodies in place** (2026-10-19). Of Komikku's 470,589 statically bound sites, 183,542 more called a small (≤ 16 code units) body that was not trivial; the leading shapes were a super call returning `this` (16,427, `StringBuilder.append`), a constructor calling only its super's (15,651), field stores around a super constructor call (about 22,000 over the `iput`/`invoke-direct` orders) and a static call whose result is returned (2,739). Such a body (`seq` in `inline.ts`: static reads, reads and stores of its parameters' fields, at most one statically bound call, a return; accessors and empty methods are `seq`s too) now runs in place: 76,296 more of Komikku's 622,849 sites, 112,133 in all. Its static reads run where the body has them, into locals, so class initialization keeps its order. The call's Wasm frame carries the method's own frame (`Code.inline`, then `FnTrace.inline`, in `aottrace` from build VERSION 20), so stack traces and caller-sensitive lookups see it; a static read that initializes a class throws in that frame too. Code holding a body records that it holds it (`Compiler.dependsOn`), which `Assumptions.undoCode` follows, now also for code compiled at run time. Code modules grew 2.7%. Over three alternating rounds against the trivial-only build (`--gpu --repeat 6`), repeat Appearance `frame` was 33.7/34.3/32.4 against 33.4/35.5/35.8 ms and Settings 17.0/20.0/17.1 against 22.3/18.9/16.9; first visits and launches were within noise once the run that first read the new build was set aside. Rejected: running a body's call of another such body in place too (three deep; 20,295 of these bodies call another and 26,891 a real method). Over two alternating rounds against the trivial-only build, repeat Appearance took 34.1/33.9 against 34.5/35.6 ms, no better than one level, Settings 19.0/20.8 against 19.2/17.8, and first Appearance visits 119/104 against 101/93 ms. A body with no step, which returns a parameter, a constant or a static field, also runs in place (`29bea6da`; 114,960 sites in all). Rejected: running the one implementation that a virtual or interface call reaches among the loaded classes in place, behind a test of the receiver's dispatch slot, with the call through that slot otherwise (7,587 of Komikku's sites: 5,662 getters, 1,425 straight-line bodies, 427 setters and 73 empty methods). The test needs no CHA assumption, so a class loaded later takes the call. Over two alternating rounds against the trivial-only build, repeat Appearance `frame` took 35.8/35.1 against 34.1/35.3 ms and Settings 17.3/20.1 against 20.6/15.4. That is no gain, consistent with a tenth of the straight-line bodies' sites, so the 45 lines of emission were not kept. With every codegen variant kept as the default, the `WEBDROID_CODEGEN` switches were removed; `?aot=<key>` still compares builds, the server resolving the key behind its source check (`pnpm aot --keep` adds one).
- **CHA only for targets the module holds** (2026-10-14). A single CHA target changes emitted code only through `callGuards`, as a guarded direct call to a method of the same module, and the guard compares slots at run time. `Compiler.devirt` now answers "many" unless the module is direct and defines a method of the call's name and descriptor. This skips the query and the assumption, and the invalidation on class arrival, for one-method first-tier units and for merged and precompiled code; codegen is unchanged. A node compiler benchmark (one unit per Komikku method, as on-demand compiling makes them) went from 138 to 129 µs per method. g1 first calls were 1.05–1.2× faster. Steady state fell ~2% (`tryCall` 0.096 → 0.134 ms): identical first-tier code upgraded later, past `longLoops`' 64 chunks, so the second tier outlined its loop. Matched cold Komikku taps were unchanged (sums 588 vs 584 ms), and launch compiling fell 2,837 → 2,712 ms. Rejected on the way: no CHA at all (steady 0.76–0.82×), and CHA only in the second tier (steady 0.91–0.97×), since first-tier batches hold callees too.
- **Compiler front-end throughput** (2026-10-14, plan 2.5). The same benchmark (an even sample of Komikku's methods, 3,000 per rep) went from 129 to ~108 µs per method; every step kept the compiled output byte-identical (`a65229da…`). `Flow` builds each region in one pass with shared sort scratch (160 → 122 ms); `typeConstants` follows only registers a float read or a receiver reaches, copying block state on write (452 → 404 ms); inference keeps operand nodes in one `Int32Array` (440 → 384 ms); `planDepth` and the facts pass test opcodes, not names or maps of every instruction. A call's function type is memoized by descriptor, and `DexFile.methodTypes` answers by proto, so no descriptor is parsed or hashed per call. What remains is spread thin: module encoding (~13%, mostly copying `number[]` bodies into the output) and helper bodies. `Array.prototype.concat` of a body (which carries `functionRefs`) made `finish` and `build` 2.5× slower.
- **Final targets called through their slot, first attempt** (2026-10-13, plan 3.2; superseded by *Final binding and trivial inlining*). Of Komikku's ~230k virtual sites, 154k name a final class, 34k a final method and 35k a single CHA target (a static census of its call sites). Calling a final target as invoke-direct does sped g1 steady state 1.05× (Chrome) to 1.07× (Node); String rows gained 1.3–1.8×. Matched Komikku `lat` runs (3 per side, alternating) were unchanged warm and within noise cold. In 2 of 4 warm launches with the change, the app was presenting a frame when the `next` tap's DOWN arrived, and the whole response region moved while pressed, so the response clock failed. This happened in 0 of 10 warm launches without the change, and in 0 of 3 with it limited to final classes; the cause is not established. The platform build is compiled without host classes, and specs also register at runtime (`rt.registered`). A spec that implements an inherited method of a final class (`HttpsHandler.openConnection`, from `HttpHandler`) is reached only through the vtable repair (`vtableRepairs`), which a slot call bypasses. Final targets record no CHA assumption, so nothing invalidates such a call. Revisit only with host-aware invalidation and an app-level gain.
- **Trivial bodies, first estimate** (2026-10-14, plan 3.3; superseded by *Final binding and trivial inlining*). By bytecode shape, field gets and puts, static gets, constants, argument returns and empty methods received 5–7% of the calls a cold Komikku launch counted in each process (iget 4.7–4.8%; `AbstractStringBuilder.length`, `Integer.hashCode`, `Object.<init>` lead). Hot ones reach the second tier, which the engine inlines. Saving a call sequence on the rest is a few milliseconds over a launch.
- **`Math` transcendentals as host imports** (2026-10-13). As natives each call was counted, caught and Promise-checked; the intrinsic imports host `Math` (Komikku tap 112k → 15k crossings, latency unchanged). `StrictMath` keeps fdlibm. Intrinsics shadow natives, so natives under intrinsic keys were pruned as unreachable.
- **Null-check fast paths, first estimate** (2026-10-15; superseded by *Final binding and trivial inlining*). Static void methods that return at once when a parameter is non-null (Kotlin `Intrinsics.checkNotNullParameter` and kin, jsoup `Validate`) are the targets of 5.0% of Komikku's invoke sites (31,424; 24,183 of them `checkNotNullParameter`), 1.7% of FitNotes', 0.2% of Mihon's and 0.1% of Feeder's. At about 6 ns saved per Liftoff call, they bound a tap's saving far below 3% of it.

### Front end and register typing

- **Floating constants and handler typing** (2026-10-04). Numeric DEX constants need reaching definitions across CFG and exception edges to tell floats from null. In `prepared.ts` an int constant assigned after the last throwing instruction was typed float in the handler, so `l >>>= 32` shifted by 0.
- **Nullable `invoke-custom/range` argument** (2026-10-07). Null-check analysis treated it as an instance invoke and marked argument 0 non-null, so an array access skipped Java's null check and trapped outside the NPE handler (present at `5f45a4ea`); the classifier uses the instance-invoke predicate.
- **Catch resolution** (2026-10-07). Catch types resolve through their Java loader only for the active try range, in order, keeping the original throwable when one fails to resolve (ART `FindCatchBlock`). Only catch-all-only methods dispatch directly (`94491b6a`).
- **VarHandle arity** (`2f92c766`). A VarHandle call with fewer parameters than required values is rejected before storage emission; the bridge throws `WrongMethodTypeException`.
- **Shared lowering primitives** (2026-10-20). Dispatch slots, field stores with their claim hook, null and bounds checks, element access, `callNamed` and `allocBytes` are lowered once in `j2w.ts` for `method.ts` and the link synthetics. The copies had drifted into three bugs: a reflective call of an Object-declared interface method took the IMT slow path, Unsafe/VarHandle/Field/MethodHandle stores skipped the claim hook, and a String `<init>` result missed move-object copies (about 50 lines of String-alias analysis in `infer.ts` went with it). The platform AOT build stayed byte-identical (2174 files) after each refactor commit. RtName (56) and HelperName (27) are typed registries.
- **Selection over the World's Program** (2026-10-20). App-build selection runs over `Program.resolve` (`reach.ts` callClosure gone), and an interface `<clinit>` is selected only when code touches the interface itself, as in ART: FitNotes 3492 → 3492 methods, AntennaPod 9746 → 9744, Mihon 27321 → 27308, Mihon selection 224 → 185 ms. The symbolic operation cache is deleted: on Calculator `--warm` no symbolic site was installed at launch or at any tap.

### Binary emission and module size

- **Unused imports pruned** (2026-10-08..09). Modules declared every host function; encoding drops unreferenced ones. Single-method sweep bytes −28% (batched platform AOT ~0.2%); helper heap per instance 6,042 → 1,642 B. Only surviving import signatures are interned, per build: 2,000 field helpers 418 → 250 B each, build+instantiate+run 45.9–53.4 → 27.7–33.8 ms; no reliable FitNotes change.
- **`ModuleBuilder` encoding** (2026-10-08). Writes ASCII names in place into section-level arrays: 737 captured builds ×30, 153.34 → 82.36 ms Node, 259.65 → 79.66 ms Chrome. Relocating in the final copy was dropped to keep relocatable bodies apart from custom-section bytes.
- **Prologue helpers** (2026-10-11). Wasm/DEX size is 5.72×. The inline call counter (~34 B) and stack check became per-module `count`/`enter` calls: module code −15%, launch lazy compile only 259 → 245 ms. Binaryen `-O1` shrinks app module code 17.5% (a small compile-time lever).
- **Inline literal tests** (2026-10-15). A string literal read and tested its global in a function of its own (`$lit`, 103k of the platform build's 542k functions), which V8 compiled on the literal's first use and Liftoff called without inlining. The test is now inline (`global.get`, `br_on_non_null`), with one `$s:lit` per module that makes and interns the String. A defined global's index follows every imported global, and units import globals while they compile, so code names a defined global with three padded LEB bytes that `ModuleBuilder.build` rewrites once the imports are known (V8 allows at most 1,000,000 globals).
- **One cast helper** (2026-10-15). A `check-cast` called `$cast:X`, which called `$instanceof:X`, `iof` and `vtab`: two functions per class tested in each module (33.5k in the platform build). `instance-of` now calls `iof`, `implements` or `instArr` with the class id and display depth, and `check-cast` calls `cast(obj, id, depth)`, `castImpl(obj, id)` or `castArr(obj, id)`. One helper that branched on the kind (depth −1, −2) cost steady state: G1's `ktLambda`, three `Integer` casts a call, ran 15% slower in steady state, and 3% faster once split (Chrome, alternating runs; `ktNullChecks` 1.22× from inline literals, the other `kt` and `casts` rows within 3%). The counts and runs below used the single helper. With inline literals the platform build's code modules define 407k functions instead of 542k and are 1.2% smaller (163,980 vs 165,940 KB). One traced warm run each against the warm Worker alone (host load 15–21): before the first tap the app Worker compiled Calculator 15.7k → 7.1k functions (125 → 73 ms), FitNotes 13.2k → 5.7k (90 → 64 ms) and Komikku 37.3k → 18.7k (537 → 479 ms), and the page thread 7.5–9.4k → 4.0–4.5k; the functions removed were small, so compile time fell less than their count. In 150 ms tap windows Calculator compiled 442 → 166 functions (9.7 → 4.4 ms), FitNotes 172 → 346 (6.7 → 6.9 ms) and Komikku 563 → 517 (65.9 → 26.4 ms; one base tap drew no frame). Over three alternating warm `--gpu` pairs (host load 9–21) the sums of tap medians went Calculator 123 → 106 ms, FitNotes 196 → 179 ms and Komikku 824 → 718 ms, but per-run sums improved in every pair only for Calculator (FitNotes in two, Komikku in one), and Komikku missed the 150 ms gate on both sides.
- **Class-global import metadata** (2026-10-07). WebKit 26.5 runs modules importing typed GC globals, but `WebAssembly.Module.imports` throws on them; unit headers list required class-global imports.

## Linker and runtime

### Class loaders, resolution and rebinding

- **Static default namespace** (2026-10-10, `64ed4a59`). ART resolves a `PathClassLoader` with the boot parent natively, so app DEX references link at compile time like boot code, not as per-site modules (`loaders.ts` `standardPath`); other graphs call `World.dynamicRoot()` before app code and drop saved app code. Warm Mihon instantiated 4,661 modules instead of 80,305; Calculator's first tap 330 → 65 ms.
- **Resolution goes through the Java loader** (2026-10-07). Recorded initiated identities failed: ChatGPT's GMS loader resolved uninitiated references to the app's stripped Chromium classes, and a child-loader reference returned the parent's class. Resolution calls the actual loader at the use point and caches it; cross-loader signature constraints are checked before class initialization.
- **Defining-loader ownership** (2026-10-07, `0190838c`). Proxies keep the Java-supplied loader; equal names in different loaders stay distinct; inherited platform classes are not the app root's; package access compares defining loaders too.
- **Shared libraries and the loader graph** (2026-10-05). YouTube's `org.apache.http.legacy`: a pending lazy-VFS read was cached as "no DEX" (now awaited), and Java `loadClass` found `BasicHttpParams` while compiler and host lookup did not. `sharedLibraryLoaders`/`…After` define parent/before/own/after order; the same DEX under another defining loader gets its own namespace. A graph change refreshes `MethodInfo`/`FieldInfo` in place, invalidates compiled callers, CHA and app AOT, and rebinds every dependent loader.
- **Native class lookup in ART order** (2026-10-08, `e94dce6a`, `21cdf1e6`). Verified ordinary graphs search parent, shared-before, each DEX, shared-after; custom, cyclic or changed graphs use managed resolution. `VMClassLoader.findLoadedClass` searches recognized path loaders even for unloaded classes, as Android 15 does; checking only initiated ones gave Lichess's `JniPlugin.setClassLoader` `NoClassDefFoundError: null class`.
- **Loader prediction for dynamite modules** (2026-10-12). Firebase loads through a `PathClassLoader` under microG's dex-less `FilteredClassLoader`; a parent-first prediction was contradicted by each define of the module's bundled Kotlin/Play classes (324 corrections, 21 s). A loader predicts its own classes first, after boot, when it is `DelegateLastClassLoader` or an app `ClassLoader` sits between it and its nearest defining ancestor. Resy 61–66 → 32 s.
- **Rebinding scope** (2026-10-07..08). Resy's onboarding-close wedge spent 85% of samples in `rebindResolution`. Rebinding rechecks only loaders depending on the changed namespace plus superclass/interface dependents, including unlinked interfaces in other loaders (same-loader-only fails rollback tests); single-name initiation narrows to matching signatures and inheritance chains. Descriptor-only rebinds keep the subtype index and positive assumptions; absence assumptions always invalidate. Samples 97.2/112.2 → 63.3/83.5 s.
- **Rebinding metadata refresh** (2026-10-08). Missing exact lookups binary-search DEX class/name instead of building every declared key (31.11 → 4.70 ms per 10k). `DexClass.refreshTypes` skips unchanged descriptors (456 → 261 ms; descriptors were already memoized, so allocation was not the cost). Cached headers drop only when their raw super/interface descriptor names the changed binding (491 → 341 ms).
- **Library rebinding touches only moved names** (2026-10-10..11). Shared libraries (`android.test.base` via `PackageBackwardCompatibility`) marked every class of affected loaders changed. `Program.libraryChanges` computes only names whose lookup moves (Mihon warm 9.4 → 8.5 s); the recheck walks declared keys up superclass chains (microG 325 → 175 ms).
- **microG's startup library rebind** (2026-10-18). microG's own processes open their dexes at boot and Java binds the root loader's shared libraries (`android.test.base`, `org.apache.http.legacy`) afterwards, so `World.bindLibraries` rechecked the ~9,000 classes resolving through them and held each process 136–157 ms. Finding the dexes that name a changed type decoded every type descriptor of every affected dex; type_ids sort by descriptor, so `DexFile.typesNaming` binary-searches each changed class and scans only the array range. The dispatch filter built each superclass's declared keys to test their signatures; DEX's virtual methods are exactly the overridable ones (ART's verifier enforces it), so `declaresVirtual` tests their protos' type ids without decoding a string. The hierarchy snapshot built every changing class's fields for a rename only static fields of a changed type need (`declaresStaticOf`). Invalidation materialized every method slot of the refreshed classes, though a slot without state holds code only if a live build module installed it (`AotRuntime.installed`), and late subtypes of every own class were computed with no assumption left to check. In Node the bind over GmsCore took 30–50 → 13–21 ms, and `android.test.base` alone (apps targeting Q or lower) 15–16 → ~2 ms. Four alternating profiled `lat komikku --gpu --warm` runs a side: processes 1002/1003's `bindLibraries` 138–157 → 49–59 ms; the app process's ~3 ms unchanged. Four alternating unprofiled warm launches a side moved work in Komikku's app process ahead of its first frame: native instructions before it went from 0.5M in every base launch to 2.9–13.8M, its own IO coroutine building the database through bundled SQLite (the slow mode of "A loaded dex's classes join CHA as they are linked"), so the first frame went 2,182–2,221 → 2,257–2,684 ms and first content 2,289–2,351 → 2,318–2,804 ms, while the app went idle sooner (median 4,490 → 4,060 ms) and tap medians stayed within 6 ms. What remains is spread: the dispatch filter's superclass walk ~10 ms, the descriptor refresh ~7, the new `Cha` ~6, invalidation ~5. Binding the libraries before the root's dexes would skip the rebind, but needs host-made library `Loader`s that Java's loader objects then adopt.
- **Main-thread context loader** (2026-10-05, `8124380a`). A cold system-loader initializer reaching `Thread.currentThread` left the peer a null loader; as in ART, the scheduler assigns it after that initializer succeeds, and an explicit set, even null, cancels that.
- **DEX order and packed archives** (2026-10-05..07). Multidex order is numeric, as in ART. `openDexes` reads only DEX from packed archives (`9ed28d3d`); rebuilding all 2,897 GMS files stalled Resy.
- **An archive the app wrote to its data directory** (2026-10-26, `6aba4562`). Firebase's C++ SDK writes its embedded `app_resources_lib.jar` to the cache directory and opens it with a `DexClassLoader`. `openDexes` read archives through the realm's VFS, which in a process Worker holds no app-data files (the page's kernel owns them), so it found no source and cached the miss for that path. Every later `FindClass` of `com/google/firebase/app/internal/cpp/*` then failed, and Bloons TD 6's START failed with "FAILED LOADING PLAYER" from `Firebase.Crashlytics`. Such files are read through their owner's descriptor (`withProcessSource`, which a library inside such an archive also reads through), still only the directory and DEX entries, and only opens that succeed are cached, since an archive missing now may be written later.
- **One unit install path** (2026-10-20). Every unit installs through `Units.link(LinkedUnit, place)` from its `UnitHeader`; `admits(head, epoch?)` gates version, natives and epoch for every route, and `World.changed` is the one Program-change fan-out. `AotRuntime.provide` must answer from the slot's state: answering from the module reported undone build code as provided, and the stub re-linked nothing until StackOverflowError (gms, warm FitNotes/Calculator). `dynamicRoot` undoes root code before `unbindApp`. A refused app numbering rolls back to the platform marks (VERSION/APP_VERSION 22). The hot-unit refresh and the combined `aot-build --app` image are deleted. Branch warm launches link fewer classes on demand (wasmClasses 171–522 against 754–852), uninvestigated.

### Dispatch, slots and CHA

- **CHA guard for running activations** (2026-10-05). Invalidating a table entry cannot change a direct call in a running module, so a native callback loading a subclass got the old implementation. Direct sites test the receiver's slot first, only where the unit has a direct function; indirect/AOT sites read the slot once.
- **CHA declaration lookup without an indexing cliff** (2026-10-06..08). A full implementor index cost ~190 ms; deferring it to query 128 moved the pause into FitNotes's first previous-day tap (102 → 181 ms). `Index.declaring` caches only requested signatures and finds DEX methods via numeric name IDs: worst lookup 126.4 → 18.5 ms, retained keys 138,421 → 535, tap → ~122 ms. A signature-only variant without the numeric index slowed the bulk probe and was dropped.
- **Declared-member lookup** (2026-10-05).
