AOT validation (`World.holds` → `Program.implFor`), CHA's wide fallback and method-ID decoding share `declaredMethod`, which scans class data without materializing members (TikTok live heap 1.94 GB → 571 MB; RSS did not fall). `Program.addClass` puts every declared method in `byKey`; a `$$host` class with an empty `byKey` broke only the layered Program.
- **CHA allocation and late classes** (2026-10-11). `World.late` indexed late classes under every supertype (~70 MB per TikTok realm); `lateBy` answers on demand. `Cha.subsOf` concatenated subtype lists per call (1 GB visiting ≤ 64 classes); walks read in place. A class load drops only memos about the new classes' supertypes and below. TikTok CHA 6.1 → 2.0 s, on-demand compiling 29.6 → 19.5 s in 60 s.
- **A loaded dex's classes join CHA as they are linked** (2026-10-18). Firebase's `DynamiteModule.load` opens microG GmsCore's five dexes (38,104 classes) in Komikku's app process during launch, and `World.addDexes` held the thread 98–129 ms: `Cha.index` ~38 ms, `Program.add` ~33 ms, the copy to the compile Worker ~13 ms, the assumption check ~9 ms, and `bindLibraries` ~12 ms rechecking dispatch for a loader with no classes yet. As in ART, where a class without instances changes no dispatch, a dex opened by a loader other than the app's and the boot class path's joins the hierarchy class by class: `World.link` adds the class and its unindexed supertypes before it can have instances, walking through linked classes too (an app class can extend a library loader's class), and rechecks assumptions against the classes that become reachable. Each recorded unit is checked against the classes linked late, because a compile can link a subclass after deciding about its supertype and a compile Worker's hierarchy holds only the deferred classes it linked. A decision they falsify is undone once the unit's code is published. A helper's getter clears its dirty flag before rebuilding, so an invalidation during the rebuild holds. A loader with no classes skips the rebind. `World.lateKnown` held every own class at its one read and is gone, along with the class scan behind the attached build's `rest`. In Node the open took 71–81 → 23–27 ms. Four alternating profiled `lat komikku --gpu --warm` runs a side: the app process's `openDexes` 98–129 → 42–51 ms (longest synchronous run 79–101 → 44–52 ms); class indexing and assumption checks over the launch fell from ~80 to ~30 ms, link-time indexing 0.2–0.7 ms. Unprofiled alternating warm launches: Calculator M's first frame 1,218 → 1,182 ms and FitNotes' 1,210 → 1,206 ms (medians of four a side), Komikku's fast launches 2,199 → 2,240 ms (ten and eight a side; first presentation to first frame 287 → 296 ms against an 11–16 ms spread). Komikku's slow mode, an IO coroutine building its database through native SQLite before the first frame (13.8M guest instructions against 0.5–2.7M, first frame ~2.6 s), came in 2 of 10 unprofiled launches against 0 of 10, 4 of 14 against 1 of 14 with the profiled ones; its native work began 300–400 ms before the dex open. What remains is `Program.add`'s ~30 ms of namespace entries and `DexClass` objects, which a lazy namespace would need every class enumeration (`own`/`all`, `libraryChanges`, `resolutionClasses`) to materialize, and the ~12 ms copy of 40 MB to the compile Worker across processes. microG's own processes still spent 136–155 ms in `bindLibraries` rebinding their root loader after its dexes were opened (see "microG's startup library rebind").
- **Host shells and CHA** (2026-10-05, `02e82805`, `a5749f0e`). Interface host shells register with the spec, not at first bridge crossing (earlier code bound the sole bytecode implementor); abstract host classes get a concrete shell before CHA.
- **Flat and shared layouts must agree** (2026-10-07, `94881d0c`, `148c7c34`). Duplicate abstract interface slots made shared-platform layouts differ from flat ones (ThreadPoolExecutor 64/58), a signature mismatch at Resy's `setKeepAliveTime`; abstract declarations are counted once.
- **Package-private dispatch** (2026-10-07, `d4534435`). Package-private declarations get distinct identities per runtime package; qualified slots select via the canonical declaration. The old selector gave `111111` where Java gives `112222` (split loaders); public-child fixtures hid this.
- **Default methods** (2026-10-07, `03e44b63`, `b40fec33`). Defaults select from canonical maximal declarations, not copied members. Concrete-default conflicts prepare and throw a lazy `IncompatibleClassChangeError` at invocation, as ART does (JDK throws AME). Wide CHA continues past an interface match, stops at a class body.
- **Slot identity keys** (2026-10-07..08, `5515b119`). The IMT key `class * 65536 + selector` collided above 65,535 selectors; nested maps keep both. `World.vslots` groups candidates by binary signature before override checks: FitNotes graph 1,047 → 508 ms, entries identical.

### Classes, initialization and monitors

- **Initialization and monitors follow strands** (2026-10-02..03). JThread-only recursion checks exposed partial statics to interleaved jobs. Monitor owners and initializers are `Strand`s (`suspend.ts`); waits form a wait-for graph, partial statics only on a cycle. Host code continuing a chain must carry it (`Cpu.call`; `caller()` for nativeLoad and JNI), else Java waits on its own native caller (Komikku's `ImageDecoder.<clinit>`).
- **Forked runtimes must not shadow the strand** (2026-10-03). `Runtime.within` stored the strand on a DexClassLoader fork's `cur`, so a monitor entered as one thread exited as another and threw IllegalMonitorStateException forever (YouTube never drew); it writes the root's slot.
- **System before SystemClock** (2026-10-05). `SystemClock.<clinit>` reads `System.nanoTime`, and a job racing `System.<clinit>` read null properties; `startMainLooper` initializes System first, as the zygote does.
- **Lookup and identity rules** (2026-09-26..10-03). Inherited static lookup must not rerun a superclass initializer; constructors do not inherit. A static belongs to its declaring class (a pinned native static nulled `Rect.CREATOR` via `Region`). A failed `<clinit>` gives EIIE once, NCDFE after. Arrays hash and compare by identity (`0ab7fe1a`).

### Reflection and stack introspection

- **Reflection semantics** (2026-09-28..10-07). Reflective calls widen Integer to long; `getDeclaredMethod` excludes inherited methods (LinkedHashMap serialized HashMap's data twice); fields keep declared types and final statics; `getConstructors` is public-only. MethodHandle lookup must not reuse public reflection, which adds inherited interface methods and drops package-private ones (`8b6991e8`).
- **Annotation materialization can suspend** (2026-10-05). Annotation constructors, boxing and enum initialization hit SuspendError under a plain callback; they run through `World.callFn` on the captured strand after saving caller state (`src/wasm/link/annotations.ts`).
- **Executable and Class metadata** (2026-10-05). Wrapping `Executable.artMethod`'s 64-bit cookie to 32 bits threw for class IDs above 65,535. `ClassValue` failed in ChatGPT because a native wrote host-only `JClass.ext` onto a Wasm Class; the intrinsic publishes the real `extData`, as ART does.
- **Member names from the host** (2026-10-23, `b0137bd7`). `Class.getName`, `Executable.getName` and `Field.getName` built their Strings with an info call per character (179M host calls in guava's Sets suite). The text import now answers the name, interned as ART interns member names: TestsForSetsInJavaUtil 23.5 → 15.1 s.
- **Stack-class lookups** (2026-10-02..13). `Reflection.getCallerClass` reads V8 wasm frame names (none in JavaScriptCore); `aotstore` fixes the URL names `compileStreaming` gives. `VMStack.getClosestUserClassLoader` walks to the first non-boot loader. Trace lines resolve from the frame's pc when read.
- **Field ids past 4095** (2026-10-20). A field id is class id << 12 | index (`compiler/fieldid.ts`), below Unsafe's array base for 2^18 classes. A class with more than 4095 fields (a large `R$id`) gave its later fields the next class's ids, so reflection read and wrote another class's field; reflecting such a field now raises InternalError, and a constant method handle to one is a gap. Widening the index to 16 bits was rejected: class ids past 16384 would cross the array base and turn negative, which Unsafe reads as no field.

### MethodHandle, VarHandle and Unsafe

- **MethodHandle, VarHandle, indy** (2026-10-07, `bbfc79fa`). Transformers run unchanged libcore bytecode; Android 15 has no `invokeBasic`/`linkTo`. Bootstraps run the real Java methods, allow races, cache no failures; CallSite targets are read per call. Atomic RMW needs raw accessors installed eagerly (a host export in the Wasm table was rejected); exact VarHandle sites bypass the bridge; MethodHandle dispatch uses the nested-entry quota.
- **VarHandle checks** (`5692dfb3`, `699ca7b4`, `ccd5dcbe`). The access mode comes from the boot enum field, not `AccessMode.valueFromMethodName`; type checks compare live Class mirrors (960,000 fewer `classOf` calls). Mismatched reference descriptors are admitted when operands are null: a null CAS had gone through `AsTypeAdapter` capturing stacks (15.07 M before Guava's first test), and Guava Maps then finished in 293.4 s instead of timing out.
- **Unsafe field cookies are 8-aligned** (2026-10-05). YouTube masks a boolean offset with `-4` and extracts the byte from `getInt`; cookies advancing by one aliased a long field. They advance by eight. Mixed native offset domains remain unverified.
- **Unsafe accessor cache in Wasm** (2026-10-09). Each Unsafe get/put/CAS rebuilt a key and closure (405,263 per 15 s Mihon scroll). Validated slots live in a Wasm i32 array keyed by cookie, operation and kind. 500,000 lookups 66.7–85.7 → 1.1–1.9 ms; scroll gains not established.

### Host classes and the spec boundary

- **Host code must keep overridable calls** (2026-09-28..10-03). `bun run bypass` found 786 TS methods skipping an override AOSP calls and an app declares (`onScrollChanged` never ran), and 406 platform `toString`/`equals`/`hashCode` answered with Object's. Host overrides set only under the public mangled name were skipped by compiled code; `implement()` installs both.
- **TS spec boundary** (2026-10-05). Platform specs fell 291 → 80; `desccheck` fails a spec method with an AOSP body outside `TS_SPECS` (`provenance.ts`). Flipping a spec exposes service calls the TS class never made. A class in `rt.markers` shadows the AOSP class.
- **Host construction of bytecode classes** (2026-10-12..13). `rt.make` of a bytecode class is a field carrier that cannot take virtual calls. A whole-host class's empty `<clinit>` overlay overwrote spec values (`EGL10.EGL_NO_CONTEXT` null); it applies only to an untaken initializer.

### Heap, memory and retention

- **Heap stays Wasm GC** (2026-10-05). Kernels favoured engine GC over a linear-memory collector with retained objects, and no measured loss was attributed to the GC heap.
- **Object model limits** (2026-10-04). Weak and soft references keep referents until cleared; none is enqueued. V8 accepts Wasm GC objects in `WeakMap`/`FinalizationRegistry`. Wasm cannot catch V8's stack overflow, so a frame-weight limit raises StackOverflowError.
- **Committed Wasm memories stall V8's GC** (2026-10-02). Committed memory counts as V8 external memory; near ~2x old-gen max marking restarts as it finishes (two 4 GiB memories: allocation 18x slower). A `byte[]` made before native code initialises is a JS-heap array (`HostBytes`), and guest memory is sized only when native code starts (Mihon tap GC 21.0 → 0.38–0.56 s).
- **Direct buffers, weak globals, owned handles** (2026-09-30..10-04). Direct buffers live in core memory (`VMRuntime.addressOf` had made every `byte[]` a permanent table entry). JNI weak globals are `WeakRef`s. `FinalizationRegistry` never returns the object, so creating natives return owned handles freed when their first owner is collected.
- **Closures retain their Compiler** (2026-10-05..08). `World.callFn` helpers shared a scope with the emitter callback, so 1,000 helpers kept 1,000 Compilers; a separate activation cut a sample heap 58.2 → 31.8 MB. `symbolicSites` resolvers, parsed AOT headers and traces of non-Java helpers are released (YouTube header samples 55.8 → 7.2 MB).
- **Merged-away and ensure() units retired** (2026-10-16, `1dddb8cb`, `038abf2f`). Retiring 32 units per collection, the next batch only once all were collected, fell behind TikTok's merges: after 150 s 74k merged-away units were queued, external memory reached 8 GB and the Worker spent 62% of its time in full collections that freed nothing. The next batch now follows the first unit a collection frees (512, or 32 while input is answered). Each class linked on demand kept its ensure()/<clinit> unit, which merging never takes (17.6k in TikTok); once the class is initialized the ensure slot takes the runtime's no-op, <clinit> its stub, and the unit is retired (2.2k live).
- **One lazy-link stub per signature** (2026-10-16, `cc4f5874`). Every call through the slot table first stores its slot in `rt.callee` (`CALLEE`, global 0 of every module that calls the table), so one stub per signature serves every slot of it: it reads the slot, links it and calls it again, and putting a stub back is setting that stub. Class modules import theirs by signature. Measured alone, a pool stub exported and set from JS cost 219 B of heap and 469 B of external memory, one copied from a second table inside Wasm 206 + 437 B (hence the rejected `put` below), one shared function about 16 B per slot. TikTok had handed out 495k pool stubs from 2,849 modules (79.8k never used); it now has 4,294. At its feed plus 60 s, after a forced collection, the Worker held 1,203 MB against 1,347 MB at matched work (66.7k/66.4k classes, 130k/129k methods). The platform build's class modules shrank 36.4 → 30.6 MB and its code modules grew 165.9 → 175.8 MB (+5.9%) with the stores. On 12 call-heavy G1 workloads (3 runs, host load 6–13 on 14 cores) the store made steady state 2% (node) and 3% (Chrome) slower by geometric mean, and first calls ran 16% and 9% faster. Calls from JS go through `invoker`. Removed with the pools: `Slot.stub`, the build's `originalStubs` and `host.stubSlot`. Made as classes asked, TikTok's 3,977 stubs came from 1,322 modules; an idle exported stub costs 198 B of heap and 480 B of external memory (one in a second table without an export 37 + 448 B), against about 38 KB of external memory per module, so the module that makes what a request lacks makes every signature the newly loaded dexes' shorties declare (`77455bde`): 5 modules and 23,082 stubs, built in 194 ms in all.
- **Program and dex metadata** (2026-10-16, `5046ba71`, `817ac8e7`). TikTok's 55 dexes define 462k classes, of which it links about 66k. In node, over its and the platform's dexes, the DexFile id caches took 53 MB before anything was read (arrays as long as the id lists), the app Program 84 MB (a DexClass with nine defined fields, the name and a cached descriptor per class) and CHA's index 65 MB (it read every class's supertypes, keeping a header each): 215 MB in all. Paged caches, names read without keeping descriptors, lazy fields that exist once set and supertypes read without a header bring it to 102 MB. At the feed plus 60 s after a forced collection the Worker held 1,156 MB against 1,223 MB at matched work. A heap snapshot of the 1.28 GB Worker never returned over CDP; node measurements of the same structures stood in.

## Code delivery and tiering

### App builds and persistence

- **Saved launch builds** (2026-10-05). A warm FitNotes launch compiled ~2.9k methods on demand and held the main thread 450 ms on a 130 MB streaming compile. A saved app build holds what a launch built on demand, and platform builds ship profiled apps' builds: warm 3.53 → 1.74–1.87 s, cold 4.06 → 2.15–2.55 s (loaded host, SwiftShader).
- **First launch without a saved build** (2026-10-11/12). Feeder took 3.2 s vs 0.76 s later; of 2.0 s compiling, 1.4 s was the compiler front end and 0.5 s instantiating a module per method. Even warm, Mihon recompiles 28 platform methods because an app class overrides one that platform code calls directly (`javax/net`).
- **A shown first launch waits for its app's build, made in shards** (2026-10-17). Supersedes the 2026-10-10 rejection of waiting for a parallel first build (six Workers then cut Calculator's first taps 181–187 → 38–48 ms but slowed FitNotes 2.48 → 2.61–2.81 s and Mihon 9.6 → 10.5 s), now that the target is in-app latency at the cost of a slower first launch. A shown launch without a saved or bundled build shows "Optimizing app…" and waits for a build of every method of the app's dexes, as dex2oat compiles an app at install. Shard workers each number the same seed (`seedApp`: the selected methods' classes first, so the likely ones get low counting slots, then the classes, array types and interface selectors their instructions name, then the host classes), freeze their World (anything else throws `NeedsNumber`, and that method is left to the on-demand compiler) and compile the code modules dealt to them by estimated size; shard 0 also builds the data and synthetic modules and the index, and `mergeApp` joins the parts. A link error's id is its message's FNV-1a hash, probed past collisions, so shards agree on it without coordinating. The build starts when the dexes are inflated, beside the zygote, under the prefix the platform build ships (`prefix.json`, which `aot-profile` writes when every profiled launch numbered the same one); a launch whose zygote numbers another prefix rebuilds after it. An app above `WHOLE_LIMIT` (250k method references) launches without waiting: the build of its roots' closure a launch could wait for covered too little of it (TikTok, below). The compile host starts with the first process Worker. Matched against the on-demand first launch (three alternating `--gpu` runs a side, medians, 2026-10-17), first taps fall to warm ones: Calculator M `7` 55 → 16 ms (warm 14–15) and `plus` 41 → 17; Komikku `next` 49 → 16, `more` 113 → 52, `settings` 78 → 45 and `appearance` 118 → 82; FitNotes' taps stay within noise. `start` stays at 101 ms against 111 (a font fetch, under *Large fallback fonts are read ahead while idle*). The first frame comes later: Calculator M 2.27 → 2.36 s, FitNotes 1.58 → 1.99 s and Komikku 5.97 → 6.69 s, of which the build takes 0.85, 0.51 and 3.0 s (6, 6 and 7 shards). Warm launches are unchanged once a rebuild leads with what the launch ran (below): first frames 1.30 s against 1.29 for Calculator M, 1.34 against 1.34 for FitNotes and 2.37 against 2.32 for Komikku (runs 2.30, 2.37 and 3.21 s against 2.26, 2.32 and 2.41 s), and warm taps within noise of each other. The `prebuild` switch that measured this was removed.
- **Shards, cores and memory** (2026-10-17). The shard count is one per two logical cores, one per 5,000 method references and as many as fit in 3/8 of `deviceMemory` up to 3 GB. On the 14-core host, 11 shards took FitNotes' build 1.14 s against 0.85 s for 6 (efficiency cores among them). Komikku's first frame came at 6.3, 5.5, 5.4 and 5.0 s with 4, 6, 8 and 10 shards (two launches each), and the compile host's summed V8 heaps (`Runtime.getHeapUsage` over its realms) peaked at 1.8, 2.5, 2.9 and 3.5 GB committed. One shard's heap peaked at 134–147 MB for FitNotes (26k references) and 330–510 MB for Komikku (157k), less the more ran at once; sixteen Komikku shards reached 4.3 GB committed (3.4 GB used) in the frame's 17 realms, and the frame survived. Only the build a launch waits for is sharded: a background process of a five-dex package with no saved build rebuilt in six shards 3–5 s after FitNotes' first frame, during its first taps; refines and such builds take one worker, and each shard's worker is retired as its part arrives. A shard's fixed cost, its script and jars, is about 290 ms.
- **One numbering, checked once** (2026-10-19). A World's ids, selectors, gaps, link errors and slot tail live in `Numbers` (`link/numbers.ts`), whose `frozen(fn)` scope replaces a public flag that five numbering paths each checked. `mergeApp` had keyed link errors by raw hash: when the lead shard's unfrozen data phase probed message D2 past D's hash h to h+1, another shard's message hashing to h+1 merged without complaint, then replayed to h+2 while its code threw h+1, so it reported D2's message. Parts now carry each link error's id and merge through the same `LinkIds` table, which refuses an id held by another message. Gaps are listed as they are numbered, the platform build's at attach, so unit headers no longer list theirs; gaps the host reports (`onGap`, now given the gap's text) are not numbered. Membership of self-loaded dexes' classes in the class hierarchy moved into `Cha` (`admit`, `reindex`, `members`). A launch whose saved build does not open now tries the shipped one before building, and an app realm whose dexes arrive without `beginAppCode` throws instead of running without app code. `DexFile.typesNaming` keeps its last answer for the set it was asked about, matched by identity and then by contents: a libraries rebind asks once per class it checks (*microG's startup library rebind*), and the identity-keyed WeakMap missed the sets its callers build apart.
- **Later builds lead with what the launch ran** (2026-10-17). `select` orders a build's methods as the app is likely to want them, those the launch compiled on demand first, and a build's modules take classes in that order. A launch with a whole build compiles few methods on demand, so the build made after it put the launch's classes behind the baseline profile and dex order: the next launch loaded 680 build modules against 614 and its first frame came 0.4 s later (Komikku `lat --warm`, which clears the app's data between its launches, 2.57–2.75 s against 2.26–2.30 s; a relaunch keeping the data was not slower, 1.98–2.18 s against 2.11–2.15 s). A rebuild now leads with the attached build's methods the launch ran (`AotRuntime.ranSlots`): 623 modules, and its first frame came at 2.30, 2.37 and 3.21 s against 2.26, 2.32 and 2.41 s for launches whose first was on demand (three alternating runs a side).
- **Refines only with methods a build can hold** (2026-10-17). A launch's on-demand methods of loaders other than the platform's, the app's and its libraries' (code the app loads itself) counted toward a refine that could never include them, so the watch kept rebuilding for them. `fresh` counts only methods a build can hold. Its check also dropped the platform's methods, whose dexes belong to the base Program's root rather than `prog.boot`, so every warm launch compiled them on demand again (2026-10-18): Calculator M 215 of its 287 launch methods, Komikku 347 of 466. Counted again, a warm launch compiled 76 and 138 (mostly host-class natives, which have no dex: 70 and 107), and GMS's two processes 186 and 338 against 423 and 560; Calculator's first frame came at 1.21–1.23 s against 1.28 s. Komikku's warm launch became bimodal: its first content came at 3.0–3.2 s instead of 2.2–2.3 s in 7 of 9 runs of the faster launch, against 0 of 3 without it and none in 20 runs on 2026-10-17; it went idle earlier (4.8–4.9 s against 5.0 s) and its taps were unchanged. The slow mode was the native JIT compiling its database startup (*The JIT takes blocks entered 255 times*).
- **Recording what a whole build's launch ran** (2026-10-17). A launch with a whole build compiles almost nothing on demand, so the watch's quiet-stretch test (new on-demand work) rarely changed and the build slots the user's first taps ran went unrecorded for the next launch's warming. Each quiet watch tick outside input now scans the count array, saving only new slots.
- **App builds with shared libraries** (2026-10-10/11). `appLaunched` fires on a first frame, so service processes never persisted builds; a 15 s start window now starts the watch. A build started with empty `AppIndex.libraries` was unbound by the first `bindLibraries`; it now starts from `watch` with the bound graph. Warm Mihon's microG inline compiles 2,491/351 → 13/0.
- **Kept builds and the library binding** (2026-10-13). Every warm YouTube launch died in `handleBindApplication` with `LinkageError: Class loader libraries changed linked hierarchy of areh`. The attached build's data module had linked `areh`, laid out under the shared libraries the build was made under, before `PathClassLoader` bound them; `rebindResolution` checked hierarchy and dispatch before consulting `keepApp`. With the build kept, classes laid out by its data modules (`ClassRt.mod` `e…`) skip those checks; classes linked on demand still fail. aottest covers an app copy of a class the library defines first. Warm YouTube then launched in 5.3 s (cold 14 s) with first responses 7–168 ms (cold 205–445 ms).
- **App builds must not duplicate reserved classes** (2026-10-08). Intermittent warm FitNotes StackOverflowError: an app data module replaced live cached code (`tagsoup/Parser.setProperty`) with a lazy-link stub while the slot said compiled, so the stub called itself. The builder uses the canonical `reservedBase` query; cache v12 rejects older builds. Not proven to explain every older overflow.
- **Refine share** (2026-10-12). A refine rebuilds the whole merged demand in a fresh Worker; TikTok's service process rebuilt ~80,000 methods for a few hundred new ones at peak main heap, crashing the shared cage. A refine waits until new demand is an eighth of the build (`REFINE_SHARE`).
- **Refinement during input: not integrated** (2026-10-09). Each Mihon scroll window completed a refinement (~30,800 methods, 6.4–6.6 s of builder work); input gating contradicted itself (52.73 vs 55.26, then 55.93 vs 54.87 presentations/s); an incremental `fresh` poll cut poll work 9.6 → 0.1 ms but not pacing (55.48 vs 55.11; reverted). The pacing cause is open.
- **A build's names for classes it lacks** (2026-10-28, `c68be81f`). After first-run setup, guest Chrome's browser process crashed on later launches with "function signature mismatch". It hit about one launch sequence in two, and every launch over the state it left. Deleting the saved app builds cured it. With the JNI stack in the message (`94e623ed`), the trap was in a symbolic site's operation for `new-instance vu5` in Chrome's `on_demand` split (loader 4). Chrome's build numbered `vu5@4` with a one-slot block. The launch's demand had named `clone:vu5@4`, and the build's World, which has no loader 4, made `clone`, `alloc` and `newarray` functions for classes it lacked, numbering each name with an empty block. The next launch's `vu5` took that block, its methods' slots ran into the following classes' blocks, and the site called another class's function. Those families now answer undefined for a class the Program lacks, as `synthOf` says they do. A class whose replayed block has another size now numbers its own (`SlotTable.blockFor`). From the crashed profile, the build without the fix crashed twice in two launches (6 traps each). With the fix it attached (`attached: true`) and Chrome stayed up both times. Its rebuild held no loader-numbered names. Before the JNI change only a Java stack was seen, from the chrome split's `c6q.k()` (loader 2), with system_server also trapping in `ConnectivityService.<init>`. That profile was lost, so those two were not traced to this cause.

### Platform build

- **Launch profiles and fewer launch modules** (2026-10-10). FitNotes' first launch entered platform methods 535k times, its own 49k (2026-10-01). Workers compile the platform C/D modules an app's launches loaded before its next launch (FitNotes 2430 → ~1900 ms). `hot.json` groups called classes into fewer C modules; runtime functions are precompiled into `y<k>.wasm`/`s<k>.wasm`, not one module each (−150 ms).
- **Source key and integrity** (2026-10-05). `host/natives.ts` emits import signatures but was outside the key, which now covers all production TypeScript and AOT scripts by path/mtime/size (max mtime missed deletions). A retry path that dropped functions V8 rejected now aborts the build.
- **Fallback class globals** (2026-10-08). Warm FitNotes had 258 synchronous link misses in one tap, all in platform code groups rejected because their data group had published fallback globals. The linker binds those imports through the canonical class-import binder and tries cached app patches after rejection; late data never replaces live class state. Launch compilations ~5,980 → 309.
- **Late data and prefetch** (2026-10-06..13). Late platform C modules link only against their original data globals. `AOT.ready` marks an unavailable module permanently missed, so a no-app realm awaits prefetch. Warm launches hung when > 16 modules were prefetched: `FetchStore.load` returned early on each completion's empty list (from `e951d359`).
- **Rejected: every boot jar in the platform build** (2026-10-14, plan 1.8). By jar, a cold launch compiled 302 methods (FitNotes) and 356 (Komikku) from boot jars the build leaves out: `framework-connectivity` 61–135, `ext` 83 (FitNotes), `framework-statsd` 45, `framework-connectivity-t` 22–32, and fewer from 16 others. Cold taps compiled 0–3 such methods and warm launches 4, since an app build keeps them. A build of all 40 boot jars (`aot --jars 40`: 54,727 classes, 265 MB against 205 MB) compiled about 300 fewer methods in a cold launch and launched FitNotes faster in each of three alternating `--gpu` pairs (medians 1,887 vs 2,073 ms) and Komikku by about 2.5% over six (6,160 vs 6,323 ms). Cold Komikku `appearance` was slower in five of six pairs, though, with the same methods compiled and modules loaded in the tap (median 160 vs 136 ms, past the gate), and the cause was not found, so the build keeps its eight jars.
- **An unprofiled build misses the zygote's modules** (2026-10-14). `bun run aot` alone writes `aot-startup`'s list, whose harness `startProcess` makes three Java calls: 199 files, none of the C modules a browser `zygoteInit` links. Warm Komikku waited for 70 code deliveries (~250 ms) per launch, each realm again. A process Worker fetching and compiling its own modules waited as long (218–226 vs 250–266 ms), so the page's relay was not the delay. After `aot-profile --classes`, `bun run aot` and `aot-profile` (397 files, `warm.json`), misses fell 46 → 12 and warm launch 3.0–3.4 s → 2.45–2.6 s. Measure warm launches only on a profiled build.
- **The startup list is the zygote's start** (2026-10-23). Every process runs `zygoteInit` before its app's dexes, and a platform class linked before its D module arrives puts its whole group in `fallbackD`: built on demand for the life of the process, after the module arrives too. Warm Komikku built 2,082–2,162 classes on demand (108–139 ms) in runs where ~50 D modules missed between `bootApk` and `dexLoaded`, and 209–497 in others: which modules reached the Worker before `zygoteInit` linked from them decided it. `aot-startup` now runs `zygoteInit` itself (`android/zygote.ts`): 356 files (C 16, 17.8 MB; D 329, 22.2 MB; y 11) against `startProcess`'s 135 (16.2 MB), which they include. On one build, swapping only `startup.json`, four alternating pairs a side: warm launches missed 0 modules against 57–80 and built 39–60 classes on demand against 733–815 (13–16 against 59–64 ms). Warm Calculator M presented at 1,458 against 1,582 ms and warm Komikku at 2.42–2.97 s against 2.36–7.53 s (its native schema build varies too); cold FitNotes at 3,041 against 3,168 ms (every pair faster) and cold Komikku at 7,053 against 7,244. The prefetch ends 30–100 ms later, yet the zygote is done sooner (`dexLoaded`: warm Komikku 1,096 against 1,467 ms, cold 1,341–1,391 against 1,431–1,509). Taps stayed within noise. Leaving out the zygote's nine further C modules let one of three warm Komikku launches reject its saved build on the order of `TrafficStats$1` and `TrafficStats$SocketTagger` (*Zygote numbering depends on timing*), so the list keeps every file the zygote loads.

### Launch path, zygote and spare

- **A launch's CPU** (2026-10-23, the browser's process tree by `ps`). FitNotes reaches its first frame at 17.6–20.8 CPU-s and Doodle Jump at 18.2, nearly all in the renderer (the whole-app build in shards and the process boots); Google Play services and the Play Store take about 15 more in the following 8 s. With `hardwareConcurrency` 2, 4 and 14 the first frame came at 18.7, 20.1 and 17.6 CPU-s (4.6, 4.4, 3.2 s): the shard count moves the wall time, not the work.

- **The start window ends at the app's first content frame** (2026-10-19). `starting` (`boot.ts`: second-tier upgrades held, no compile Worker, no `ahead`) ended at a process's first presentation, which composes only window colors. After the microG rebind cut, Komikku's first app draw came 510–810 ms later, with its own IO-coroutine database build and Firebase's dynamite open inside that window. There the compile client started (its `init` post took 18.8 ms), took units and copied each added dex to its Worker. The main thread's page calls (`fileAccess`, `transact`, `fileMkdir`, `systemService`) waited 237–451 ms in the window, against 52–63 ms before the cut. The page answered each in about 1 ms; the app Worker, busy, held the answers. The compositor now tells `present` whether a producer's buffer composed (hwui, canvas, GL and video all queue one), and the launch ends at the first presentation that did. The 15 s timer still ends it for a process that never presents, and now runs once: after a presentation it had started a second `watch` loop. Warm `lat --gpu --warm`, 4 alternating unprofiled pairs: Komikku's first frame 2,649 → 2,281 ms (every run faster), first presentation to first frame 661 → 327 ms, idle 4,274 → 4,014 ms, taps unchanged. Run alone, Calculator M went 1,237 → 1,216 ms and FitNotes 1,252 → 1,222 ms, idle unchanged. Interleaved behind Komikku runs they read 50 ms slower with identical compile counts, spread over phases before `dexLoaded` that the change cannot reach. Calculator's tap medians summed 112–115 ms with the change and 91–105 ms without over three batches (14 pairs); every tap stayed under 35 ms, within run-to-run spread. Against the commit before the cut, Komikku with the change was still 2,292 vs 2,197 ms (taps 260 vs 284, idle 3,917 vs 4,089): when the dynamite open lands in the window, `openDexes` adds microG's five dexes in one 32 ms call, and each fetched entry's SHA-256 digest copies its input on the calling thread for 15–20 ms, so main's longest page waits are 66–75 ms (at most 23 ms before the cut). Native slices in these launches ran at most 6.2 ms and main resumes within 1 ms of an answer, so the rejected 4 ms turns, priority inheritance and background priority were not revisited.
- **Rejected: joining a loader's dex files one per turn** (2026-10-19). `openDexes` added each fresh dex in its own turn when the opening strand could wait, and another loader copied dexes still joining. Main's longest page wait in Komikku's launch window fell from 48–76 to at most 27 ms, but its first frame did not: 2,269 vs 2,290 ms over 4 pairs and 2,222 vs 2,292 over 6 more, with the change slower. One run with it crashed the page during its cold preparation launch. Removed.
- **Zygote before the package** (2026-10-11). An app build records its `prefix` (what the World numbered past the platform build before app dexes) and attaches only to a World that numbered exactly that, or nothing. Zygote numbering is deterministic, so the spare Worker, spawned at page load, runs the zygote before the package is known: Calculator 1,373–1,399 → 768–779 ms from `wd.boot()`. With `?apk=` it waits: an early system_server boot competed with the app's AOT loads.
- **Zygote numbering depends on timing** (2026-10-13). Every warm TikTok launch rejected its saved build: `prefix: names […]`, the same five classes (`TrafficStats$1`, `TrafficStats$SocketTagger`, three `$$host` overlays) in a different order. All five are numbered on the main thread by `preForkInit`/`commonInit`, so this is not a thread race. When `RuntimeInit.commonInit`'s C module has arrived, `loadC`'s class imports build the pending classes and their vtables link the overlays first. When it has not, `commonInit` compiles on demand and links `SocketTagger` first. A process now keeps what it numbered before its app's code (`Persist.prefix`, per platform build) and the next process numbers that first through `World.adopt`. Timing then only appends, and the kept list grows until it stops changing. Warm TikTok attaches its build: 287 methods compiled at launch instead of 27,154, and launch 12.3 s → 8.2 s. The claim in the entry above, that zygote numbering is deterministic, does not hold.
- **Spare refill policy** (2026-10-11). Refilling after every process start chained spares that each warmed and ran a zygote: FitNotes 9–21 scroll intervals > 25 ms vs 0–1, 0.9 s CPU per unused spare. A new spare starts only when a launch takes one whose warm had not completed. (Since 2026-10-15 no zygote warms, and every launch starts a spare; see "Warming in a Worker of the page".)
- **Bitness before zygoteInit** (2026-10-02). `System.<clinit>` and `Build.<clinit>` read the ABI, so `startZygote` loads the platform app-free and awaits bitness from the install's central directories (known at 368–478 ms, before `zygoteInit`). Fixing it in `bootApk` would serialize `zygoteInit` (~0.4 s warm, 2.5 s throttled) behind the download.
- **Platform-first class path** (2026-09-30). PathClassLoader asks the boot loader first, so the platform is indexed and linked at page start, overlapping the APK download: cold first frame −150 to −220 ms.
- **Rejected: boot image** (2026-10-05). Of the zygote's 453 ms to `startZygote`, 12 ms was Java and 172 ms module loading. A heap loader would save ≤ 8%: statics live in globals of modules it instantiates anyway.

### Modules and instantiation

- **Stubs by name, saved headers as loaded** (2026-10-23). A data module's instantiation listed all its imports (`WebAssembly.Module.imports`, String constants too) to name its shared stubs; the stubs' import object now answers each name and makes a stub on first ask (in node, 330 synthetic modules of 300 stub imports each: 120–150 ms listed, 22–36 through the object). Warm Komikku spent 79.5 instead of 95–100 ms instantiating the 329 data modules loaded by `appCodeSaved` (141–142 vs 163–171 ms for the launch's 544). `wasmModuleMs` now holds the ~30 ms of building that stub module inside the instantiation that asks, and counts a module made there once, not twice. Opening a saved build parsed every module's header for its version, which `units.admits` already checks as each loads. Platform seeding and a saved build's replay reserve their slots at once. Over three alternating pairs, `appCodeSaved` came 41 ms and `firstPresented` 51 ms earlier on average, the first frame 7 ms, all within run-to-run spread. Making every loaded dex's stubs before a data module instantiates, so that no stub module is made inside one, is unsound: an app build's data groups take classes earlier launches loaded from other dexes, which this launch may not have loaded yet.
- **Rejected: other warm-launch candidates** (2026-10-23). Deferring a data module's header touch list until its classes are used loaded 353 modules instead of 329. Reading and compiling the saved build ends before the zygote does, so splitting or pipelining it gains nothing. `vtableRepairs` (~24 ms), `loadC`'s absent set (~12 ms) and `vcall`'s `dispatchFor` (~13 ms) are small or would move their cost to other callers.
- **What a module costs** (2026-10-12/13). V8 charges 37–40 KB of external memory per instantiated module with code (a code-space page for jump tables), 7.5 KB globals-only, freed once its functions are unreferenced; external memory drives full collections. No V8 lazy/tiering/Liftoff flag changes Chrome's per-module compile cost, so the lever is fewer modules.
- **Rejected: larger build modules** (2026-10-19). V8 inlines only calls within a module, so a frame whose callees share a module could run faster. Over three alternating rounds against the inline build (Komikku `--gpu --repeat 6`, means of each run's median `frame` of visits 3–7: Appearance 33.8, Settings 22.3 ms), app code modules of 4 MiB instead of 1 MiB took 36.0 and 19.4 ms (Settings below the base in every round, Appearance above it in two of three), and hot platform modules of 4 MiB instead of 1 MiB 34.1 and 20.3 ms. At 6 MiB the first hot module came out at 7.9 MB, over the 7 MiB limit of a main-thread compile. In the same rounds, keeping build code from the second tier (no upgrade for a slot a build module installed) took 32.1 and 22.1 ms, within noise; it was not adopted, since its effect on long-running work was not measured. With idle burning (below), app code modules of 16 MiB were no better either (2026-10-22, two alternating pairs, Komikku `--gpu --warm --repeat 4`, median `frame` of visits 3–5): Appearance 31.8/33.1 ms at 1 MiB against 32.8/34.1, Settings 14.8/15.7 against 16.2/15.5, launch 2.21/2.22 s against 2.17 s.
- **Fewer modules per on-demand compile** (2026-10-12). Classes linked on demand are globals-only modules with slots stubbed from pools shared by signature; units export base-typed helpers later units import; class globals linked since the last read form one module. Komikku launch modules ~23,000 → ~17,700.
- **Entry batches kept, callee folding rejected** (2026-10-12/13). `World.batch` adds a method's static/direct calls before its first branch (≤ 32), yet Calculator's 3,957 links compiled 3,711 methods; over TikTok's demand static batching removes ≤ 45% of modules and compiles unrun methods. 64% of unlinked single-target callees were linked later; folding saves ~85 µs per hit and wastes ~200 µs per miss, breaking even near 70%.
- **Merging first-tier units** (2026-10-12). Once 1,024 units are loose the upgrade Worker recompiles up to 512 of their methods into one module; calls stay through the table, so invalidation is per method. Merges wait for the first frame and any app build, then idle twice their duration (back to back, a Worker reached 1 GB in 20 s). TikTok's first 100 s: 97 mark-compacts (12.7 s) → 26–35 (1.5–1.8 s). Merging in launch cost Feeder 70 ms.
- **Engine recompiles after a merge** (2026-10-14). A cold Komikku tap ran 725–3,170 `wasm.CompileLazy` events (V8 compiling a function on its first call), 9–57 ms of 46–130 ms taps; a CPU profile charges them to the calling Java frame. Most were in functions at indices 100–999, which only merged modules reach: a merge re-emits already-run methods into a new module whose functions the engine compiles again when next called. With merging off they fell to 7.5–15.6 ms per tap, but taps did not get faster (sums of tap medians 534 vs 536 ms over three alternating pairs): the compile worker then answered more second-tier jobs inside the taps.
- **Rejected: merging only dormant units** (2026-10-14). Taking into a merge only units whose call counts had not changed for 5 s, so running methods keep the code the engine compiled, left cold Komikku taps unchanged (sums of tap medians 524 vs 519 ms over three alternating pairs).
- **Freeing merged-away units stalls the thread** (2026-10-13). Cold Komikku taps intermittently stalled 80–170 ms with the Worker silent: no timeline event, no rAF, not even the 33 ms fallback timer. A GC-category trace (`WEBDROID_TRACE_CATEGORIES=disabled-by-default-v8.gc,v8`) showed one task, `V8.GC_HEAP_EXTERNAL_SECOND_PASS_CALLBACKS`, covering each stall. A FinalizationRegistry count found ~3,950 first-tier unit instances freed in a single tap window. A merge leaves the units it replaces unreferenced; between major collections thousands accumulate, and the engine frees each module's native code in the second-pass weak callbacks after the collection, all in one task. Every traced cold run had one or two such tasks (90–170 ms); keeping every instance alive removed them. `World.retire` now holds the units a merge replaces and drops them `RETIRE_BATCH` (32) at a time, the next batch once a FinalizationRegistry reports the last collected, or after 5 s. The largest second-pass task fell to 3–8 ms (10–17 ms with batches of 128). The longest silent stretch in any tap fell from 79–169 ms to ≤ 24 ms over 10 cold runs. The units are released over minutes instead of at once, so the external memory merging frees arrives later. `wasmRetired`/`wasmCollected` count the drain.
- **Rejected: prefetching the platform modules a build's code names** (2026-10-17). An app build's code names platform classes whose code and data modules a first visit then waits for (FitNotes `start`: 6.7 ms of module waits). Listing them in the index and fetching them once the launch was quiet cut that to 3.5 ms, but queued hundreds of fetches and compiles (4 Workers, 8 requests each) beside the app's own startup work, which a first tap can wait on (Komikku `next` above). Removed.
- **Flat statics are not a tap lever** (2026-10-14, plan 5.1). Cold Komikku taps built 13–87 classes in 0.3–2.4 ms, and all their modules took 3–17 ms to create; the launch built ~7,340 classes in 195–218 ms of 16k modules' 810 ms. Flat statics would remove class modules and most imports, which bounds the gain to part of that launch time and a few milliseconds per tap.
- **Reverted: synthetic stubs per header** (2026-10-11). ~550 of a realm's ~1,250 pre-first-frame modules are single-function lazy-link stubs (`ensureSynth`); one module per header cut warm Calculator ~1,605 → ~1,565 ms. Reverted over e2e settle failures later traced to a harness clock fault; marking stubbed slots done on loading an app build's synthetic module (`holdsSynth`) crashed the renderer.
- **Rejected: inherited vtable targets** (2026-10-14). `vtWords` took 218 ms of a profiled cold Komikku launch, mostly resolving each slot's target. Reusing a superclass's targets for slots a class and its added interfaces do not declare must read `byKey`, not `methods` (a `$$host` overlay keeps methods it hosts for a superclass only there; `hostclass` caught `HttpsHandler.openConnection`), and must drop kept targets whenever classes load. Correct that way it saved nothing (cold launch 6239 vs 6240 ms, three alternating pairs); app classes mostly extend platform classes, whose vtables come from the platform build.
- **Rejected: one array supertype for `array-length`** (2026-10-14). Plan 3.4's single cast: a `$Array` struct whose immutable data field is `(ref array)`, as every element kind's wrapper's supertype, so `array-length` is one `ref.cast` and `array.len` instead of up to four `ref.test`s. Steady state was unchanged in node g1 (ten workloads, both directions within ±5% under load ~4), and modules grew 8 B on average because every module declares the base types. The type tests are cheap once optimized.
- **Rejected: one prologue call** (2026-10-14). Plan 3.1 without its `WARM_AT` change: a method that both counts its frame and its calls made one `enterCount(slot, weight)` call instead of `enter` then `count`. Node g1 over 74 workloads was neutral (first calls 1.007×, steady 1.001× geomean), and the benchmark's modules shrank 1 B per method. TurboFan inlines both helpers, and most first-tier methods are leaves that only count.

### Engine compilation and code cache

- **The engine code cache works but is not a tap lever** (2026-10-11, 2026-10-19). The 10-11 probe compiled eagerly or from bytes, which V8 never caches. Under the tier's settings (lazy compilation, dynamic tiering, the compile options) Chrome 153 caches a module's optimized code once it holds 1 KB of new top-tier code and has compiled nothing for 2 s (`--wasm-caching-threshold`, `--wasm-caching-timeout-ms`), or at once at 1 MB: a later launch's first call ran at optimized speed from an `immutable` HTTP response (from launch 2) and from a Service Worker answering out of Cache Storage (from launch 3), on the page and in a Worker, never from `compileStreaming` of a `cache.match` response (`research/wasm/platform-status.md`). In a real warm Komikku launch the platform's streamed modules qualified rarely (16 serialized, 19 read back of 523 streamed), and disabling caching (thresholds at 2·10⁹) left warm launches and taps unchanged (three alternating rounds: launch 2,465–2,527 against 2,263–2,436 ms, taps within noise). A warm tap compiles lazily for only 3–6 ms on the app thread (`wasm.CompileLazy`), while 50–210 ms of top-tier compiling runs on V8's background threads. Serving app builds through a Service Worker so they could cache was therefore not built. Chrome refuses synchronous `new WebAssembly.Instance` over 8 MB on a page's main thread, so chunks stay under 8 MiB.
- **Warming engine code in the spare** (2026-10-11). Lazy Liftoff was the largest launch cost (~20k compiles, 259 ms); eager compilation cannot ship and Chrome ignores hints. `World.warm` calls the 18,117 methods of `warm.json` with zero arguments while a linear-memory word makes `count` trap on entry, warming the tab-shared `Module`: Calculator 786–797 → 615–620 ms. Completed before boot it made FitNotes 1,746 vs 1,578 ms, so it runs in 10 ms slices and stops at `bootApk`. A helper Worker cannot warm app code: only a World numbering the same classes supplies an instance's GC-typed globals. (Superseded 2026-10-15: a module's own bytes supply them, below.)

- **Warming in a Worker of the page** (2026-10-15). V8 shares the code it compiles lazily between the Workers of a renderer for one native module, whether a Worker receives the posted `Module` or compiles identical bytes: a function another Worker had called first took 2–4.5 µs on its first call, against 20–25 µs cold. A build module's imports can be made from its own bytes, so warming needs no World: `host/warmshim.ts` copies the type section into a small module that defines the tags and globals at their defaults, gives throwing functions for function imports (the host's `Math` functions stay themselves, as V8 compiles calls of those differently) and one table and memory with the warm word set; `host/warm-worker.ts` calls each first-tier method into the trap. The page's warm Worker warms the platform slots launches ran (`warm.json`, now slots: 97.8% of about 15.9k ran slots recur from one launch to the next); each process asks for its launch's recorded slots (`ran.bin` beside `files.json`, kept against the id of the build that numbered them) and then for its saved app build, whole until a launch has recorded slots against it. Only Chrome warms (JSC and SpiderMonkey compile modules up front), and only with at least 4 cores. `World.warm` in the zygote is gone; most launches got theirs from the second spare `launch()` started while the platform warm had not completed (about 21.5k compiles). A launch still starts a spare, as the processes the app starts next (Google Play services, billing) start from it. Without it, and with the launch record scanning all 2^20 count slots (a `Map.has` for each uncounted one) and saving on every quiet 2 s watch tick, quick-gate runs against a cold server wedged Doodle Jump in 3 of 4 (its billing race, *Inverse billing locks*; base 0 of 2) and failed Komikku or mihon-app in 3 of 4 (base 0 of 2); with the spare, the record scanning only the count array and slots once per quiet stretch, 0 of 2 (4 of 7 flows in 137–175 s, base 4–5 in 121–136 s). A build no launch recorded is warmed whole only in the process launched to show the app; a background process gets its recorded slots (Google Play services had been warming its 45k methods twice per launch). The traced runs below predate these three fixes. In one traced warm run each (bundled builds off, under host load ~20 from other sessions), the app Worker compiled before the first tap Calculator 25.1k → 13.0k functions (372 → 102 ms), FitNotes 22.6k → 13.2k (300 → 97 ms) and Komikku 55.1k → 39.2k (1,186 → 461 ms); in 150 ms tap windows Komikku's compiling fell from 26–50 ms to 1.5–8.5 ms, Calculator's from 1.4–16.3 to 0.2–3.5 ms and FitNotes' from 1.3–19.4 to 0.3–8.1 ms. Wall-clock launches and taps were not comparable: in the same three alternating pairs unchanged code took 100–569 ms for FitNotes taps that take 16–27 ms on a quiet host. An app's first warm launch of a build warms all of it, which ran on into Calculator's second tap (10.9k compiles in its window); later launches warm only recorded slots. What processes still compile is mostly per-module helpers, which run only when a method calls them, so a trap at entry cannot reach them: the platform build's 1,077 code modules define 318k methods, 103k `$lit`, 34k `$new:`, 27k `$dispatch:`, 17k `$instanceof:` and 16.5k `$cast:` functions.

### Compiling ahead

- **Linking ahead what earlier launches loaded** (2026-10-22, `AotRuntime.linkAhead`). With a build attached, a first visit still waits for the code modules it first calls to be instantiated and linked (`loadC`: the module, its class imports, its header's natives). From 250 ms after the last input, idle time now links, in 4 ms slices, the platform code modules the app's LaunchRecord lists (`files`, in the order a launch wanted them), when each links with nothing to fetch, compile or wait for (`linkable`); one that does not is left to its first call. It is the training run of HotSpot's ahead-of-time class loading and linking (JEP 483) and the Zygote's preloading, taken from the device's own record. FitNotes linked 11 modules ahead and Komikku 7. Matched `--gpu --warm --repeat 4`, 3 alternating runs per side: FitNotes' first `start` 48.0–57.1 → 38.7–39.3 ms intended (every run below every control), Komikku's first Appearance 55.6–79.6 → 52.0–55.4, other taps and the sums of per-tap medians within noise (Komikku 571.9 → 570.8, FitNotes 119.3 → 102.8), first frames unchanged (Komikku 2.29–2.36 s against 2.31–2.32). Komikku's `next` was 3–4 ms slower in every run with it, all of it in the wait for the vsync after the input (6.1–8.7 against 3.0–5.0 ms), its frame unchanged: the frame had been asked for before the input by the press ripple, so no early frame started (fixed in "Early frames for input"). A record's ran slots are keyed by its build's id, which builds after launches change, so the app's own modules are not linked ahead.
- **Compiling ahead in idle time** (2026-10-13). `World.ahead` compiles on the app thread after the looper has waited 100 ms with no other thread runnable and no input for 1 s, in ≤ 4 ms slices: single-target callees of methods that ran, transitively (16–45% of a cold tap's links; one level only 10–25%). Gating on the looper alone cost Komikku's launch ~70 ms, and a 250 ms input window its `start` tap 273 → 320 ms; with a saved build it slowed Calculator's first warm tap 27 → 41 ms, so it runs only without one. Cold medians: Calculator `7` 179 → 127, Komikku `appearance` 337 → 264, `start` 267 → 291 ms (ranges overlap).
- **What a cold heavy-app tap compiles** (2026-10-13). Cold YouTube taps compile 1,000–1,850 first-tier methods (`you` 205–297 ms, `settings` 387–445 ms to the first response), about one module per method: `World.batch` takes only straight-line direct calls. In the profiled `you` window (296 ms), compiling and installing took 127 ms (`buildUnit` 74, synchronous `WebAssembly.Module` 41), guest ARM 58, main-thread upgrade planning 16 (`likely`/`allocatedSubtypes`) and first-tier Java 110. `lat --demand` records each tap's on-demand methods (`World.demandSince`). The app's baseline profile (37.8k methods) names 6–33% of them. 50–95% are in classes linked before the tap, but those classes then held about 91k methods without code. The call frontier of methods with code (22.6k, every target) covered 12–30%, single targets 10–20%, and the frontier with the profile 15–50%. `ahead` had reached `AHEAD_MAX` (8,192) during launch. Prediction cannot take such taps off the compiler; the cost per method and per module can.
- **Compiling ahead runs once per wait** (2026-10-14). The input gate is checked once, 100 ms into a looper wait, when the input that started the work is always under 1 s old, so after the first tap `ahead` effectively stops (Komikku: 0–87 methods per tap window). Waiting out the window while the looper still waits ran it after every tap, and then the call frontier emptied within 85–165 methods; adding the remaining methods of linked classes that ran filled `AHEAD_MAX` in two processes yet left each tap's on-demand count unchanged (`more` 709). Waiting out the window alone made cold Komikku taps slower (medians of 3, alternating: `start` 105 → 139, `more` 132 → 145, `appearance` 148 → 161 ms; Calculator mixed), so the single check stays.
- **Rejected: compiling ahead in a Worker** (2026-10-12). Compiling callees of on-demand methods in the idle compile Worker made Komikku's launch 8,645–8,670 → 8,792–8,911 ms, on-demand compiling unchanged: the app thread reaches most methods first, adopting a unit costs ~1.1 ms and renumbering more. Only relocatable code would free ahead units from the page World's numbering.
- **Rejected: seeded app numbering to attach the first build mid-session** (2026-10-14). Plan 2.1, for a cold launch, whose first app build arrives ~30 s after Komikku's first frame. `World.seedApp` numbered, after the prefix and in dex order, app classes (an id and a `methodCount+1` slot block), the dexes' array types and interface selectors, in the page and the build Worker. With the Worker's numbering frozen, 99.9% of Komikku's 131,921 methods compiled without a new number (FitNotes 22,376 of 22,395); the rest named link errors (94), `$$host` overlays (33) or a platform class outside the build (1). Seeding took 23 ms for Komikku (slots 365k → 532k) and 4 ms for FitNotes; TikTok's 462k classes took 474 ms and need 2.83M slots, past `COUNT_SLOTS`. Warm launches were neutral. A build of code modules alone attached 4.0 s after the first frame with method-granular selection (30–75% of each cold tap's on-demand methods) or 6.4–7.3 s class-granular (63–88%). Data modules cannot attach late: their stubs and `ensure` functions overwrite slots the page already compiled, and their vtables name synthetic slots beyond the seed. Taps compiled less (`more` 41 → 20–39 ms), but a late code module links every class it names when loaded (6,657 classes; 180–245 ms in the next tap with 1 MB modules, 59–68 ms with 64 KB), so the first tap after attaching took 140–263 ms against 45–91 ms and the rest stayed within noise. A cold tap is mostly not compiling: unprofiled `appearance` took 155–219 ms with 15–22 ms compiled; profiled, `linkSlot` (emit, `WebAssembly.Module`, instantiate) was 59 ms of `more`'s 261 ms and of `appearance`'s 285 ms, the rest Java (Compose recomposition, coil loads on coroutine threads) and, in `appearance`, 97 ms idle.
- **Rejected: building a cold first visit's app code before its launch** (2026-10-16). A build of TikTok's static roots alone (baseline profile, manifest classes and a call closure of up to 20,000 from them), as an install-time build would make it, held 92,998 methods in 422 modules (~130 MB in OPFS) and compiled in 11.5 s in its Worker. These roots cover 52.7% of a cold launch's 89,651 on-demand methods, at 49.5% precision. Two matched pairs ran on a new device each time: every app reinstalled, the SSAID dropped, platform caches warm. With the build attached at launch, TikTok reached signup in 31 s against 37–40 s, and its For You feed in 51–55 s against 67–68 s. On-demand compiling to signup fell from 75–79k methods (12.5–12.9 s) to 38k (7.1–7.3 s). Made before the launch, the build would delay the launch's start by its Worker's Programs (1.9–2.1 s for the platform and TikTok under node), its World, the 11.5 s compile and the save. Signup would come later, and the feed would at best break even. A build cannot attach once the launch has numbered past its prefix (above), and splitting it across Workers needs a Program in each, which TikTok's size rules out (*A shown first launch waits for its app's build, made in shards*). A shipped build (`aot-profile`, `apps.json`) gives a hosted APK the same gain for ~130 MB more download (TikTok's APK is 462 MB). On 2026-10-17, with a shown first launch waiting for its app's build, TikTok (2.55 million method references) waited 14 s for that closure, first presented at 25.5 s against 11.4 s on demand, and still compiled 60.7k methods on demand. A first launch now waits only for a whole build, up to `WHOLE_LIMIT` (250k references); TikTok launches on demand as before (11.1 s) and is built after.
- **Earlier compile-ahead trials** (2026-09-29..10-03, JS tier). Compiling Mihon's 18,274-method baseline profile at boot cut the first tap 147 → 124 ms but moved the first frame 2.2 → 3.3 s. RTA from the launcher Activity needs its first 8,000 of 20,117 app methods (37% precision) to cover 78% of a Calculator M launch. 20,000 methods ahead cut Komikku's first-tap cold calls 12,256 → 5,050 (198 → 185 ms); newest-first beat oldest-first; starting beside the zygote or after the first frame was worse.

### Second tier and hot upgrades

- **Burning first-tier code in idle time** (2026-10-22, `src/wasm/link/burn.ts`). V8 runs a Wasm function as Liftoff code until its calls and loop back-edges have charged a budget scaled by its size, so the flat first-tier code of a screen, run some hundreds of times, stays baseline code for the launch at a third to a half of its optimized speed, and the second tier covers only what gets hot. First-tier methods now begin with a warm exit (`Compiler.emitWarmExit`: when a linear-memory word is set, the call returns through the function's end, charging the whole function; C modules +2.2%, 178,239 → 182,146 KB). From 250 ms after the last input the burner calls each first-tier method that ran 64 calls or asked for the second tier 32,768 times with zero arguments, in 4 ms slices, so the engine optimizes it in the background with the inlining feedback of its real calls; nothing of the method runs. It is the training run of HotSpot's AOT method profiling (JEP 515) taken in idle time on the device's own counts. Komikku `--gpu --warm --repeat 4`, 3 alternating runs per side: repeat Appearance (visits 3–5) intended 59.6–68.5 → 47.1–49.8 ms (`frame` 33.5–38.2 → 27.1–30.1), Settings 39.0–49.2 → 36.9–42.3, the first-visit `start` frame 38.9–44.0 → 28.1–37.8; launch 2.20–2.22 s against 2.17–2.29. An app process burned about 7,800 methods over a run, 1,428 in the 1.66 s of launch idle. The burner remembers burned code by its function: keyed by slot and forgotten only by `Tiering.install`, it never burned merged first-tier code or build modules placed in a burned slot. Linking ahead, burning and compiling ahead run in that order through one idle loop (`host/boot.ts`), and a failure turns off only the work that failed.
- **Rejected: burning more** (2026-10-22). Burning second-tier units too (the installed methods of a hot unit, 2 s after its install) against first-tier code only, 3 alternating runs: Appearance `frame` 33.0/31.9/29.6 against 30.6/24.0/31.5 ms, Settings 17.8/16.3/19.6 against 20.8/20.5/15.0, launch 2.18–2.19 against 2.21–2.31 s. Burning from 4 calls instead of 64: Appearance 32.3/32.0/25.1 against 28.9/26.6/32.6, Settings 15.0/23.0/20.3 against 17.1/19.7/16.5. Neither gained; both removed.
- **Repeat taps bound a shipped second tier** (2026-10-14, plans 0.5 and 2.3). Komikku's Appearance screen opened ten times in one session (back, then Appearance) took 167, 93 and 52 ms on its first, second and tenth visits cold, and 82, 64 and 50 ms warm; cold compiling fell from 33 ms to 0–6 ms after the first visit. Second-tier code shipped in app builds could take a warm first visit from about 82 toward 50–60 ms, a tap already well inside the gate; a cold launch has no app build to ship it in. Not pursued.
- **Second tier kept** (2026-10-10/13). Without it, steady state fell from 1.59× to 0.82× of the removed JS tier on Bench. Hot methods overflowing the Worker's 16-job queue ask later rather than compile inline; planning waits for the starting window (`holdUpgrades`); virtual calls with > 32 implementations get no guards (`39bbe2f1`): Mihon `upgradeJob` 217 → 61 ms.
- **Inline upgrade policy** (2026-10-06). Timing-dependent upgrades made workloads bimodal; leaves wait, cost is a fixed 1 ms, and the oldest still-hot method and outermost frame go first. Guards cover implementations that ran (Chrome listIter 0.145 → 0.080 ms).
- **Hot-unit refresh** (2026-10-07, `c6d40dff`; deleted 2026-10-20). Hot units compiled with unresolved symbolic imports were refreshed once dependencies were ready: late steady MapInt 0.502 → 0.153 ms, first calls unchanged. It rested on that one microbenchmark, whose control was already gone, and was deleted with the linker's one install path. Rejected: publishing requested hot copies slowed both workloads without AOT.
- **Rejected promotion policies** (2026-10-08). A 12-method group cap nearly doubled deque steady; large roots alone worsened JSON; active-callers-only starved promotion; starting the budget at first Java entry raised boot+first on all ten primary workloads; cost per root word starved HashMap; whole-method promotion with saturated loop counters re-ran `ZipFile$Source.initCEN` (listIter 0.2191 → 0.5036 ms). Open: the oldest-refused override can pick `initCEN`, and the 1 ms admission estimate admits 21–25 ms compiles.
- **Planning indexes** (2026-10-08/09). Planning filtered the allocated-class set per queried base (7,166,810 classes per Mihon scroll window); each newly allocated class is now indexed once under its supertypes: 70.0 → 2.9 ms per window, intervals > 25 ms 48/39 → 19/20. `World.likely` keeps the two busiest targets instead of sorting (38.9 → 28.0 ms).

### Compile Workers

- **Bounded compiler Workers** (2026-10-05). ChatGPT's compiler Workers each built Program metadata and a World (729–791 MB each); four crashed the renderer. App builds take an origin-wide Web Lock and transfer modules before termination; the upgrade service starts on the first hot request. The app Worker alone held 1.41 GB, which this does not bound.
- **Worker follows Proxies and DEX namespaces** (2026-10-08/09). Annotation Proxies and non-root `World.addDexes` retired the Worker, moving Mihon's hot compilation to the main thread. Proxy definitions now cross as wire data and the protocol mirrors DEX ownership and library namespaces (a bootstrap-only version failed: Mihon's Proxy belongs to a `BootClassLoader` object). Main-thread scroll compile 732.8/845.2 → 476.8/373.5 ms; FPS unchanged. Other host additions still retire it (a stale Worker is unsound).
- **Worker answers wait out input** (2026-10-14, plan 1.7). ~6 compile-worker answers (second-tier or merged units, each a main-thread adoption plus the planning of the next job) landed inside each cold Komikku response window. `CompileClient` now holds planning and adoption while the realm answers input (`answeringFor`, 250 ms after the latest pointer event). Two series of three alternating pairs: sums of tap medians 541 vs 621 and 561 vs 626 ms (`start` 92–123 vs 163–167, `more` 133–135 vs 152–155); `settings`, which follows a swipe, was slower (107–132 vs 92–97) as fewer upgrades were in place by then. Holding only adoption and planning during input measured the same (548 vs 540). A third series at `a5e24b4bb` (four alternating pairs, the hold switched by a temporary page parameter on one server) agreed: every run with the hold was faster than its pair (sums 486–569 vs 551–582 ms), medians summed 503 vs 578 ms, and without the hold `appearance`'s median was 155 ms (130 with it).
- **Platform jars to compile workers as Blobs** (2026-10-17). A compile worker fetched the 43 MB of platform jars through the realm that started it; posting them with a shard's init instead structured-cloned them on the app Worker's thread, about 130 ms for six shards, which made Calculator's `equals` 86–128 ms when a refine started beside it. The page fetches the jars as Blobs, which cross realms as handles; the compile host hands them to each worker it starts, which reads its own copy while its script loads, and process Workers take the page's Blobs too. `equals` took 14–20 ms.
- **Compile workers that end or never load** (2026-10-17). A compile worker whose module failed to import never answered (the classic shim left the rejection unhandled), and a compile host process that ends left its ports silent. The shim reports a failed import as the worker's error, and the port's `close` event, which Chrome fires when the other end's worker or document ends (checked for both), fails the worker; a failed build lets the launch continue without one. Neither caused the first launches that hung at the install build (about 1 in 80, with the build lock held and no compile host frame appended): the compile host's fetch of its 8 MB worker script never settled. Its headers arrived in 25–41 ms and Chrome's NetLog showed the network transaction reading the body to its end, but the URL request never completed, so neither `text()` nor `blob()` settled; read as a stream, the body stopped partway (4.2–7.0 MB in, 4 of 9 measured stops at exactly 4 MiB) and had not resumed when the launch ended. The dev server held no request in flight, and a fetch of the same URL afterwards took 27 ms. In launch-only first launches (Calculator M, FitNotes, Komikku, a fresh profile each), `text()` or `blob()` hung in 4 of about 330 without memory pressure (none in 182 consecutive) and 2 of 145 with 12 GB held by another process, and a stream stopped for over 5 s in 7 of about 230 with it. A page fetching the same script from a plain server the same way (chunked, page thread busy, 40 fetches alongside) never stalled in 200 fetches, and switching off Chrome 151's SQL disk cache trial (`DiskCacheBackendExperiment`) showed no difference; the renderer-side cause is open. The host now reads the script as a stream and fetches it again (up to three times) when no bytes arrive for 5 s after its headers: in 90 consecutive launch-only first launches with the memory held, 4 fetched it again and none hung. Two failures remain unexplained: a first launch that did not wait for a build (Calculator M's warm preparation with the install build switched off) hung, its page state uncaptured, and one launch-only FitNotes run crashed its renderer.
- **Units from the sandboxed frame, merges warmed first** (2026-10-16). Since `32867b8c1` the compile workers run in the sandboxed frame's renderer, and each unit they posted held a `WebAssembly.Module`, which another agent cluster cannot deserialize: the port fired `messageerror`, nothing handled it, and `CompileClient` waited on its first job for good. Komikku's app process had 1 job and 0 adopted, merged nothing, and never compiled the hot method inline either. A unit now crosses as its bytes, which the receiving realm compiles with `WebAssembly.compile` off its thread (the worker no longer validates them first); a `messageerror` retires the worker. Komikku then adopted 33 of 33 jobs in 25 s (30 upgrades, 1,536 merged slots). Merging again cost the taps that follow: a merge moves up to 512 methods into a new module, which V8 compiles again on their next calls. In traced runs `settings` had 1,878 lazy compiles (50 ms) against 346 (7 ms) with units dropped, and `appearance` 1,603 against 601; with merging switched off, 440 and 738. Over three alternating pairs (`--gpu`, load 4–14), Komikku's cold taps summed 632 against 470 ms (every `appearance` over 150 ms) while its ten Appearance visits summed 662 against 829. A merged module now goes to the page's warm Worker before it is adopted, as a request with its own answer port, warmed apart from the process's other requests, waiting at most 1 s. Its methods are first-tier code, which traps at entry once compiled. Every merged slot was warmed (1,024 of 1,024), and the in-tap compiles fell to 637 and 834. Over three more pairs: Komikku's cold taps 433 against 516 ms (`settings` 57 vs 84, `appearance` 113 vs 134), its ten visits 612 against 788 (visits 4–10 at 41–58 vs 58–81 ms), FitNotes 164 against 157, Calculator 233 against 207. A process now asks for warming only where the page runs a warm Worker (one `warming()` test), where it had posted build bytes into a closed port. Limits: Calculator's first tap is slower (medians 106 and 116 against 85 and 87 in the two series). Hot units adopted after the launch add about 600 lazy compiles to it: rejecting them for a test brought the app thread's in-tap compiles from 1,289–1,511 to 513–940, but the tap still took 84–109 ms. Hot units have no warm trap and their copies are reached only from inside them, so the warm Worker cannot compile them ahead. The TikTok cage figures under *Compile workers in a sandboxed frame* were measured while no unit arrived.
- **A build module over code compiled on demand** (2026-10-23). Calculator M's launch at 5 jobs once died of `IllegalAccessException: Class q4.d can not access a member of class q4.g`. `AtomicReferenceFieldUpdater.newUpdater`, made in `q4.g`'s initializer, finds its caller through `Reflection.getCallerClass` (`VMStack.getStackClass2`, the fourth frame), and the platform build runs that forwarder in place, so its frame comes from the inline entry of `newUpdater`'s trace. Traces were keyed by function name, which was the method id for both the build's code and code compiled on demand. A store that has not yet fetched a code module answers a miss, so the method is compiled on demand and its trace registered; when the module arrives, it installs over that code, and its frames are read with the on-demand trace, which has no inline entry. The frame is lost and the caller is read one frame too deep (`q4.d.a`, which started the initialization). Under load, fetches arrive later, hence 5 jobs. Every unit but a build's now names its functions `id#unit`, as the second tier and copies did, so a name only a build uses is resolved by `AotRuntime.trace`. `aottest` withholds the code modules, makes an updater, lets the module install and makes another.

## Host boundary, threads and scheduling

### Strands and thread identity across waits

- **Resume as the thread that waited** (2026-09-28..10-05). Work resuming after an await ran as whatever thread was current: two Java threads shared one native CPU, service continuations ran off main (microG, Komikku), Reddit's launch waited on a class its own thread was initializing, and Amazon's cookie handler after `await fetch` entered a monitor main had released by parking, so javac's catch-all re-ran its own `monitor-exit` forever. Driven generators re-enter the calling thread at each resume; `.then` and host-timer callbacks are the same hazard; a thread is released when its work suspends.
- **Restore strands inside resumed Wasm** (2026-10-07). Restoring sid/thread/depth in a Promise reaction is too early, since another completion can idle the globals first. `5ad21add` restores inside the resumed stub, exceptional exits included; `2b23baf2`/`e9e8d126` do the same after an immediate suspending-import exception.
- **Exact strand on nested and resumed entry** (2026-10-07..12). A fast nested JNI callback cleared the guest CPU turn's strand, losing monitor reentry (Resy deadlocked in Mapbox `nativeLoad`): `onStrand` restores the exact strand in a synchronous finally. Async host-entry continuations capture caller Thread and native parent (`a83afdb5`; a thread-only fix lost monitor ancestry). `MediaCodecList.initCodecList` holds `sInitLock` across a wait, and resuming as the thread's root strand deadlocked it on itself: `asThread` resumes as the parent's host strand.
- **Detached entries and Looper identity** (2026-10-03). Fire-and-forget Java entries use `detached(call, escape)`; `scripts/generators.ts` rejects a `void` or bare-statement entry. A process's calling identity is one token (the main Looper once kept the zygote's uid and logged "Thread identity changed" per message).

### Natives that wait (JSPI)

- **Suspending versus plain imports** (2026-10-04..10). No JS frame may sit between a `promising` entry and a suspending import. A native that may wait (a `driven` generator, an async function, or marked `waits()` where it is registered) gets a Suspending import (`src/wasm/host/suspend.ts`); others stay plain, as a Suspending import costs ~50 ns against 4 ns. Conditional waiters (`NativeConverter.charsetForName`, `FontFileUtil` reads) must be declared. Returning Promises only when waiting, and no main-thread fuel-check yield before its turn is due, cut Calculator's launch from 49.7k to 1.3k Suspending calls; launch time barely changed.
- **Import ABI is fixed by name** (2026-10-05). An emitted plain import was rebound as suspending when its implementation was async and resumed under the root strand. `#s` suspends, `#p` is the conditional landing form, unsuffixed imports park explicitly. The plain call saves strand context before entering the native. Always wrap with `Sched.suspending`, never raw `WebAssembly.Suspending` (bypassed nested-stack reset: 1.56M emergency-yield captures); its quota resets only when the import returns a Promise.
- **Pending results can be shared** (2026-10-02..05, 2026-10-20). While each pending Promise kept one continuation list, a cached pending promise carried its asker's frames: a `??=` memo of `theNetwork` wedged Harmonic, Reddit, TikTok, ChatGPT and YouTube; `applicationInfoOf` caching `rt.drive`'s result hung Mihon's first launch 7/22; a cached service constructor made a second `pms()` wait on itself. `shareable` gave each caller its own continuation stack, and `scripts/generators.ts` flagged pending results kept by `??=`, `||=`, `Map.set` or stores. `Runtime.drive` now continues with a plain `then`, so many callers may keep and await one pending result: `shareable` and that lint are gone, and `once` shares its computation directly.
- **Promises must not reach Java values** (2026-10-05..13). `DisplayEventReceiver.nativeInit` stored a pending `WeakReference.get`, failing at vsync; GMS got host Promises from `getPublicFieldRecursive` callbacks and `newArrayList` (`InternalError`). These now drive the generator/`after`. `JavaClass.new` zero-allocates without class init (a pending allocation killed Resy's GMS). Host code with no Java frame below enters on a new strand that suspends when a lazy link yields after 8 ms; `box`'s Promise reached a cursor row and aborted the owner; `valueOf` now links synchronously.
- **Heap pressure in synchronous entries** (2026-10-08). Over budget in a host byte-array conversion, the allocation slow path tried to suspend across a JS frame and raised `SuspendError` instead of OOM (YouTube Shorts), even for an immediate return. Synchronous entries collect, recheck and throw OOM; suspendable callers keep two timer turns.
- **The facade's methods cannot wait** (2026-10-15). Calling a Java method on its facade runs Java synchronously. In YouTube's Litho layout thread, minikin's `BreakIterator.next()` reached Thai text, whose break engine paged ICU's dictionary in, and raised `SuspendError`. A host loop over Java that can reach a lazy page, class or lock calls through `vcall`. In Chrome that costs 2.2 µs a boundary against the facade's 0.65 µs (a strand entry builds a JSPI stack); boundaries stay cached per text. Under node's test runner the per-entry cost grew round after round from its promise hooks, which a browser does not have.
