- **A waiting native holds only its own scope** (2026-10-15). `Bridge.hold` used to set aside every open scope, including those of the natives that had entered Java on the waiting strand's way (a binder transaction into an in-process service, a `vcall` from a driven native). Those natives then went on and ended on an empty scope stack, so their copied arrays were never stored back, and the copies stayed in `live` for other natives to write into. A YouTube walk ended a scope on an empty stack about 6,000 times. The parked path also ended its scope once on return and again on resumption. `hold` now takes the innermost scope's undo entries, live copies and scratch bounds, and its resume function opens them over whatever is open then; `end` closes the innermost scope from a marks stack, and a native that held does not end its scope on return.
- **Rejected: synchronous outer native driver** (2026-10-09). Completing `hostForm`'s immediate values synchronously gained nothing (56.66/54.86 vs 56.40/55.66 presentations/s; FitNotes exercise list 188.5/194.8 vs 195.8/196.4 ms): 6,582 of 6,848 native scopes returned Promises anyway. Find remaining wait producers and nested callback paths first. Most Compose-app suspensions are legitimate (coroutine parks, contended monitors, background SQLite).
- **A reloaded page stayed in memory** (2026-10-15, `c5e53b56`). After one Google Maps launch, navigating the tab to `about:blank` left the renderer's main isolate holding 135 MB of heap and 455 MB of ArrayBuffers, retained from `(Stack roots)` through `WasmImportData` into the bundle's closures (`wd.vfs` files, CanvasKit's heap, jar promises, string pools). A standalone page confirmed the engine behavior: a stack suspended on a Promise that never settles stays a root after navigation, with everything its frames reach, and settling it in `pagehide` frees it. Same-site reloads reuse the renderer, so pages added up: three Maps reloads held 253, 373 and 491 MB of heap (897, 1341 and 1787 MB backing), and a Chromium reloaded for about an hour held 8.1 GB, with launches spending ~78% of the page's time in GC (first frame 22.8 s against 9.8 s fresh). `module.ts` now keeps each pending wait's reject; on `pagehide` they all reject, as does any later wait. Natives pass that error on unconverted and `yieldNow` rethrows it, so the stacks unwind without Java handlers. The first version hung Maps' unload: `startMainLooper` restarts `Looper.loop` after an uncaught exception, and each restart threw at once, a microtask loop. Host entries (`callOn`) now never return once the waits have ended, as after a process exit. Ending the page realm through `exitProcess` was rejected because its listeners would also record and save a `system_server` exit. Worker realms keep the process-exit rule (suspended frames never resume); the page terminates them. After the fix, reloads stay at 135 MB of heap and 454 MB backing, and `about:blank` keeps 1 MB after Maps or FitNotes. e2e and `lat` open fresh pages, so their numbers were unaffected.
- **Natives that retry** (2026-10-20). A `waits` native pays for a Suspending import and copies its arrays out and back. For 12,000 Paint measurements that was about 0.95 µs a call, about 70% of it in landing. A native that answers a Promise only while something it reads loads, with no effect before, is marked `retries`: it runs as a plain parkable import over views and is called again over fresh views once the Promise settles (the parked call's scratch region stays reserved). TextBench, alternating under the same load: measure 29.5–30.1 → 19.1–19.3 ms, layouts 6.0 → 5.3 ms. `withFonts` must not re-run the native inside its Promise for such a native, since that would write through stale views; `fontsOrRetry` answers the wait alone.

### JNI and guest natives

- **Guest calls carry the caller's chain** (2026-10-02, `6a755f69`). `callGuestNative` and `loadModule` awaited before capturing the chain, so the guest ran as a fresh chain waiting on a lock its native caller held: `KevaImpl.<clinit>`'s `JNI_OnLoad` and YouTube's video-open wedged. A later `removeSyncBarrier` IllegalStateException (2/17 loaded runs) did not recur in 15 runs: not reproduced, not fixed.
- **Synchronous JNI entry** (2026-10-02..03, `1a500de2`, `0abb33aa`). `Call<Type>Method` parks only for class init or a suspended callback (100k calls 64.71 → 47.60 ms); a JNI call enters the guest without awaiting when the base frame is free and completes on its caller's stack if done in the first slice (0.70 → 0.37 µs per trivial call). App effect was noise (Doodle Jump 33.2 → 34.4 fps). Parking, signals or a spent slice still suspend the caller.
- **JNI semantics** (2026-10-05..08). `FindClass` enters the referring class's loader on a nested strand without initializing (ART) and leaves a pending exception on failure; an abstract target raises AbstractMethodError (`87facb0e`, per JDK 17). Suspended native array results travel through per-call tokens and resumed Wasm restores scratch before checking exceptions. Tracing prints `<reference>`, since `String()` throws on opaque Wasm objects.
- **Empty accesses touch no page** (2026-10-15). Cronet's `NewString(env, NULL, 0)` (a JNI string of no chars) read zero bytes at address 0, and the memory bounds check raised SIGSEGV, killing YouTube at launch. A zero-length access returns before any page is checked.
- **Class initialization from JNI** (2026-10-25). APKPure's VMP thread (`api_cfas`) died with NoClassDefFoundError, then NoSuchFieldError(""). A class initializer called a native that allocated its own class through `AllocObject`. The allocation's host call ran the class's initialization check again, on a root strand that cannot wait, so the initializer's own thread failed it and the class was marked erroneous. `construct` and `allocate` now take a class `init` already readied (`allocInstance`), as ART's `EnsureInitialized` lets the initializing thread through. `GetMethodID`, `GetFieldID` and `AllocObject` answer 0 with the initializer's failure pending, as ART does, instead of throwing past the guest; they and `NewObject` answer without suspending the guest once the class is initialized.
- **A String constructor called through `Call*Method`** (2026-10-26, `f21053d2`). Bloons TD 6's obfuscator native (libAPKVISION) makes strings with `AllocObject(String)` and then `CallNonvirtualVoidMethod(<init>([C)V)`. That ran String's constructor body, which is unreachable since strings come from `StringFactory`, and the trap killed the main thread at startup. As ART's `InvokeWithVarArgs` does, a `<init>` of `java/lang/String` through any `Call*Method` now constructs the String with its factory and points the receiver's reference (local, global or weak) at the result, inside `invoke` so that pinned arrays sync around it as around any call.

### Bridge and host values

- **Boundary shape** (2026-10-04). A ~3,000-native survey kept registration keys. Imports derive from descriptors (`host/natives.ts`), strings cross as externref, primitive arrays copy through linear-memory scratch (Wasm GC cannot alias guest memory), other references are opaque behind generated accessors (`host/bridge.ts`), and JNI uses Wasm references directly (`host/jni.ts`).
- **Array copies sync at the call boundary** (2026-10-12). In a native's scope host array copies sync both ways, respecting ranged views. Copies whose scope ended stay snapshots: syncing them would cost ~2,600 copies per Calculator tap and overwrite Java's newer contents.
- **Owner-backed host buffers** (`94216e14`, `40c78785`). Host ByteBuffer consumers (decoders, audio, Bitmap, AEAD, fonts) do checked owner reads/writes and commit before returning; local heap buffers stay synchronous. A synchronous view of an owner mapping in `Typeface.nativeReadTypefaces` blacked out Doodle.
- **Host-to-Java calls are prepared once** (2026-10-08). Per `MethodInfo` (weak keys; each hit rechecks declaring class and key, since rebinding rewrites descriptors in place). Trivial body per batch: `call` 42.61→19.96 ms Node, 32.80→19.75 Chrome; `invoke` similar.
- **Facades share host prototypes** (2026-10-08). Reading a Java class from a facade's JS constructor (`constructor.$i`) broke unboxing (Lichess KeyMint lost its algorithm) and sent every exception as `java/lang/Throwable`. Use `jentry.classNameOf`; Throwable facades answer `$javaClass`.
- **Handle lifetimes** (2026-10-05). Release deleted by `deref`'s host view, not the stored Wasm array, leaving stale handles; a pending `callFn` leaked its return/throw global and is cleared only while its completion token owns it.
- **Bridge scope cost** (2026-10-05). FitNotes boundary self samples: 18,988 µs (scope begin/end 7,270, array copies 5,624). Skipping empty-scope cleanup gained nothing measurable.
- **Vsync data reads are scoped** (2026-10-13, corrected 2026-10-14, plan 1.3). `fill()` in `deliverVsync` reads `frameTimelines` through a facade, outside any native when the looper was idle in `pollOnce`'s wait. Only host-spec getters were scoped, so the facade read pushed an undo closure and a `live` entry with no scope to run them: in a dragged Komikku session, 1,186 unscoped reads pushed 1,156 closures, about one a frame. They landed in the arrays `hold()` swaps in and were dropped when the native resumed, so nothing accumulated, but the `live` entries could hand a later read in that window a stale copy. A facade read now opens a scope when none is open.
- **One owner per native** (2026-10-05). A stale `initWait(I)V` in `classes.ts` overwrote the scheduler's `initWait(I)I` after a module-order change and trapped `Th.initRace`; the scheduler owns it.

### Turns, yields and wakes

- **Yield channel and turn start** (2026-09-28..10-03). Wakes and `Thread.yield` use a `MessageChannel`, avoiding the 1–4 ms nested-`setTimeout` clamp. Main-thread back-edge yields resume first, and the 8 ms gap counts from the turn's start (`032c41c5`): Mihon warm theme taps 112 → 100 / 97 → 89 ms. Pointer events dispatch on arrival (Calculator tap p50 ~70 → 55 ms).
- **A Node turn is a setImmediate** (2026-10-22, `5dded1bb`). Node delivers up to 1,000 queued port messages before it runs a timer, so a thread yielding every 8 ms turn held a sleeper's wake back ~8 s; `wasm threads` took 36 s. Node turns are now `setImmediate`s, between which timers run (4 s); pages keep the `MessageChannel`. Collection callbacks now run between more turns, which exposed the harness hazard under "Test-runner and tooling traps".
- **Woken threads first** (2026-10-02). `LockSupport` wakes rode the yield batch behind every native thread's slice (TikTok main's FutureTask wait 3.19 s, others 24–28 s). Woken threads and the main looper go first, one yielded thread per turn: 0.67–0.76 s and 5.7–8.0 s. Rejected: batching behind woken threads alone (no change); 4 ms turns (halved waits); a background share across throttled threads (wedged steps stayed 75–84% native).
- **Short slices while the page kernel answers** (2026-10-26, `09ed4567`). A process Worker's descriptors and path operations on app data are requests to the page's kernel, answered in about 0.3 ms. The thread that made one runs again only between other threads' slices, so each call took 11–30 ms behind a 3M-instruction slice (about 15 ms), and Pokémon TCG Pocket's asset fetcher, at about 25 calls per downloaded file, downloaded 139 KB/s. While such a request is outstanding, slices are 100k instructions: calls take 1.6–2 ms, the download runs 720–996 KB/s, and the pre-download cache scan finishes in 49 s rather than several minutes. UnityMain's instruction rate drops about 16% meanwhile. 300k slices, the input-answering budget, gave 349 KB/s at about 4 ms per call. Rejected: posting the next turn after the resumed thread's slice, so a reply that arrived during it runs first (850–996 KB/s, indistinguishable).
- **Background slices are short** (2026-10-14, plans 4.1 and 4.2). In every gated window of cold and warm trio timelines the longest uninterrupted background-strand slice was 0.1–9.7 ms (above 5 ms only in Komikku's cold `next` and Calculator's cold `plus`), while main slices ran to 20 ms of app work. Time-bounded preemption of background strands, and background task priority on top of it, have little to recover; 4 ms turns and priority inheritance were rejected earlier.
- **Rejected: a turn clock from the task's start** (2026-10-14, plan 1.1). The turn clock starts at a turn's first `turnElapsed` ask. Starting it when the macrotask begins (the yield channel's handler, each host rAF, each served call), so a turn that ran host work before its first ask yields sooner, traded warm for cold over eight warm and six cold alternating Komikku `--gpu` pairs. Cold sums of tap medians were 494 vs 523 ms (`start` 86 vs 111). Warm `start` was 108 vs 92 ms: without the change it split between 55–70 and 113–121 ms, and with it stayed at 100–124. Scroll was unchanged (p95 17.5–17.6 ms both ways). Warm `next` failed its known race in 2 of 8 runs without the change and 0 of 8 with it, which is not significant at this count. The plan's other half, background strands yielding near the predicted rAF, has nothing to recover in these scrolls: of the intervals over 25 ms, one was main-thread Java (two stretches of about 11.5 ms) and one was a late host rAF with the Worker idle.
- **Rejected: longer back-edge gaps** (2026-10-01). Extending the 50 ms back-edge gap to insets/layout gave `dark` 144/269 ms outliers vs ≤83: input waits for the next yield.
- **Link admission at turn budget** (2026-10-07). Lazy links yield once the turn budget is due: TikTok's largest renderer task 21,604 → 2,341 ms in failing diagnostic runs, while data modules grew 41,930 → 69,802 KB. One compile unit or GC can still exceed it.
- **A Worker's timer due on arrival** (2026-10-15). Under a controlled clock a Worker's timers live on the page's clock (`src/shell/clock-port.ts`). One posted before the Worker heard of an advance past its time (a looper's wake-up for a message due at 5,340 ms, reaching the page at 5,388.9) was filed at the current time and ran only at the next advance. A settle stepping a frame waited on that Worker's looper, which waited on the timer: CalculatorM `launch` (1 in 5–12 launches) and FitNotes `unit-metric` held 50 s with the probe's job queued behind due messages. A timer due when it arrives now runs at once, in an advance to the current time: 25 of 25 launches, then CalculatorM 12 of 12 and FitNotes 4 of 4. It fires about 3 times in a FitNotes run.
- **Host timer overflow**. Host timers take a signed 32-bit delay; Adjust's 30-day `ask_in` wrapped, re-signing every ~300 ms. Guest delays are capped.
- **One turn policy** (2026-10-20). `Sched.turn` decides yields, collections and lazy-link turns under one turn budget. An absolute park is timed against the guest wall clock Java reads (it used `Date.now()`). Initialization waits follow a chain of any length; past 64 links they used to proceed and expose partially initialized statics. Thread start (`run`, the uncaught handler, `exit`, the finish) goes through `callFn`; `Strand.call` still unwraps a plain host helper because harness entries pass `hostFn` results.

### Looper and vsync

- **Early frames for input** (2026-10-22, `vsync.ts startEarly`). A frame the main thread asked for began at the next virtual vsync, on average half a period after the request, and a tap's answer takes two or three frames (Settings: the input frame, a layout frame, the drawn one; Komikku animates a new screen in and answers in its third), so each frame boundary quantized the response to the refresh. As Chrome's compositor scheduler begins a frame on demand once input arrived or its deadline passed, a frame the main thread asks for within a period of a dispatched touch down or up or key (not a move), or when the latest vsync is a period old (an idle display, or a frame that overran its period), now begins in a microtask once no main-thread frame is open, and the vsync phase re-anchors on it. Frames never overlap, an input allows one, and under a controlled clock frames still come only as time passes. Matched `--gpu --warm --repeat 4`, 3 alternating runs per side, sums of per-tap median intended latency: Komikku 623.6 → 559.5 ms (−10%; every Settings visit 40–52 → 35–45, Appearance mixed within noise), Calculator M 98.0 → 70.4 (−28%), FitNotes 141.7 → 117.5 (−17%; `start` 65.4 → 58.4); first frames unchanged (Komikku 2.28–2.34 s against 2.17–2.39). A first version that started early frames only for an idle display barely fired: press ripples keep frames running through a tap. Those ripples also ask for their next frame before the up arrives, so no request followed the input and its frame still waited for the refresh (Komikku's `next` began its frame 3.0–8.7 ms after the input); the input's dispatch now also starts a frame already asked for, queued behind the input. Against the version without that, 3 alternating runs per side: Calculator M 84.9 → 54.3 ms (−36%, every tap lower), Komikku 572.4 → 553.5 (`next` 16.7 → 12.0, its frame beginning 1.1–1.2 ms after the input; `more` 53.4 → 43.9; Settings visits 2–5 lower), FitNotes 105.0 → 104.0; first frames unchanged.
- **Composite and frame close wait for nothing** (2026-10-14, plans 1.2 and 1.4). In cold Komikku timelines `queueBuffer` to composite start was 0 ms at p50 and at most 0.2 ms, and draw end to frame end the same, so compositing at latch and completing the frame close as a host continuation have nothing to recover. Vsync to `doFrame` was 0.1 ms at p95, and input reached dispatch within 0.2 ms. Scrolling FitNotes and Komikku, `doFrame` p95 was 2.5–3.6 ms, draw at most 0.9 ms and composite at most 0.5 ms, so Stage 7's layer cache, damage replay and lighter recording have no frame cost to remove in the gate apps; draw time includes any filter readback (7.4).
- **Frames at a frozen controlled clock** (2026-10-15). CalculatorM timed out about 1 in 6–8 quick-gate runs (`7`, `plus`, `7b` or `eq`), drawing 1,000–2,800 frames at one guest time. The host frame loop delivered a vsync at every host refresh while the controlled clock stood still, and time-driven animations asked for the next frame forever: RippleDrawable's background fade (a ValueAnimator invalidates on every update, value changed or not), re-recording the ripple host, the key and the display (26 ops a frame), and the ripple's render-thread animators, whose exit needed ~300 ms more. Under SwiftShader that redraw buried the GPU process and the page thread (one readback 17.9 s, one 64 ms advance 58 s), so the settle could not advance the clock the animation waited for. Whether the settle had stopped advancing when the ripple started decided the run, which is why timing shifts moved the failure rate. A request for the next vsync made inside a frame (from the dispatch until the looper next waits) is now answered only once time has passed that frame, as a display's vsyncs are a period apart; a renderer's next-vsync hook waits for a vsync later than the latest, stepped or not (hwui's `canDrawThisFrame`); the settle steps a pending frame itself with `testFrame`, past its 2 s bound too. A tap held at a frozen clock now draws 3 frames instead of ~30/s; CalculatorM passed 12 consecutive runs (11.6–15.9 s), FitNotes 13 of 13 interleaved with the starting commit (36–44 s against 46–63 s), and a quick gate took 180 s against 214–230 s. Stepping past the bound also waited on a Worker's late timer (**A Worker's timer due on arrival**). GMS's idle pid 1003 missing the 5 s diagnostic deadline during a game step (FlappyBird, Doodle Jump) reproduces at the starting commit under load. A real clock is unaffected, since its time always moves between vsyncs.
- **Looper polls on the immediate path** (2026-10-09). A Wasm override always awaited `NativeLooper.pollOnce` (5,409 Promise-returning calls per 15 s Mihon scroll, 2,068 zero-timeout with nothing to do). It is removed and a pending wake is consumed immediately when no callbacks are queued: suspending-import entries 6,504/6,667 → 3,032/3,172; presentation rates overlap.
- **Virtual-vsync phase** (2026-10-09). The pacer dropped rAF callbacks >0.75 ms early (21 of 874). `recordVsync` rounds to the nearest virtual period, skips missed periods and re-anchors to the host timestamp after a miss: synthetic 60 Hz with 1.8 ms jitter 559 → 600/600; Mihon scroll 55.13/55.26 → 56.53/56.73 presentations/s; replay 853/872 → 872/872. Rejected: tolerance only (catch-up frames); `max(time, deadline) + interval` (no fractional cadence at 90/120, 90/144, 120/144 Hz).
- **Virtual vsync from a faster host** (2026-10-13). A process Worker's animation frames come at 120 Hz on a 60 Hz virtual display, so every other host frame lands on the half-period boundary and jitter decided which frame was the vsync: of about 490 virtual intervals in a Komikku scroll, 26 were 8 ms and 19 were 26 ms (presentation p95 25 ms with doFrame p95 3 ms and no busy vsyncs). A frame is now at most half a host period early, the period being the mean of recent rAF spacings without skipped frames (a lower quartile collapsed under jitter), and an accepted frame pulls the phase an eighth toward itself. Synthetic 120 Hz/60 Hz with uniform ±1.5–3 ms jitter: 69–142 skipped vsyncs per 600 → 0 up to ±2.5 ms (24 → 2 at ±3 ms with the phase pull). Warm Komikku, 3 alternating pairs: scroll p95 25.3 → 19.3 ms, p99 27.6 → 21.8, intervals over 25 ms 18 → 2; transition p95 21–23 → 18–19 ms; Worker-side present p95 25.5 → 18.7 ms. Tap first-response times overlap.
- **Native choreographers** (2026-10-25). Chrome's GPU and renderer threads pace frames with AChoreographer, which libandroid lacked. Each looper thread now has one, whose frame callbacks run at the first vsync after they fall due, on that thread between its looper's messages, as DisplayEventDispatcher's fd wakes the looper; a vsync callback's frame data has one timeline. A process with no activity (Chrome's GPU process) now gets vsyncs from its start, as every process on a device does. A Java looper thread's ALooper fd callbacks also run between its messages (`22035baf`).

### Caller stacks and engine limits

- **Caller frames across JSPI stacks** (2026-10-05..10). Each `callFn` enters a fresh engine stack, so caller lookup saw only the physical stack (null `MethodHandles.lookup` caller). World keeps a strand-owned chain of lazy caller Errors split by a named marker, captured only when a host call suspends, skipped for host-only entries at the scheduler root (~33 ms in FitNotes; frame depth is no guard), and shared within one Suspending import's synchronous work (captures 21,287 → 4,411; latency unchanged). Outer frames still vanish after an explicit native wait, and nested callbacks lose outer ancestry.
- **Caller frames as call sites** (2026-10-23, `60a92b2b`). Caller-sensitive lookups (`getClosestUserClassLoader`, asked on every `readObject`; `stackClass`; `javaFrames`) took three 128-frame captures, formatted each and parsed it with a regex: 8.6% of a guava Lists profile. Frames now come from `Error.prepareStackTrace`'s call sites (function name and module offset), text stays the fallback, a search stops at the first frame its predicate accepts or at a capture that is not truncated, and frames carry their class's key. Guava's ArrayList subset (3,244 tests): 38.8 → 14.6 s.
- **Eager throwable frames only after a suspension** (2026-10-23, `dd1f2ab2`). A throwable made under a host→Java bridge read its Java frames at once whenever a bridge was open, though the engine's stack holds them until a caller suspends. Capture stays lazy unless a caller in the bridge chain has suspended: TestsForSetsInJavaUtil 28.7 → 23.5 s.
- **Host helpers owe stacks too** (2026-10-23, `23ac849d`). Annotation creation enters Java from host helpers through `mayWait`, and each entry keeps an engine stack until the strand waits; only method handles honoured `Sched.owes`. A class initializer reading hundreds of field annotations without a wait (`Settings$Global.getPublicSettingsForClass`) exhausted the engine's stacks, and `new WebAssembly.Instance` threw RangeError with a shallow JS stack, depending on whether slower code reached a turn yield first. `mayWait` now waits once when its parent owes.
- **Engine support** (2026-10-07). Chrome 153 and WebKit 26.5 support GC, exnref, tail calls, extended constants, js-string builtins and JSPI; both reject suspension through a JS frame. WebKit rejects multi-memory, memory64 and relaxed SIMD, and fails suspension after a cross-module tail call (16/16; Chromium 64/64 pass); the guarded tier that relied on them was removed on 2026-10-13 and the compiler emits no tail calls ([platform status](../research/wasm/platform-status.md)). WebKit stack lines are `function@URL` with no Wasm byte offsets, so compiled PCs are -1 there (`39d01a2c`).

## Processes and system services

### Workers and the system_server page

- **App IPC is off the tap path** (2026-10-14, plans 0.4 and 6.2–6.4). `scripts/lat/census.ts` over cold and warm trio timelines: the page started serving each Worker call within 0.3 ms (6.4's condition is a p95 over 8 ms), and the main thread waited at most 7.5 ms a window on broker calls. Komikku's `more` makes 194 SQLite calls (14–20 ms of waiting), all from background threads; FitNotes' windows make 284, 0.4–0.6 ms of them on the main thread, so a batched SQLite protocol would not shorten a tap. The largest main-thread wait is FitNotes `start`: 34 `LIST_config` calls (`DeviceConfig` properties; ART caches them through a generation tracker the provider's `call` does not return) costing 5.4 ms cold and 2.4 ms warm over a 48-entry table. Settings gets took under 0.5 ms a window, and no binder transaction (`transact`) landed in one, so neither a Settings cache nor a binder pool (6.3) has a tap to shorten.
- **Every app process in a Worker** (2026-10-11). The page realm is system_server only. Workers reach the page through the broker for MediaPlayer (a page media element), system activities' browser UI, localStorage (a written-through replica) and WebView cookies (own jar). The page owns WebView iframes and passes each renderer `MessagePort` through the app Worker's bootstrap.
- **system_server boots real Java** (2026-10-05). `src/shell/system-boot.ts` runs bundled `ActivityThread.systemMain()` (uid 1000, 64-bit) on the main queue before publishing `android/system_server`. With no bound app it gets no `bindApplication` font map, so boot registers `hostWindowManager` and calls `loadSystemFontMap` itself (their absence broke a FitNotes toast).
- **Page/Worker ownership** (2026-10-05). The page owns each process's canvas, input binding, frame requests and control proxy, and disposes them on failure, death or pagehide. It imports only a control method allowlist: evaluating Worker JVM/rendering modules in the page threw `OffscreenCanvas is not defined`.
- **Bootstrap ordering** (2026-10-05). `connectProcess()` runs before `bootApk` registers the PID; eager port acquisition raised `DeadObjectException: Process is dead: 1000` (Komikku), so ports open lazily. A MessagePort does not detect a dead peer: the broker closes its owners on death. Bootstrap passes archive ports, not live `PackedArchive`s (`4f090a6f`).
- **Readiness is not health** (2026-10-05). A Worker without its path binding reached ready, then hit `SQLITE_CANTOPEN` in WorkManager and drew nothing. A control reply does not prove Java queue liveness, so `wd.alive(ms)` checks the root and every shown Worker's main queue.
- **Per-process memory** (2026-09-30). Calculator M's three processes retained 2.1 GB, mostly 256 MiB native-core Wasm memory and 128 MiB CanvasKit heap each; untouched Wasm pages are not resident.
- **Deferred: cross-origin isolation** (2026-10-11). Nothing needs `SharedArrayBuffer` (mappings use leases, audio crosses the media port); isolation would constrain cross-origin WebView frames.
- **Reverted: starting Play services earlier** (2026-10-10). Mihon idled ~2 s on a synchronous GMS provider query while the GMS Worker booted. Starting GMS at process registration left Mihon unchanged (that boot waits on the page) and cost Calculator up to ~70 ms.
- **One ProcessRecord, one RPC module** (2026-10-20). A process has one ProcessRecord (starting, ready or dead), and each failure step is isolated. `process-port.ts` became `rpc.ts` (CallFailure, options-object `callProxy`/`duplex`), and `javaFailure` lives in broker-contract. The disk store is a typed Worker (`disk-worker.ts`) over `serveCalls`. One bounded `captureLogs` (4000 lines) serves the page and Workers; the Worker buffer had been unbounded and copied per snapshot. `wd` is typed in `src/shell/harness.ts`. Each realm picks one ClockSource: `setSystemTimeout` had followed host timers in a Worker while `clearGuestTimeout` cancelled on the page's clock. A fixture Worker must not statically import `broker-contract.ts`, which pulls in the runtime and times browser tests out.

### Binder, Parcels and Intents

- **Hosted services cross a parcel** (2026-09-30..10-02). TS AIDL Stubs (`hostStub`) act as system_server (uid 1000). Apps get BinderProxies and `execTransact` runs over parcel copies, so >200 KB throws TransactionTooLargeException (microG's GET_META_DATA provider query: 1.4 MB). Cost (bun): trivial call +6 µs, relayout +58 µs; FitNotes makes ~140 calls to first frame.
- **No synchronous binder call across the client's own queue** (2026-10-03/04). startActivity/finishActivity awaited a pause the caller's parked main thread had to run (deadlock when onCreate starts an activity). ATM returns once the start is under way, as ActivityStarter does.
- **Parcels and Intents** (2026-09-28..10-05). A ParcelFileDescriptor crosses as bytes plus a writer binder written back on last close, so imported parcels must be recycled. Bundles stay parcelled until read, so the broker routes without the sender's classes; keys iterate by hash (androidx.startup order follows). An `Account[]` sent as object array failed AndroidX `getParcelableArray`. IntentFilter.match takes 0.1-0.4 ms for YouTube's ~90 filters, 16-20 ms on first build.
- **Framework data and providers** (2026-10-03/04). Data classes framework callers cast must be AOSP's: a TS RunningAppProcessInfo failed Firebase's cast and aborted bindApplication (Mihon's Injekt error). Providers install by descending initOrder; a local holder needs `mLocal`, or ActivityThread waits 20 s.
- **Worker Binder lifetime** (`5ae61c70`). Death watches go through the process owner; only tokens cross, recipients stay in their JVM, and a late ack/unlink/death cannot resurrect a removed one.
- **System callers and URI grants** (2026-10-20). SYSTEM_CALLER stays pid 0, because AttributionSource skips its pid check only for pid 0 (pid 1000 broke the settings tests). PendingIntent senders are `{pid: SYSTEM_SERVER_PID, id: -record}`. URI grants are one system_server table (`server/urigrants.ts`) keyed by target uid, recorded at resolveStart, startService, result delivery and system activities. Not modelled: forceUriPermissions, path permissions, revoking persisted grants and grants on broadcast intents. Activity watchdogs stay on host time, since the controlled guest clock jumps to pending deadlines before another Worker could answer. One static cycle remains (atm, broadcastqueue, permissions, pm, appdata, packageinstaller).

### Cross-process storage ownership

- **Worker store contract** (2026-10-05). Canonical stores (SQLite, cookies, clipboard, settings, accounts, preferences, notifications, private files, exit history...) stay with the page. Worker commands bind the registered PID, the owner resolves UID (supplied identity is ignored), and acknowledged granular mutations, never whole-file writeback, land before main-looper callbacks. Recurring bugs: a returned Promise read as a value, and a port opened before PID registration.
- **Per-store authority** (2026-10-05). Private preferences need same UID or system. PendingIntents match with Android's `Intent.filterEquals` in the owner; only serialized Intents cross. Alarms stay in the broker and can fire into a replacement. Account visibility grants identity, not token access; a password change invalidates tokens.
- **SQLite process service** (2026-10-05). Connections share one canonical sql.js file so connection-local TEMP/PRAGMA/transaction state survives. Statement tokens retire at finalize dispatch (SQLite destroys the statement even if finalize errors); a `close_v2` zombie keeps its file owner until its last statement finalizes.
- **Service starts and location** (2026-10-05). Service lifecycle steps await owner acks; no ServiceRecords or callbacks cross. Location subscriptions carry a generation token, so stale events miss a replacement listener.

### Descriptors and shared memory

- **Canonical regular-file descriptors** (2026-10-05). ChatGPT's DataStore `…json.tmp` ENOENT had a split owner: canonical `File.mkdir` succeeded but Worker-local `open(O_CREAT)` saw no parent. `src/shell/file-descriptors.ts` owns regular files behind PID-bound commands that Worker libcore and ARM calls await; `dup` shares offset and flags, CLOEXEC is per descriptor. Pipes/sockets are unsupported.
- **Path natives follow the owning realm** (2026-10-12). Mihon's DataStore failed every rename: a Worker's `UnixNativeDispatcher` path calls read its own VFS while app data lives in the broker's. They and `java.io.File`'s mtime/permission natives now go to the owner, as `Os` did.
- **Descriptor rights in Binder** (2026-10-05..07). A transaction sends a one-shot token; the broker installs a CLOEXEC receiver reference sharing backing and offset. Clipboard and notifications keep rights in a broker `StoredParcel` that outlives the sender and dups into each reader. PendingIntent creation rejects descriptor-bearing Intents, as Android does. Shared ashmem rights fail.
- **Live mapping leases** (`fb4867ae`, `e2655f19`, `ab801620`). File-range leases outlive the descriptor and back SharedMemory, `Os.mmap` and FileChannel mappings, sharing pages across close/unlink/truncate; mutation epochs close a release-before-flush hole. Raw JNI addresses, synchronous byte views, shared atomics and native MAP_SHARED (ENOTSUP) stay unsupported.
- **`fixupAppDir`** (2026-10-05). Follows Android 15 vold, repairing only existing components (package UID, category GID, 02770). No quota or ACLs; no app run showed a call.
- **A Worker's stats stand while the page's change counts hold** (2026-10-27). A process Worker's stat, fstat and lseek of the page's files each waited 60–210 µs for an answer the page made in 2–13 µs: the reply waits behind the slices the Worker runs for its other threads. The page now counts every change a stat can see (a node's existence, content, size, attributes and mtime, and with them each directory above it) in 4096 hashed slots of a SharedArrayBuffer its Workers share, bumped within the turn that makes the change. A Worker keeps each path stat and each descriptor's fstat (an open's reply carries one) with the count it read before asking, and answers from it while the count holds; denials are asked again, as they follow the installed packages. Without cross-origin isolation nothing is shared or kept. Over RimWorld's menu load the Worker's page round trips fell from 237K to 126K (path stats 96K to 21K, fstats 37K to none) and UnityMain's file waits from 17.8 s to 15.1 s, under 1% of the load: alternated runs differed by ±2 s while the host drifted more. A position the Worker held for files it opened for reading, with 64 KiB read-ahead, was measured and rejected: it made 26K of UnityMain's 79K reads synchronous and its lseeks local, yet its read waits stayed at 11.2 s, as each remaining fetch waited longer, and only lseek's 0.9 s came off.
- **Unix sockets between processes** (2026-10-25). Chrome passes each child one end of a socketpair in its ChildProcessService bundle, and Mojo's channel runs over it. A socket in a Parcel now crosses through the broker: the page's kernel holds the end's MessagePort while it travels, the receiver takes it, and a bridge on each side carries bytes, end of input and close (`socket/bridge.ts`). Open files passed with SCM_RIGHTS cross as rights the kernel parks for the reading process, or, read-only files no other realm can open, as copies, as Binder sends them.

### Services and process death

- **System work outlives app processes** (2026-10-02). Broadcast resolution, PendingIntent matching, alarms and death unbinds ran on app JVMs or guest timers that a dead process never resumes. The broker owns them (`setSystemTimeout`); a separate system realm was rejected then at 0.6-0.7 s, ~75 MiB.
- **Service restarts follow ActiveServices** (2026-10-02/03). Broker ServiceRecords own starts and bindings. After a host dies, unfinished starts redeliver (≤3), sticky ones return with a null intent, auto-create bindings rebind; delays 1 s, x4 within a minute. Repeat crashes restart only foreground starts, up to 16. Not modelled: procIsBoundForeground, the bad-process mark.
- **Service lifetime after onDestroy** (2026-09-30). onDestroy posts runnables whose LifecycleRegistry holds the service weakly; dropping it on return broke ChatGPT. A main-queue job holds it, as CLEAN_UP_CONTEXT does (3/6 → 0/8).
- **Bound processes inherit priority** (2026-10-02). DoodleJump deadlocks unless billing binds before its game loop, but the service booted at background priority (bind 7-8 s). A process now inherits a waiting client's priority and scheduling group (OomAdjuster): bind 0.8-0.9 s.
- **Process death and crashes** (2026-10-02..05). Exit paths and SIGKILL never return (`src/process-exit.ts`). The broker's `fail(pid, message, death)` fires death recipients, throws DeadObjectException to callers and cancels bindings and activities. Oneway stubs follow execTransact (RuntimeException warns, Error aborts). AMS shows a dialog only on a repeat within MIN_CRASH_INTERVAL; exits persist per package (16).
- **Terminal classification** (2026-10-05). Dead rows carry `terminal: {expected, cause, record}`; only EXIT_SELF status 0 or USER_REQUESTED without a crash is expected (`NOT_CRASHED` alone is not).
- **Isolated service instances** (2026-10-25). Chrome starts each renderer with `bindServiceInstance` and an instance name, which was ignored, so every renderer bound the one running service. As `retrieveServiceLocked` does, each instance of a FLAG_ISOLATED_PROCESS service is now its own record in its own process, named for the instance (`…:SandboxedProcessService0:N`); an instance name on another service throws IllegalArgumentException. `updateServiceGroup` checks only that the connection bound, since no process has an OOM adjustment here. A request that would not start its process now answers null when the host dies before answering (a service's last unbind may end its process), where it had failed the caller.
- **A task outlives its process** (2026-10-28, `16360af7`). Chrome's Relaunch (chrome://flags, `restartBrowser`) starts BrowserRestartActivity in `:browser_restart_process`. That activity kills the browser process, starts the main activity in a new browser process and kills itself. The restarted browser came up with its tabs, but the page watched only the pid it had launched and showed "The app closed" over it. A watched death now waits for the activity changes it set off (`ActivityStack.settled`). It then follows the package's process on top, if one is there, and shows "The app closed" only when none is. After a Relaunch the shell stayed `running` throughout, on the restored chrome://flags tab in the new browser process.

### Activities, windows and input

- **ATM fidelity** (2026-09-26..10-04). Launch modes, task flags, Back and home follow ActivityStarter. Clients hold BinderProxy ATM tokens (local Binders made `recreate` a no-op). A second pause waits on the first; a start ignores a record a finish destroyed (TikTok's splash). `wd.back()` returns a Promise settled by the owner's ack.
- **System windows and input routing** (2026-10-05). The root canvas composites transparently above app canvases. Page input applies WindowManager focus and touch-modal/`NOT_TOUCHABLE` rules before routing to a Worker; navigation keys go to the root while a root window has focus.
- **IME across the Worker boundary** (2026-10-05). The page owns a hidden text control per canvas; the Worker keeps the InputConnection. Commands carry an editor generation rechecked on the main queue. Typing `60` mirrored `6060` because an async partial ExtractedText landed on the optimistic update; partials now apply to a last-reported baseline. Root IME shares the path.
- **The IME mirror's own selection** (2026-10-24, `5084647c`). `setSelectionRange` on the page's hidden textarea fires `select` asynchronously, and its handler sent the editor's own selection back as an edit, which undid any edit made since: FitNotes' select-all-on-focus came back after the typed weight and left "65" selected in 2 of 4 runs. A selection equal to the editor's reported one is not sent.
- **A physical rotation reaches the app** (2026-10-15). `wd.configuration` asked only the page realm's top activity and turned only the page's display, so a Worker app filling the display kept its orientation while the page's turned. ActivityStack now sends the rotation to the process whose activity fills the display, as DisplayRotation resolves the sensor's rotation against that activity's request, behind queued activity changes. That process keeps a fixed, nosensor or locked request and otherwise turns; the page follows the turn it reports. FitNotes (unspecified) turns with the page; Flappy Bird (portrait) keeps both. The rotation is now device-wide sensor state, as DisplayRotation reads the sensor's proposed rotation: ActivityStack keeps the last one (the configured screen's until then), and a process whose activity comes to fill the display resolves it against its own top activity's request by the same rule, as updateOrientation does when the orientation source changes, unawaited so the queue never waits on the page's main loop. The page's `ownLandscape` is gone, so its own fixed request holds when it fills the display again. With microG's launcher activity in its own Worker over FitNotes, microG's process, left in portrait, now turns landscape when it fills the display again after a rotation made with FitNotes in front, and FitNotes turns back to portrait after one made with microG in front; at fbdc6a0ab both kept their old orientation. Flappy Bird (portrait) keeps portrait throughout while microG takes the sensor's landscape. Each realm now keeps that state too, from its bootstrap and from every rotation, which ActivityStack records in all processes, and its atm resolves by the same rule whenever an activity starts, resumes or changes its request: the request it resolves is that of the top activity that is visible and either specifies an orientation or occludes, as WindowContainer.getOrientation takes it. So a new Worker launches its activity in the resolved rotation, and a finished activity's orientation gives way to the one it reveals. FitNotes rotated landscape turns portrait for TrainingLogActivity (portrait) and back to landscape for the exercise list a back returns to; at a6f01af84 the atm turned only for a fixed request, so by its code the list kept portrait. Nosensor takes the natural rotation, as rotationForOrientation gives it; locked, and a realm with no activity that sets the orientation, keep the display as it is. Limits: a translucent process shown over the one that fills the display resolves only its own activities, so it keeps its orientation when that one turns, where AOSP gives every window the display's rotation; no flow has shown one across a rotation. A behind request (3) resolves as the sensor's rotation, where getOrientation takes the orientation of the activity beneath it.
- **Multi-pointer touch** (2026-10-15). The shell dispatched one pointer per canvas through `MotionEvent.obtain(JJIFFI)`, so no pinch or rotation reached an app. `pointer-input.ts` now keeps every browser pointer of a gesture and numbers them as InputReader does (lowest free id, listed in id order): the first press is DOWN, later ones POINTER_DOWN with their index, POINTER_UP still carries the lifting pointer and the last lift is UP; a `pointercancel` cancels the whole gesture, and the pointers still down are ignored until they lift. `wm.dispatchTouch` builds every event with the PointerProperties/PointerCoords `obtain`, with fresh objects per event because another dispatch can run while one waits on a construction. The tool type is now FINGER (UNKNOWN before); pressure and size stay 1. The window under the first pointer takes the whole gesture; split touch across windows is not modeled. Chrome runs a microtask checkpoint after each browser-dispatched listener, so a microtask flush still sent one MOVE per finger (CDP: 37 MOVEs for 24 two-finger steps). Several fingers' moves now wait for `drainPointers` in the animation frame, which Chrome runs right after the frame's rAF-aligned input (24 for 24); a lone pointer still dispatches each move at once. A mouse or pen press with Alt held adds a second finger mirrored through the display's centre, as the emulator's pinch does. Process canvases lacked `touch-action: none` (only `#screen` had it), so Chrome took a real two-finger touch as its own pinch-zoom: the app got CANCEL after three MOVEs, and the page itself zoomed, which looked like the map zooming further after each later gesture. In Google Maps a CDP two-finger spread zooms in, an Alt-held drag toward the centre zooms out, a two-finger turn rotates the map (the compass appears) and a one-finger drag pans, with no new missing APIs or uncaught errors. `lat` kept every gated tap under 150 ms (slowest Komikku `appearance`, 144 ms).
- **Trackpad pinch** (2026-10-15). Chrome sends a trackpad pinch, like Ctrl + a mouse wheel, as `wheel` events with `ctrlKey` set and deltaY = -100 ln(scale): a synthesized 3× touchpad pinch arrived as 45 events summing to -109.86. The shell treated every wheel burst as a one-finger drag, so a pinch scrolled the app. A Ctrl burst now drives two fingers about the cursor. They go down (DOWN, POINTER_DOWN) once each finger's travel passes the touch slop and lift (POINTER_UP 0, UP) when the stream idles; a burst that gains or drops Ctrl lifts the gesture it drove. They come no closer than one `config_minScalingSpan` (27 mm, 510 px at 480 dpi), since ScaleGestureDetector ends a scale below it. One burst therefore zooms out at most 2×, while zooming in may carry the fingers off the screen, as the scroll finger can. Moving the fingers straight to the accumulated scale failed in Google Maps. With one or two MOVEs after POINTER_DOWN it scaled nothing, and neither did a CDP two-finger touch with two MOVEs; with three it scaled. After one of those gestures a dropped pin appeared at the first finger's press point. Scale detectors begin on the MOVE that crosses their slop and measure from that span, so the first MOVE only crossed the slop and each later frame covered half the remaining ratio (until the easing below); a wheel notch (2.7×) arrived as about 14 MOVEs. In Maps, Ctrl+wheel notches and synthesized touchpad pinches (3×, 2×, 0.6×) zoom in and out about the cursor and a plain wheel still pans. The process reported no missing APIs, native requirements or draw errors.
- **Pinch finger placement** (2026-10-15, `c416bdd7`). The fingers first went down two minimum spans apart (1,022 px) along the display's longer side. In portrait Maps both landed on the map only for a cursor in a band about 60 px tall between the chip row and the sheet. Elsewhere the first pressed the search bar or a chip, and split touch gave the map one pointer, so nothing scaled. A phone's layout stacks full-width bands, so the pair now goes down level on the cursor's row. It slides along the row only to keep both fingers out of ViewConfiguration's 12 dp edge slop. Maps' gesture controller (`bjst`) reads `getScaledEdgeSlop` and, like AOSP's early ScaleGestureDetector, defers a pinch while a finger is inside it. ScaleGestureDetector needs the minimum span only once a scale begins, so a spread starts one minimum span apart. A squeeze starts two apart, or as wide as the display allows outside the edge slop. In Maps, spreads and squeezes zoom under the search bar, above the sheet and on the compass row. A finger pressed on the compass still zoomed the map, which takes the pinch through its container.
- **Pinch easing** (2026-10-15, `9f4b6ca0`). The note that a squeeze arriving within one frame scaled nothing in Maps came from shots taken mid-gesture, with five or six of its twelve events dispatched. Shot after the UP, the halving steps kept 0.86× of a requested 0.7× and 1.14× of 1.43×. Maps' gesture controller (`bjst`) keeps the samples of the last 250 ms since the last pointer change, at least three and at most 20. Its scale recognizer (`bjsy`) takes the gesture at the third sample once the span has moved a tenth of its 160 base unit, which fit 48 px (16 dp) here. Its listener (`bjdc`) then zooms by each event's span ratio, starting with the ratio into the third sample. So Maps drops the slop MOVE and the step after it, and halving made that step the burst's largest (958 → 822 px). Each MOVE now carries a finger as far as it has come, or half the way left if that is less, but at least the touch slop and a pixel. A one-frame 0.7× squeeze now goes 958, 908, 808, 757, 707, 706 px and kept 0.774× and 0.782×, and a 1.43× spread kept 1.19× and 1.21×. A 2.7× wheel notch reaches 1,389 px from 611 at Maps' second sample (2.27× by the model), where halving reached 1,380 from 880 (1.57×). Trackpad-paced bursts (30 events 16 ms apart) follow each event, and Maps' own 48 px threshold leaves 0.78× and 1.21×, as it would of a finger's pinch. One-frame squeezes and spreads zoom under the search bar, mid-map and above the sheet. Scales were measured by normalized cross-correlation of satellite-layer shots. Streams injected straight into the pointer route matched the model to 0.002, with MOVEs 8 or 60 ms apart alike. Open: one halving squeeze that began about a second after a spread's UP scaled nothing; it was not reproduced.

### Packages, settings and notifications

- **Platform semantics from the AVD** (2026-10-02). Runtime-permission AppOps are IGNORED until granted. Non-@Readable settings throw at every target SDK; WRITE_SETTINGS holds only below targetSdk 23. SSAID is HMAC-SHA256 of signing certificates under a per-device key. Two ungrouped notifications add a `ranker_group` summary cancel() cannot remove.
- **Clear data and uninstall** (2026-10-02). Per services.jar a clear force-stops and wipes data, grants, keystore, notifications, jobs and alarms; accounts and exit records survive (`src/shell/packagedata.ts`). Bugs found: a dead process's SQLite timer rewrote a cleared database, and processes re-registered a removed package.
- **Split resources publish with the package** (2026-10-05). `registerApk` keeps the first entry, so a base-only table gave Worker YouTube `NotFoundException` for a split drawable; split tables and lazy assets are added before `registerPackage`.
- **Platform state outside app preferences** (2026-10-20). The doze allowlist, night mode and locales (`android/packageconfig.ts`) and notification channels, groups and delegate (NotificationStore) have stores of their own. Preferences are only shared_prefs files, with a one-time import of an older build's `wd:prefs:` files; the platform preference store and its broker domain are gone. Signature permissions follow the declaration scan, so WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS and the like are no longer reported held. One alarm table and timer live in AlarmManagerService. `procIdentity` is the single process identity. `registerPackage` lives in `installpackage.ts`: a vfs↔packages cycle made rolldown emit `await` inside a synchronous `__esmMin` wrapper, which typechecks but fails to parse in the browser bundle. Keystore entries persist under a key each, so processes of one uid no longer overwrite each other's keys. Unfinished: the broker does not yet construct the settings, permission and notification stores (boot reads settings before `connectProcess`, and node hosts rely on module-level stores), and the page holds two PermissionStores over the same keys.
- **Store installs** (2026-10-25). APKPure downloads Pokémon TCG Pocket's XAPK and commits one session of three APKs: a 46 MB base, a 191 MB `config.arm64_v8a` split and a 555 MB `bundledtree` asset pack. A commit installed only a single base, and any failure shows "User canceled." in APKPure, whose receiver maps every status other than success, pending user action, conflict and storage to that string. The broker now installs a base with its splits once each split matches the base's package, version code and signer (`validateApkInstallLocked`). The staged files leave the VFS and their bytes are transferred, trimmed of spare capacity, and the base and splits are persisted as blobs in one IndexedDB record, so the store can keep them on disk. At commit and at restore alike, system_server opens the splits over the base lazily (`openInstalledSplits`): every split entry is read when opened and only its resource table is resident, so restoring the 191 MB library split reads none of it. `registerPackage` takes the merged `Install`, keeps the splits on the `PackageRecord` and joins their entries and tables to the base's, so a committed, restored or dropped install registers the same way. A failed install reports its INSTALL_* code (a downgrade, a signer change, a signature failure) rather than one invalid-apk status. Results carry AOSP's extras: `STATUS` and `STATUS_MESSAGE` from `PackageManager.installStatusToPublicStatus`/`installStatusToString`, `LEGACY_STATUS` and `PRE_APPROVAL`. `requestUserPreapproval` answers `INSTALL_FAILED_PRE_APPROVAL_NOT_AVAILABLE`, as on a device whose `config_isPreApprovalRequestAvailable` is false, and APKPure then commits. Limits: inherited-split sessions (`MODE_INHERIT_EXISTING`) fail, and an app process's package manager gets no split resources for another package. The installed game's process started from APKPure's OPEN but answered no diagnostics for 4 minutes; that launch was not investigated.
- **Apps open their base apk lazily** (2026-10-26). An app process read its whole base APK into its heap at boot and kept every entry it inflated, and system_server read it whole again for its signing block. RimWorld's APK is 713 MB in 8,710 entries (`libil2cpp.so` 121 MB, Unity data under `assets/`). A downloaded or local APK is now one Blob that the page shares with each Worker, which reads it directly instead of through a served port. `openApk` keeps resident only what a process reads synchronously as it boots: everything outside `lib/` and `assets/`, plus `assets/dexopt/`. It reads those in as few ranges as they lie in (20 of RimWorld's entries), and every other entry is read from the Blob when opened. Certificates come from the signing block, central directory and end record alone (`apkSignersOf`). Lazy reads matched `openInstall` byte for byte over all 8,710 entries, partial reads included. At the main menu the renderer's footprint fell 10.0 GB → 8.7 and 8.1 GB in two runs. ArrayBuffer and Wasm memory (VM tag 255) fell 7.4 → 5.6 and 5.4 GB, dirty plus swapped. The rest is mostly each process's guest memory and its JIT code (RimWorld: 36–37k regions in about 3,500 modules, none evicted). One of three runs died of SIGSEGV while loading definitions: an il2cpp XML DOM walk (`libil2cpp+0x65167cc`) loaded a child pointer `0xc62a021`, which is misaligned. The crash did not recur on a fresh profile or on a copy of the profile it crashed with; it is unexplained, and earlier runs of the old code had not crashed there.

## Java and framework semantics

### Libcore natives and contracts

- **TS copies kept breaking documented contracts** (2026-09-26..10-03). Most pre-migration failures were a TS copy missing one: copied `subList`/map views (Compose, Glide), an `LruCache` without `entryRemoved` (SQLCipher close BUSY), TextView skipping virtual `getCompoundPadding*`. `bun run bypass` and `desccheck` guard what TS still implements; TS exchanges collections through their Java API (`jcoll.ts`), where comparators and iteration can suspend.
- **Semantics the intrinsics and JNI must keep** (2026-09-27..10-01). Android's `u_islower` is category Ll, so `ª` is not lower case (OpenJDK says it is); `link/chars.ts` must follow ICU. `StrictMath`'s fdlibm port (`fdlibm.ts`) is bit-exact on 435 AVD probes. JNI strings use ART's modified UTF-8: NUL as two bytes, a valid surrogate pair as one four-byte sequence.
- **Regex over a JS RegExp translation** (2026-10-01). Pattern/Matcher run over `PatternNative`/`MatcherNative` in `jregex.ts`. `.`, `^` and `$` use ICU's line terminators and UNIX_LINES; Java's `\x{h}` is JS's `\u{h}`; under the `v` flag a property escape in a class set needs its braces. hitEnd/requireEnd follow ICU's RegexMatcher (kept across `find()`), computed only when read by derived patterns consuming an appended sentinel.
- **Charsets are ICU's** (2026-09-29..10-01). UTF-32 encodes LE after `FF FE 00 00` and decodes BE unmarked. After malformed input a decoder sits past it, an encoder before it; an unmapped single-byte-table byte is unmappable, not malformed (`src/jvm/charset.ts`). Open device mismatch: `new String({0xc3}, "UTF-16")` is "" on the device, U+FFFD here.
- **zlib natives run pako at z_stream level** (2026-10-01). okio and GZIP trailers read unconsumed input back through `getRemaining`, which fflate cannot report. pako's `updatewindow` skips resizing after `inflateReset`, so a reused Inflater failed "invalid distance too far back"; the reset native sizes the window.
- **zlib in Wasm** (2026-10-28). Archive entries (fflate), java.util.zip's natives and ARM32 libz (pako) now run zlib 1.3.1 compiled by Zig into a Wasm module of its own (`src/zlib.ts`, built by `scripts/generated.ts`), each call copying a window of input in and output out. Standalone in Chrome: a 149 MB `.so` inflated in 330 ms against fflate's 671 and pako's 582; 4 MB streamed through 8K buffers in 14 against 27 ms; deflate level 6 25 against 38 ms; CRC-32 4.6× faster with the copy. In matched profiles of first launches, inflating took 387 against 958 ms (Bloons TD 6), 267 against 854 (Subway Surfers) and 213 against 363 (Doodle Jump's libz); wall-clock launches varied by ±1.5 s run to run and do not resolve the difference. zlib-ng was 20–50% faster still but compresses differently from zlib, failing five DeflaterInputStream conformance tests that compare exact byte counts. The app's dexes still inflate through DecompressionStream (`inflateTree`) beside the zygote: on Komikku's dexes it took 73–147 ms against the module's 64–68, but it overlaps with other work, which a synchronous inflate does not.
- **Bootstrap order and references** (2026-10-07/12). FileSystems init reached VMClassLoader, whose JarFiles re-entered the incomplete default filesystem; `preloaded-classes` initializes VMClassLoader first, as `66e6b7d2` does in the async harness bootstrap. core-oj's `Reference.enqueue()` only queues and keeps the referent.
- **Direct buffer arrays kept** (2026-10-20). `ByteBuffer.array()` on a direct buffer still answers a copy made at the call, with `hasArray()` false. Throwing UnsupportedOperationException, as the Buffer contract allows, would break Cronet, which reads native writes through `array()`. The `android_getaddrinfo` native was unreachable, since the Wasm tier's intrinsic always answers in its place, and is deleted.

### Device identity, ICU and startup costs

- **Device properties** (2026-09-28..10-02). Properties are the AVD's 208 read-only app-readable ones; build identity is the Pixel 9 image's (`tokay-ap3a.241005.015`). The zone follows `persist.sys.timezone` via the RuntimeHooks supplier (else GMT), `user.locale` `persist.sys.locale`; the Monet overlay replaces 140 `system_*` colors.
- **ICU data on paged mappings** (2026-09-29..10-02). ICUBinary maps the 27.3 MB `icudt75l.dat`; a date format touches 165 4 KB pages. Read-only mappings are 64 KB pages outside guest memory, fetched on first touch while the caller suspends: first date format 1.64 vs 4.95 s at 30 ms/50 Mbit. Rejected: a lazily zeroed ArrayBuffer with a residency bitmap (libmalloc zeroed the 28 MB calloc from its resident cache). Page-shared mapping pages took process realms' `Typeface.<clinit>` from 92–137 to 42–47 ms warm.
- **LikelySubtags in `Typeface.<clinit>`** (2026-09-30..10-03). The lookup cost ~60 ms cold (Calculator M boot 425 → 510 ms). A JS-tier intrinsic over a page-shared `langInfo.res` decode cut it to 17–21 ms; it never applied to Wasm and was deleted 2026-10-13 (`e65f0a6e`). Rejected then: building only `LikelySubtags$Data` (first call 39–74 vs 21–29 ms), prefetching at zygote start (3 ms), skipping clinit lookups (Calendar moves the decode into launch), non-blocking `nativeWarmUpCache` (nothing).
- **System and shared libraries** (2026-10-01/02). ICU4J cannot answer ICU's version natives during `System.<clinit>` (properties still null), so `jicu.ts` reads them from the .dat as ICU4C does. ApplicationInfo carries SharedLibraryInfos (SystemConfig jars plus implicit Apache/`android.test.base` for old targets), asked before the app's own classes (YouTube's `org.apache.http.legacy` exocache server had died on NoClassDefFoundError).
- **First-use costs** (2026-10-01). TikTok's v1 signature made the first class-path resource read ~500 ms cold (JCA provider list ~100 ms, first `Calendar.getInstance(UTC)` 116 ms). OverlayConfig's scan: ~27–35 ms a process.

### Files, storage and SQLite

- **Files and descriptors on the VFS** (2026-09-28..10-01). symlink/link fail EPERM, readlink EINVAL; rename under a non-directory parent fails ENOENT/ENOTDIR. ProfileInstaller needs installd's `/data/misc/profiles/cur/0/<pkg>/` and catches IOException, not ErrnoException. `jniCreateFileDescriptor` goes through `FileDescriptor.setInt$`, which records append; writable provider descriptors need a backing file; a length-0 asset descriptor made AssetFileDescriptor drop every write.
- **Large files on OPFS**. Files past 4 MiB move to OPFS, one worker holding sync handles and running requests in order; IndexedDB keeps `{id, size}`. All opens of a path share one `VfsFile`, and shared writes refresh open views (Organic Maps' range downloads overwrote each other; a short native view failed Monaco's BLAKE3). SQLite and preferences stay in memory for synchronous readers. Genshin's full download exceeds the 4.7 GB quota.
- **Private file flush order** (2026-09-27). `PrivateFiles.flush` committed a spilled file's record before its bytes, the likely cause of Cut the Rope failing `Assembly::Load("mscorlib.dll")` after an interrupted first boot; bytes now land first.
- **SQLite over sql.js** (2026-10-05/06). One sqlite3 handle per connection keeps its TEMP state (sql.js export reopens a connection, destroying TEMP objects). Snapshots follow transactions through a nonblocking PASSIVE WAL checkpoint, wait for autocommit on every connection, and one staleness rule stops a pending one overwriting a restored database. The vendor build binds real int64 (unsafe numbers reread as BigInt). Open+close of 40 MB 7.7 → 1.4 ms. Error codes and collator strengths are unverified.
- **One VFS node per path** (2026-10-20). One Map read per lookup; owners attach through `vfs.watch()`/`useOwner()` instead of overwritable onWrite/prepare/flush slots, and a rename moves the whole node. ns/op old → new: writeAt 78–89 → 70–73, read 131–141 → 123–125, whole-file write 341–347 → 148–149, isDir 142 → 107–108. Directory listing order follows the node table. A DiskFile read in flight during an overlapping write cached the old bytes.
- **Implicit directories by index** (2026-10-26). `exists`, `isDir` and `list` scanned every node for a path's descendants, so each stat of a missing path and each file created cost O(nodes). RimWorld's first launch extracts ~34k files into its app directory with about ten broker calls apiece; at 34,458 nodes the page took 3.7–3.9 ms per `open(O_CREAT)` or missing-path `fstatat`, 93 opens took 483 ms of a 2 s window and the Worker idled 86%. Each directory now counts the nodes under each of its names, kept at insert and delete. With 36,004 nodes (node): a missing path's `exists` 0.34 → 0.0034 ms, `list` 5.6 ms → 1 µs; inserting a node costs 6 µs instead of 1. Listing order follows when a name first gained a node.
- **Written files keep no growth slack** (2026-10-26). A file written through a descriptor grows by doubling, and its node, its open `VfsFile` and the persisted value all kept a view of that buffer. RimWorld's first launch extracts 32,320 files (624 MB) into `/sdcard/Android/data`, and those held 757 MB of buffers in the page. A flush already copied such a view before storing it. That copy now replaces the view in memory as well, unless the file changed since: after extraction the buffers hold 624 MB. A writer still appending regrows from the copy, at most once per flush. The 624 MB itself stays in the page, because the private file store keeps every file under 4 MiB resident and loads them all from IndexedDB when a package's data is prepared.
- **Devices, library paths and libcore stat** (2026-10-25). APKPure's native libraries aborted in bionic's FORTIFY `fread`: they read their own image from its `dladdr` path, where no file stood. They also opened `/dev/null`, `/proc/uptime` and `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`, which did not exist. `/dev/null` and the random devices are now character devices that drop writes. Uptime comes from the guest clock, and the boot id is a UUID derived from boot time, the same in every process. A module now records the path dlopen, `System.load` or a class loader's library path found it at; a class-path library takes the path the installer left it at, the extracted copy or `base.apk!/lib/<abi>/<name>`. libcore's `Linux.lstat` is `stat` (the VFS has no symbolic links, as `lstat0` already assumed), and `posix_fallocate` grows the file as fallocate(2) does. `getrusage` (arm64 165, ARM 77) reports the CPU-time clocks `clock_gettime` reads as user time, and zero for children.

### Networking, crypto and TLS

- **HttpURLConnection through the proxy** (2026-09-27..10-03). Requests use the proxy's `manual=1` mode and follow redirects hop by hop for CookieHandler; redirect URIs escape `|`, `{` and bare `%` as OkHttp's `HttpUrl.uri()` does. Akamai rejects requests lacking `Sec-Fetch-*` or browser Accept-Encoding, or with headless `sec-ch-*`; a service worker cannot see forbidden headers it forwards, so it rebuilds them from `request.mode`/`destination`.
- **JCA and TLS** (2026-10-01). JCA runs from libcore over conscrypt NativeCrypto natives; async WebCrypto cannot back it. AndroidKeyStore uses a keystore2 service with a software KeyMint. TLS ends at the relay, whose CA and per-host leaf pass hostname checks; certificate pinning fails.
- **Crypto costs** (2026-10-01). SHA-256 in JS tops out at 3.1 ms/MB, so digests ≥ 64 KB use `crypto.subtle.digest` (1 MB 3.3 → 0.46 ms). ECDSA P-256 verify 467 → 310–322 µs (Barrett reduction, point tables). Rejected: Int32/Float64 limbs, Lehmer inversion, per-verify WebCrypto (a suspension each).
- **TLS on the socket's descriptor** (2026-10-20). `startTls` swaps a connected TcpSocket's relay for a TLS one, so SSLSocket is a libcore Socket plus its handshake, session, ALPN and cipher methods. Shutdown is real, `isBound` no longer reports the relay, and a failed connect throws SSLHandshakeException, SocketTimeoutException or NoRouteToHostException instead of ConnectException. SSLEngine and client KeyManagers are refused, since the relay terminates TLS. The crypto primitives live in `src/crypto/` (one digest registry, typed RSA paddings, shared CBC/ECB/PKCS#7 modes, one certificate builder), and node-forge is gone: v1 signature certificates are read from PKCS #7 over `der.ts`. ICU's Unicode blocks are recorded by gen-framework.

### Resources and input

- **Resources follow libandroidfw** (2026-09-27..30). Bags flatten with parents and key-sort; `obtainStyledAttributes(resId, attrs)` falls back to the theme. Unqualified `drawable/` PNGs are mdpi: density comes from the table configuration, not a path qualifier (resource shrinking removes it). `SoundPool.load(AssetFileDescriptor)` never completing blocked Flutter's Dart init (Lichess blank; found by a logcat diff).
- **Browser input** (2026-10-01/03). Text goes through InputConnection and Editable methods; wheel input needs timestamped drag/inertia events; touch goes through Activity, Window and decor (Unity's `Activity.onTouchEvent` fallback). Coordinates pass through `calculateTransformedXY` (100.3 stays 100.299805); bionic's `atan2f` is ported.
- **PdfDocument rasterizes** (2026-10-02). Pages rasterize at 300 dpi (2 letter pages 173 ms). Rejected: a vector writer (CanvasKit 0.38 has no SkPDF; display lists hold Path2D/gradient objects a writer cannot read back).
- **One AssetManager2 model** (2026-10-20). Resources has per-package-id tables, 1-based cookies and selection under `deviceQualifiers()`; `configuration.ts` owns the device configuration. A Worker boot reads only the manifest and table of other packages, in parallel: FitNotes 1229 reads (1.36 MB, 215 ms) → 5 (0.88 MB, 2.6 ms), Resy 6296 reads (8.4 s) → 6 (12 ms). Input constants live in `input-codes.ts`; InputDispatcher keeps per-gesture state in `wm.ts`, and MotionEvent pointers and samples are records. Unchanged: `primaryAbi` reads only `record.apk.zip`.

### WebView

- **Renderer origins** (2026-10-02). Same-origin frames could read the app page's storage and `top.wd`, so each load renders on an origin hashing package, web origin and install time, sandboxed and hidden by visibility. Renderer load 63–79 vs 33–36 ms same-origin; a new origin adds 131–548 ms of service-worker install. rAF 50–61/s is headless Chromium's OOPIF rate.
- **Synchronous JS bridge** (2026-10-02). Without site isolation (Playwright's default, Safari, Android Chrome) the renderer shares the owner's thread, so synchronous calls deadlock. Calls probe (1.5 s) whether the app answers while the page waits, else post void calls and throw for value-returning ones; no browser mechanism does better.
- **Interface discovery** (2026-10-08). `javaInterface` uses `Class.getMethods`/`Method.isAnnotationPresent` as Chromium does, in driven generators that keep the calling strand. Limits: static methods excluded; overloads bind the first method by name.
- **Subframes and navigation** (2026-10-02/03). Frames are held before navigation (DOM hooks plus a ~5.5 ms tokenizer) and routed through the app under a `frame-src` policy; data:, blob:, gmsg: and custom schemes reach `shouldOverrideUrlLoading` as on the AVD, POST forms do not (Amazon's cart). Rejected: a never-ending bootstrap response to suppress extra load events (an HTTP/1.1 connection per frame).
- **Proxying and resource URLs** (2026-10-06). Proxying keeps method, form body, UA and cookies across redirects; `userAgentData` still reports the host platform. Document clients resolve resource URLs against the document site, not the renderer origin (CSS 404); rerouting Worker fetches too broke Harmonic's launch and AOT loads, so the rule checks `Client.type`.
- **Provider over bytecode clients** (2026-10-06). Probing the client's JS prototype via `Runtime.link` fails on Wasm (killed Harmonic's Worker); it uses `jentry.classNameOf`. Headers built with `rt.make` skipped `HashMap`'s constructor; host code constructs Java objects with `newObj`.
- **Lifetime and errors** (2026-09-27, 2026-10-05). A frame registry and the draw functor's `size.place` closure held the provider, so dropped WebViews never finalized; they keep WeakRefs. Uncaught page exceptions (AdMob in Flappy Bird) reach `onConsoleMessage`.
- **Proxy wire and the server boundary** (2026-10-20). `proxywire.ts` owns the proxy path and answer encoding. Interception through the proxy costs +1.4 ms median on dev (3.8 → 5.2 ms over 200 requests). `/proxy`, `/tcp`, `/udp` and the bridge socket require Origin to be the page's own origin, or else Sec-Fetch-Site same-origin, and the Worker refuses loopback, private, CGNAT, link-local, metadata and reserved literals (IPv4-mapped included). Gaps left: non-browser clients can forge both headers; names that resolve to private addresses are checked only as literals; redirects are not checked hop by hop; `/dns` stays open. Fetch's bad ports (4190 among them) make wrangler dev hang without a message.

## Rendering and text

### Display lists and replay

- **hwui replay keeps display lists** (2026-10-02). `hwui.ts`'s RenderProxy replays the root RenderNode into the surface buffer at `nSyncAndDrawFrame`, replacing the migration plan's forced `drawSoftware` on purpose: unchanged views keep their display lists and re-run no `onDraw`. Nested nodes and video are read at replay.
- **Recorder costs** (2026-10-02/03, `4ae6a100`). The recorder keeps its own affine and save stack; path ops are record-only (micro 487 → 115 ms; Mihon scroll flat at ~700 recorded ops/s, Java draw code dominating). RenderNode transforms compose into the retained matrix instead of building 13 Float64Arrays (100k updates 292.8 → 2.3 ms). A display-list rewrite is unproven; per-command Wasm adds crossings, so it needs a batched boundary.
- **One render-thread draw per frame** (2026-10-03, `bdb6be6a`). RT animators drew via `onNextVsync` before `doFrame`, so a vsync could composite twice (Calculator 291 draws / 126 main frames); the RT draw now runs behind `doFrame` and is skipped after a draw: 2.09 → 1.19 draws per main frame.
- **No layer promotion for fading nodes** (2026-10-01). hwui's layer for a fading overlapping RenderNode doubled Compose draw time (58 ms compositing) and was dropped; alpha multiplies each draw, differing only where children overlap.
- **Native handle lifetimes** (2026-10-05). RenderNode and HardwareRenderer JNI each hold a strong ref and `VirtualRefBasePtr` its own, so handles keep per-object counts (freeing on any release freed held nodes). `InputChannel.nativeDispose` freed its wrapper while its Cleaner stayed registered, which later freed a RenderNode reusing the slot (FitNotes); as in Android 15 JNI, dispose only clears the channel.
- **A native's own store claims** (2026-10-15). A handle is tied to its Java owner when compiled `iput-wide` stores it into a long field of a class with `finalize()`. `SurfaceTexture.nativeInit` and `Camera.nativeConstructor` store their handle from the host, so nothing claimed it: the native state leaked, and stayed claimable by any long that named it. `Bridge.write`, which a facade's field assignment and JNI `Set<Type>Field` both reach, now claims as compiled code does. A test collects a Camera and finds its state released; on device the SurfaceTexture is claimed, and YouTube keeps it alive through the walks checked.
- **A recording reaches its whole target** (2026-10-15). A display list records over a one-pixel mirror, so geometry taken from the mirror's canvas confined a BitmapShader fill, `drawPaint` and an inverse path or clip to a one-pixel strip at replay: YouTube's thumbnails and avatars stayed gray. A recording's target bounds are now the plane (±1e5), and a bitmap shader's pattern, which covers its target, is made at replay as a RuntimeShader's is.

### Canvas2D semantics

- **Layers, filters and clips**. `saveLayer`, compositing layers and `setLayerType` share one offscreen path (a plain save broke Lottie's DST_IN/OUT masks); offscreen bitmaps present atomically. Layers honour a PorterDuff filter's mode; radial gradients keep their focal circle. Rejected: a direct ClipBox replacement and an axis-aligned clip shortcut (regressed retained cases).
- **Canvas2D numerics** (2026-09-30..10-03). Canvas2D rounds arguments to float, ignores non-finite ones and transforms under a singular matrix; its rotation sin/cos differ from V8's in the last bit. Color natives are Skia's SkRGBToHSV/SkHSVToColor in float; Matrix follows SkMatrix (quarter-turn snapping, near-singular invert). Path natives are SkPath ports; drawing an inverse path subtracts the plain fill.
- **Shadows** (2026-09-27). Popup shadows filled at `globalAlpha` 0.01, and canvas shadows take opacity from the fill (1%). Elevation shadows are kept as a property, not drawn: flat Material 3 goldens cannot calibrate them.
- **One paint plan** (2026-10-20). `NativeCanvas.planDraw` classifies blend, mask and colour filter once per draw. A solid colour is filtered by its filter's own blend mode, where every non-matrix filter had been treated as SRC_IN. Shaders and nine-patches take the colour filter, so tinted nine-patches draw tinted, and vector drawables draw through a paint. Aliased bitmap fills use the SkScan port that `Region.setPath` uses. Deferred draws replay through their replay canvas, keeping clip and target, and line, point and text-on-path draws no longer mutate the caller's paint. PDF pages are still rasterized one at a time: in parallel, every page's RGB buffer would be held at once.

### Shaders, bitmaps and surfaces

- **RuntimeShader surface** (2026-10-03). The first RuntimeShader draw resized the shared WebGL surface from 1x1 (150–300 ms per launch; V8 profiles blamed `atLeast`, only in-page timing found it); allocating it at display size (`76343eea`) took Calculator ripple taps' drawMs 981 → 197 per 15 s, worst present interval 207–333 → 73 ms. The ripple program compiled ~270 ms on first use per realm then (raster 1.8 ms); measured again with `--gpu` (2026-10-14, plan 1.5) Calculator's first tap compiles it in 8.1–8.7 ms cold and warm, and Compose ripples never reach it, so prewarming saves under 9 ms once per process. Settles can catch mid-transition pixels.
- **Bitmaps and surfaces** (2026-10-01/04). HARDWARE bitmaps are raster-backed (null broke Coil/Glide). RenderEffect blur sigma is 0.57735 r + 0.5. Reused handle ids left a stale shader. `lockCanvas` returned the surface's own handle, which unlock released, freeing the window surface (Unity). SurfaceView sits at relative Z -2 under ViewRootImpl's bounds layer.

### Presentation and pacing

- **Presenting from the Worker** (2026-10-11). Shown on the page, SwiftShader's GPU cost slowed the page rAF (the Worker's vsync) to a tick per 75–97 ms. Each process canvas moves to its Worker (`transferControlToOffscreen`, `bitmaprenderer`), whose vsync is its own animation frames; the page gets copies only while `wd.mirrorFrames` is on. SwiftShader warm Calculator taps 36–178 → 27–113 ms.
- **GLSurfaceView pacing** (2026-09-27..10-11). GLThread waits until `present()` latched the last frame (swap vsync plus a tick gate gave 30 fps at 60 Hz; the `vsync()` watchdog alone let headless GL queue ~60 frames/s against ~8 shown). Under SwiftShader Chrome starves a Worker's WebGL while a page context saturates the GPU process (GL calls 5–8 s beside flappybird, Metal 3–5 ms), blocking microG in `eglMakeCurrent`, so the swap watchdog releases only once host frames stop (250 ms). The host-frame timer fallback stays 40 ms: raising it made Mihon's next tap 340–434 ms (237–264 without).
- **Composite lag is inside the drawing message** (2026-10-03). On Mihon taps 5 of 16 composites started >2 ms after `queueBuffer` (p90 ~11 ms). Rejected: ending the looper turn while a composite was pending did not shorten it.
