- **Scroll is bound by SwiftShader raster** (2026-10-11). The GPU process spent 5.5 of 9 s in `SwapBuffers`, the app Worker 0.4 s busy; with Metal, p50 is 16.6 ms.
- **Swaps that skip the vsync wait** (2026-10-27, rejected). `eglSwapBuffers` presents and then waits for the next vsync, where a BufferQueue blocks only while the previous frame is still queued. RimWorld's UnityMain spent 46–52 s of a ~350 s menu load in that wait (about 8.5 ms per swap at 15 frames/s). Presenting at once when a vsync had passed since the last frame, and otherwise at the next vsync, let 93–96% of swaps return immediately. The menu still came at 315–358 s against 347–357 s, because UnityMain's wait moved to a futex (43 s): Unity paces its frames to the Choreographer itself. An app that paces only by its swaps would gain; none measured here does.

### Text and fonts

- **Device advance rules** (2026-09-30..10-02). Size truncates to whole pixels and advances are hinted unless LINEAR_TEXT; SUBPIXEL_TEXT does not change measurement (subpixel advances broke 13 of 72 layouts). FreeType rounds HVAR deltas to whole units (`variedAdvances`); hinted advances round the 26.6 value (wght 900 `l` at 20 px = 6). The auto-hinter hypothesis was wrong.
- **minikin line breaking** (2026-09-30..10-02). Letter spacing skips joining scripts and loses half at line edges. Breaks follow WordBreaker (none after a hyphen without hyphenation or inside emoji ZWJ). The optimal breaker computes in float (doubles changed 8 of 72). `hyphenator.ts` ports HyphenatorCXX over the 53 `.hyb` files: 184/184 device lines once shapers applied hyphen edits and pieces sat at their own advance, not a running float sum.
- **Large fallback fonts are read ahead while idle** (2026-10-17). A platform font file loads whole on first shaping (`FontFile.load`), and drawing registers the face as a CSS `FontFace`, so the first text a process shapes in a fallback font waits for both. Komikku's empty library shows one of eleven kaomoji at random, eight of them with CJK characters (`･`, `﹏`, `益`, `；`, `￣`), so most `start` taps read all 32 MB of `NotoSansCJK-Regular.ttc` in their frame and the rest only the 211 KB Kannada font (`ಠ`, `ಥ`): over 31 runs, `start` took 82–179 ms when its frame waited 34–81 ms and 51–69 ms otherwise. On the page thread the CJK file's read took 23–51 ms (a repeat read 23–38 ms: the bytes are per realm, so the HTTP cache saves little), `FontFace`'s constructor 10–16 ms (it validates the face synchronously), the face's extraction from the collection 3 ms and HarfBuzz's copy 2 ms. A device maps the file and pages in what it reads; reading by table cannot do the same here, because the CSS face that draws needs the whole face. After a shown process's first frame, each fallback file of the default typeface over 1 MB (the CJK collection, the color emoji and Tibetan, 36 MB) is now read the next time the main looper waits with nothing due (`whenMainIdle`, ahead of compiling ahead), and the CSS face of the first family drawing from it registered at the next such wait. Matched against no read-ahead (five alternating `lat komikku --gpu` runs a side, 2026-10-17), `start` took 55–68 ms cold (median 66) against 51–105 (87) and 55–71 ms warm (64) against 62–105 (90); the other taps and the first frame did not change (cold 6.64 s against 6.62, warm 2.30 against 2.33). A shown process holds the files' bytes and the browser its validated faces whether or not the app draws in them.
- **Fonts and typefaces** (2026-09-30..10-05). Each realm parsed the font map twice (37–177 ms each); `/font-index.json` carries `serializeFontMap` bytes as SharedMemory (7 ms). The AVD's emoji is a GMS update (2026-09-30) seen by app processes, not `app_process` probes (50 code points differ); `gen-framework --fonts` records it. Chrome leaves `ital` at the default on canvas fonts, so `cssfonts.ts` pins it in a HarfBuzz instance. Android 15 keeps exact weights, clamped 1..1000. Paint's constructor reads LocaleList, so locales precede preloading.
- **One LayoutPiece** (2026-10-20). Measurement and layout share minikin's LayoutPiece, and Paint advances match all 549 device values that had been an ulp off. A piece measured with no right-to-left character shares the LTR layout's key: cold text shaped each word twice (1202 → 601 pieces; cold 6.27 → 4.65 ms against 5.90 on base). shapeTextRun with getTextBounds went 23.2 → 12.3 ms. The six array-writing text natives `retry` (see [Natives that wait](#natives-that-wait-jspi)).
- **Glyphs at minikin's positions** (2026-10-24, `760d274a`). Measurement matched the device's hinted advances, but `fillText` laid a whole run out at Chrome's unhinted ones, so a long row ended about 2 px short and every glyph after the first few sat off the device's; on FitNotes' lists that, more than the scrollbar, made the list steps' 17–24%. A run with no right-to-left script or bidi control, no joining script, no warp and no hyphen edit is drawn a cluster at a time at minikin's advances (a cluster's advance is its first char's; Chrome still shapes each cluster): FitNotes 6.12 → 2.92%, mihon-app 35.7 → 22.8%, mihon-onboarding 15.3 → 10.9%. Frames cost 0.02–0.03 ms more (draw natives about 0.05 ms a run). `textcmp`: 108/108 layouts within 0.00 px, 184/184 hyphenated lines.

## Native code, media and transport

### CPU cores and JIT

- **Interpreter/JIT agreement**. A region discovery budget let a branch enter with stale carry (Lichess P-384 TLS, JIT only); JIT exits omitted a pending compare's flags, so a resumed handler saw stale NZCV.
- **JIT regions**. Forward branches use direct `br`; a region spills at every entry/exit (Among Us ~13.8M entries/s); until 2026-10-16 none spanned a guest page. Compiling counted `fallback`s cut exits 774k/s → 71k/s; 37% of memory accesses take plane translation. Rejected: dead flags at A64 call/return exits (no gain, breaks interpreter equality); scalar FP in cached v128 locals (within ±5%).
- **ARM32 JIT coverage** (2026-09-27). VFP, vldm/vstm, exclusives, barriers and NEON exited to the interpreter, spilling every register; `jitneon.zig` and `jit32thumb.zig` compile them (Thumb loop 20.7 → 1.0 ns/instr; Cut the Rope's top fallback sites 35.4M → 1.6M exits per 50 s). Diffing 48,280 IT blocks against the interpreter found stray Wasm stack pushes (invalid only where a block lacks a terminal branch).
- **Genshin JIT profile** (M4 Pro). ~290M guest instr/s; 14.8M region entries/s of ~20 instructions, transitions ~30% (21 ns each), 103 Wasm bytes per guest instruction. Rejected: regions taking blocks from three other pages (half the transitions, but 166M instr/s from duplicated code). In-core `clock_gettime` cut 45K exits/s, no fps change. 60 fps needs ~2.3×.
- **Writes into code and exclusives**. JNI array copies into code left stale decoded instructions; they use checked helpers (`e3b51392`). An asynchronous file read (`readFd` from a lazy source) stored without that check, unlike a synchronous one; both invalidate now. A64 STXR/STLXR/STXP wrote with no reservation held; they now fail without writing (`924414a3`).
- **TikTok's NEON fallbacks** (2026-10-16, `9e0fa68f`). A per-opcode histogram of interpreter fallbacks on the feed was led by across-lane reductions (ADDV.8H about a million a second), SRI/SLI, fixed-point FCVTZ, SHLL and CNT. Compiling them cut fallbacks 15.3M → 0.24M per interval, leaving mostly SVCs and CRC32X; SRI by the element size had trapped in the interpreter (`0a04c760`). Native code runs about 10% of a core on TikTok's feed, so the absolute gain there is small.
- **A64 regions across pages** (2026-10-16, `a7d82ba3`). A third of the feed's region exits were direct branches to another page. A64 blocks may now lie on any decoded page; an edge into another page checks that page's decoded pointer and epoch and exits otherwise, and installing checks every block's page. Instructions per exit rose 13.1 → 20.4 and exits to a known target fell 34% → 0.2% (on different workloads); a loop between two pages runs in half the time. Genshin's earlier rejection (duplicated blocks) is not re-measured.
- **ARM32 regions name their registers** (2026-10-16, `252effea`). Emitting each block once into a buffer that keeps nothing records the register locals it reads and writes, so a region loads what it uses and each exit spills what its path wrote; a loop exiting twice per iteration runs in half the time.
- **ARM32 regions cross pages at observed entries** (2026-10-21, `8c3ed67b`). ARM and Thumb regions now cross pages, but only at entries with interpreter heat or an installed slot, keeping the 40-block/640-instruction bounds, install-time epochs and edge-time page checks. A 32-bit Thumb instruction straddling pages stays interpreted: one recorded epoch cannot validate its second half. A million cross-page loop iterations ran ARM 6.603 → 0.994 ms and Thumb 5.054 → 1.013 ms with same-page controls unchanged; Doodle Jump's native time fell 5.545 → 5.350 s while its launch and frame rates stayed within run ranges. Admitting cold targets on decoded pages also sped the loops but lowered Doodle's presentation rates 5–8% in three pairs, so it was replaced. [Measurements](performance-design.md#measurements-and-costs).
- **A64 guarded in-region returns** (2026-10-21, `890faa58`, `f18e23b1`). A direct call within a region brings up to four continuations into it; a RET compares its destination with each and branches inside the region on a match, else exits as before. Return edges join writeback propagation. A continuation validates its page on every return, even beside its callee: without that, a callee rewriting its caller's continuation on its own page ran the stale code (7 instead of 9). A million leaf calls ran 2.957 → 1.463 ms across pages and 2.907 → 1.257 ms within one. [Measurements](performance-design.md#measurements-and-costs).
- **The JIT takes blocks entered 255 times** (2026-10-18). Komikku's warm launch (`lat --warm` clears its data, so `SQLiteOpenHelper.onCreate` builds its schema through requery's bundled native SQLite) came at 2.2–2.3 s or 3.0–3.3 s. At a heat of 48 that startup code crossed the threshold in bulk: 1,442–1,917 regions by the first frame of a slow launch, about 2,100 (34 MB of Wasm, 187 batches) by idle. Every native thread shares the app Worker's one JS thread, and a batch held it 8–21 ms: `jit_emit` 2–6 ms, `new WebAssembly.Module` 2–7 ms and V8's lazy compiles of its functions inside the next slices. The main thread's broker replies queued behind those turns, so its calls took 20–150 ms; with `?jit=0` no native slice ran over 8 ms. At 255, the most a block's u8 heat holds, a launch compiled 8–54 regions by its first frame and 567 (8.3 MB) by idle. Eight alternating `lat komikku --gpu --warm` runs a side: first frame median 3,092 → 2,248 ms (5 of 8 slow against 1 of 8 at 2,836 ms), first content 3,181 → 2,360 ms and idle 4,927 → 4,371 ms; taps run no native code and stayed within noise. TikTok's launch ran the same native instructions by its first frame (107–108M) in 948–1,082 ms against 1,128–1,147, compiling 2,347–2,404 regions against 4,961–4,986, and its first frame and idle stayed within noise (7.47–7.73 s against 7.50–7.65 s, two runs a side); Calculator M's and FitNotes' launches and taps were unchanged, and so were Flappy Bird's and Doodle Jump's frame rates (three alternating quick e2e runs a side). In profiled slower launches the main thread also waited 110–150 ms behind `World.addDexes` opening microG's GmsCore dex in the app process (Firebase's `DynamiteModule.load`, 80–130 ms of `Program.add` and class indexing), and the first ICU BreakIterator load (`Memory.peek` over RPC) waited 136–155 ms behind busy turns. Not tried: compiling lukewarm blocks while idle, and native slices bounded by time.
- **SIMD encodings that ran as their neighbours** (2026-10-22). An expanded hardware oracle (1,576 more A64 SIMD&FP words across every arrangement, recorded on an M4 Pro as `scripts/simd-oracle.ts` does) matched 1,369 in both tiers; the rest are unimplemented: FP16 arithmetic, the SQDMULL family, SUQADD/USQADD, FCVTXN, FRINT32/64, FJCVTZS, URECPE/URSQRTE, i8mm, BF16 and FCMLA/FCADD, with crypto and SHA3 raising SIGILL as optional extensions. Three of those had run as a neighbour instead of faulting: FRINT64X (vector) as FSQRT in both tiers, SQRDMLAH by element as SQRDMULH, and FCMLA by element (rotations 0 and 180) as FMLA/FMLS in both tiers. None is in AT_HWCAP, but a SIGILL feature probe would have taken them as present.
- **Cryptography extensions** (2026-10-26, `0309842e`). Pokémon TCG Pocket's 3.2 GB asset download ran at about 175 KB/s with its fetch thread CPU-bound in OpenSSL's table-driven `AES_encrypt` and GHASH. AES, PMULL and SHA were SIGILL, and AT_HWCAP advertised CRC32 alone. The interpreter now runs AESE/AESD/AESMC/AESIMC, PMULL{2} .1Q, SHA1C/P/M/H/SU0/SU1 and SHA256H/H2/SU0/SU1, which the JIT reaches through its per-instruction fallback; the hardware oracle covers them. AT_HWCAP advertises FP, ASIMD, AES, PMULL, SHA1, SHA2 and CRC32. Advertising the crypto bits alone changed nothing, because OpenSSL's armcap reads them only when ASIMD is set. The fetch thread now runs `aes_v8_ctr32_encrypt_blocks` at under 1 M instr/s.
- **libchrome.so's NEON and VFP** (2026-10-25). Chrome's ARM32 libraries faulted on encodings the A32 decoder lacked. It now decodes VLDn/VSTn single and all lanes, VCVT between fixed and floating point, the narrowing and widening moves, VCNT, VMVN, VCLS/VCLZ, VQABS/VQNEG, VPADDL/VPADAL (also compiled), VQDMULH by scalar, VFMA/VFMS, VMAXNM/VMINNM, VCVTB/VCVTT, VRINT and ARMv8's unconditional VFP encodings, and every integer three-same op through its A64 opcode (`4fdc2dec`). A64 gained the three-different SQDMLAL, SQDMLSL and SQDMULL. Faults on Thumb VFP encodings report a pc 4 bytes before the instruction; not yet fixed.
- **Hot libc calls served in the core** (2026-10-25). Chrome's renderer made about 960K host calls per 10 s, each an exit from `run_slice`: libm rounding (`floor`, `round` and their float forms), `bzero` and the EABI memsets, `strcpy`, `strchr`/`strrchr` and `__tls_get_addr`. `libc.zig` now serves them. `__tls_get_addr` reads a per-thread table of dynamic TLS block addresses after the pthread key slots, which `dl.ts` fills as it makes each block (64 modules) and clears on unload; window addresses and modules without a block still go to the host. Host calls fell to about 40K per 10 s.
- **Registers stored through, slow accesses handed back** (2026-10-27, `11ca3b56`). TurboFan spilled every live register at each call a region made (`tx`, `on_store`, `exec`), usually at the value's definition on the hot path: about 40% of Pokémon TCG Pocket's machine instructions were stack traffic, and its ~7,000 hot regions averaged 27 KB of machine code at 177 ns per uncached entry. A block now loads the registers it uses and stores every write through to the Cpu, so nothing is live across blocks, exits or calls. Memory paths call nothing: plane addresses translate inline, and a plane miss, a store to a code, guard or read-only page, or a low or misaligned address returns status 4 with the instruction's pc, and `run_slice` interprets that block whatever its slot. That needs an instruction to write nothing before its last check, so stores write their base back after the store, and a hand-back from a compiled Thumb IT body writes its ITSTATE (Chrome's renderer crashed without it). Offline, many-region code ran 2.3× faster and hot kernels 55% (A64) and 40% (Thumb). Pocket's title screen went 101M → 156M guest instr/s and 27.5 → 41.8 presentations/s; Chrome on vgpu.sh 5.2 → 8.4. Rejected: caching registers per block in locals (307 against 184 ns/entry); a shared epilogue shrank A64 regions 31% and Thumb 18% but left Pocket at 101M, so code size was not its limit.
- **ARM32 IT branches, calls and window pages in regions** (2026-10-27, `21f32629`, `6673eb85`, `de1b8dee`). Chrome's renderer on vgpu.sh ran 2.5M IT blocks/s through `exec_it_block32` because their last body branched; the branch now runs under its condition with a direct target as the taken edge, and PLD/PLI are hints. A BL branches to its callee's blocks in the region and records the continuation that BX, POP/LDM/LDR of pc compare against before leaving, as A64's RET does (26M region chains/s → 19M while running 9% more instructions). Window accesses had handed every block back: the page-to-pool table now lives in the core's memory and ARM32 modules import the pool as a second memory, so the access checks flags, translates and bounds-checks inline (a helper call cost the fast path about 15% in spills). DMB/DSB emit a fence when the process has a window. The renderer went 371M → about 550M instr/s and vgpu.sh 5.2 → 10.6–11.3 presentations/s against the starting commit.
- **Table slots freed by reference** (2026-10-27, `0b9cbeb9`). Slots returned to the host only when the table filled and the oldest half of the batches went. Chrome's renderer rewrites code steadily (V8's own JIT, 700–850 new regions/s) and filled the 254k slots within minutes; each fill then evicted about 131k slots in one ~190 ms pause, measured inside a tap, and discarded live code that the next taps recompiled. The core counts each slot's page entries (`cpu.refs`); installing over an entry and dropping a page release them, and `jit_install` returns a free slot taken round the table. Live slots stay at 65–69k while installs pass 108k.
- **Region code density** (2026-10-27, `ffe4fb65`, `680bc82f`, `2743f2e8`, `d4e532d5`, `cf4d95bd`). On vgpu.sh at about 710M instr/s Chrome's renderer chained regions 23M times a second (one entry per ~31 guest instructions), took 26.5M in-region back-edges and interpreted 0.3% of its instructions; guest V8 runs its optimizing tiers (a 2M-iteration loop in 21 ms). A bench of n generated Thumb functions called through a table ran 0.92 → 2.33 ns per guest instruction from 16 to 256 functions, so the renderer is bound by host code footprint. Changes: a batch no longer compiles a block again as a region of its own when it heated while its function's entry waited (3× → 1.19× the blocks); a branch-hint section marks exits, hand-backs and sticky-flag updates unlikely (256 functions −23% under node's `--experimental-wasm-branch-hinting`; Chrome applies hints by default); a region keeps its registers in locals from prologue to epilogue and stores the written ones there, and every call out of it (fallback, IT block, FP helper, estimate) stores them before and reloads them after, so TurboFan still has nothing live across a call to spill (1.70 → 1.45 ns/instr at 256 functions, 1.07 → 0.85 at 32); run_slice runs the thread's Cpu at a fixed address (`CPU_CUR`) that a region folds into immediate offsets (a bench region 1,536 → 1,248 bytes of machine code, a call-heavy bench −6%); and the flag spill sets ARM32's T bit instead of every chain. Alternating builds on vgpu.sh beside another session's emulator (load 7–11): the renderer ran 436–453 → 610–612M instr/s, and the menu's open and close after each run's first tap 486–833 → 378–415 ms (first taps 975 and 1,470 → 763 and 599 ms). Splitting the bench into short and 16×-longer callees at the same call count puts a chain at about 13–15 ns (60% of the short bench; an estimated 30% of the renderer at 23M chains/s): stores and loads of the registers and flags, the slot lookup, `return_call_indirect` and the entry `br_table`. An A64 version of the bench, whose callees call `fmaxf` through the FP helper, ran 1.01–1.07 → 0.80–0.86 ns/instr at 16 functions and 1.78–1.80 → 1.64–1.66 at 256 against the starting core, alternating.
- **Calls as host calls, exits chained from their sites** (2026-10-27, `2d0185a7`, `64abd829`). Counters on vgpu.sh put Chrome's renderer at about 21.8M region exits a second, mostly returns and direct exits from regions at their 40-block cap or already holding four calls' continuations: a region filled by following its calls into their callees. A region now stops at a call and keeps the continuation as a block; the call stores the region's state and runs the callee's region through `call_indirect` while `Cpu.ret_to` holds the continuation, and any region whose exit reaches it returns. The caller goes on in place when the returned pc and the continuation's page match, or returns that pc to its own caller (a status, a longjmp, code the callee rewrote). Calls nest at most 32 deep; the in-region return list is gone. A continuation joins a region only once a return has reached it (heat or a slot): compiling those after noreturn calls brought data into regions and exposed the emitter faults below. A direct exit then chains from its own site, reading its target's page and slot at constant addresses, rather than from the shared epilogue's one indirect call. Exits halved (21.2M → 10.6M a second). Three alternating rounds on vgpu.sh beside another session's emulator (load 4–15): renderer 595–679M (parent) → 740–779M (calls) → 709–803M instr/s (both), and the menu's taps after each run's first 420 → 373 → 333 ms. The generated Thumb and A64 benches stayed within noise (identical checksums): their callees fit in one region either way.
- **Emitter faults on code no run reaches** (2026-10-27, `f951aa44`, `9f7c3b05`). With call continuations compiled, Chrome's renderer died in about 1 of 6 launches: a region module failed with CompileError "extra bits in varint", which `jitDrain` rethrew, Chrome's renderer took as a Java InternalError and its exception reporter turned into LOG(FATAL). An SBFX/UBFX whose field runs past bit 31 (UNDEFINED, which the interpreter raises) underflowed `32 - lsb - width`, and ReleaseFast wrote the shift as an over-long LEB; such words now fall back. Compiling regions over random words behind a branch no run takes found a second fault, on the parent too: a conditional pc-writing instruction exited through `exitRet`, which settled and dropped every pending flag, so the not-taken path went on without the compare's other flags (ReleaseSafe asserts). Every exit now writes pending flags without dropping them. `cache.test.ts` keeps a 32-page sweep per instruction set.
- **Region code footprint** (2026-10-27). After per-site chaining Chrome's renderer on vgpu.sh compiled about 97 Wasm bytes per guest instruction (window access paths 32%, calls 17%, exits 8%, register stores 7.7%), each instruction in 1.46 regions on average; a profile put 87.5% of its time in region code over 5,154 modules, 8.3% in the core and 1% in compiling. 42% of compiled blocks (35.5% of the bytes) had no heat or slot when compiled. It made about 13M calls, 13M returns, 24M back-edges and 4M exits at the block cap a second, and a chain costs about 55 machine instructions. Variants, each alternated with the unchanged core in rounds of renderer instr/s and three menu open/close taps: handing unrun ARM32 blocks back lost a third of throughput (500M vs 770M, taps 430–780 ms); compiling window paths only at sites that had missed, others handing back and dropping their page, ran 750M vs 780M; holding written registers in locals until exits and calls (a forward dataflow over both edges) ran 774M vs 790M in its one valid round, and its first version, which cleared the written set inside a conditional call and so lost the writes on the path that skipped it, crashed the renderer with the native tests passing; 64-block, 1,024-instruction regions ran 746M vs 709M with each round inside the other's range and taps 368 vs 392 ms; and a block that branches to itself looping in place rather than through the entry table ran 809M vs 760M (788/807/684 vs 819/779/830), taps 331 vs 327 ms and the generated-function bench unchanged. None was kept: TurboFan already moves the unlikely paths out of line.
- **Region transitions and instruction delivery** (2026-10-27). Counters on vgpu.sh put Chrome's renderer at 12.3M calls a second: 66% reached no further call, half went to such leaves of under 32 instructions, 14% were indirect, and 0.2% called a region in the caller's own module, so direct calls within a batch cannot stand in for `call_indirect`. Inlining small callees into the calling region (the call branches to the callee's blocks with LR set, and every indirect branch compares its target with the region's continuations before leaving) took only 0.77M of 6.4M calls a second while callees had to call nothing, since 5.4M went to callees with a call on some path; letting the callee's own calls stay host calls ran a bench of six calls per 51 instructions 0.92 → 0.33 ns/instr, checksums identical. In Chrome, alternating with the unchanged core (load 8–18), it was flat: 650/634M instr/s and taps 429/370 ms unchanged, 652/632M and 404/419 ms inlining up to 32 instructions and 6 blocks, 580/601M and 394/449 ms up to 64 and 10. Not kept. Instruments' CPU Counters (CPU Bottlenecks) on the renderer's guest thread put 46.9% of pipeline slots in instruction delivery, 23.4% in processing, 8.9% discarded and 20.4% useful, and 48.7/21.2/8.6/21.1% with inlining; the generated-function bench goes from 21.3% delivery (38% discarded, its calls through a random table) at 16 functions to 46.5% at 256. The renderer is bound by fetching its regions' hot code, so removing host instructions at the price of code size does not show there, and the 256-function bench, not a warm one, predicts it.
- **Store checks** (2026-10-27). A store in Chrome's hot regions cost 13 machine instructions, 10 of them its check: the first and last bytes' page flags each took a shift, a 64-bit add of the flag table's base (a memarg offset, which a register-indexed load cannot take) and a byte load. A store of 2, 4 or 8 bytes now tests its first page's flags ORed with its misalignment, without which it stays on that page, and the exact two-page test runs out of line where that is nonzero. The table's base is an i32 add, which TurboFan folds into the shift (`add w, wK, wA, lsr #12`) only while no other use shares the shift: the out-of-line test reuses the first page's flags from a local, and the window path indexes its table with a shift of its own. The store goes to the address the check computed rather than repeating the add for a memarg offset, 8 instructions in all with an offset. A block store (STM, PUSH, STRD, VSTM, VSTR.64, VST1, STREXD, STP, ST1/ST2) takes one range check before its first store rather than one per register, so it also reaches the interpreter before writing anything. The 256-function bench went 1.463 → 1.396 ns/instr over three alternating rounds (−4.6%, faster in each, checksums identical), 16 functions −0.8% and the A64 bench −0.5%; its delivery stalls went from 47.3% to 46.1% of slots (two runs each). In Chrome, alternating with the unchanged core (load 6–10), the renderer ran 613M → 632M instr/s (591/617/631 vs 628/606/661) and taps 416 vs 431 ms, both within noise.
- **Register traffic at region exits and calls** (2026-10-28, `315694b5`). In one of Chrome's hot regions on vgpu.sh each exit stored about 13 registers and packed the flags into CPSR in about 11 instructions, reloading and masking CPSR each time; register stores, flag packing and the chain's slot lookup were about 6%, 5% and 13% of its machine code. A call also stored `Cpu.ret_to` and `Cpu.depth` before running its callee's region and restored both after, and every exit and chain read `ret_to` back. Regions now take the continuation and the depth as parameters, which a call sets and a chain passes on. On a quiet host the 256-function bench ran 1.132 → 1.127 ns/instr and 16 functions 0.679 → 0.671 (six alternating rounds, checksums identical), with delivery stalls at 43.6/44.1% → 43.5/43.3% of slots. In Chrome, three alternating rounds on vgpu.sh from a fresh profile with WebGPU enabled (load 2.3–4): the renderer ran 893/896/913M → 910/897/889M instr/s at 99% busy, first taps 390/431/423 → 387/437/488 ms and later taps a median 313 → 306 ms, all within noise. Without the flag the page draws no hero and the renderer is 46–49% busy, with taps a median 182–192 ms after the first. Rejected on top of it: exits storing only the registers their path wrote since entry or the last call (a forward dataflow over the region's edges to a fixpoint), skipping the flag spill where no flag was pending, and setting ARM32's T bit only where a status or a fallback reads it, so the spill's mask is one immediate. The 256-function bench ran 1.143 against 1.139 and 16 functions 0.686 against 0.677. In Chrome beside other sessions (load 15–43) the renderer ran 649/667M against 618/586/665M instr/s with taps unchanged; one run's renderer stayed at 33M instr/s, which a relaunch did not reproduce. The benches store about 4 registers per exit and Chrome about 13, and neither moved, so fetching the hot code still bounds both rather than the stores.
- **Region emitter structure** (2026-10-28). The access guards and the hand-back live in `jitaccess.zig`; ARM32 loads and stores take one store guard whose slow path is the window or the interpreter. A ReleaseSafe assertion holds the hand-back's precondition: no register local written since the instruction began (`Emit.begin`, `Buf.nwrites`), which caught a base writeback moved ahead of its load. Region statuses are `cpu.RegionExit`, the locals and module types are derived, and `run_slice` refuses to nest and copies CPU_CUR back after a trap (`slice_abort`). A table slot whose last page entry goes is queued for the host to unlink before the next batch links, so a module whose regions were all invalidated can be collected rather than held until the slot is reused. The region-transition benches (`scripts/native-bench.ts`, three alternating rounds, load 7–8) stayed within noise.
- **Rejected JIT experiments** (2026-10-27). `--no-liftoff` for the renderer: no change. Draining the compile queue in one batch: bench repetitions 145–160 → 265–379 ms, compiling 3,060 regions instead of 1,766. Draining early and invalidating at word rather than page granularity: Pocket's title (25 s) and next screen (13.7–18.3 s) within noise of the build without them. Testing for entry 0 before a region's entry `br_table`, since most chains enter at the first block: within noise on the chain bench. Marking unlikely the side of a branch that leaves the region (inverting the condition where the fall-through leaves), so that TurboFan lays its chain sequence of about 45 instructions out of line: 1.364 → 1.377 ns/instr on the 256-function bench, 0.728 → 0.754 on 16, and in Chrome 686M → 662M instr/s with taps 391 vs 398 ms.
- **Lanes: native threads on other cores** (2026-10-27). A process's guest pthreads ran on its Worker's one JS thread, where RimWorld's loader got 150–330 M instr/s beside UnityMain. Lanes (`lanes.ts`, `lane-worker.ts`, `lane.zig`) instantiate the process's core module again in nested Workers over its shared memory, each with its own stack, TLS and JIT table (every batch the process links is linked into each). A thread whose slice ran at least 200K instructions per exit runs its next one on a lane, up to `hardwareConcurrency − 2` of them; every exit comes back to the process Worker, which handles it as before, so the host stays single-threaded. What lanes share changes under one recursive spinlock (allocators, planes, decoded pages, the JIT queue); a dropped decoded page is reused only once every lane has left the slice it was in. LDXR/STXR map to a compare-and-swap against the value the load read, 128-bit pairs run under the lock, and barriers are `atomic.fence`. Only lane 0 moves a plane, after the other lanes' slices end: a lane that would move one faults, and the process Worker's core runs that access again, growing the plane. An earlier fallback continued a plane in an adjacent extent, which broke the contiguity the JIT's inline translation assumes and corrupted RimWorld's heap only with lanes. A round trip costs about 65–75 µs; lanes first spent a quarter of their wall time waiting for the process Worker to take their exits (24 s of 94 s), and 4 s once the process Worker's own slices run in 100K-instruction pieces and end when a lane's exit waits (a shared counter). On RimWorld the loader thread reached 30 G instructions in 105–111 s rather than 151–152 s, and the main menu came 347–357 s after the drive started rather than 398 s. UnityMain's own ~190 G instructions (about 270 s of compute, 50 s blocked in `eglSwapBuffers`) now bound it. A 50K threshold quadrupled the round trips (483K) for the same menu time. `?lanes=0` disables lanes; ARM32 processes run none.
- **A64 regions for IL2CPP code** (2026-10-28, `dff4489f`, `afd8903f`, `bed9650d`, `56b910f6`). RimWorld's menu load left a region about every 24 guest instructions. Half of those exits were at the 40-block budget's edge: discovery was breadth first, and IL2CPP's null, bounds and class-initialization checks each branch to code that never runs, so those blocks took the budget. A branch target the interpreter has not entered (no heat, no slot) now gets its block only after every target that ran (23.9 → 28.5 instructions per entry; nbench's string kernel 1238 → 1656 M/s). A census of interpreter fallbacks by instruction form (`wd.native.fallbacks()`, counting from its first call since shared counts contend across lanes) showed every Advanced SIMD scalar instruction (bit 28: DUP to scalar, FADDP/FMAXP, by-element FMUL/FMLA, scalar compares, shifts and conversions) falling back with a full spill; they are compiled, the lane-wise ones through the vector emitter keeping lane 0 (nbench fp 395 → 2362 M/s). A region also keeps each V register it touches in a v128 local from its entry, found by a dry emission as ARM32 registers are, and spills the written ones only at exits and fallbacks; the rejected scalar FP cache above invalidated at every vector instruction. Booted side by side under the same host load, the hot-first discovery alone brought the menu in 538 s rather than 665 s (lane CPU −14%, Worker CPU −23%). Remaining exits: RETs to a continuation outside the region about half, budget edges most of the rest, BR/BLR about 4%. Rejected: guarding BR/BLR targets inside the region (instructions per entry unchanged at the menu), dropping never-run targets (string kernel −40%, since the cold paths that did run then left the region), calls as nested Wasm calls into the callee's region (equal wall time, 7–23% more CPU) and budgets of 64 blocks, 1024 instructions and 8 returns (menu 457 vs 435 s, JIT compilation 14.1 vs 5.1 s). On a quiet host the four together, against the commit before them, are measured in RimWorld below.
- **Lanes on the per-site JIT** (2026-10-28). Main's JIT ran every region against one Cpu at `CPU_CUR`, which `run_slice` copies a thread's state into, and freed table slots by reference for reuse. Lanes run concurrently, so each lane's slice runs on its own copy at `CPU_CUR + lane × stride` (all within the first 64 KiB) and an A64 region takes it from its first parameter; ARM32, which no lane runs, keeps the constant. A slot the core frees while lanes run is held (`jit_hold`, refs `HELD`) until every lane's table has unlinked it (`jit_unhold`), since a lane may still hold the old region where the core would install a new one; a full table's flush holds its live slots the same way and the host unlinks the rest. A lane's empty slot returns `REGION_INTERPRET`: an A64 chain or call stores its target in `Cpu.pc` before `call_indirect`, which the interpreter resumes from. V registers stay in v128 locals across the region and move at its exits and around fallbacks. On RimWorld's first 203 s the process ran 246 G instructions (176 G on lanes), against 161 G with lanes over the old JIT and 137 G before the A64 region work, with no faults. `native-bench` medians (3 alternating rounds, node) matched main within noise: cross arm 0.92 against 0.92 ms, cross thumb 0.97 against 0.98, call a64 4.45 against 4.42, call-same a64 4.23 against 4.31.

### Guest memory and mappings

- **Shared guest memory in 64 MiB steps** (2026-10-02). V8 counts a Wasm memory's committed bytes toward the isolate heap limit, shared or not (two grown 4 GiB memories: 23.7 vs 0.27 s GC). Committing the ABI's size up front (4 GiB arm64, 2 GiB ARM32), since growth detached direct buffers' views, kept incremental marking running: Mihon taps spent 10.4-11.7 of ~23 s in GC, TikTok 52%. Guest memory is now shared (no cross-origin isolation needed in Chrome), grows in 64 MiB steps without detaching, and `mem_grown` refreshes cached views: TikTok GC 2.8%, `--live` failures 20 → 1. TextDecoder, `getRandomValues`, Blob, ImageData, `WebSocket.send` and structured clone reject shared views. Rejected: lazy commit (non-movable arrays pin memory), resizable buffers (Web APIs reject views; reads 4-13× slower), per-realm isolates, a 3 GiB reservation (wedged). In isolation 64 MiB steps did not slow allocation; some single large grows did (206-219 vs 127-137 ms).
- **Address planes** (2026-10-02..03). The core's 32-bit pc needs plane 0, so executable and low-hinted mappings stay there. Planes move across awaits: `anchor(p)` keeps a guest address, and `scripts/generators.ts` rejects load-time PTR/KLAYOUT/MEM_SIZE reads (msghdr offsets computed while PTR was 4 wedged YouTube's CronetNet). Writable limits commit on touch, bypassed by raw typed-array views. Rejected: per-2-MiB plane units (host ops holding an offset across an await wrote the old place; Unity 0 vs 19.5 fps).
- **File mappings** (2026-10-02..05). `MAP_SHARED` file mappings are copies; msync, munmap, reads and new mappings carry changed bytes to the file and every mapping (Realm read stale arrays), and flushing stops at the file length (`SharedMaps.flush` grew a 3-byte file to 8). A bulk get over an external mapping waits for its pages (Normalizer's NFC data read zeros).
- **Cross-process `MAP_SHARED` needs one owner** (2026-10-05). Each process's memory (`core.ts`) holds all its pages (`mem.zig`) for Java and JIT accesses; sharing it would alias fixed-address MMU/core state, and RPC flush/diff is insufficient. A design must keep control state private and route all accesses to one owner. Native remote `MAP_SHARED`/ashmem stays unsupported, except ARM32 ashmem since 2026-10-25 (below).
- **/proc/self/maps lists segments, and apk-mapped libraries name the apk** (2026-10-26, `ddaaa98b`, `4793eca8`). Maps had one `r-xp` line per image at offset 0, naming `base.apk!/lib/<abi>/<name>`. Under `extractNativeLibs="false"` that names neither a file nor the split that holds the library. Pokémon TCG Pocket's libil2cpp opens the file of its own maps line and, failing, branches to a deliberate crash routine about 10–60 s in (`SEGV_ACCERR` from a stack-smashing `br` to `adr*9041`). Maps now lists each PT_LOAD's pages with PF_* permissions and file offset, then its `[anon:.bss]` tail. A stored library names the apk holding it (base or split) at its entry's data offset, as the kernel shows bionic's mapping; dladdr keeps the `apk!/` name. The package manager hands the loader that file mapping as a typed `{path, offset}` rather than a path to re-split. A dlopen of a path the app's library is not at (Unity tries `nativeLibraryDir/libunity.so` first) now fails as bionic's does, where loading by basename had named the module by a missing path. The game also probes for emulators (BlueStacks, Nox, MuMu, `su`) by path and finds none.
- **ARM32 ashmem through a window** (2026-10-25, `d7c32bdd`). Chrome's browser, GPU and renderer processes exchange ipcz messages through ashmem they all map shared, which copies per process cannot serve. The page is now cross-origin isolated (`68d85b90`), and pooled ashmem regions are runs of one shared memory every process Worker receives. An ARM32 process's addresses from 2 GiB up are a window onto it, where each shared mapping shows a run of its region's pool pages through a per-process page table, so two mappings of one region need not share an address. LDREX/STREX there are a compare-and-swap on the value LDREX read. Compiled code in the window falls back to the interpreter, and the core's libc copies there go through the host. A64 processes and Java mappings still copy. `ASHMEM_PIN`/`UNPIN` answer as for a region never purged. Regions are their own file kind answering ioctl(2), which a remote region forwards by value, so pin ranges go unchecked.
- **mmap hints and mprotect** (2026-10-25). `map_va` ignored an mmap's hint; PartitionAlloc's 32-bit pools step their hints past super pages they may not use. A mapping now goes at its hint when that space is free, as Linux places it. mprotect without PROT_WRITE now marks plane-0 pages read-only: a guest store faults and a host store answers EFAULT, as base::ProtectedMemory's probe (a system call writing into the page) expects, while a file's changes still reach a read-only mapping of it. The first gate run with it lost Mihon's process at boot: `bootBionic` stored the heap dispatch into `__libc_globals`, which libc's init leaves read-only; it now stores as WriteProtected::mutate does, making the page writable around the store.

### Signals, faults and threads

- **Guest faults are synchronous signals** (2026-10-02). Null, unbacked and write-protected accesses raise SIGSEGV, misalignment SIGBUS, BRK SIGTRAP, with si_addr and the kernel's frame (fpsimd_context, which Mono asserts). Bad pointers in syscalls and stub-like libc bodies answer EFAULT. SIGILL reaches a handler only for `.undefined` and optional encodings HWCAP does not advertise (OpenSSL armcap probes); `.unimplemented` stays a host fault, so crash SDKs see no fabricated signal.
- **Debuggerd and defaults** (2026-10-02). A host debuggerd handler logs the tombstone and ends the process (libunity looped on a fault 9,000 times in 3 s). Unhandled signals take signal(7)'s default. Cpus start with ART's mask (`SigBlk 0x80001204`), so SIGPIPE stays pending.
- **ucontext restored inside `Cpu.call`**. Restoring after the call let a frame whose host call completed run, then lose registers to the signal snapshot (Boehm's signals crashed Among Us); a nested-frame tripwire found it where `?jit=0` (~4M instr/s) was too slow. Futex and yield completion defer queued signals.
- **Handlers that never return** (2026-10-02). libmetasec_ov's SIGRTMAX handler restores pre-raise registers by hand; returning, it hit the sentinel with the interrupted JNI call's sp and resumed a stale frame into .bss, so anti-tamper killed TikTok. Such a return now also returns from the interrupted call, keeping the handler's mask as Linux does without sigreturn.
- **A parked system call's result and a running handler** (2026-10-26, `39f169a3`). A system call that parked wrote x0 when its Promise settled. If a signal handler was running on top of the parked frame then (IL2CPP's GC stops the world with signals), the write went to the handler's registers, and the handler's return restored the interrupted frame's x0, which held the first argument. The call therefore returned its own fd. `getdents64` returned 90 on Pokémon TCG Pocket's `blob/9c` cache directory, so bionic's `readdir` walked 176 KB past its buffer and the pre-download cleanup scan never finished. A `read` that returned its fd explains the game's intermittent asset-load crash. About one launch in six died on Loading.PreloadM with "archive ... is corrupted", a 70 GB allocation, or `RandomAccessSectorStream.Read` passing a negative count to `Buffer.BlockCopy`, yet a file-layer trace showed every read complete. The parked Promise now settles to a closure that writes the result, run where host calls write theirs, after the frames above have returned.
- **A handler waiting above a completed futex** (2026-10-27). A futex wait that completed deferred signals at the depth current when it completed. When a signal handler running above the wait had parked in `sigsuspend`, that depth was the handler's, and the deferral never cleared. Boehm's restart signal (SIGXCPU) then never reached the suspended thread, and the world stayed stopped. Lanes' timing exposed this (RimWorld's loader); a `cpu.test.ts` case reproduces it. The deferral now names the wait's own depth, and a signal ends a wait with EINTR only while no handler runs above it.
- **Signal frames on the interrupted stack** (2026-10-27). siginfo and ucontext were malloc'd. The kernel's `setup_rt_frame` puts them on the handler's stack below the interrupted sp, and Boehm's suspend handler relies on that: it pushes none of the interrupted registers itself, and scans from its own sp. Pointers held only in registers were therefore missed. Frames now go below the interrupted sp, or the alternate stack's top under SA_ONSTACK; a Java thread between native calls takes the top of its thread stack.
- **Threads and children**. A process's guest pthreads share its Worker's JS thread, apart from compute-bound slices on lanes (CPU cores and JIT); sustained background work (ThreatMetrix, Adjust `libsigner`) gets about a quarter during input, and during presentation at least that plus whatever the frames leave (below). Joinable pthread handles stay until join (reuse caused Lichess EDEADLK). fork, vfork and non-thread clone answer EAGAIN (no Android kernel gives ENOSYS), wait ECHILD; emulating `sh -c` would fabricate a child.

### bionic and the syscall layer

- **bionic integration** (2026-10-03; decision in `docs/architecture.md`). It needed this build's TLS/TCB offsets, `.symtab` helpers, attached host threads, the malloc dispatch table (libc also calls malloc directly), the linker's CFI shadow for `dnsOpenProxy`, and a root-owned `/dev/__properties__` with per-property serials (else localtime ran in GMT); Scudo needed core extents, CRC32 and TBI. FORTIFY aborted TikTok on `readdir(NULL)`; `pthread_getattr_np` needs the main stack in `/proc/self/maps` (YouTube). There is no libc flag: an A/B against the HLE libc needs a worktree at `33b23028` with `?libc=bionic` unset.
- **ABI slips**. `Args64` read stack arguments from plane 0 (Lichess drew no text); ARM32 mmap64 read its off64_t from the padding slot.
- **Sockets are open files** (2026-10-02..03). `OpenFile.socket` lets dup, poll, epoll and SCM_RIGHTS see one kind; sendmsg/recvmsg had been ENETUNREACH. AF_UNIX has bind/listen/accept, keeps message boundaries and SO_RCVTIMEO on the guest clock; SO_SNDTIMEO is unenforced. `F_DUPFD` returning 0 made mio's epoll fd stdin and failed every Tubi connect.
- **Per-ABI syscall tables** (2026-10-20). Each ABI numbers its system calls over bodies their modules own; `nr === 124` in `Cpu.syscall` had been ABI-blind (adjtimex on ARM32). Open files carry typed state their ops own, and pipes, counters, epoll and sockets wait through one wait queue. The arm32 libc's stubs are real: readdir goes through getdents64, sigwait through sigtimedwait, gethostby* and inet_aton through the resolver, and timed locks end at their deadlines. ioctl answers ENOTTY or EBADF, mremap carries another process's shared mapping, and the names dladdr answers are interned. The JNI table is laid out by name and matches `jni.h` slot for slot (233 entries).
- **A futex wake that read its timeout** (2026-10-26, `d6c6d8a6`). FUTEX_WAKE through variadic `syscall(2)` leaves junk in the timeout register; translating it as a pointer threw, `copyFault` turned that into a silent EFAULT, and the wake was lost. Tokio's parked workers then never woke, so hyper's gRPC to Pokémon TCG Pocket's API stalled on the title screen. A Rust repro (tokio and parking_lot, built for aarch64-linux-android against the vendored bionic and run under `scripts/nrun.ts`) failed about half its runs before and none of six after. Only FUTEX_WAIT and FUTEX_WAIT_BITSET translate the fourth argument.
- **One monotonic clock** (2026-10-25). Native CLOCK_MONOTONIC was each realm's own `performance.now()` plus 1 s, while System.nanoTime and SystemClock count from the page's start in every realm, so Chrome's `base::TimeTicks` disagreed between its processes and with its Java side. Every clock but the wall clocks now reads that uptime in the core and the kernel alike, as do MediaCodec's render times and FRAME_RENDERED events (which had read `performance.now()`). The core reads the clock through on each call, since a realm may replace its clock after instantiation.

### EGL, GLES and WebGL

- **Synchronous WebGL queries** (2026-10-02). `getError`/`getParameter` block; the first after a present waits for the frame (24-185 ms). gles3 tracks pixel-store state (getParameter took ~770 ms of a Snapseed open), whether WebGL can have latched an error (`keepsClean`: Lichess getError 1.12-1.71 → 0.62-0.64 s per 12 s) and recorded upload levels (TikTok's per-upload `glGetError` took 5.06 s of a 10 s step). Bounding pixel views by unpack extent cut TikTok `getError` 718/772 → 4/8 ms; tracking `glUniform1i` instead of `getUniform` lifted Genshin 19.4 → 26-27 frames/s. Rejected: a fence per present (100-130 ms polling, no gain).
- **Buffers, formats and surfaces**. Client-array draws read indices back via `getBufferSubData` (since 2026-10-16 from the buffer's kept contents); WebGL rejects `GL_FIXED`. ReadPixels commits only initialized rows (`8d3437e8`). `eglQuerySurface` ignored `setBuffersGeometry` and `getWidth` returned the set geometry, not the consumer default (Among Us 375×188; now 900×450 as on the API-33 emulator).
- **Current-state EGL14 natives** (2026-10-13). YouTube crashed a HandlerThread with `UnsatisfiedLinkError` for `EGL14.eglGetCurrentDisplay()`; EGL14 lacked the current-display and current-surface queries, `eglQuerySurface`, `eglBindAPI`/`eglQueryAPI`, the `long` `eglGetDisplay` and the waits, which EGL10 had. Pixmap, client-buffer, tex-image, copy-buffer and surface-attribute calls remain unregistered.
- **A destroyed window surface's last frame** (2026-10-16, `146f0989`). Native `eglDestroySurface` closed a window surface's presented frame unless a BLAST queue held it, but the frame is its consumer's: a TextureView went on drawing the closed ImageBitmap ("The image source is detached"), 27–55 failed draws per TikTok feed step as each swipe tore down the last video's surface. Only pbuffer frames are closed now, as EGL14 does.
- **getError round trips by app** (2026-10-16). TikTok's feed makes none. Google Maps makes two real ones per frame through the Java GLES20 bindings (`bkzb.ab`, from its frame start and frame end), with GL work in both gaps; `keepsClean` cannot vouch for calls those bindings make (`untracked`), so removing them needs tracked Java bindings and draw and uniform validation as exact as WebGL's. Treating only the untracked calls as unknown removed none and was reverted. An indexed draw from client arrays without a range now bounds its indices from the element buffer's kept contents instead of reading them back (`263fb2cc`).
- **One GLES adapter and one EGL core** (2026-10-20). The Java GLES bindings run over libGLES through one descriptor-driven adapter, where a second hand-written ES 2.0 emulation had reported a different version on the same context. `android/egl.ts` holds displays, configs, contexts, surfaces and per-thread errors for libEGL, EGL14/EGLExt and framework.jar's EGLImpl, so the TS EGL10/EGL11 classes and EGL11's eglSwapInterval (which AOSP never declares) are gone. Each follows AOSP: calls before eglInitialize answer EGL_NOT_INITIALIZED, eglGetDisplay past NUM_DISPLAYS answers EGL_BAD_PARAMETER, a pbuffer swap has no effect, and a null config or attribute list throws. An RGB888 config joins RGBA8888, so GLSurfaceView's default chooser finds one. `glGetUniformLocation` answers one handle per program and name until relink.
- **Vulkan for Unity** (2026-10-26). Unity 6 picks Vulkan when libvulkan offers a device and window-system integration. The Pokémon TCG Pocket branch had grown a second Vulkan driver of its own; its features were ported onto the one Chrome brought (`src/native/vulkan/`) and the branch's driver dropped.
  - *Swapchain* (`121a5f83`). `VK_KHR_android_surface` makes a surface of an ANativeWindow, and a swapchain's images are presented to its consumer as an EGL swap presents a frame: copied through one OffscreenCanvas per swapchain into an ImageBitmap (`FrameCanvas`, shared with AHardwareBuffer publishing). A present paces to vsync (FIFO) and answers OUT_OF_DATE once the window's buffers change size. The scheduler favours threads that produced a frame recently, which an EGL swap recorded and a Vulkan present did not, so Unity's render thread ran at background priority: about 2 frames/s on the title screen, 30 once a present notes the frame.
  - *Attachment clears, blits and unaligned copies* (`473756f0`). `vkCmdClearAttachments` and scaled `vkCmdBlitImage` draw with internal pipelines; an unscaled blit of one format is a copy, and scaled integer, depth or 3D blits throw as unsupported. The pack screen drew nothing because a 23,706-byte `vkCmdCopyBuffer` failed WebGPU's 4-byte rule and invalidated the submission; unaligned copies go through a staging copy of the source's whole words and a shader whose invocations each merge the copied bytes into one destination word, and aligned ones stay `copyBufferToBuffer`.
  - *A uniform block past the bound range* (`5a156642`). Pack opening failed on dynamic uniform buffers bound near the end of a 2 MB buffer: WebGPU binds the shader block's whole size (8,176 or 65,408 bytes), more than the range Vulkan binds. Each uniform buffer has maxUniformBufferRange (64 KB) of room past its size, and a binding takes up to that much.
  - *Texel buffers* (`b7133af1`). After the player name the game died on UnityGfxDeviceWorker at `pc 0`: Unity called `vkCreateBufferView`, a Vulkan 1.0 core command libvulkan did not export. Buffer views exist, descriptor writes and update templates take them, and SPIR-V `Dim Buffer` images become WGSL storage arrays of their 32-bit components, read by OpImageFetch/OpImageRead, written by OpImageWrite and sized by OpImageQuerySize. UNIFORM_TEXEL_BUFFER is offered only in single-component 32-bit formats and STORAGE_TEXEL_BUFFER in those a storage image declares, so an app that checks features never binds a view the translation would misread.
  - *Compressed images of any size* (`94ad2ea9`). WebGPU requires a block-compressed texture's size to be whole blocks, Vulkan does not. A 2D image whose size is not is kept in the format's lossless decoded counterpart; copies into it fill a whole-block texture that a draw then decodes into the image. A 3D one answers FORMAT_NOT_SUPPORTED.
  - *A black title screen after the port* (`597dd11f`, `8891feff`, `0d3daa3b`, `81eedd6e`, `a4b5fd3d`). On Chrome's driver the game presented 707×1414 frames with no WebGPU error, but every scene pass's targets read back (0,0,0,0), even with blending and depth tests removed. Unity keeps its uniforms in host-visible memory, and a submit uploaded only the vertex, index, indirect and transfer buffers its commands named. Shaders therefore read zero matrices and every vertex fell on the origin. Buffers that bound descriptor sets reach are now uploaded too, and storage ones are read back. Other faults found on the way: the translator dropped `gl_PerVertex`'s members (a WGSL compile error) and passed a combined sampler's texture as its sampler; an aspect view of a depth/stencil image named the combined format; a one-row compressed copy passed an unaligned `bytesPerRow`; and Unity's graphics jobs record a secondary after its primary records the execution, so secondaries are now read at submit. The title screen then draws as on the pre-port branch (`3c91a7b7`).
- **EGLImages of hardware buffers** (2026-10-25). Chrome's GPU process takes each decoded frame as an AHardwareBuffer, imports it through EGL_ANDROID_get_native_client_buffer and EGL_ANDROID_image_native_buffer, and binds it with `glEGLImageTargetTexture2DOES`. WebGL textures share no storage, so the bound texture name comes to stand for the buffer's own texture, keeping the parameters the name had, and deleting the name leaves that texture to the buffer. A buffer's pixels live in the texture of the GL that bound it, or a decoded frame until one does; a CPU lock reads them back. `eglGetSyncAttrib(KHR)` answers a fence's type, condition and status, and a config with alpha bits now gets a WebGL context with alpha (every context had been opaque). Chrome logs repeated `eglCreateSync` failures with EGL_BAD_ATTRIBUTE (0x3004); not investigated.

### Media codecs

- **MediaCodec over WebCodecs** (2026-10-02). Decoders are `c2.webcodecs.<type>.decoder`, vendor and hardware-accelerated like the AVD's `c2.goldfish.*`; Exynos names were rejected because apps key quirks on them. Codec-config buffers lead the next key frame or become an audio description; input before a key frame and length-prefixed samples decode to nothing, as on devices. YouTube plays VP9 at parity.
- **A new buffer per `getBuffer`** (2026-10-15). `MediaCodec.getInputBuffer` invalidates the cached buffer at that index and `queueInputBuffer` frees it, since JMediaCodec wraps the codec's memory with `NewDirectByteBuffer` on every call, read-only for output. Returning the codec's own buffer each time left the one ExoPlayer wrote into inaccessible and failed every YouTube playback; each call now hands out a new view.
- **Why TikTok decodes in software** (2026-10-16, `17625472`, `63b57892`). TikTok's player configures the HEVC MediaCodec that WebCodecs backs, and its drain thread gives up after 1,000 ms without an output buffer and switches to its ByteVC1 software decoder. The codec produced frame 0 at about 0.7 s, but its callback reached the app late: callbacks were posted from the main thread (JMediaCodec posts from the codec's looper), and sample units framed by 4-byte lengths before the sample entry was read decoded to nothing. With both fixed, later launches still went straight to ByteVC1 in some runs, which varies between runs with settings the server sends; nothing reported about the codec was found to be wrong.
- **Images of raw outputs** (2026-10-26, `a60440fe`). After Tap to Start the screen stayed black: CRI Mana decodes the opening movie with an H.264 MediaCodec and reads each frame with `getOutputImage`, and `getImage` answered null. It now wraps a raw output's buffer in a `MediaCodec$MediaImage` over the MediaImage2 description JMediaCodec builds (YUV, three planes with their offsets, increments and subsampling), cropped to the visible rect; a decoder's input has no image, as in JMediaCodec.
- **OpenSL ES** (2026-10-26, `ab6729cf`, `f1ffbf9e`). libOpenSLES had no engine (`slCreateEngine` FEATURE_UNSUPPORTED). It now has an engine, output mixes and PCM buffer-queue players over the PCM output AAudio uses. Interfaces are laid out at the process's pointer width, and their vtables name each method, so one implementation serves the interfaces that share a method. Callbacks run one at a time on each player's own thread, set up as AAudio's are. URI and fd players and recorders stay unsupported.
- **Audio pacing** (2026-10-26, `bd5a464e`, `7c7684b6`). CRIWARE sizes its OpenSL buffers from `AudioSystem.getPrimaryOutputFrameCount`, which answered 0; it is one 10 ms burst, as AAudio's FramesPerBurst. Players released a buffer when it finished playing, so one with two buffers queued got about 17 callbacks/s. As AOSP's AudioPlayer copies buffers into a minimum-size AudioTrack and releases each once the track takes it, so do they now (100 callbacks/s).
- **A player stays valid while it is destroyed** (2026-10-26, `005fb319`). `Destroy` removed the player before draining its queued callbacks, so a callback that enqueued again found no player and the process died in `BUFFERQUEUE.Enqueue`. As in AOSP, the object stays valid until destroy returns; queued callbacks that have not started are dropped.
- **AAudio for Chrome** (2026-10-27, `53ac173d`, `59a86e26`). Unmuting a YouTube video killed Chrome's browser process with SIGSEGV at 0 on its AudioThread: libaaudio.so did not define `AAudioStreamBuilder_setUsage`, which Chrome's wrapper calls, nor `setContentType`, `setInputPreset`, `setChannelMask` or `AAudioStream_getTimestamp`. The builder keeps them with Android's defaults, a channel mask sets the channel count, and the timestamp reports frames presented at CLOCK_MONOTONIC's time. Sound then played 0.78 s per second: Chrome asks for 4096-frame callbacks over a two-burst buffer, which clamps to one callback, so the next was requested only after the queued buffer had ended and the page→Worker→guest round trip left a gap at every boundary. A stream now refills while about 100 ms is still queued (`minFrames`, as OpenSL ES and AudioTrack), standing in for AudioFlinger's buffering below it: 10.0 s of sound per 10 s.
- **Chrome's video path** (2026-10-25). Chrome's GPU process decodes with MediaCodec into an AImageReader's window (`ANativeWindow_toSurface`), acquires each frame as an AImage's AHardwareBuffer and shows video as an ASurfaceControl transaction above the page's compositor surface. libmediandk now has AImageReader and AImage, libnativewindow AHardwareBuffer, and libandroid ASurfaceControl and ASurfaceTransaction, whose tree is composed into a frame for the Java SurfaceControl it hangs from (`surfacecontrol.ts`). The decoder now holds up to 16 output buffers, as a surface's BufferQueue gives a device's decoder, so frames Chrome holds for its renderer do not stop the decoding.

### Relay, proxy, TLS and DNS

- **UDP relay** (2026-10-02). Replies framed with the resolved address were dropped by sockets connected to the synthetic one, stalling YouTube's QUIC; replies name the dialed address. UDP connect to IPv6 is ENETUNREACH. The deployed Worker has no UDP endpoint.
- **TLS, DNS and the proxy**. Native TLS verifies against pinned AOSP roots; Java TLS ends at the relay, which presents a P-256 leaf for the host from its own CA in the system trust store; a pinned key does not match it. Guest resolvers take DNS from `LinkProperties`; port 53 goes through `/dns-query`. Bun may strip `Content-Length`, so it travels separately (AntennaPod).

## Performance

- **Research-led optimization experiments** (2026-10-21, starting at `3ab71fd2`). The [design investigation](performance-design.md) separates three substantial changes: Java value optimization across calls, feedback at individual virtual call sites, and native traces with fewer register transfers; independently installable cached code addresses first visits. Two narrower implementations were measured and removed. Extending the trivial inliner to arithmetic and conversions passed the existing 1,145 differential cases plus 320 additional boundary cases, but three alternating Chrome runs of six allocation/call/Kotlin-shaped workloads gave no repeatable improvement: `ktUpdaters` 5.990 → 6.085 ms, `ktStateMachine` 0.570 → 0.585 ms, getters 0.360 → 0.360 ms. One Komikku repeat pair was also unconvincing (third-and-later Appearance frames 38.5 → 37.9 ms); its first visit worsened, but one pair with differing diagnostic reads is not a regression measurement. Broadening instruction coverage alone is not the proposed SSA/escape-analysis tier. Switching only native engine compilation to asynchronous compilation, leaving emission on the app Worker, passed a pending-code-write correctness test but saved little blocking: three alternating browser Worker probes of 64 regions × 400 A64 adds gave median blocking 2.850 → 2.475 ms, readiness 2.850 → 8.320 ms, and subsequent execution 6.050 → 5.985 ms. Move emission with engine compilation if revisiting; the asynchronous switch alone is not retained.
- **Sampled Java call-site targets** (2026-10-21, `82c725c9`). Hot units chose guards per signature from whole-app counts, so two sites with different receivers shared one plan. A first-tier activation whose count is 1 modulo 64 now records the dispatch slots its virtual calls reach in a 384 KiB sketch per Wasm World (compile workers included; two targets and at most 1,024 observations a row), and the planner keys guards by caller and DEX PC. Unseen sites keep the global ranking, broad or colliding rows get no guards, and every guard still tests the live slot. Profiles are not persisted; AOT versions advance to 23 because the transfer window moves. The two-site G1 workload ran 0.640 → 0.145 ms with controls unchanged, but first on-demand calls cost more (two-site 7.600 → 8.295 ms) and platform code modules grow 3.4%. In three Komikku pairs the repeated Appearance frame fell 36.25 → 28.70 ms at the median (ranges overlap; one pair unchanged) and launch rose 125 ms. [Measurements](performance-design.md#measurements-and-costs).
- **Chrome 151's Wasm wrapper SIGILL** (2026-10-21/23). Startup stress crashed Chrome on its 22nd launch with the native region changes, before Java feedback; the final code passed 30 launches and the starting runtime 130. A browser adaptation of [V8's wrapper-publication regression](https://github.com/v8/v8/commit/42a63bb146721f2175fa7e557594ce698b3d8cbe), fixed upstream by flushing ARM64's instruction cache before publishing, crashes the same browser without webdroid. This is consistent with the app crash but unproven: the failing run lacked browser stderr. [Record](performance-design.md#verification). On 2026-10-23 a gate lost mihon-app's renderer 8 s into its launch ("Target crashed"). Its crash report names the same Chrome 151 build (binary UUID) and fault, EXC_BAD_INSTRUCTION on 0xd2800650, a valid `movz x16, #0x32`, which is what executing a stale instruction-cache line looks like. So did 23 of the other 24 headless-shell reports macOS kept from 2026-10-19..23 (renderer main threads and dedicated Workers, every checkout on the host; the 20:45 cluster on 10-21 is the reproduction). The fix first shipped in Chrome 156.0.8070.0, and the reproduction crashes 151.0.7922.34 in 3 of 3 runs and passes on 156.0.8078.4 in 3 of 3, so Playwright moved 1.62.1 → 1.64.1, whose Chromium is 156.0.8078.4.

### Launch and tap attribution

- **Launch critical path** (2026-10-11). Warm Calculator: the app Worker was busy almost without a break from 0.25 s to the first frame at 1.56 s; the page is off the path after bootstrap. Realm setup ~400 ms (`Program.add`, `Cha.add`), AOT loading ~250 ms, Java ~850 ms (40% a JS tail of instantiation, DEX metadata lookups and crossings). `zygoteInit` (260–370 ms, much of it `System.<clinit>` and LogManager reading configuration through `ZipFile`) now runs in the spare.
- **microG provisioning slips the first frame** (2026-10-02). When microG's boot lands before the app's first frame, the frame slips ~400 ms (bimodal); deferring provisioning gave 945–998 ms 8/8. Not committed.
- **FitNotes exercise-list tap** (2026-10-08/09). The warm `start` tap was 300–317 ms, ~267 ms before platform-global binding, then mostly 188–222 ms; its window held 288–331 compiled methods and 68–79 ms of compiling, and the thumbnail `sig` observer took 95–103 ms of sampled self time. It is within the gate now (`docs/performance.md`).
- **FitNotes History tap self-cost** (2026-10-05, shared load, one profile). After removing 24,321 µs of harness UI-tree work, app Worker self time was 200,736 µs: known boundary 9.46% (upper bound 70.71%; 122,960 µs unresolved), runtime 43,124, native bodies 15,664. This cannot establish boundary < 5%.
- **Cold Komikku tap anatomy** (2026-10-13, `lat --timeline`). `more`: UP was handled 2.2 ms after `pointerup`; one 85 ms `doFrame` did the recomposition, with 699 methods compiled on demand (61.5 ms of compiling in the window). The cold floor is compile plus first-tier Java, not input delivery.
- **Pointer pipelining is not a lever** (2026-10-13). UP reaches the Worker 0–6 ms after the page event, so posting pointer events ahead of the page's turn (plan 1.6) has nothing to recover.
- **V8 flags are not tap levers** (2026-10-13). A tenth of `--wasm-tiering-budget` left warm taps within noise; `--no-liftoff` made Calculator's first tap 710 ms.

### Tails and scheduling

- **The tail, not steady state** (2026-10-03). Mihon's list presented at p50 9.4 ms even at 4× throttle, but p90 17 ms and p99 75–200 ms from single 100 ms tasks of background threads sharing the thread (a theme tap was 100 ms of `MessageChannel` turns; Firebase DataTransport ran 107 ms mid-scroll). A warm theme tap (95 ms = main 77, app threads 15, host 3) recomposed the whole Scaffold (55–75 ms). Rejected: background threads yielding behind input with priority inheritance (98 vs 96 ms), and ending the looper's turn while a composite is pending (the lag lies inside the drawing message).
- **Mihon Reader scroll** (2026-10-09). 11,586 of 15,007 sampled ms were idle; `implementations` 65.0, `ran` 31.8, `isSubclass` 29.6 ms self. Unprofiled windows compiled ~2,600–2,900 methods (185–210 upgrades) at ~54–57 presentations/s (p99 34.5–35.3 ms); post-GC heap ~812 MiB. 32 KiB allocation sampling estimated 216–291 MiB transient allocation per window; it slows the app and is not retained-heap evidence.
- **Background share takes idle time** (2026-10-26). While a process presented, a background native thread slept `min(250, 3 × slice)` between slices, even when nothing else wanted the JS thread. RimWorld loads its defs on a pthread while UnityMain presents a progress screen at ~56 fps: the Worker idled 29% and the loader ran 92 M instructions/s. The sleep now also ends at a `scheduler.postTask` turn of background priority, which the host runs only when no frame, input, timer or yielded thread is queued (input keeps the plain sleep). In the same phase the Worker no longer idled, the loader ran 158 M/s and UnityMain still presented 47 fps; from the mobile UI profile to the main menu went from 7.8 to 5.2 min, a run that also had the implicit-directory index.
- **Virtual vsync at host rate** (2026-09-27). A 60 Hz virtual display swapping on raw rAF presented 60 of 120 frames on a 120 Hz host. The shell measures the rate (`?hz=` pins it) and `eglSwapBuffers` waits for virtual vsync (Calculator taps 57 → 105/s at 4× throttle); harness swipes step at the refresh interval.

### Not levers

- **Call bookkeeping, runtime helpers and input delivery** (2026-10-22). Under node, 2×10⁷ warmed `call_indirect`s took 1.28 ns each, 1.42 with a callee `global.set`, 1.65 with depth enter/leave in an imported global (1.55 in memory) and 1.90 with both: a few percent of call-heavy code at most. In a profile of 13 repeat Komikku taps (263 ms of Java, burning on) the out-of-line runtime helpers took about 7.5% (`$implements` 4.1 ms, `$countLoop` 2.1, `$vtab` 1.9, `$imtslow` 1.7, `$count` 1.5, `$enter` 1.4, `$castImpl` 1.2), host JS about 6.7 ms a tap with no item above 1.3 ms, and `Math.pow` and HCT color conversion are the app's own work. Calculator M's input reached its publish in 2.4 ms on a launch's first event and 0.1–0.3 ms after it. GC in responses (trace, burning on): a 4.9 ms finalization in `start`, one 2.9 ms scavenge in Appearance, otherwise 1–1.5 ms scavenges; majors (8–9 ms) came after the responses.
- **No optimized code from the engine's cache in Chrome for Testing** (2026-10-22, Chromium 151 `--headless=new`, persistent profile). A 200 KB module with 40 tiered functions, compiled with `compileStreaming` from an HTTP-cached (`max-age`) response or from Cache Storage, or with `compile` from bytes: after a reload its first call took 410 ms (Liftoff) in all three against 90 ms tiered. Inconclusive for a real Chrome profile; consistent with "The engine code cache works but is not a tap lever".
- **Tier-independent costs** (2026-10-03, M4 Pro, headless, SwiftShader). Frame production 0.25–0.37 ms `drawMs`/frame scrolling FitNotes and Mihon; text < 1% of every profiled window; guest↔host crossings 38 ns/call; GC 3% of busy time at 4× throttle; Binder ≤ 0.5 ms/tap; idle apps ~2% of the thread.
- **The CPU profiler speeds up repeat frames** (2026-10-19). Under `lat --cpuprofile` (V8's sampler at 200 µs in the page and every Worker), Komikku's repeat Appearance `frame` was 23–28 ms and Settings 15–17 ms, against 32–36 and 17–22 ms in unprofiled runs of the same build and query. The cause is not established (one hypothesis: the sampler's load keeps the cores at higher clocks between frames). Compare frame times only between runs with the same instrumentation.
- **GC in heavy frames** (2026-10-19). In CPU profiles of Komikku's `doFrame` runs of 15 ms or more (cold, `--gpu --repeat 6`), the collector took 3.7% of the sampled time with the trivial-inlining build and 1.7% with nested straight-line inlining; Java took 62–66% and the renderer's JS (display-list `replay` and the Canvas2D calls it makes, 7.6% of all `doFrame` time) most of the rest. A smaller object header and `long`-handle natives without BigInt (plan Change 4) were therefore not built. The first ripple of a process compiled its AGSL effect in the frame (`compileEffect`, about 17 ms in a fresh browser profile); `RuntimeShader`'s constructor must report invalid AGSL, so the status query stays synchronous; whether the browser's GPU program cache shortens it on later visits was not measured.
- **Micro gains did not reach apps** (2026-10-02/03). Large matrix speedups and fewer JNI parks left matched Mihon scrolling unchanged; type-check marks (jbench `casts` 3.45 → 2.55 ms) showed no launch or tap difference over 3 alternating `lat` rounds.
- **Zig/Wasm kernels** (2026-10-03). Wasm wins on contiguous numeric work with few crossings. Yes: ELF RELR/APS2 relocation (600k RELR: 21–30 ms BigInt vs 3 ms u32 pairs), Bitmap lock/unlock conversion. No: layer filter/blur kernels (remove the readback), minikin breaking (7–25 µs/paragraph), SHA/AES/inflate/regex/charsets (the browser covers them), display-list replay (0.1–0.2 ms/frame ceiling), single Canvas2D ops.
- **A Zig compiler** (2026-10-16). Under node, on the production loader layout, compiling TikTok's first 20,000 on-demand methods one unit each took 109–121 µs per method (1,568 B each). Of that, emission took 57–64 µs, typing 14–15, encoding 10–12, facts 9–10, structuring 6.5–7 and preparing 1; a launch's page counts ~165 µs per method. The profile is flat. Of `buildUnit`, `emitOperation` takes 36% (invokes 13%), `inferTyping` 13% (`analyzeWebs` 12%), `finishModule` 11%, `structureAny` 8%, `emitEnsure` 7% and `analyze` 6%, and no compiler function has more than 6% self time. Emission interleaves Program and World lookups (resolution, slots, CHA, linkage) with each instruction, so a phase moved into Wasm would cross the boundary for each of them. A Zig compiler therefore means porting the DEX model, the Program and the World's numbering. Compiling is a third of a cold TikTok launch's 37 s to signup, so even at twice the speed it would save about what a prebuilt roots build does (6 s).
- **Lichess native profile** (2026-10-02). libflutter 62%, libapp 25%, libc 1.6%; no libc work warranted.

### Wasm tier benchmarks

- **Wasm GC over linear memory** (2026-10-04, G1). Over 42 jbench workloads Wasm GC beat the JS tier 1.5–2.4× on allocation-heavy code, so the linear-memory trigger was not met. Slow rows: no bulk copy between linear memory and a GC `byte[]` (~0.25 ns/byte; direct buffers moved to linear memory), String as struct + `i16` array vs a JS rope (≤ ~0.8×), `sha256` needing suspension.
- **First call was the deficit** (2026-10-07..08). On the production loader layout, JS/Wasm primary-ten geomeans were Node first 1.091×/steady 0.793×, Chrome first 0.230×/steady 1.048×: every Chrome first call regressed, and linkedList steady was ~0.29 vs ~0.07 ms (earlier selections differed by host and were unattributed). Chrome's first-call cost was inferred as six-module instantiation plus Liftoff (~1–2 ms) and inline tier-2 compiles. ArrayList kept improving past 101 calls (0.12–0.14 → 0.054–0.090 ms by 1,001), though instrumentation shifts promotion timing. Calls 52–101 make no native or resolver crossings; boxing/trampoline cost is unquantified, and separate `ArrayList$Itr.next` calls are a specialization lead.
- **Loop shapes** (2026-10-07). Reducible, irreducible and exception-cycle loops reach stable reference speed in 20.07–20.32 ms; guarded irreducible loops did not within 100 ms.

## Apps

- **Flow state** (2026-10-12..13). The quick e2e gate passes all 7 local flows (calculatorm, doodlejump, fitnotes, flappybird, komikku, mihon-onboarding, mihon-app) with no wedges; their older failures below are kept for the mechanisms. Before the JS tier's removal the serial `--live` matrix (harmonic, lichess, resy, reddit, tiktok, youtube, chatgpt) reached 13 of 14 flows; TikTok missed its 20 s launch budget (33 s). Large first launches: YouTube 42.9 s, ChatGPT 38.2 s, Reddit 20.1 s. On the Wasm tier (2026-10-24) the live flows passed 6 of 7: TikTok's cold launch reached its first-run gate at about 32 s, past its 20 s bound, as on its base (PLAN, TikTok's launch). The service shows its consent or, before it, the guest sign-up, so the launch now waits for either (`55bdf8ac`).

### Among Us, Cut the Rope and Subway Surfers

- **Among Us: live match over UDP** (2026-10-02..04). It completed a live Airship match over the relay's UDP; a cold Skeld load (~6.4 s) exceeds the host's client-load timeout. It holds its 30 fps target (60 as a probe: 49-51/s); FMOD Vorbis decode costs as much as UnityMain.
- **Among Us on the Wasm tier** (2026-10-15). AOSP's `DexPathList.findLibrary` searches `nativeLibraryDir` and, only with a `primaryCpuAbi`, `apk!/lib/<abi>`; a `/lib` placeholder left il2cpp unfound ("Failed to load il2cpp"). The booted package now has PackageAbiHelper's cluster layout, its primary-ABI libraries mounted lazily there. A MAP_SHARED read of `global-metadata.dat` got a source's first 1 MiB chunk of 15.9 MB until `contentAt` read whole ranges. The app Worker's display turned landscape while the page's stayed portrait (a squashed frame); the page now follows the orientation of the process whose activity fills the display, as DisplayRotation takes it from the top opaque activity: a translucent one over it leaves it to the one beneath, and the page's own activity brings back the orientation the user's rotations last gave the page. A browser pane holding storage from earlier runs aborted in `Loading.PreloadM` ("Size overflow in allocator") and later faulted in libil2cpp: OPFS `webdroid-disk` had lost the metadata's spilled file while its IndexedDB record survived, and the DiskStore Worker recreated it empty on read. No reset or orphan removal ran in between; the remover is unknown. A load now drops a disk record whose file no longer holds its size, as a restore without the file would, and Unity re-extracts it. microG's sign-in dialog (targetSdk 29) laid out under a 0xcc scrim over the gesture bar: `bindApplication` passed no disabled compat changes, so ENFORCE_EDGE_TO_EDGE (since SDK 35) and every other target-gated change applied to every app. It now passes the reference device's (`dumpsys platform_compat`, without its per-package overrides). The navigation source carries SystemUI's FLAG_SUPPRESS_SCRIM unless `config_navBarNeedsScrim`, and framework-res takes every value the device's overlays resolve, not only its Monet colors.
- **Cut the Rope**. It exposed Thumb SIMD operand order, IT, wide-branch and VFP faults; level one presents 36.0 frames/s. AAudio outputs real 24 kHz mono float.
- **Cut the Rope on the Wasm tier** (2026-10-15). Level one completes from a swipe and level two loads. The intro stayed black because Unity's video blit declares `GL_OES_EGL_image_external` in its vertex shader, which has no `samplerExternalOES`, so the directive survived and the stage failed to compile; it is now stripped from every stage that names it.
