With the JIT on, Om Nom drew as a sliver: Spine's `CosDeg`/`SinDeg` call libm's `cos`/`sin`, and `libc.zig` read a softfp double from the full 64-bit `c.x` pair, while a compiled region spills only each register's low word and leaves a stale upper half (the interpreter's `set` zero-extends). A temporary shadow checker, which re-ran each compiled region in the interpreter and compared 32-bit state, found no divergence, which pointed outside the JIT. Unity's AdvertisingIdClient fallback threw: GetMethodID resolves a superclass's `<init>`, which NewObject must run. Cronet's BoringSSL faulted on unimplemented NEON: by-scalar VMLAL (Montgomery multiplication) and VMULL.P8 (GHASH); the A32 long, wide and narrow forms now take every A64 op but the saturating doubling ones.
- **Subway Surfers** (2026-10-04) runs after inotify; background Firebase init throws.

### Calculator, FitNotes and Harmonic

- **Calculator: Back** (2026-10-05). Back differs 99.36%: the reference shows its launcher, webdroid a blank screen. Since 2026-10-24 (`113faef4`) the flow ends at its result: no change to webdroid, which runs no launcher, could lower that step, a seventh of the flow's mean.
- **FitNotes under host load** (2026-10-15). With 24 busy loops beside the quick gate (load about 45), FitNotes steps end in completion timeouts: 14 at base `89868cac`, 11 on the Maps branch. On an exercise's TRACK tab the HISTORY and GRAPH pages that the ViewPager keeps off screen show their loading spinners (ListFragment's progress container, `lce_loading`) until first visited. Each Holo spinner is a LayerDrawable, so ProgressBar's legacy animation calls `postInvalidateOnAnimation` from every draw and vsync stays requested. The reference AVD does the same: its main thread spends 60-110 ms per 5 s there and none once the pages have loaded, while gfxinfo counts no frames because hwui skips frames whose damage is empty. The quiescent wait then rests on pixel stability, whose readbacks stretch under load until the real-time bound. This is harness load sensitivity, not a webdroid fault, though without damage tracking each of those frames replays the whole display list here.
- **FitNotes: intermittent RenderNode NPE** (2026-10-05). NPEs in `RenderNode.nHasIdentityMatrix` (absent native `hasTransform`) wedged the main queue; a disposed InputChannel's Cleaner had freed a RenderNode that reused its slot (fix under Rendering).
- **FitNotes: list scrollbars stay drawn** (2026-10-23; explained 2026-10-24). The reference fades a list's scrollbar after scrolling stops, and the list steps (cat-biceps, back-to-exlist, cat-chest, scroll-chest, back-1) differed 17–24%. The fade did run here, after the capture: the controlled clock jumped past the first frame that arms it (e2e, "A controlled dwell at the app's pace"). Most of the difference was text drawn at Chrome's advances (Text and fonts, "Glyphs at minikin's positions").
- **Harmonic: WebView** (2026-10-06). The real article loads in its own renderer origin; a run that hit upstream HTTP 502 proved nothing.

### ChatGPT

- **Startup failures on Wasm** (2026-10-05..08). The Worker hit V8 `javascript OOM (Scavenger: semi-space copy)` before its first frame (the ~30 MB young isolate did not identify it); later the process died of an NPE in `ConnectivityManagerWrapper.<init>` on the Cronet init thread, because the GMS loader had not initiated those Chromium classes. It passes the 2026-10-12 live matrix.

### Clash, McDonald's and Play Integrity gates

- **Clash of Clans/Royale** (2026-10-04) fail anti-tamper.
- **McDonald's** (2026-09-27). It blocks on Play Age Signals, which microG 0.3.16 lacks; the user chose to wait for 0.3.17 over building master or bypassing via `DeepLinkRouter`.
- **McDonald's: Realm on a Worker's remote files** (2026-10-15). With microG 0.3.17 Age Signals binds, but the splash still cancelled every draw: it waited on the McDonald's SDK, whose Realm `DB::open` never finished. Realm needed, in order, `fallocate` to grow the owning realm's file (only the Worker's placeholder grew, so the lock file stayed empty and open retried forever); `mknodat` (bionic's `mkfifo`) for its condition-variable fifos, whose ENOSYS it takes as an old-device quirk before aborting on the next open (the owning realm holds the name, volatile; each process keeps its own pipe, so fifos do not signal across processes); and one process's mappings of a remote file sharing its stores, as one page cache would (its read-only section mapping never saw the header written through another, "Invalid top array size … file size: 0"). A new mapping flushes the others and waits for every pending lease write of the file. Realm builds its RealmFileException through JNI `NewObject` on a native thread, so the Java stack was empty; logging the constructor's string arguments in `jni.ts` found the message. Then the welcome gate, home, all tabs, the store locator, the menu and Nutrition work; item details, deals and the bag ask for sign-in. About one launch in three then died in inflation ("You must supply a layout_width attribute", a null `findViewById`, AppCompat's theme check, "Can't convert value at index 2 to dimension"): the host bridge copied each array a native reached through scratch it reserved at the top of linear memory, growing memory when a copy did not fit, and growing detaches every view a native already holds of its copied arguments (`nativeApplyStyle` found its `attrs` empty after reading `outValues`). Copies now go in chunks through a fixed 256 KB window below the natives' scratch; 0 crashes in 18 launches after, 2 in 6 before. The Order tab's heading overlapping "Learn about pickup" is not a text fault: line tops, baselines and height agree (Compose's accessibility bounds are trimmed under the overlapping node, so they are not sizes). The heading's Row is as tall as its image, and the XAPK ships only `config.mdpi`, whose `ftu_hero` decodes unscaled for a targetSdk ≥ 28 app, so the image is a third of its xxhdpi height. Open: a launch can hang after "Upgrade Later" (two StartupBlockerActivity instances destroyed, SplashActivity never resumed). A splash campaign's https mp4 failed in MediaPlayer with (1, -1010): Akamai answers 403 to the host's User-Agent and 200 to `http.agent`, and the media element's proxied request carried neither the device's headers nor its own Range. The proxy now takes a media element's headers from its query and forwards its Range (206), and MediaPlayer sends `setDataSource`'s headers with `http.agent`, as MediaHTTPConnection does; the campaign did not show again to check in the app. Realm keeps two read-write mappings of its lock file without `msync`; they stay separate copies, which works only because each field is reached through one mapping.
- **McDonald's: black screen after "Upgrade Later"** (2026-10-16). SplashActivity starts its update prompt twice, and both starts were in flight together. Each paused the activity on top when it began (Splash), so the second StartupBlockerActivity launched above a first that stayed resumed. "Upgrade Later" finishes both, and `finish` resumed the record directly below the top, which was the other finishing instance, so Splash never resumed. As in AOSP, where starts run one at a time under the WM lock and a finish resumes `topRunningActivity`, a start now pauses whatever is on top at the moment it pushes, and sends its launch transaction with the push so that a later start's pause reaches the client after it. A finish not covered by a running activity resumes the top activity that is not finishing. Three cold launches that each showed the prompt reached home.
- **Play Integrity** (2026-10-04). DroidGuard supplied zero bytes and Google returned HTTP 500; attestation stays opt-in after it regressed ChatGPT startup.

### Doodle Jump

- **Inverse billing locks** (2026-10-02..08). The unchanged bytecode takes two monitors in opposite orders: main holds `IabHelper` `mServiceLock` (from `onServiceConnected`) and enters billing `m_lock` in `InAppBilling$8$1.onIabSetupFinished`; a JNI Java thread holds `m_lock` in `checkBillingInitState` and waits in `IabHelper.getSetupState`. On a device billing binds long before the game loop. Here the service realm once booted at background priority (bind 0.7 or 7-8 s), fixed by bound processes inheriting their client's priority. Wasm wedged at Play again through 2026-10-08 (the JS tier ran the service callback first); 95 scheduler controls found no defect, and no lock reordering, forced ownership or fake billing success was applied. The 2026-10-13 gate passes; timing can still expose the race.
- **Frame rate** (2026-10-04). ~40/s on SwiftShader, 60/s on M4 Pro.

### Genshin Impact

- **Reaches login** (2026-10-04). 7.1 reaches login and a Geetest captcha at ~29 GL frames/s (60 needs ~2.3× JIT speed).

### Google Chrome

- **Launch and pages** (2026-10-25). The Play XAPK (ARM32 `libchrome.so`, isolated feature splits) searches Google and loads pages such as vgpu.sh across its browser, GPU and sandboxed renderer processes; vgpu.sh's hero area above its title stays empty, and whether the guest Chrome offers WebGPU was not checked. Reaching that took an activity-alias that carries its target's theme and flags (`7010cd31`), isolated splits with their own class loaders and components (`4f7825aa`), `readlink("/proc/self/exe")` copying its target (base::PathService aborted on an empty DIR_EXE, `4b62c73a`), general-dynamic TLS blocks that start from the relocated PT_TLS image (a `thread_local`'s vtable pointer was the file's unrelocated value, `fc18e364`), loosely matched ICU property names (the omnibox's `\p{script=latin}` threw, `d5d593bd`), /dev/ashmem's identity and protection ioctls (`ed103cce`), and the cross-process mechanisms under Native code (ashmem, sockets, isolated instances, native choreographers, the video path). A Surface parceled to another process now carries its owner and a token: the reader connects through the broker, its frames reach the owner's queue over that port and the queue's size comes back. A local VP9 WebM over MSE plays in sync.
- **YouTube plays** (2026-10-26). The watch page's playback errors were two emulation faults, not the renderer's speed. The 2026-10-25 explanation (a 1–2 s `updateend` handler and YouTube's watchdog, with 330M guest instructions/s on one host thread) was wrong, as were earlier JIT and PartitionAlloc suspicions. (1) `new ArrayBuffer` failed from 1 MiB upward: Chrome's array-buffer allocator (libchrome+0x3627d1c) caps its total at half of `RLIMIT_DATA`, read once by a static initializer, and `getrlimit` reported a fabricated 1 MiB for every resource except the stack. Found with a single-step trace triggered by the requested size in a register, then the allocator's disassembly. `proc.ts` now reports the reference device's app-process limits (`/proc/<pid>/limits` on the API 35 AVD) and rejects resources past `RLIM_NLIMITS`. (2) The renderer's Media thread then faulted on Thumb-2 `SMULWB` in audio DSP code. Chrome reports the Java InternalError from that JNI call as FATAL and raises SIGTRAP, and its crash handler then waits forever in `__read_chk` on fd 101, so the pipeline never started (no `sourceopen`, readyState 0) while YouTube kept fetching. Thumb lacked SMLAW/SMULW, SMLAD/SMUAD/SMLSD/SMUSD, SMLALD/SMLSLD, SMLAL<x><y> and USAD8/USADA8. ARM lacked the dual and long-dual forms, and its SMUAD was decoded as a plain multiply. Thumb SMLAW/SMULW now JIT as ARM's. Big Buck Bunny (`aqz-KE-bpKQ`, 640×360) then plays at about 1 s/s, with 408 of 841 frames dropped from its 60 fps source. Open: the crash handler's hang on SIGTRAP (Chrome should die and restart the renderer), ANGLE's repeated `eglCreateSync` failures (we do not offer `EGL_ANDROID_native_fence_sync`), chrome://media-internals stopping updating, and one launch from a persisted build crashing.
- **WebGPU on vgpu.sh** (2026-10-26). Chrome's Dawn reaches the GPU through Vulkan, so `libvulkan.so` (`src/native/vulkan/`) is a Vulkan 1.1 driver over the host's WebGPU device: one physical device (the host adapter, with its real vendor ID), one queue, and the AHardwareBuffer, external-memory and sync-fd extensions Dawn's shared textures need. Dawn's GL backend was no alternative, as it needs GLES 3.1 through ANGLE. Command buffers record closures that a submit replays into one GPUCommandEncoder. Host-visible memory is guest memory, uploaded at each submit; buffers the work wrote are read back before its fence or semaphore signals. Pipelines use WebGPU's `layout: "auto"`, so bind groups are made per pipeline from descriptor sets (Vulkan binding b is WGSL binding 2b, a combined sampler's sampler 2b+1). Shaders go through a SPIR-V to WGSL translator (`spirv.ts`), checked offline with naga against Dawn's captured Tint SPIR-V and naga-compiled loops, switches, textures, compute and atomics. The naga npm build, maintained by one person, was not taken as a dependency. The compositor's own `vkCreateInstance` failed, since `VK_KHR_surface` and `VK_KHR_android_surface` were not offered, so Chrome kept GL compositing. Both have been offered since Unity's swapchain (Pokémon TCG Pocket's "Vulkan for Unity"), and Chrome has composited and rasterized through the driver since (*Chrome on Vulkan*, below). Chrome's Android WebGPU blocklist blocks Apple's vendor ID, so the page needs chrome://flags#enable-unsafe-webgpu (saved in the guest's Local State); reporting an allowlisted vendor would misidentify the device. The bugs that showed on the way: VkPhysicalDeviceProperties' `limits` is 8-aligned (offset 296 on both ABIs; struct specs take a zero-length `u64[0]` field for this), `VkExternalMemoryProperties` puts its features first where the semaphore and fence properties put them last (Dawn read "Vulkan memory is not importable"), a GPUAdapter makes one device (a page reload's second `vkCreateDevice` now asks for another adapter), Vulkan's +Y is up for a negative-height viewport, as Dawn sets, and down for a positive one, and Chrome begins every AHardwareBuffer access from `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_UNDEFINED` (crbug.com/327111284), relying on drivers keeping the contents, so every acquire from the foreign queue takes the buffer's pixels. Frames keep their premultiplied pixels on both copies. The prism hero then draws as on the host, with its background, shadows and spectrum, at about 3.6 frames/s, with the renderer's guest CPU (9.9 s per 10 s) the limit rather than the GPU process (2.3 s). Open: after `kill -9` of the GPU process Chrome never starts another, so each driver change needed a Chrome relaunch (about 10 min); push constants, sparse resources, clamp-to-border colours, external (YCbCr) AHardwareBuffer formats and non-zero clears of non-renderable formats are not supported. `pnpm check` passed (165 s); under another session's load (load average 20–35) four `--corpus` runs each missed one launch by 1–2 s against the 30 s limit (TikTok twice, lichess, and Doodle Jump's flow wedging once), and the base without the driver missed Google Maps the same way. None of those apps calls Vulkan: TikTok's libraries name no Vulkan symbol, and lichess, whose `libflutter.so` does open `libvulkan.so`, opts out of Impeller and made no Vulkan call.
- **Chrome on Vulkan** (2026-10-26). With the surface extensions offered, Chrome's GPU process composites and rasterizes with Skia on Vulkan, which put Skia's shaders through the translator for the first time. A minimal page (2-, 3- and 4-stop gradients, rounded fills and borders, a JPEG and a PNG, each also clipped round) found three faults, after which YouTube's watch page and vgpu.sh draw as on the host. Skia's uniform block leaves its first 16 bytes to the stage it shares the block with, so the fragment stage's first member is at Offset 16; WGSL placed it at 0 and every member read 16 bytes early. Gradients drew another draw's stops, and JPEGs, which Chrome decodes to Y, U and V planes, were converted by the wrong matrix and drew yellow, with green-tinted thumbnails (`24e4b618`). A struct whose first member is offset now leads with a padding member, which its constructors fill. `IsInf` compared against `3.40282347e+38`, which rounds above FLT_MAX and fails WGSL's f32 check, so every pipeline with that shader was invalid (`05d430f0`). And SPIR-V may decorate a lone output `Index 0`, while WGSL accepts `@blend_src` only as a 0/1 pair under `dual_source_blending` (`81e6f4af`). The 19 shaders Chrome created for that page pass naga, Pokémon TCG Pocket's title screen still draws, and `pnpm check` passed (133 s). Chrome's `LoadWithDlopenExt` errors for `libchrome.so` were already logged on main, and Chrome runs regardless.
- **Chrome's open issues** (2026-10-26). (1) A killed GPU process was never restarted because the browser never saw its mojo channel end: a MessagePort reports nothing when the Worker holding its other side is terminated (no `close` event, in HeadlessChrome 156, even for an explicit close), so a socket crossing realms (`socket/bridge.ts`) never ended. Each side now names a Web Lock its realm holds for life, and the peer waits on it; the lock is released about 5 ms after `terminate()`. Chrome then starts `privileged_process2`, and vgpu.sh draws after a reload. (2) ANGLE's repeated `eglCreateSync` failures were for `EGL_SYNC_NATIVE_FENCE_ANDROID`. Native fences are now sync_files (`syncfile.ts`: POLLIN once signalled, `SYNC_IOC_MERGE`, `SYNC_IOC_FILE_INFO`), which Vulkan semaphores export and import. Advertising the extension alone blanked every page: ANGLE takes native fences as present once `eglDupNativeFenceFDANDROID` resolves, which turns on DrDc's CompositorGpuThread, and that thread called entry 29 of ANGLE's FunctionsEGL table, `waitSyncKHRPtr`. ANGLE advertises waitSync for EGL 1.5 but loads that entry only when `EGL_KHR_wait_sync` is listed, so the call jumped to pc 0. A thread's signal log now names a fault's pc, address and caller, which together with libchrome's dispatch (`ldrd r3,r0,[r0,#36]`; `bx [r3+idx*4]`) found it. `eglWaitSync` and `eglWaitSyncKHR` now wait on the server side. (3) The crash handler's hang was our libc: `__cmsg_nxthdr` was a stub returning null, so Chrome's `CrashHandlerHost` read SCM_CREDENTIALS and never the SCM_RIGHTS after it ("Death signal missing descriptor"), and the renderer waited in `__read_chk` for a reply. A crashed renderer now dies and the tab shows "Aw, Snap!". crashpad's handler then fails to fork (EAGAIN, as every fork here does), so no minidump is written. (4) The Vulkan driver gained the following, checked offline with naga (Dawn makes none of these calls on vgpu.sh, which still draws). Push constants are a read-only storage buffer at WGSL binding 999 of group min(sets, maxBindGroups − 1); with four sets that group is shared with the last set. Each draw or dispatch gets a 256-byte slot of a per-device buffer, written before the submission that reads it. std140's padded arrays wrap each element in a struct sized to the stride. Row-major or padded matrices are arrays of their rows or columns, which loads, stores and access chains reassemble; an `spirv-as` shader covered padded scalar and vector arrays, a mat2, a row-major mat4, an array of them, whole-struct extracts and stores. Images WebGPU cannot render to (snorm, 3D, rgb9e5) are cleared by copies of the colour packed as their texel. A decoder's AHardwareBuffers (IMPLEMENTATION_DEFINED, Y8Cb8Cr8_420) hold RGBA, so they are external formats whose suggested conversion is RGB identity. Still absent: sparse resources (not advertised) and clamp-to-border colours, which core Vulkan has with no feature bit; edge texels stand in for them. (5) chrome://media-internals does not stop updating; it never loads. A navigation trace shows `DevUiLoaderThrottle` deferring it: the developer pages ship in Chrome's on-demand `dev_ui` module, which the XAPK does not include. microG's companion, with split install disabled, logs "service is disabled" but answers `onStartInstall(13)` (13 is `CommonStatusCodes.ERROR`). Play Core takes that as session 13, which never progresses, so the throttle never fails. chrome://version and other pages in the base module load. `pnpm check` passed (139 s).
- **Speed and taps** (2026-10-27, after the JIT, Vulkan upload and AAudio changes above). YouTube's watch page plays with sound, presenting about 29 frames/s of a 30 fps stream with the renderer about 40% busy. vgpu.sh draws completely at 7–9 presentations/s while the renderer warms and 10.6–11.3 after. The GPU process uploads about 2 MB/s, so it is not the limit. Taps were timed from the tap's end to the first presented frame whose crop of the answering region differs (`wd.mirrorFrames` regions, per process canvas): opening and closing YouTube's comments sheet 565–1245 ms, vgpu.sh's controls 400–720 ms. The renderer is CPU-bound there (about 92% busy at 450–550M guest instr/s, V8, Blink and Skia with video or WebGPU running), so Chrome misses the 150 ms target. Open: the browser process raises SIGTRAP (a CHECK at libchrome+0x3e12d34) when the first navigation starts soon after the renderer appears: 1 in 10 launches on the starting commit, about 7 in 16 with the inline window path, none in 3 when the navigation waits 30 s. Its condition is not yet identified.
- **After the region work** (2026-10-27, history, "Region code density"). vgpu.sh renders completely; its menu answers in 378–415 ms after a run's first tap (renderer 610M instr/s). YouTube's watch page plays Big Buck Bunny at 640×360 in real time (media time 10.0 s per 10 s; 65–233 frames dropped while warming, then about 3 per 10 s). Chrome still misses the 150 ms target: its renderer stays CPU-bound, and two of its threads share the one JS thread with the main one.

### Google Maps

- **Map, search, places and directions** (2026-10-15). Cold and warm launches draw the Filament vector map, POIs and the puck; typed search, results with icons, ratings and photos, place sheets and driving routes with alternatives work. In a fresh renderer the first frame shows about 10 s after the page opens, cold or warm, and the map with labels and POIs 9-15 s after the sign-in screen's Skip. Launches of 60-100 s were measured in a renderer that still held earlier pages (see "A reloaded page stayed in memory").
- **Native startup** (2026-10-15). The library loader died of SIGSEGV on JNI `NewString(NULL, 0)`: a zero-length host access checked its null address. Filament's thread calls `APerformanceHint_getManager` after an API-33 check (the weak import was NULL), `eglCreateSyncKHR` because the display advertised EGL_KHR_fence_sync, and `perform(NATIVE_WINDOW_GET_NEXT_FRAME_ID)` through `<system/window.h>`'s function pointers, which read the bare handle as a struct; ANativeWindow is now that struct. Images decode through AImageDecoder. Cronet reads native writes through a direct buffer's `array()`, which ART backs with a `byte[]`; here it answers a copy of the guest block made at the call and `hasArray()` stays false.
- **No features on warm starts** (2026-10-15). `glMapBufferRange` without an invalidate bit handed back uninitialized memory, so Filament's per-frame MaterialParams updates wiped the rest of the uniform block. A buffer mapped that way keeps a host mirror of the bytes host writes leave, dropped when GL writes the buffer itself.
- **JNI on the wrong thread** (2026-10-15). After a generator native's first wait `sched.running` still named its strand once the resuming reaction ended, so a native thread's later JNI call took that thread's monitors and environment. A night-mode or locale change also posted work into `system_server`'s realm, which hosts no app.
- **Typing in search** (2026-10-15). Fast typing gave "coffee shopsee s". `IInputMethodSessionInvoker` posts calls to a non-proxy session through its binder-emulation thread with the arguments unparceled, and TextView reuses one ExtractedText, so the keyboard read a report after the next edit rewrote it. The client now gets an `IInputMethodSession.Stub.Proxy`, parceled at the call as for an input method in its own process. The field also calls `acceptStylusHandwritingDelegationAsync`, answered false.
- **Bitmap shaders in display lists** (2026-10-15). Elements drawables paint bitmaps through a BitmapShader whose fill covers what the target shows; recorded against the 1×1 mirror it covered one pixel, so icons, stars and photos were missing. The fill now runs at replay.
- **Magenta puck** (2026-10-15, `584cca26`). The puck's IBL cubemap held NaN and huge values. Maps ships it as PNGs whose RGBA bytes pack R11G11B10F texels, tagged with a gAMA of 1.0; AImageDecoder decodes in the image's own color space, but the browser's default conversion applied the gamma. AImageDecoder now decodes without conversion. A whole, unscaled decode that must stay unpremultiplied (AImageDecoder, `inPremultiplied=false`, `setUnpremultipliedRequired`) takes WebCodecs' frame, since a canvas keeps translucent pixels premultiplied; the canvas remains the fallback.
- **Lost class loader** (2026-10-15, `e1ff7336`). A Directions tap from a place sheet crashed with `BadParcelableException` (`ClassNotFoundException: awvd`) from `ObjectInputStream.resolveClass`. The frame walk was sound: `getClosestUserClassLoader` returned `Loader.object`, which the app's default namespace never holds (`loaders.ts` binds the PathClassLoader as `appLoader`), and now answers `objectOfLoader` through the bridge (`745050ac`, as bytecode-wasm's `751a2f53` does). Returned raw, that Java reference failed the native's return conversion, and every Maps launch that read saved state through `ObjectInputStream` died. The javaStack-segment hypothesis was wrong. The same stack traces showed a `DeviceProductInfo$1.createFromParcel` bridge frame (line 225) between `readParcelableInternal` and the app creator `alnn.createFromParcel`. Dispatch itself was sound: `alnn`'s IMT entry and vtable index 11 both hold its own slot (530896 + 1), and `DeviceProductInfo$1` keeps its platform block (143244). The platform build compiles that call as an unguarded IMT `call_indirect`, and the app build's patch of the module holds no Parcel code. The bridge is function 381 of the caller's own module, with the same type, so it could be a target of V8's speculative `call_indirect` inlining. A Chrome 151 test of that shape (a site warmed on a same-module bridge whose own `call_indirect` takes the receiver's slot, then called with another instance's function) named both stacks correctly, as did the earlier test of an inlined guarded call. The trace path no longer throws, so this mislabel was not reproduced; the method it reached was the right one. FitNotes' stacks show the same kind of ghost frame (an `AnimationUtils.lockAnimationClock` frame where `Choreographer.doFrame` calls through `CallbackRecord.run`), and with Chromium's `--js-flags=--no-wasm-inlining` they come out right, so V8 names frames of inlined `call_indirect` targets wrongly. Anything that reads Java frames from a JS stack (`getStackTrace`, caller-sensitive lookups) can see the wrong method there.
- **Sampler parameters** (2026-10-15, `080a4b49`). The texture-swizzle rewrite left samples of sampler parameters unswizzled (`stylePropertiesTexture`, `screenGradientTex`, `ssaoTex`). Each sampler parameter now gets an `int` map parameter after it, and each call passes the map of the sampler it hands over. Parameter types are read by position, since `samplerIndex` is a name and not a type. Array parameters and overloads that place samplers differently are reported, not rewritten.
- **Crash card label** (2026-10-15, `61869046`). The shell and the uninstall prompt resolve a reference label through the package's resources (`PackageRecord.label()`). Before this, the crash card showed the label's resource id.
- **Google Sans fonts** (2026-10-15). No change. microG's FontsProvider answers one row (file 1337, weight 400) with no `_id` column, so `FontsContract.getFontFromProvider`'s `getColumnIndexOrThrow("_id")` reports STATUS_UNEXPECTED_DATA_PROVIDED and nothing is cached; `openFile` serves Roboto. The first requests also exceed `getFontSync`'s 500 ms while microG's process starts (Worker boot about 1.4 s). A device with this microG build behaves the same.
- **Active navigation** (2026-10-15, `effb589f`). Start from a place sheet or route preview killed the process: navigation registers a `GnssMeasurementsCallback`, and the hosted `ILocationManager` had no `addGnssMeasurementsListener` (AbstractMethodError). GNSS measurement, navigation-message and antenna-info registrations are now accepted and never called, as LocationManagerService answers them without a GnssManagerService. Navigation then draws the maneuver banner, route, signals, 3D buildings and the puck, and ends cleanly. Double-tap, double-tap-and-drag and pinch zoom and two-finger rotation work (pinch since "Multi-pointer touch"); sign-in needs the account owner.
- **No start location** (2026-10-15, `8610ceba`). In a fresh run Directions opened on "Choose start location". A browser watch reports only when the position changes, so microG's provider, which subscribes after the last change and waits for a fresh fix, heard nothing. GNSS reports at 1 Hz while it runs; the held position is now re-reported as of now once a second passes without a new one.
- **Units held the main thread** (2026-10-15, `2431b137`). The compile worker validated each unit with a synchronous compile, and the page compiled the bytes again synchronously while taking the unit, so large units held a busy app's main thread for seconds. The client now compiles the bytes asynchronously before taking the unit; bytes that do not compile fail the job.
- **uiTree overflowed the stack** (2026-10-15, `5d301257`, `92c72323`). A provider node can list a real view as its child (`addChild(View)`, virtual id `HOST_VIEW_ID`): asking the provider for it returned the host node again. Finding that view by `findViewByAccessibilityIdTraversal` from the root then recursed too: Compose overrides the `@hide` method and calls View's through reflection, which dispatches back to the override. On a device the hidden-API policy denies the lookup and the override answers null; here no policy is enforced. uiautomator now finds the view in `AccessibilityNodeIdManager`, as AccessibilityInteractionController has since Q.
- **Street View** (2026-10-15, `841a1d00`, `6898f1bf`, `91116bd9`, `d7422886`). Filament renders Street View into a TextureView. Three gaps left it black. (1) Its streams take the Java SurfaceTexture through the NDK's `ASurfaceTexture`, which was missing. (2) Its HDR targets are RGB16F wherever `GL_EXT_color_buffer_half_float` is named. Chrome's WebGL 2 renders RGBA16F, R11F_G11F_B10F, RG16F, R16F and RGBA32F but not RGB16F or RGB32F (`FRAMEBUFFER_INCOMPLETE_ATTACHMENT`), even with both color-buffer extensions. The name now follows a one-time completeness probe. (3) Streams attach only where `GL_OES_EGL_image_external(_essl3)` is named. The emulation already provides them (external textures are 2D storage; `samplerExternalOES` compiles as `sampler2D`), so they are now advertised. The place label then stayed blank: Maps draws it with `lockHardwareCanvas` on a `Surface(SurfaceTexture)`, and a HardwareRenderer on such a surface found no buffer and dropped every frame. Canvas frames now draw into a buffer sized to the consumer's and post as the texture's next frame. Street View imagery, its label, rotation and the navigation arrows render, as does Immersive View's 3D scene.
- **ArrayStoreException in a place sheet** (2026-10-15). Once, opening a place sheet killed the process with an ArrayStoreException, and it recurred after a reload in that browser context. The context held an app build saved under an older platform key (`WEBDROID_AOT_STALE=1`), and the exception has not recurred in fresh contexts since. A `?units=0` run meant to rule out the second tier proved nothing: no such switch exists. Neither the stale build nor the second tier was isolated.

### Komikku and Mihon

- **Splash floor** (2026-10-04..11). `MainActivity` keeps the splash while `elapsed <= 500 || !ready && elapsed <= 5000`; only `handleIntentAction` sets `ready`, and a launcher `ACTION_MAIN` returns first, so a launcher start holds the splash 5 s from `onCreate`, on a device too (Mihon on the AVD: `am start -W` 5,428 ms). Both apps have it.
- **Mihon onboarding timing** (2026-10-01..11). The theme tap races two app timers (250 ms debounce vs ~250-300 ms collector drop), so a faster main thread loses about half the time; "next" requires the old page to leave the tree after its ~300 ms exit animation. Rejected: a CFS-weighted 4-core scheduler (20/36, cost 7% on Komikku).
- **Komikku SQLite log** (2026-10-04). Its bundled requery callback logs codes 28/284 at priority 6 (disassembly), so `E/SQLiteLog` is correct.
- **Komikku DataStore in a Worker** (2026-10-07). `NativeSharedCounter.nativeCreateSharedCounter` failed with `Operation not supported on transport endpoint` (no remote shared-file mapping), crashing startup with empty missing-API telemetry. The native MAP_SHARED leases (`mappingAcquire`/`Read`/`Write`) carry it; a page-side `SharedFile` page-mapping path that nothing requested was removed on 2026-10-14.
- **Komikku one-off `Proxy(JThrowable)`** (2026-10-08). One launch aborted before its first checkpoint; logs hid the cause and repeats did not reproduce it.

### Lichess

- **Black despite a rendering label** (2026-10-08..12). The Wasm flow drew black (Flutter `SharedPreferencesAsyncApi` channel-error) while smoke labelled it 15/15 rendering; after loader and unbox fixes it completes 15 actions (280 GL frames, moves, Stockfish replies). Its reference shows a notification-permission request the emulator does not.

### NewPipe

- **Playing on the Wasm tier** (2026-10-15). NewPipe 0.29.1 (ExoPlayer 2.19.1, Coil 3) searches, plays Big Buck Bunny at content rate (the position advanced 20 s in 20 s of wall time, 720p over the async MediaCodec adapter), keeps playing across the fullscreen rotation, switches to the background and popup players, opens a channel, subscribes, refreshes What's New, downloads an m4a into a picked folder and shows its Settings screens. The fixes:
  - `MainActivity` died in `Parcel.readException` from `IAppTask.getTaskInfo`: `TaskInfo.writeToParcel` writes `token` unchecked and the task's info had none. It carries the task's window token.
  - Coil's disk-cached thumbnails drew blank. The decoders took an fd's bytes with `readRest`, which is null for a descriptor the page owns (a file under `/data/data` opened in an app Worker), so they decoded no bytes; they read through the process (`processFdBytes`).
  - `ObjectInputStream` (the play queue in an Intent extra) threw ClassNotFoundException: the closest user class loader was null for a class of the app's default namespace, whose loader has no object of its own. It is the app's loader object, returned as a host value.
  - The popup player asked for "display over other apps" on every tap: special access was held only below targetSdk 23 or by a system package. `ACTION_MANAGE_OVERLAY_PERMISSION`, which since Android 11 opens the app list rather than the package an intent names, grants it to the app that opened it by the policy runtime requests follow. Since special access now reads the grant store, a `requestPermissions` request records only runtime permissions, as GrantPermissionsActivity answers any other name with its current state; recording every name let an app grant itself SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW. The synchronous `holdsPlatformPermission` answers special access held at install only: an app Worker reaches the store through the broker.
- **Not webdroid** (2026-10-15, on the reference AVD). A live stream's DASH segments sometimes answer HTTP 403 (`Source error`) on the AVD as well; another run of the same stream played in webdroid. `ResourcesCompat`'s "Failed to inflate ColorStateList" warnings for `ShapeableImageView` and What's New staying empty after a subscription until refreshed also occur on the AVD. Headless Chromium shows no directory picker, so a headless download needs `showDirectoryPicker` stubbed with an OPFS directory.

### Pokémon TCG Pocket

The game reaches its title screen (Tap to Start) about 25 s after boot (2026-10-27; two minutes on 2026-10-26, on the ported driver). The terms, birth date and player name, the 3.2 GB asset download, the opening movie and pack opening were reached on the pre-port branch (`3c91a7b7`). They were not run again after the port.
- **Time between screens is guest CPU** (2026-10-26). After Tap to Start, about 25 s pass before the next request. Over 30 s the main thread and IL2CPP threadpool workers stay runnable, and slice-boundary samples spread flat over IL2CPP managed code, its metadata runtime and libc mutexes, with no hotspot. There are 35,613 JIT regions with no evictions and synchronous compiles, about 70M guest instructions/s. This is A64 JIT throughput, not one stall.
- **Faster screens and uploads of what changed** (2026-10-27). With the JIT changes under CPU cores and JIT, the title screen comes 25 s after boot (sign-in cancelled at 4 s) and runs at 59 frames/s (209M guest instr/s); the next screen comes 13.7–18.3 s after Tap to Start (noisy across builds), and the country and birth-date screen runs at 54 frames/s (239M instr/s). Its Month dropdown opens and closes 88–103 ms after the tap's end (six taps). A submit had written every host-visible buffer it used whole, and the game binds a 16 MB and a 2 MB ring each frame: about 296 MB/s through `writeBuffer` for a static screen (`ef79c8a3`). Host-visible memory is now whole pages carrying `PAGE_WATCH`, which the first store clears whether it comes from the guest, the core's memcpy or the host, since all reach `code_write`. A submit gives a cleared page a new version, sets the flag again and uploads the runs of each buffer's pages whose version that buffer has not seen, so buffers aliasing one memory each take a page's changes: about 5 MB/s.
- **A split's dex and libraries from its entries** (2026-10-26, `95943b11`, `83a956c3`). Each launch read the 555 MB asset split whole twice more in the app process: `DexFile.openDexFileNative` reads every split on the class path (PathClassLoader lists hasCode=false splits too), and `Runtime.nativeLoad` searches each `split.apk!/lib/<abi>`, both indexing the whole archive. Both now read the central directory and the entries they need, through the zip module's `entryBytesOf`. The 191 MB library split is still read whole in the app process, since the class path serves only resident entries, and Unity maps that split whole twice (MAP_PRIVATE, read-only), each mapping an eager copy.
- **The asset download** (2026-10-26). After the terms, birth date and player name, the game downloads 3.2 GB. It first scans its cache directories, which never finished while `getdents64` could return its fd (parked system calls above). The download then ran at about 175 KB/s on OpenSSL's table-driven AES and GHASH (cryptography extensions above), then at 139 KB/s on the page kernel's latency (short slices above), and at about 900 KB/s after both, roughly an hour. The intermittent "archive ... is corrupted" crash at Loading.PreloadM was the same parked result.

### Reddit

- **One Tap sheet** (2026-10-02..04). The welcome screen pauses for microG's One Tap activity, shown 0.6-3.3 s later by another process; taps meanwhile reach the paused screen, as on a device, so the flow is timing-sensitive.

### Resy

- **View ids and visibility** (2026-10-02). UI-dump `resId`s were empty: View sets ids only while AttachInfo carries `FLAG_SERVICE_REQUESTS_REPORT_VIEW_IDS`, which `dumpWindows` now sets. An alert raised after the launcher stopped stayed shown until the window manager kept per-token client visibility (`dispatchAppVisibility`).
- **Announcement sheet** (2026-10-03). ResyListActivity re-shows it on every venue update and the API repeated one bookmark, so paging re-showed it ten times; the flow taps until it stays away 2 s.
- **Search input** (2026-10-08). One run kept the search hint with no keyword request: the fast input helper waits for an editable target only under a controlled clock, and the IME returns silently without a served editor. A readiness wait was not integrated: the editor was already present.

### RimWorld

- **Playable, several times slower than the AVD** (2026-10-26). RimWorld 1.6 (a Unity 2022.3 IL2CPP arm64 port by ports1de) boots, loads its definitions and the main menu, generates a world and a map, and plays with touch input. On the reference AVD (arm64, 1080×2160@480) the main menu came about 50 s after the mobile UI profile line and world generation took under 25 s. Here they took 5.2 min and 6–8 min, map generation 3–5 min, and colony play ran at 12–17 fps at normal speed and 7 fps at double speed, with Chrome's footprint at 11.5–12.3 GB (8.1–8.7 GB at the main menu). The whole process executes about 790 M guest instructions/s (263 G in 333 s of native time) on its Worker's one JS thread, where a device spreads Unity's job and loader threads over cores. While UnityMain presents a progress screen, the loader gets 150–330 M/s of that. Since lanes (2026-10-27, CPU cores and JIT) the loader runs on a core of its own and the menu comes about 10% sooner. UnityMain then bounds the menu: about 190 G instructions (270 s of compute), and about 18 s parked in some 220K file system calls. Of those, 75K `fstatat` and 27K `fstat` on its GameData under `/sdcard` were requests to the page's kernel until a Worker kept their answers (2026-10-27, Descriptors and shared memory); of its 79K reads, 40K are of its GameData through the page and 39K of its APK through the Worker's uncached Blob, mostly 4 KiB each. A native profile of definition loading is flat: a byte-table hash loop and an atomic bitmap OR lead with about 5% each, and no thread spins (YIELD and WFE are no-ops, and making them yield would gain nothing here). The port's own faults appear identically on the AVD: a Russian UI by default, an `InvalidCastException` overlay (`UIRoot_Entry` to `UIRoot_Play`) on the world map, and empty Save/Load dialogs.
- **JIT regions for IL2CPP code** (2026-10-28, CPU cores and JIT). Booted side by side with the commit before that work on a quiet M4 Pro, each from a copy of one browser profile, the menu's first-run dialog came at 296–306 s rather than 316–327 s (lane CPU 194 vs 209 s for the same ~175 G instructions; UnityMain's ~189 G still bound it) and world generation took 151 s rather than 170 s. At normal speed a fresh colony (random sites, so different maps) ran at 10.9 rather than 10.0 fps, its UnityMain at 227 vs 201 M instructions/s. Taps timed from pointerup to the frame whose inspect pane changed (five each) took a median 150 ms (128–224) to select a colonist by its portrait and 171 ms (112–227) to deselect by the gizmo, against 264 ms (134–318) and 262 ms (139–295). A tap on the map deselects only after 340–480 ms: the port's touch layer waits to tell a tap from a double tap. Reaching the colony takes manual steps: the first-run dialog returns on every launch, and the world map's buttons move once a site is chosen.

### Snapseed

- **Export from an app Worker** (2026-10-15, Wasm tier). Welcome, picking a photo, Tune, Crop (Square, Rotate), Details, Selective, Healing, undo/redo and export work with the hardware GPU. Export said "Could not save photo!": `systemProviderHolder` built a local MediaProvider stub in the app's Worker, whose `vfs.write` of the inserted row's file stayed Worker-local while `ParcelFileDescriptor.open` on `/sdcard` went to the page, which had neither the file nor its directory (ENOENT). A provider whose state lives in the page (`systemRealm`: MediaProvider's files and index) now resolves to system_server's binder over the broker; SettingsProvider stays local (its store is reached through the settings transport) and ExternalStorageProvider's picked handles persist in IndexedDB. One earlier run logged `SQLiteDatabaseLockedException` at `PRAGMA journal_mode` on `image_edit_local_db`; it did not recur, and sql.js connections have no busy-timeout emulation.
- **Looks needs GLES 3.1 compute** (2026-09-27; unchanged 2026-10-15: `request_gpu_info` gets GL_INVALID_ENUM from the ES 3.0 context, then `auto_correct_filter` fails a check). Editor, Tune, Crop, Details, Healing, Brush, undo/redo and export work (hardware GPU). Looks `_exit(1)`s: MediaPipe/LiteRT's ML Drift GL delegate needs ES 3.1 compute, and a real ES 3.0 device fails the same way.

### TikTok

- **The shared 4 GB cage** (2026-10-11..12). Process and compile Workers share the renderer's pointer-compression cage; TikTok's launch crashed it (`V8 javascript OOM`) at 4.1-4.4 GB committed, every realm holding a Program and World over its dexes. After `REFINE_SHARE` the e2e flow completes, but the cage still swings 3.7-4.2 GB. V8 sizes each isolate from its own allocation rate (compiling churns ~200 MB/s); allocation cuts in `lateBy`, vtable initializers and pool-stub bodies did not change the GC count.
- **Compile workers in a sandboxed frame** (2026-10-14). The crash recurred at ~105 s (cage 4.39 GB). Sampling profiles: the main process held ~1.34 GB live at 200 s (~300 MB Wasm instances/exports, ~240 MB Program/dex metadata, ~80 MB pool stubs); the app build's Worker ~330 MB live of 691 MB used. A sandboxed `srcdoc` frame (opaque origin) gets its own renderer and cage under site isolation: 4 Workers × 450 units in the page crashed, 2 in the page plus 2 in such a frame × 600 survived. Every process Worker's compile workers now start in one such frame (`host/compilehost.ts`); an opaque origin rejects a module Worker even from a blob, so a classic blob Worker `import()`s the module blob. Platform jars and builds are fetched through a port to the page's origin. TikTok's e2e flow rendered 13/13 in three runs with app-cage peaks 2.5-2.6 GB (compile host 1.9-2.1 GB); none exceeded 3.5 GB. Limits: if the host's renderer dies its workers vanish without an error, so the app keeps first-tier code and refines stop; a browser that keeps the frame in the page's process (Android Chrome, Safari) runs them in the shared cage as before (untested on WebKit).
- **Units lost across the frame** (2026-10-15, `8392a936`). A second-tier unit carried its `WebAssembly.Module`, which does not deserialize in another agent cluster. The process Worker therefore got a `messageerror` that nothing handled, and its client stayed busy after the first job. From the move into the frame until this fix, no app in site-isolated Chrome upgraded or merged a method. Google Maps launched with 27,811 first-tier units and 0 upgrades; sending the validated bytes, it adopted 562 of 566 jobs and merged 512. An unreadable message now retires the client.
- **Restored units cost Komikku's taps** (2026-10-15, open). With units adopted again, Komikku's cold `settings` tap took 100–127 ms over five `lat` runs against 65–98 ms at `cae91309`, which loses them. Both used one platform build, made from the same hot profile, on a host at load 7–13; `appearance` medians were about 155 and 125 ms. In that tap the branch compiled 2.5× the first-tier methods (175–224 against 80–93) in the same compile time and dropped a retire batch. One run with the client refusing every job matched the base (68 ms, 76 methods). The likely cost is the engine compiling merged functions on their first call in the tap, since they replaced code it had already compiled; holding waits out only the input window. Measure on an idle host before changing merge pacing. `lat`'s Mihon scenario fails at launch ("Rendered app launch unavailable") on both sides.
- **One build per app** (2026-10-14). Every process of a package built under the same key, each with a compile Worker holding a World over the app's dexes and replacing the others' build. A `webdroid:wasm-app:<key>` Web Lock now lets the first process to launch make and refine the build until its watch ends.
- **Rejected: slot-indexed pool stubs** (2026-10-14). Filling the table with `put(i, slot)` instead of exporting each stub saved ~5 MB of ~80 MB: V8's per-entry funcref cost is about that of an export. Superseded by one stub per signature (2026-10-16).
- **First launch** (2026-10-11..12). On-demand compiling took 26-29.6 s and native crypto/bignum 27.6 s. Before unit merging, 186 mark-compacts (130 for external memory, which reached 7.7 GB) took 27 s of 130 s.
- **Swipes and the first video page** (2026-10-02..05). Wedged swipes stalled 4-17 s on the first video page's cold code or `KevaFuture.getKeva` waits while guest threads took 47-85%.
- **Video** (2026-10-02..05). ByteVC HEVC decodes 71 frames in 776-785 ms with the native JIT vs 3,913 ms without; NEON software decode runs JIT-compiled (fallbacks 0.07%) at 0.15-0.7 G instr/s, 2-5 frames/s. Its player configures MediaCodec before extradata and sends length-prefixed samples until the NAL size is known.
- **Other gaps**. 27 missing classes are unshipped SDK classes probed by reflection; statx answers ENOSYS as a pre-4.11 kernel. Missing `HardwareRenderer.nDumpProfileInfo` is reached only from `LaunchCrashGuard`/`uncaughtException` after a stall, so no no-op delegate was added (2026-10-08). After ~40 installs from one host TikTok stops offering a guest session.
- **A new device for each experiment** (2026-10-16). `wd.reset()` reinstalls every app but keeps the SSAID (`localStorage["wd:settings:ssaid"]`), so TikTok answers the next launch with "Log in to TikTok". Dropping the key brings back the signup sheet, though 6 of 16 such launches still got the login wall.
- **Skip's lost first tap is the app's own activity** (2026-10-16). About 2.5 s after the signup sheet first draws, TikTok starts its translucent `AuthorizeActivity` above it, and that activity finishes 0.3–0.7 s later. A tap in between lands on the activity's full-screen window, which handles neither DOWN nor UP, so the sheet seems to ignore the tap; on Android a translucent activity on top takes the touch the same way. The start is TikTok's own: in a run that waited 8 s before tapping, the last UI dump ended 1.35 s before it, and the later tap worked. A cold UI dump takes 0.5–2.3 s, so a walk that taps as soon as its poll sees the sheet lands in that window. A walk that taps a gate still showing after 4 s gets through.
- **The branch against its base** (2026-10-16, `7dda4f2a` against `2f48474e`). Each side had its own server and a platform build made from the same hot profile. Each cold first visit started in a new browser context, alternating sides at host loads of 4–19 (one-minute averages, mostly the previous visit's own tail). Times are the page's clock from navigation. The table gives medians and ranges of the four visits per side that reached the feed; the heap is the main Worker's after a forced collection.

  | | base | branch |
  |---|---|---|
  | signup sheet | 37.4 s (35.3–38.2) | 37.6 s (35.9–39.6) |
  | last gate to For You | 45.3 s (28.9–58.7) | 17.1 s (13.8–19.4) |
  | For You feed | 95.1 s (77.9–108.9) | 66.9 s (63.9–73.9) |
  | heap at feed + 60 s | 1,358 MB (1,271–1,382) | 1,127 MB (1,089–1,176) |

  The main process's `__wdWasm.stats` at each point:

  | | base, signup | branch, signup | base, feed | branch, feed |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | methods | 77.4k (75.5–79.3k) | 77.7k (75.1–79.9k) | 106k (99–112k) | 98k (91–107k) |
  | compileMs | 11.6 s (11.4–12.1) | 12.0 s (11.7–12.6) | 20.6 s (19.9–21.9) | 15.9 s (14.9–17.4) |
  | moduleMs | 3.2 s (2.9–3.3) | 3.0 s (2.8–3.1) | 12.8 s (9.9–16.1) | 6.8 s (3.7–10.9) |
  | instantiateMs | 0.97 s (0.93–1.00) | 0.88 s (0.85–0.91) | 9.0 s (5.2–14.1) | 1.1 s (1.0–1.3) |
  | classMs | 2.6 s (2.5–2.7) | 2.4 s (2.3–2.6) | 5.8 s (5.2–6.5) | 2.9 s (2.8–3.1) |

  The feed gap comes from the base's merges. It retires merged-away first-tier units 32 per collection, so by feed + 60 s it had retired 3.0–3.9k units against 55–91k merged methods; the branch, which drains them as merges make them (`1dddb8cb`), had retired 24–47k against 43–86k. V8 charges each unit's module external memory, and in a traced minute after the feed the base's main Worker ran 199 full collections taking 25.4 s, 22 of the first 25 for external memory pressure. The branch ran 90 taking 7.0 s, 57 of them to finalize incremental marking and 30 for external memory. Those collections run inside module compiles and instantiation, and they fall in the stretch between the interests screen and the feed: the base's feed time rose with its instantiation before the feed (5.2, 7.1, 10.9 and 14.1 s in visits that reached it at 77.9, 92.7, 97.5 and 108.9 s).

  Signup is unchanged, though the branch compiles each method a little longer: 155–158 µs to signup against 149–153 µs. In node over 20,000 of TikTok's on-demand methods (three alternating runs), its compiler took 101–105 µs and 1,568 B a method against 96–98 µs and 1,488 B, with the difference in emit. That is the `rt.callee` store before every slot-table call that replaced the per-slot stubs (`cc4f5874`). In one matched `lat` run per side, Mihon's launch failed on both ("Rendered app launch unavailable"), as before, and the base also missed Komikku `appearance` at 158 ms (the branch: 140 ms). Launches were within 6% (Calculator M 3.1/2.9 s, FitNotes 2.4/2.3 s, Komikku 6.3/6.5 s), and TikTok's report launch took 11.5 s on both.

  Of the cold launches, 2 of 12 on the base and 3 of 14 on the branch got "Log in to TikTok" instead of the signup sheet. Two branch launches showed no gate for 400 s: a black TikTok window, with the main process idle long enough to compile ahead to its cap (8,192 methods) after about 71k methods. Neither stall kept its first minute of logs, so the cause is open; none of the base's 12 launches stalled. A first series was discarded because its base platform build lacked the hot profile (see *Worktree setup*). Feed playback under `--full` was not compared.
- **After the rebase onto Node** (2026-10-16, `68182929` against `794ded21`). Each side ran `e2e --full tiktok` from its own server and platform build, which had the same 1,103 code and 1,081 class modules, at host loads of 5–13 unless noted. The rebased branch, on Node and Vite+, reached the feed and played through both swipes in 4 of 4 runs. Its liveness probe (3 s, after each step settles) stalled after the interests step in 3 of them, answering after 3.3–7.7 s, and after the first swipe in 1.

  The pre-rebase commit, on Bun, ran 3 times. One run, at a load of 20–24, stalled at launch and then played through. One stalled after the first swipe. One drew two frames at launch and then stalled idle on a black window, the stall above. The morning's code, before the window-surface fix `146f0989`, ran 3 times too: 2 runs reached the feed, one with an interests stall, and the third stalled on the black window. In two alternating runs per side, each side stalled once. Every stall's hang showed the main thread runnable and no native thread running.

  At the interests step, the two alternating rebased runs executed 2.9 and 3.9 billion native instructions (7.9 and 8.2 s), against 1.1 billion (3.9 s) in the pre-rebase run that reached it. On-demand compiling was similar (4.0–4.5 s). Live feed content differs from run to run. Comparing the interests step's stall rate and native time across the rebase needs more runs per side, and the cause of the native difference is not established.

### Tubi

- **Playing on the Wasm tier** (2026-10-15). Onboarding, home, search, title details, Live TV (EPG and a live stream), My Stuff and clear VOD playback with audio work. The fixes: `MediaPlayer.native_getMetadata` (the splash VideoView); its Rust HTTP client never saw a relayed non-blocking connect finish, as the relay marked the socket connected after waking it and the epoll watch re-armed on a later microtask, so every request waited out 30 s and details came without the playable video (now 5-6 s; the watch re-arms at once and the relay wakes a snapshot of its waiters); CHIP binds `[::]:5540` IPv6-only and then `0.0.0.0:5540`, which Linux allows; closing a bitmap's decoded ImageBitmap while a recorded display list still drew it ("image source is detached", the details page lost its nav bar); a landscape request relaunched the player about four times a second, as `CONFIG_WINDOW_CONFIGURATION` was not dropped from the relaunch decision; and a Worker's extrapolated audio clock stepped back at each page report, which ExoPlayer read as a 32-bit wrap. Pause, resume and ±10 s seek work inline and full-screen; an earlier "taps never show the controls" was the driver: its settle-after-tap commands take seconds on a playing video, and the controls hide after ~3 s, so a timed check sends its taps in one sequence inside the page. An ended Home preview's video layer, reparented to null, stayed drawn under the window through its relative-Z link and was kept alive with its last frame by the display's root set; a layer reparented to null now goes offscreen as `Layer::onRemovedFromCurrentState` takes it.
- **Clear streams play; Widevine blocked** (2026-09-27). ExoPlayer HLS plays clear VOD at content rate (24 fps, audio, seek; relay 12-13 MB/s). Many films and all inline previews are only `hlsv6_widevine_psshv0`, needing MediaDrm over EME/MSE, untestable in headless Chromium. `PulseView` invalidates every frame by design (~1.4 ms).

### Walmart and Yelp

- **Walmart on the Wasm tier** (2026-10-15). Onboarding by zip code, home, type-ahead, search results, a product page with video (ExoPlayer's cache needed `IStorageManager.allocateBytes`, whose absence aborted system_server), add to cart and the cart work without a harness seed: the location now resolves to San Francisco. microG's "Use your security key" sheet appears once, as androidx.credentials falls back to it without a `credential` service.
- **Walmart: its consent lock deadlocks a slow start** (2026-10-15). `glass.platform.consent.a.z()` (ensureInitialized), called off the main thread before setup finishes, holds the object's monitor while it waits for the setup coroutine, which then needs that monitor in `r0()`. Location, analytics and geofence components call `z()` 8-10 s into the process, while setup reaches `r0` at 10-13 s; on a device setup takes milliseconds. It formed in nearly every cold launch under four concurrent sessions, and then the cart tap and the precise-location consent hang. SharedPreferences load waits were all under 115 ms, so it was not traced to one slow path; ~20 coroutine threads also queued briefly on one SQLCipher connection-pool lock (`hearting.db`). At a host load of ~4 a cold launch reached home by zip and opened the cart (one run). Sparky does nothing: it is an on-demand feature split the XAPK does not ship, and microG's companion rejects `SplitInstallService.startInstall` ("service is disabled").
- **Walmart: server-side geo** (2026-09-27). Location GraphQL uses the egress IP (Canada) and ignores device coordinates, so search returns 0 items without a US egress; the harness seeds a US store. Animated screens were GPU-bound on SwiftShader (`CrGpuMain` ~95%).
- **Yelp on the Wasm tier** (2026-10-15). Onboarding (location, microG's Add Account sheet in `gms:ui`, Skip), search suggestions, a category search with the MapLibre map, a business page and every bottom tab work. The fixes, each a crash: Kotlin `isSealed` (`Class.getPermittedSubclassesFromAnnotation`) and `Method.getParameters` (`Executable.getParameters0`, from `MethodParameters`); `Context.startActivities` (each intent resolved, then started in order); `IWindowManager.setInTouchMode` (touch mode is kept per realm); `System.load` of a library microG's MultiArchLoader copied into the app's cache, which the app Worker must read through the page; Adjust's `libsigner.so` reaching bionic's `timer_create` (syscall 107: bionic's SIGEV_THREAD helper waits in `rt_sigtimedwait`, running the callback on SI_TIMER and exiting on SI_TKILL, so the siginfo must say which); and a Cleaner whose `FileChannelImpl.unmap0` suspended on the FinalizationRegistry callback's root strand, failed, and made Cleaner call `System.exit(1)` (Tubi hit the same). Cleaners now run on a strand of their own. The UI dump overflowed the stack: `addChild(View)` children carry HOST_VIEW_ID as their virtual id, so the walk re-entered the host; real views are found through `AccessibilityNodeIdManager`. Compose overrides the hidden `findViewByAccessibilityIdTraversal` and calls it reflectively, which recurses here because hidden APIs are not restricted.
- **Yelp: home timeout was a priority inversion** (2026-10-16). The first home load's two `GET /home/initial_content/v2` calls were answered on the wire within 0.6 s (HTTP/2 streams closed both ways), yet both Singles timed out at 10 s with nothing compiling in between. Both OkHttp threads were BLOCKED on a monitor that "Bunsen Background Dispatcher" held through a JNA `BunsenInterfacer.bunsen_update`, about 1–2 G ARM instructions. `cpu.ts` gives background native threads a quarter share while frames present, and the home shimmer kept them presenting, so the update took over 11 s. A native thread that another thread is blocked on (a lock it holds or a class it initializes) is now exempt from the share; three runs out of three loaded the home feed. The earlier hypotheses (address lookup, host load) were downstream: the geocoder answers in under 1 s.
- **Yelp: first-launch home times out** (2026-10-15). The first home load can show "Can't connect to the internet" (`TimeoutException` after 10 s) and Refresh loads it. In one cold run the address lookup answered just after the 10 s; in another the main thread skipped 4,900 frames and `RootSingleActivity.performCreate` took 16.4 s under a load average of ~23 (four concurrent sessions). Nominatim answers in under 1 s; no missing API was found.
- **Yelp: search works; fixes go stale** (2026-09-27). Yelp rejects fixes older than 15 min and a stationary browser's `watchPosition` never re-reports, so the harness re-sets geolocation. Adjust's `nSign` second call spins >70 G instructions (cause unknown). DataDome is present. `Geocoder` has no backend.

### YouTube

- **Playing on the Wasm tier** (2026-10-15). After the empty-access, line-break, codec-buffer and recording fixes, the quick flow reached all 16 steps and a full capture played Big Buck Bunny and swiped Shorts with thumbnails drawn. The full lane's video-open tap is a coordinate (540,1350), which opened a Short placed first in the live results, so the player-control steps found no controls: live content, not a fault.
- **A stale long named a slot's next occupant** (2026-10-15). In about 1 of 9 walks the fullscreen step killed the app on the main thread, in the player-overlay pipeline `gvt.e` → `nvz` → `ViewStub.inflate`: twice a `TouchImageView` element's layout params lacked `layout_width` (`youtube_controls_fullscreen_button`, a literal 60dp; `youtube_controls_button_group_layout`, a dimen reference), and once `nuf.a` found no view `0x7f0b18d6` in what had been inflated. A handle's long was its slot number, and `claimHandle` ties an owned object to the first object of a class with `finalize()` that stores its long. A long kept after its object was released, or any long field holding a small number, therefore named whatever reused the slot: with generation-tagged instrumentation, a walk freed a live SurfaceTexture (its handle is stored from the host, so its own SurfaceTexture never claims it) when a holder of slot 372's previous long died. A slot freed under a live holder is reused, and the holder's next release frees the new occupant; a parse state that shared its slot with another parser's would read that parser's elements, which fits both crashes. That last link is inferred: no crash was caught with the chain logged, and instrumented runs crashed less often. Before this fix the same checks saw nothing for a native array copy stored back over newer Java writes, `applyStyle` given a non-array target or no node, `layout_width` resolving to null inside `applyStyle`, a parse state released other than by `nativeDestroyParseState`, or a Java array's handle released. A long now carries its slot's generation (1–127 above 24 slot bits, below 2^31), which each release advances, so a stale or plain long derefs, releases and claims nothing. After the fix 22 walks had no crash; 20 reached the player and passed fullscreen, and 2 lost the live search results. The SurfaceTexture's handle was never claimed, since only compiled stores claimed; see *A native's own store claims*.
- **Heap budget** (2026-10-08). The ConstraintLayout OOM's 8 MiB is the periodic `ALLOC_WINDOW` check; the browser's real budget is 2 GiB heap growth, host compiler growth included. TikTok/YouTube built 174,326/176,096 Wasm instances; a YouTube heap sample retained 2.14 GB, 800 MB under `instantiate`.

## Deployment and tooling

### Production build

- **Pages needs its generated renderer** (2026-10-20). Branch previews failed resolving `webpage.gen.ts`: the Pages build command still ran only `bun install`, while the Node migration moved renderer bundling into the shared generator. `build:renderer` uses the same generator as dev and Worker builds, with `--pages` selecting only the WebView scripts. A clean checkout reproduced the missing import before generation and compiled Pages Functions afterward without native-core or sql.js outputs. Pages now relies on automatic dependency installation and invokes the renderer script when present, preserving the older production branch until merge.

- **Import cycles break only in production**. Chunking reorders modules under cycles, so a top-level `extends` hit the TDZ only in the production bundle. Remove top-level uses of cyclic imports (`tests/import-cycles.test.ts` rejects them) and verify shell changes on `build:prod` under `wrangler dev`.
- **Gzip transport for WebKit** (`9e208dd2`). Bun's synthesized gzip filename/MIME made WebKit keep compressed bytes despite `Content-Encoding`; binary MIME with inline disposition decodes in both engines.
- **Deployment checks**. They skip arbitrary APKs and the UDP path; the Worker/Pages split and its same-site reason are in `docs/deployment.md`.
- **The page on Pages, the backend on a Worker** (2026-10-23). The page and its static assets moved to a Pages project; the Worker keeps the proxy, DNS, TCP relay and bridge and became the renderer's origin, which retired the renderer Pages project and its preview forwarding. The page's backend routes stay same-origin through a Pages Function (a service binding in production), since a cross-origin `/proxy?url=…` would need a CORS preflight per URL. Pages' static serving also ignores `Range` (a ranged request to a Cloudflare static site answered 200), so platform files still pass through code. Traps found on the way: without a `404.html` Pages answers every unknown path with `index.html` and 200, and the optional `aot/current.json` fetch then failed the launch parsing HTML as JSON; `wrangler pages` reads the `wrangler.jsonc` of the package root, not of the directory it runs in, so the root config is the Pages project's and Worker commands name `worker/wrangler.jsonc`; a Pages project and a Worker of the same name made the local `BACKEND` binding resolve to the Function itself, which recursed until workerd ran out of heap; and importing `gzipHeaders` from `serve-routes.ts` pulled acorn and eslint-scope into the Function (343 kB, 42 kB without). Under local `wrangler dev` the TCP relay reports `ok` and then closes without data, as much reached directly as through Pages; the desktop app's browser pane refuses service workers from wrangler's servers that headless Chromium registers.
- **The shell before the emulator** (2026-10-23). The page's controls had waited for the whole runtime: the 860 kB bundle and the native core's fetch and compile, which its top-level await holds. `main.ts` now builds the shell and imports `emulator.ts` behind it; on a 20 Mbit/s, 40 ms link the local Pages build answered at 150 ms and had its runtime at 620 ms. The shell had shown an app running once its activity came to the front, while its first frame came 2 s (FitNotes) to 11 s (Reddit) later; the progress bar now runs to the first presented frame, and that stretch is 50-85% of a cold launch. Preloading CanvasKit before `mountPlatformData` cached the rejection of its font read, which the launch then awaited.
- **Compile workers without a core in production** (2026-10-23). The compile host's workers (`compilehost.ts`) run from blobs in a sandboxed frame's opaque origin, and the production core fetched `/native-core.wasm` by path, which such a realm cannot resolve; dev bundles the core, so only production builds with an AOT set (the only ones that spawn compile workers) failed, five compile workers per launch, and no app build ran or persisted. The host now lends its workers the core as a blob URL. On a local Pages build FitNotes' warm launch reached its first frame in 8.1-8.4 s (8.7-11.2 s before) and its cold launch was unchanged (10.8-12.9 s against 10.4-11.5 s). One of 16 fixed cold launches crashed the headless renderer and none of 16 before did, now that the compile workers do the work they do in dev; the unexplained renderer crashes above remain open.

### Shared host and worktrees

- **Shared-host hygiene**. Other sessions' servers and runs share the host: never `pkill -f vite` or `pkill -f e2e`; stop your own server by port (`lsof -ti tcp:<port> | xargs kill`). Restart the dev server after changing `server.ts` or vendor files (a stale server served 404s that failed flows). Never `git stash -u` while a server runs from the tree.
- **Worktree setup**. Agent worktrees start from `main`; reset them onto the working branch while clean. Symlink `apks`, `node_modules`, `vendor/aosp`, `vendor/microg` and `conform-cache`, and give each its own port. Check that a link target does not exist first: `ln -s` into an existing symlinked directory nests a link inside the real vendor directory (`vendor/aosp/aosp`, 2026-09-27). sql.js builds are cached per patch and script under `~/.cache/webdroid` (`vendor/sql.js/rebuild.sh`). A worktree that makes its own platform build needs the main checkout's untracked `vendor/wasm-aot/hot.json`. Without it, `pnpm aot` lays the build out differently, Calculator M's cold launch loaded 600 build modules instead of 339, and the worktrees are not comparable (2026-10-16).

### Reference AVD and gen-framework

- **Environment**. Use `JAVA_HOME=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk@21`; `scripts/android-sdk.ts` finds the SDK at `/opt/homebrew/share/android-commandlinetools`. Boot `webdroid_api35_ref` with `emulator -avd webdroid_api35_ref -no-window -no-snapshot -no-boot-anim -no-audio`.
- **What gen-framework pulls**. Boot jars (`BOOT_JAR_PATHS`), `/apex/com.android.{i18n,tzdata}/etc` into `vendor/aosp/apex/` (`PLATFORM_DATA`), Monet colors, OsConstants (`scripts/DumpOsConstants.java` under `app_process`) and the boot-image profile (`profman` as root). `--settings`, `--properties` and `--identity [url]` record only those parts.

### Node toolchain (2026-10-16)

- **Flags**. Node 24 strips types, so imports name `.ts`. `NODE_OPTIONS` rejects `--experimental-wasm-jspi` (and `v8.setFlagsFromString` does not install `WebAssembly.Suspending`), so package scripts start through `scripts/node`, which execs node with the flags and the preload, spawned children take them from `scripts/node.ts`, and Vitest from `test.execArgv`.
- **Dev server**. `server.ts` is a Vite plugin that serves `/` itself; `appType: "custom"` keeps Vite's HMR client and socket out of the page. `sql.js`, a CommonJS workspace package, needs `optimizeDeps.include`. A config bundled by Vite misses the preload's polyfills, hence `--configLoader native`.
- **Single-file bundles deadlocked**. Inlined dynamic imports wrap their modules in init functions, async under the core's top-level await, and Rolldown awaits a wrapper's imports one at a time: an import cycle entered after the first wait waited on itself, so process and compile workers and the browser test fixture never finished loading. `scripts/bundle.ts` starts each run of imports together and then awaits them all, as ESM evaluation does, and fails a bundle with an awaited wrapper in any other form. Instantiating the core lazily would keep the wrappers synchronous, but each of the page, the workers and about 50 script and test entries would then have to await the instance before native code runs.
- **Vitest**. Its Vite module runner rewrote `import()` inside functions tests pass to `page.evaluate`; `experimental.viteModuleRunner: false` imports the sources natively, which also caught an extensionless `node-forge` deep import. Vitest's `toEqual` compares a Java array's `$t` and `toMatchObject` reads only own properties, which a Wasm throwable's facade lacks.
- **Production build**. `vp build` sets `NODE_ENV=production` in its own process, where the runtime would fetch its core as a page does; scripts that load the runtime run as children without it (`runNode`).
- **Bun replacements**. `serve()` binds `127.0.0.1` because `localhost` binds only `::1` on macOS. Static files answer ranges, which `RangeFile` reads as 206. `html-rewriter-wasm` stands in for Bun's `HTMLRewriter`; its chunks view Wasm memory and must be copied before the next write. Killing `vp dev` leaves its Vite child listening, so stop servers by port.
- **`wrangler dev` ignores Range**. Its local asset store answers every asset 200 with the whole body, and `RangeFile.range` accepts only 206, so a production build under `pnpm worker:dev` stops at its first chunked font read. `2f48474e` has the same Wrangler and Worker; deployed Cloudflare was not checked.
- **JavaScriptCore fallbacks removed**. Bun was the only host that ran the tier without JSPI or V8's stack format; a strand cannot start without `WebAssembly.promising`, so the non-JSPI link and block paths, WebKit frame lines, the lexical-name call marker and the frame-name probe served nothing, and a missing JSPI now fails with its own message.
- **Vite-built process Worker (rejected)**. Importing `process-worker.ts?worker&url` lets Vite serve the Worker as modules in dev and build it in production. Interleaved `pnpm lat` in dev (3 runs each, load 6-8) showed no difference: launch to the rendered app took 5900-6750 ms with the spike against 5834-6908 without (CalculatorM), 2957-3053 against 2919-4185 (FitNotes) and 6892-7378 against 6781-8480 (Komikku), with taps in the same ranges and the same gate failures. The compile worker must stay one script (its sandboxed host runs it from a blob, which cannot import chunks), so the spike added a second way to build workers beside `bundle()` and was dropped.
- **Mihon's ICU initialization under concurrent launches**. Four Mihon launches at once from one dev server ended its process during Firebase's start: `ExceptionInInitializerError` from a `NullPointerException` in `android.icu.impl.CalendarUtil.getCalendarType` (or `DateFormatSymbols.getExtendedInstance`). The same four launches killed 8 of 8 processes after the review cleanup and 6 of 8 at its start `edb357ff8`; one launch at a time, 0 of 4. The quick gate at 5 jobs lost both Mihon flows to it, and at `--jobs 1`, with other checkouts loading the host, Mihon onboarding once. A background thread's in-process `getPackageInfo` or `getInstalledPackages` runs the package manager's generator natives (`pm.ts`). The Wasm tier's `drive` (`src/wasm/host/suspend.ts`) made the strand the running one after a wait and cleared it when that step returned, so a step after a value yielded at once built `SigningDetails` with no strand: `nativeOf` found none, and the X509 → `Calendar` → `CalendarPreferences` initialization it entered ran as the main thread. Class initialization lets its own thread re-enter one in progress, and the main Looper was inside `DateFormatSymbols` initialization, waiting for an ICU data page, so the background construction read `CalendarPreferences.INSTANCE` while it was still null; with the roles swapped, the Looper read the half-initialized class. Load lengthens the page waits and widens the overlap. `drive` now keeps the strand through every step after a wait and clears it when the native waits again or ends, as `Runtime.drive` does: on the same dev server, the same four launches killed 4 of 8 processes without the change and 0 of 28 with it (onboarding 2×4 and 2×6, app 2×4). `pnpm wasm threads` checks the case: a generator native on a Java thread waits, takes a value it yields at once and enters Java, which ran on another thread without the change. Back to back at a load of about 20, the 5-job quick gate reached 2 of 7 flows with the change and 1 of 7 without, sharing pid 1003's missed diagnostic reads, Flappy Bird's and Doodle Jump's wedged main loops and FitNotes' and Komikku's completion timeouts; Mihon onboarding passed in each of three runs with the change and died at launch without it. Async natives have the same exposure (next entry). Since 2026-10-20 the host driver is gone: `Runtime.drive` drives every generator native.
- **Natives that continue after a wait**. Code after an `await` or in a `.then` runs as the host's current thread, so a native that enters Java again after it waits does so through `after` (its doc says so now), which keeps the thread and call chain that called it, or as a generator native. A TypeScript AST scan of `src` for jentry calls, and module functions that make one, after an `await` or in a `.then` callback found 58 sites. All but the media ones are host turns that name their thread (`onMain`, `callOn`, the main loop, boot), test-only `canvasOver`, or calls that enter no Java. `MediaExtractor.open` made its codec data's direct buffers (`directReady`'s first `ByteBuffer.allocateDirect` and each `csd`) after its reads, as the main thread for a player thread's `setDataSource`, and `MediaMetadataRetriever` reached it after an `await`. `open` now reads without Java and makes them through `after` from its call; the retriever follows its MP3 check through `after`, and `readSampleData` continues through `after` instead of `.then`. Logging each Java entry that fell back to the host's thread across the seven quick flows found only host code that `Runtime.drive` steps, which set the thread, and, with `drive`'s fix reverted, the package manager's path above. `pnpm wasm threads` checks that `after` keeps a Java thread across a wait; an `await` in its place answered from another thread.
- **Doodle Jump under load**. With other checkouts' browsers holding the host at a load of 12-27, the quick gate missed Doodle Jump on pid 1003's diagnostic read and `05 back` completion, as `2f48474e` does (Gate state). Interleaved with that commit's Bun stack, the Node stack's `playing` and `back` steps ran long in 5 of 7 runs against 0 of 6, but crossed harnesses and servers mixed the result. On the title loop without the harness's readbacks, both servers drew about 30 frames a second at 0.6-0.7 ms of native time a frame, without page long tasks, and one baseline run stalled at 15 ms a frame. A stalled frame runs the same guest instructions and host calls as a healthy one while its slices take 33-54 ms: synchronous GL calls wait on SwiftShader's GPU process.

### Cleanup

- **Cleanup policy**. Home starts a fresh session because class definitions survive a failed boot. Changes without Android evidence (dialog teardown, unconditional layout replay) were withdrawn; splitting follows ownership, not file size.
