- **JS tier removal** (2026-10-13). The removal deleted the DEX-to-JS tier and its tools; `smoke`, `fwtest` and `jbench` run the Wasm sweeps, and `src/jvm/runtime.ts` stays as the native registry. Removing the cold interpreter (`7f6fc18f`) cut `bun run aot` from 49 to 21 s.

### Code-quality pass (2026-10-19)

- **Scope**. A structural review of the Wasm-tier branch against `knlnks/bytecode`, implemented on `knlnks/bytecode-wasm-quality` with the shell, scripts, compiler front end and fd/path work in parallel worktrees. World is now a composition of slots, planner, tiers, ahead, dispatch, class modules, stubs, helpers, stacks, symbolic sites, units, assumptions and namespaces; the compiler reaches it only through `LinkEnv`, and the host import record is typed by the compiler's `HOST_IMPORTS`. The front-end restructuring (one primitive table in `PRIMS`, value kinds in `infer.ts`, block leaders and try handlers computed once in `prepared.ts`) left every platform and FitNotes module byte-identical; `index.json` lists its intrinsics in `PRIMS` order, which `aot.ts` reads as a set.
- **Strand switching**. `Sched.become` and `idle` are the only writers of the strand globals, and one required `ThreadHooks` replaces the nullable hooks; the host's thread and strand ride in `host` slots instead of WeakMaps. Blocking waits went around `sched.suspending`, so they now count in `wasmSuspensions` and reset `nested`, as the counters' docs said they did.
- **Small behavior changes**. Synthetic first-tier units no longer count in `stats.methods`; tier clocks start when the World is built; `heapBase` is measured after the runtime module is instantiated; the page installs the World's `JavaHost` (entry, JNI, makers, boxes, field names) in one step once it exists.
- **Suspension markers**. A native that hands on an `after` or `awaited` result without being `driven` or async is wrapped in `waits()` where it is registered, replacing suspend.ts's list of about 90 ids. Comparing `suspends()` for all 3,959 natives before and after differed only where intended: Memory's pokes, Unsafe's puts, `setMemory` and `MappedByteBuffer.force0` became plain imports (a store to an external mapping now faults synchronously), and `Icu4cMetadata.getIcuVersion`, which shares ICU's body, now suspends. One fwtest run in three reported `Bitmap.nativeCreate` answering a Promise from a plain import: it takes an `int[]`, so it cannot be parked, and its Java entry is inside `javaBitmap`, out of the regex's sight. It and `nativeExtractAlpha` are marked. The body regex and `waiting.json` (703 ids, 343 of them host services) went on 2026-10-20, once the remaining array natives were marked (next section).
- **One model of a shared mapping**. Java's remote mappings had used a `LiveRegion` that made a round trip per access (no JNI direct address, deferred GL errors); every non-immutable remote mapping is now copied in through a lease, as native and local Java mappings were, and immutable files keep Region paging. Changes go back to the owner at msync, `force0`, munmap, a new mapping of the same region and a Binder stage of the descriptor. Known limit: a store made after a handoff is invisible to a process that already mapped the region until it maps again. A mutation test confirmed the stage flush is needed.
- **fd and path ownership**. Every operation on another realm's open file goes through its `RemoteDescriptor`, which fixed `Files.copy` and `sendfile` writing an empty placeholder and `fstat`/`openat`/`fdopendir` answering EBADF. Path operations and attribute changes go through one dispatch: renames follow fifos, `setLastModified`, `utimes` and `truncate(2)` reach the owner, native stat reports the file's real mtime, chmod and chown are real (mkdir still ignores its mode). A named pipe has one holder process; another gets ENXIO until it dies, since routing it through the owner would need remote poll readiness. Package path ownership is the Android 11+ rule (`packageOfPath`), which `authorizePath` now shares, so Android/obb is private too. SQLite natives declare their handle order and lifecycle at registration.
- **ARM32 register table (rejected)**. Replacing the JIT's dry pass with a write table would need a second raw-word decoder matching every emitter. Over 1,500 seeded programs (10,346 regions, ReleaseFast) the dry pass cost about 60 ms of 224-235 ms, about 27% of ARM32 compile time and none of run time; a table could be checked against the dry pass in ReleaseSafe if revisited.
- **Kept on purpose**. `mhInvoke` stays one import; cursor protocols have no suspension point (the lists live in Wasm locals); `lower/*` stays split by instruction family. `serveCalls` takes per-method `undelivered` and `transfer` hooks, which remote files use to give away their read buffers.
- **Gate in a fresh checkout**. `pnpm e2e` failed every flow on a 404 for `/aot/<key>/prefix.json`, which only `aot-profile` wrote; `aot-build` now writes a null `prefix.json` and an empty `warm.json` with each build. `boot-probe` and lat's Mihon scenario fail at `dbfc6dfe1` too, and gms's Worker diagnostic timeouts came and went with host load (14-28) on both trees. A Worker's CookieManager read and wrote a jar of its own that no WebView request saw, since only the page wired `cookieTransport`; it now reaches the broker's. The loaders suite's flag-only controls never ran in `wasm all`: the abstract-default, promotion-copies and memory ones pass and now run by default, and the hot-refresh one, which failed its first assertion at `dbfc6dfe1` too, was deleted.
- **FitNotes step timeouts**. Interleaved runs on the fd/path branch timed out a FitNotes step (custom-graph, a barbell curl, a save) in 3 of 33 runs against 0 of 32 at `dbfc6dfe1` (Fisher p ≈ 0.12), all in its first 13 runs. The app kept drawing about 25 frames a second through the 20 s limit where a save animates for about 4 s, with the same final UI tree and no diagnostics: the controlled-clock class, not attributed either way. Three gate runs after the merge reached 7/7 without one.
- **Gate at the end of the pass**. `wasm all`, fwtest (31/31), the FitNotes sweep and 1,171 unit tests pass; three `e2e --jobs 1` runs reached 7/7 at a load of about 6, every process reporting `ok` with no missing APIs, errors or required natives. lat's gated taps took 7-98 ms, and its Mihon launch failed as at `dbfc6dfe1`.

### Code-quality pass (2026-10-20)

- **Scope**. A review of `knlnks/bytecode-wasm` against `main` (2,636 commits) found one concept implemented through several mechanisms in each area. Ten workstreams fixed them in parallel worktrees from `c1354b8f7`, then two more (WebView and audio services, system_server hosting) from the merged tree. Each kept its area's suites green; the merged tree's gates are recorded at the end of this section.
- **One native registry, waits declared**. A native is registered once, by the module that owns its class, and a second registration throws (JNI `bind` is the one replacement); `hostnatives.ts` and graphics' `delegates.ts` are gone, their natives moved to their owners (2,503 JNI keys and their wait marks unchanged). A native is a Suspending import only when it is `driven`, async or marked `waits()`: the source regex, `waiting.json` and the `webdroid/` service prefix are gone, and the regex survives as a desccheck lint for array natives that look like they wait (0 found; 17 of the 67 marked array natives wait inside helpers it cannot see). Suspending imports went from 520 to 508 of 4,019 natives (22 service natives that never wait became plain; the `ImageDecoder.nCreate` family, `nativePollOnce`, `transactNative`, `nGetFillPath` and the service lookups were marked). A non-array native that waits undeclared now parks and is reported instead of being guessed Suspending.
- **One generator driver**. The host's async driver and `hostForm` are gone: `Runtime.drive` stays synchronous until a Promise, then continues with a plain `then` as the strand that entered it, which also restores the current thread. Two waits took 572–624 → 543–594 ns, a nested wait 880–901 → 834–898 ns; completing at once is unchanged at about 350 ns. Method mangling (`m$name$hash`, `Bridge.invoke`) is gone: the bridge calls Java by name and descriptor, and `sched.running`, which nothing read afterwards, went too.
- **JNI and lookup fixes**. JNI `NewObject(String, <init>(StringBuilder))` read the JS tier's `.s` and built an empty string; String's constructors now live with its methods in `jstring.ts` under `<init>` keys. One `assignable()` serves JNI `IsAssignableFrom`/`IsInstanceOf` and `Entry.iof`, which said `int[][]` and `String[]` were not `Object[]`. `GetFieldID` searches fields of the requested kind, as ART's `FindStaticField`/`FindInstanceField` do.
- **String bodies back to bytecode**. A String method with a body ran its bytecode unless `HOST_FIRST` named it, so 33 of `jstring.ts`'s 44 TS bodies were never bound; they are deleted. Of the live ones, `matches`, `replaceAll` and `replaceFirst` stay in TS (29–55× faster warm: `matches` 0.31 vs 16.26 ms, literal `replaceAll` 0.99 vs 54.33 ms) and are differentially tested against Pattern and Matcher; `getBytes` and `replace(char, char)` measured as fast or faster as bytecode and returned to it, which gives `getBytes` CharsetUtils' `?` for a lone surrogate.
- **Regex in one pass**. The translation to JS RegExp handles atomic groups, possessives, classes and escapes in one pass: luni-util's regex classes pass 123 of 127 (from 111), and 5,731 of 5,936 corpus patterns match the JDK. Lone braces, a mid-pattern `\G`, bare script names (`\p{IsLatin}`), `(?-i)` inside a case-insensitive pattern and escapes Java rejects now throw `PatternSyntaxException`; a scoped `(?i)` still applies to the whole pattern and capture reset follows JS. A compiled-pattern cache (up to 4,096) took compiling 1,000 corpus patterns from 6.2–6.5 to 2.45 ms.
- **Zip and zlib**. The zip reader and CRC-32/Adler-32 are one runtime-free module (`src/zip.ts`) for jzip, the shell, libz and scripts; a DexFile open of an archive it cannot read throws IOException, as ART does. ARM32 libz runs over pako on the z_stream's own buffers, for zlib's dictionaries, `Z_FULL_FLUSH`/`Z_PARTIAL_FLUSH`, windowBits 9–14 and `zError`'s messages, which the fflate version lacked: inflate costs the same (6 MB `.so` 24.3–24.8 vs 24.5–25.0 ms), native deflate at level 6 about twice as much (197–204 vs 102–110 ms for 6 MB, output 1.8% smaller). APK and asset loading inflated with fflate until *zlib in Wasm*.
- **Address width**. `Unsafe.addressSize` was an intrinsic that answered 8, so a 32-bit process's `readv`/`writev` read its 4-byte iovecs 8 wide; it is now a native answering the process's width. Memory's peeks and pokes and both Unsafe classes share one accessor table.
- **Open files and the kernel**. `fs.ts` went from 1,037 to 414 lines: each open file's kind is one `FileOps` (`fd.ts`) in its own module (files, `pipe.ts`, `eventfd.ts`, `inotify.ts`, epoll in `poll.ts`, sockets, `process-fd.ts`), and reads take `(target, at, n)` so a guest read writes into guest memory without a view: a 64-byte local `pread` takes 45–53 ns, against 62–91 ns with a view and a 35–38 ns copy floor. Kernel bodies return −errno and are wrapped once for the ARM32 HLE libc (one extra call per syscall, not measured). Raw `getpriority` answers 20 − nice, as bionic's wrapper expects. poll, epoll, socket blocking, `rt_sigtimedwait`, AAudio's `waitForStateChange` and ALooper wait on the guest clock through one `waitUntil` (epoll, ALooper and AAudio had used wall time), and a wake for an event nobody asked for no longer ends a wait early. `vfs.renameDir` keeps directories' times, and fts walks the owning realm's paths with real stat data.
- **Link layer**. A method slot's tier (stub, first, hot) and its pending and asked marks are bits in one `Uint8Array` that `Tiering` owns, with `invalidate` and the reset; slot objects keep one shape. A unit made elsewhere links through one `Units` path. The platform and app builds make their modules in one `link/aotbuild.ts`: the two copies' size estimates had drifted (methods without code counted in one, `methods.length` against `methodCount`). Every synthetic function is made from its name through one table of families (APP_VERSION 21 rebuilds saved app builds). Ten modules' per-World state is one `PerWorld`. The compile-worker protocol is `units`/`build`; `init`'s 13 optional fields, `refine` and `rollback` are gone. A namespace rebind now clears stale reflection signature ids.
- **Compiler front end**. A method's block graph is built once in `prepareMethod` and read by Flow, infer, facts and outline (`cfg.ts` deleted); depth planning counts calls in Flow's natural loops, and `Planner.leaf` uses an exact cycle test (435 more platform methods count as leaves, none lost). Instructions and invoke kinds are classified by opcode number (`opinfo.ts` merged into `opcodes.ts`, the name regexes gone). `DexFile` stays raw and each loader resolves through its own `LoaderTypes` view (`loadergraph.ts`). Every hand-made class is one `PlainClass`. Emitted platform and FitNotes code stayed byte-identical through each step. The planner now resolves and classifies calls as `MC.binding` does: over the platform and FitNotes (1,065,424 sites) it plans 8,725 `invoke-super` sites with their target, drops 61 that fail to link, and with an AOT build plans 1,288 statically bound virtual sites. An operand interface in place of `SymbolicMC`'s subclassing was rejected: it adds an indirection on the emitter's hottest path.
- **One paint bracket**. Every draw goes through `beginPaint`/`endPaint` in `canvas.ts`, so lines, points, text and bitmaps get the paint's blend, shadow, shader alpha and mask filter; a plain paint touches nothing (recorded `drawRect` 195 → 75 ns, raster 765 → 660 ns; raster SRC_IN 4,705 → 4,860 ns). A mode Canvas2D cannot bound (SRC, SRC_IN, DST_IN, SRC_OUT, DST_ATOP) reduces to a bounded one for an opaque or transparent colour and otherwise blends through the draw's coverage (`drawMask`). Clipping to the shape was rejected because Chrome's `clip()` is aliased. Against CanvasKit over 336 cases, direct draws went from 133,057 to 821 wrong pixels and recorded ones from 135,248 to 2,863 (2 cases worse: an aliased SRC_IN rect in a recording is anti-aliased, 61 → 93), text outside its ink from 379 to 0. Strokes follow Skia's device-pixel hairline with one `applyStroke`; linear REPEAT/MIRROR/DECAL gradients draw from a 1,024-texel strip (0 wrong pixels apart from exact seams; making one costs 10–17 µs against 0.7); radial gradients with those modes still clamp and are reported missing; `MAX_SIGMA` (135) caps the emboss blur too.
- **Package and permission state**. Requested permissions are parsed once, by `parseUsesPermission`'s rules (the sdk-23 tags, min/max SDK, required features), so `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE maxSdkVersion="28"` is not requested on API 35; split permissions are not added (that needs grant inheritance). `RUNTIME_PERMISSIONS` is framework-res's dangerous list (42 entries; 5 added and ADD_VOICEMAIL's real name). PackageManager's flags are declared once, which fixed `appwidget.ts`'s swapped `MATCH_DIRECT_BOOT_*`; `getMemoryInfo` answers the device profile's memory; binder's eight parcel helpers are four; localStorage state goes through `persisted<T>` (one-entry cache by stored text). The errno table stays apart from `OS_CONSTANTS`, which lacks ESOCKTNOSUPPORT, names some codes differently and sits above `native/` in the layering.
- **Redirects, touches and WebView requests**. Proxy redirects follow one `nextHop()`: the media player had followed any status with a Location and forwarded every app header, Authorization included, to other origins. Touches come from the advertised touchscreen (device 12, source 0x5002, precision 32768 ÷ display size); reusing the pointer objects took a two-pointer MotionEvent from 34–37 to 14–16 µs. A frame response Chromium would download (attachment, or a type Blink cannot show) reaches the app's `DownloadListener` and commits no document; the frame then shows an empty document, as reloading the previous page would loop on pages that download on load. A MediaStore index cache was rejected: `vfs.onWrite` misses adopted, mounted, volatile and other processes' writes.
- **Shell plumbing**. `ProcessHost.setLayer` shows, stacks and enables a process's canvas, WebView layer and IME, replacing the MutationObservers that read the canvas's DOM state. The dev server and the Worker answer their shared routes from `serve-routes.ts` (both now cap a TCP host at 253 characters). Each realm is driven through its WorkerControl (`wd.page`, `wd.control(x, y)`, `wd.foreground()`). The clock and WebView surface ports are call contracts with per-method transfer; the frame port stays a raw message (0.72–0.81 µs against 0.97–0.99 µs as a contract notification), and a contract round trip is 2.3–2.5 µs. Console capture was not unified: a Worker's `console.error` lines, `[logcat] E/` included, do not count in e2e `newErrors` while the page's do, and counting them would make every app's own `Log.e` an e2e error.
- **Tooling**. The `research/wasm` workspace package is gone apart from `platform-status.md` and its probes; `binaryen` is a root devDependency for watdump. lat's in-page driver is a bundled module (`scripts/lat/page.ts`; `run.ts` 754 → 312 lines). One `SUITES` table drives `pnpm wasm`, the JDK is found once (`scripts/java.ts`), scripts parse arguments through `scripts/args.ts` `cli()` (unknown options exit 2) and the AOSP location lives in `scripts/aosp.ts`. `nrun` faulted before any guest instruction since the JS tier's removal, because it loaded the app's dex without the boot class path; it now loads it as tests do.
- **Cookies, sinks and audio**. WebViews and CookieManager in every realm reach the broker's one cookie jar (`cookieNetwork`, `cookieDocument`, `cookieWatch`; jar changes reach watching processes as one `cookiesChanged` notice per microtask turn), and the third-party choice lives in each WebView's `WebSettings`. The page realm's web layer, media elements and audio context are gone; their default factories throw. audio, media_session and media_router join notification and mount in `SYSTEM_SERVICES`, hosted once in system_server, so focus, volumes, sessions and per-uid capture policy are the device's; players are tracked by piid with their owner, `isMusicActive` and MediaRouter's `isPlaybackActive` read started players, and `onCallerDeath` drops a dead process's players, focus and sessions. A oneway transact to system_server whose parcel carries no objects no longer waits for the broker. Limits: `AudioSystem.newAudioSessionId` stays per process (a synchronous JNI), AAudio streams report no players, SoundPool plays never count (PlaybackActivityMonitor ignores their states), controllers in other processes miss a dead owner's `onSessionDestroyed`, a WebView without a surface factory stays inert, and no tracked player's IPlayer binder is released (no cross-realm binder is). `wd.web` read the page realm, where no WebView lives; the probe reads the foreground Worker's.
- **One home for each service**. clipboard, alarm, location, account, content, jobscheduler and shortcut joined `SYSTEM_SERVICES`: system_server hosts them and app processes reach them by Binder, so a clip, an alarm's PendingIntent, a location request, an account change, a content observer and a job are the device's, and ContentService replaced the broker's settings fan-out. Services that hold a process's own state (activity, window, input_method), answer from the device profile, or read a store the broker owns stay per process. `hostService` replaced 67 register-and-host pairs; `connectivity.ts` and `power.ts` own the network, power, thermal and battery models `services.ts` held, and `service.ts` is gone. AMS/ATMS stubs are still combined in `services.ts`: registering them in `am.ts` failed with `Cannot access 'intentSenderMethods' before initialization`, an import cycle through activeservices, broadcastqueue and pendingintent. An unconnected transport throws RemoteException (`notConnected`) instead of answering locally, except where the page owns the state or a node harness has no broker (SQLite, whose MediaProvider swaps sessions under synchronous natives, web storage, exit records). The process Worker imports the broker's contract from `broker-contract.ts`, not `processes.ts`: the minified Worker bundle went from 5,219,040 to 4,862,282 B (gzip 1,753,418 → 1,613,279), and an RPC echo stayed at about 2.05 µs. Path operations and descriptors of another realm go through one `pathOwner()`. Limits: clipboard URIs are not granted to their readers, ContentService skips `checkContentProviderAccess`, jobs lose `isPersisted` on reload and have no BIND_JOB_SERVICE check, shortcuts are not persisted, and MediaStore flags are read once per process.
- **Review of the service move**. A geocode provider stayed bound after its process died, now that system_server keeps it for every app; a failed call drops the binding, as ServiceWatcher rebinds. An app process asked the broker for a system_server service's handle on every `hostedService`, two round trips per LocationManager read; the handle is now asked once. A job run's death recipient was never unlinked, so a periodic job piled them on its service's proxy. `registerContentObserver`, `unregisterContentObserver` and `notifyChange` are now two-way calls to system_server, as on a device; no gate app showed a cost, and no CursorLoader-heavy app was measured. `pnpm worker:check` failed on `/native-core.wasm`: the production build's AOT key imported `scripts/aosp.ts`, which reached the native core through `apex.ts`, `vfs.ts` and the fd modules; `aosp.ts` now imports no runtime module and hands callers `localPlatformData` for `mountPlatformData`.
- **Gate at the end of the pass** (`06f79ff3a`). Typecheck, 1,180 unit tests, all 16 `wasm all` suites, fwtest (31/31), desccheck (the two baseline mismatches), `build:prod` and the Worker dry run pass. On an unprofiled build (`pnpm aot` only) two `e2e --jobs 1` quick gates reached 7/7 in 160.4 and 160.0 s with no missing APIs, errors or required natives. A first gate on the merged tree had timed out FitNotes' `11 1-weight`; eight alternating `--test fitnotes` runs passed 4/4 on each side (39.4 against 38.9 s). Matched `lat` against `c1354b8f7` (two interleaved passes a side, both built by `pnpm aot` from the same `vendor/wasm-aot/hot.json`): cold first-frame medians 3.40 against 3.35 s (Calculator M), 2.95 against 2.95 s (FitNotes) and 6.63 against 6.78 s (Komikku), warm 1.38/1.39, 1.34/1.36 and 2.22/2.29 s; every gated tap passed 150 ms (slowest cold Komikku `appearance` 111 ms, baseline 103), and Mihon's launch was unavailable on both. Without the shared `hot.json` (which `aot-build` groups modules by) the baseline build had loaded 153 data and 35 code modules at process start against 118 and 7 and launches built 695 against 988 classes on demand, which first read as a regression. `wasmAotMs` reads 6–18 ms higher because `Units.recordHeader` moved each code module's held-method records (about 115,800 in the platform build) inside the timed region; platform headers carry no hierarchy decisions. Under concurrent test load two merged launches took 4 s longer (`wasmRetired` 8 against 2); a quiet rerun did not repeat it.

### Second code-quality pass (2026-10-20)

- EGL's single core refuses attributes outside EGL 1.5 table 3.4 with `EGL_BAD_ATTRIBUTE`, as the spec says. AndEngine's
  chooser (flappybird) asks for `EGL_SAMPLES` first and, finding no config, retries with `EGL_COVERAGE_BUFFERS_NV`, and
  any false return kills its GLThread. Devices answer the first query, so the table has multisampled configs (4 samples,
  sorted after the single-sample ones) whose contexts get an antialiased WebGL2 drawing buffer.
- A retrying native (`retries`) is called again through one `run` closure built inside the wrapper's try block: the
  call text refers to the block-scoped host values of object arguments, which a closure outside it could not see.

- **Scope**. A second review of `knlnks/bytecode-wasm` against `main` was implemented in 15 worktree lanes merged one at a time (binder, webview, resources, linker, shell, text-input, compiler, tooling, device, libcore, graphics, native, gl-media, VM runtime). The findings are recorded under their topics with this date. Not done: one broker accessor in place of the per-module transport slots, a registration record that makes an intrinsic shadowing a native impossible, the main thread's context loader as one realm-start step, reflection names passed as strings, one intrinsic registry, and the compiler's `vtOf`/`idOf`/`natives` pass-throughs (about 45 call sites for three one-line methods).
- **Tooling**. Tools boot Java headlessly through `scripts/wasm/lib.ts` (`platform()`/`world()` attach natives and host classes by default) and `scripts/tier.ts`. `scripts/page.ts` is the one way to open webdroid, and "up" means the foreground app process drew its first frame within a deadline. gen-framework runs `--only <recorders>` from one RECORDERS table. jbench compares with ART's recorded answers (`Bench.expected.json`), and BLOCKED fails. lat counts realms whose diagnostics did not arrive. `pnpm aot` writes empty `apps.json`/`prefix.json`, so bundled app builds need `aot-profile`.

### Rust TypeScript checker (2026-10-20)

- `pnpm typecheck` uses [tsrs](https://github.com/oxc-project/tsrs), pinned to the experimental
  `0.18.0-ts7.1.0-dev.20260929` release, for both configs. Generation still runs first; `noEmit`, strict checking
  and `noUnusedLocals` remain in the configs. TypeScript 5.9 stays as a dev dependency for the parser API used by
  `scripts/generators.ts` and the import-cycle tests. The newer DOM types require `randomBytes` to express the
  non-shared buffer type already guaranteed by its guard; this is an erased assertion, with no allocation added.
- Three interleaved runs of both configs on this checkout measured medians of 4.109 s with `tsc` 5.9.3 and 0.165 s
  with `tsrs` (about 25× faster; compiler commands only, excluding generation). `pnpm typecheck`, frozen-lockfile
  installation and all 1,187 unit tests in 65 files passed. An existing ignored `scripts/wasm/scratch-cbench.ts`
  fails under both compilers; it was temporarily set aside for the typecheck and timings, then restored unchanged.
  No runtime, framework or browser behavior changed, so the broader APK matrix was not rerun. The pinned tsrs
  package supplies native binaries for macOS ARM64 and Linux x64/ARM64, but not macOS x64 or Windows.

### Third code-quality pass (2026-10-25)

- **Scope**. A structural review of `knlnks/bytecode-wasm` against `main` was implemented from `48a85fb1a` in eight worktree lanes (host, scripts, graphics, native, services, compiler, link, shell), at most three at once, merged one at a time (325 files, +6,303 −6,452). Its blockers were the caller-asserted system identity, diverged duplicates (path syscalls, mmap, the package registry, blend modes, fdsan), slot state in about seven places, per-native metadata in about eight channels, and dead migration leftovers.
- **Host and natives**. Every registration is normalized to one `NativeImpl` descriptor that carries its wait and array marks; `mayWait`/`waitsAlways` and the per-native side tables are gone. The tier's host is one `JavaHost` object read at each use, compile Workers share one session and fetch helper, and the `?bytecode=` provenance cut, which no registration consulted, is removed (`TS_SPECS` stays). The reachability analysis moved to `scripts/`.
- **Native**. The ARM32 HLE libc is `src/native/libc32/`, owning its thread data through bionic's thread hooks; libcore's Linux syscalls are one module (`jvm/natives/linux.ts`) registered once; guest and libcore path operations answer through one fs core; `Os.mmap` and `FileChannel.map` go through the kernel's mmap; each ABI's struct offsets are in `KLAYOUT`; fdsan's tagged close owns the close it checks. `getcwd(2)` answers its length and `Os.stat` a real dev/ino.
- **Services**. Each realm reaches the broker through one `broker()` (`shell/broker-access.ts`) instead of per-module transport singletons. system_server's calling identity comes from the caller's pid in the broker, not from the caller's claim. Committed packages are installed through the broker into every process's registry. Each persisted store has its own prefix and a registered scope. The page runs its SQLite locally.
- **Graphics**. Each shape draws as one NativePath through `drawShape`; blend modes are SkBlendMode numbers through one composite table; shaders that need a clip fill it themselves; path ops are numeric tags in one flat list, with PathMeasure (`pathmeasure.ts`) and the CanvasKit bridge (`skia.ts`) apart. conicTo splits through scan.ts's one SkAutoConicToQuads. A DST layer draws nothing, and an arc of 360° or more draws as an oval, as Skia does.
- **Compiler and link**. One opcode family table, one invoke binding (`bindInvoke`), one value-kind table per descriptor letter, labels on Code's own block stack, and a method's String sites found once with its analysis. A slot's code and tier are one state in `SlotTable`; each code module has one record; reflection finds a declared member in one host call; upgrades and merges are out of `Tiering` (`upgrades.ts`, `merges.ts`); app-patch slots count as app-build code.
- **Shell**. Each realm's display loop and visibility live in `vsync.ts` (`startDisplayLoop`), so the page's pointer drain runs on every page frame. Frames, media and input (pointers, IME edits and IME state on one port) go over rpc, and frames go only to an observer. Each descriptor operation has its own broker method, and a process's surface ports are one table on each side.
- **Scripts**. `lat` drives scenarios with e2e actions and state conditions in the harnesses' Chrome, which now gets `--use-mock-keychain` (its Chrome had prompted for the macOS keychain). Flow selection is in one place, scoring runs on a worker thread instead of a `--json` rerun, browser tests open pages through one `withPage`, and AOT suites run over the current platform build.
- **Not done or partial**. The World split and the thread-identity merge are partial (the latter blocked by layering). Staged rights for every parcel were skipped, since Workers own their descriptors. Also skipped: clip trackers, moving the wasm suites to vitest, and replacing `SymbolicMC`. The conic verb is only partly carried through. One system pid, WeakSet binder marking and scoped descriptors everywhere were skipped, as was boot.ts's node path. `HOST_IMPORTS` and `RtName` are kept. boot-probe, g1 and app.browser are not on `withPage`.
- **One fdsan table** (follow-up). Each descriptor's owner tag lives in fd.ts beside the descriptor. The checks and the error level are in `fdsan.ts`, which libcore's natives, the reference bionic and the ARM32 libc share. Before, 64-bit bionic kept a table of its own in guest memory that libcore never saw, and the ARM32 libc had no fdsan entries. The reference `libc.so` calls its fdsan entries (all weak exports) through its PLT from `close`, stdio, dirent and `fork`, 18 sites, so interposing exchange, get-owner, close-with-tag and the error level covers its own uses. The ARM32 libc exports the whole API, and its `close` is a close with no owner, as bionic's is. The level starts at FATAL. The reference AVD (API 35) shows why: bionic's `__libc_init_fdsan` sets FATAL when `debug.fdsan` is unset, the zygote's `ForkCommon` restores the zygote's level in each child, and nothing else in an app process sets it (`app_process64`, libart and libnativeloader never call `set_error_level`). A fatal violation aborts the calling guest thread with the message as its abort message. With no guest caller, the process dies of SIGABRT, through the `abortProcess` that binder's fatal report now shares. Before, Java's violations only warned once. `pnpm check --corpus` passed in 204 s, with no fdsan report in any process. Limits: the ARM32 libc's FILE* and DIR* streams do not tag their descriptors, and bionic's fdtrack hook in `close_with_tag` no longer runs.
- **Gate**. `pnpm check` passed in 134 s, with content 2.94 (FitNotes), 21.57 (Mihon app), 10.95 (onboarding) and 0.65% (Calculator M). `--corpus` passed in 213 s. 2,551 unit tests passed. The native lane's corpus run caught a zlib ReferenceError on Amazon.xapk (libz's version string read before its table existed), fixed before the merge.
- **Latency**. Head's `lat` harness was run against both trees, alternating (n = 3 for Calculator M and FitNotes, 10 for Komikku). Every gated tap passed. Calculator M and FitNotes were neutral, Komikku's launch was 7.46 against 7.54 s, `more` 51 against 62.5 ms and `settings` 39 against 43 ms. `start` was 62 against 54.5 ms, while its main-thread busy time fell (42.3 against 44.8 ms). Timelines show the cause is vsync phase. The answer follows one 34 ms frame and a 3.5 ms frame. On the base a host animation frame landed inside the long frame, its vsync started the short frame, and an early vsync started the answer (43–46 ms). On head no host frame landed inside it, so an early vsync started the short frame. The answer then waited for the next real vsync, since an early frame needs the latest vsync to be a period old (56–61 ms). The vsync policy is unchanged.

### Chrome branch code-quality pass (2026-10-26)

- **Scope**. A structural review of `worktree-chrome` against `main`, applied in focused commits. Ashmem regions are their own file kind (`REGION`, fs.ts) answering ioctl(2), which a remote region forwards by value. The core's libc sends a scan with an argument in the window to the host's bodies (`scanWindow` is gone). ARM and Thumb exclusives share one load and one store. The window's pool views, page table and core imports are `window.ts`, and the faults the host raises are `faults.ts`. Each CPU-readable AHardwareBuffer format has its own RGBA codec.
- **Behavior**. A parceled Surface's export now ends when its queue is destroyed or its import's port closes. Its reader ports end when the queue goes, when either side closes or when the other process dies, and a reader drops an import whose owner died (MessagePort's `close` does not fire when a Worker is terminated, so death goes through the broker's process watch). A socket bridge keeps its port until the other side answers its close, and it lets go of rights that arrive after its socket ended, so a write already across no longer leaves its parked rights in the page's kernel. A right in flight to a process that dies still leaks. Deleting a texture name that stands for a buffer's texture no longer marks that texture deleted, so another image of the buffer binds. A CPU lock no longer reads back a format it cannot convert (RGBA16F and RGB10_A2 ran the RGB565 conversion).
- **Not done**. vsync's native choreographer requests stay apart from `Receiver`: one on the main looper would join the main thread's frame accounting. (`on_store32` and the leak of a right in flight to a dying process were settled by the next pass.)

### Fourth code-quality pass (2026-10-26)

- **Scope**. A second structural review of the Chrome work, applied in focused commits. Struct specs type their field names and take nested structs (`src/native/struct.ts`), and handles are one `Handles<T>` table (`handles.ts`) for Vulkan objects, hardware buffers, image readers and NDK media objects. The SPIR-V translator is split by stage under `vulkan/spirv/`, with WGSL naming free of side effects; its output was byte-identical on 138 captured translations. ASurfaceControls are references on SurfaceFlinger's layers rather than a second compositor (`composeTree` costs 0.28 ms per transaction apply). An AHardwareBuffer keeps one private storage behind methods, and its frame reaches Vulkan through `withFrame`. EGL syncs are one interface with GL and native-fence implementations, and EGLImages live in egl.ts. Hardware buffers, window posts and Bitmap's 565 share one RGBA codec table, and one clock gives monotonic nanoseconds, frame size and timestamp. `window.ts` owns window mappings, the pool and their protection, with one access check at each ARM32 access's width. The Thumb interpreter is `arm_thumb.zig`, and Thumb multiplies reach the interpreter and the JIT through one ARM translation (`thumbarm.zig`). NEON and VFP have their own files. Socket crossings are `FileOps.cross` with stage, take, park and claim RPCs. Service instance names are parsed in `server/process.ts`, a package's splits once, and the cross-origin isolation policy is in `serve-routes.ts`.
- **Behavior**. Bitmap's RGB565 widens by bit replication, as Skia does, where it had shifted. Production COOP/COEP headers cover only `/` and the Worker scripts. A socket bridge claims back and lets go the rights a write parked when the other realm ends before claiming them, which closes the in-flight leak the previous pass left. The host's forwarded unix connections close after 60 s without a poll. `on_store32` was deleted: a Chrome page load made 349k stores on that fast path, which saved about 5 ms against the fault path.
- **A regression on the way**. Sending all of the Thumb 0xfb block to the JIT took in the media multiplies (ARM's 0x07xxxxxx: SMMUL, SMLAD, USAD8 and similar). The JIT does not compile those, so it fell back on them with the ARM word's flag handling instead of a wide Thumb fallback's. Chrome's renderer died with a SIGTRAP at libchrome.so +0x3368938 about 77 s into a YouTube watch page, and vgpu.sh's renderer died before reaching WebGPU. The commit had already reached main in the Chrome squash (`9cad150ac`). A bisect found it, and `asArm` now leaves the media forms to the interpreter (`9c85105b8`). Big Buck Bunny then plays with the hardware AV1 decoder, stepping up from 426×240 to 640×360. vgpu.sh's prism animates at about 10.8 frames/s (canvas frames requested over 15 s, not taken under the performance protocol), with no missing `libvulkan.so` symbols. The JIT still has no emitter for the media multiplies.

## Removed designs

On 2026-10-13 the DEX-to-JavaScript tier and the Wasm cold interpreter/slow runtime with compiled resume and deoptimization guards (`7f6fc18f`) were deleted; on 2026-10-11 process iframes were replaced by one Worker per app process.

### DEX-to-JS tier

- **Suspend only when waiting**. Async-by-default methods suspended the whole stack on common operations; synchronous-unless-waiting took Mihon scrolling 27 → ~44 fps at 4× throttle.
- **Throw cost is stack capture** (2026-09-30..10-01). A throw caught one frame up cost 2.9 µs, 2.2 µs of it a 64-frame `new Error()`; nested suspension promises made each capture walk the chain (200 vs 14 µs). Measure capture separately and skip it where handlers discard the exception.
- **Per-frame overhead**. TS copies of libcore collections beat it 1.4–2.5× (~2 ns per frame; V8 would not inline suspension scaffolding), the origin of measured intrinsics. A non-inlined prologue thread lookup cost 2.4–3.0 ms per Mihon tap (once credited to thread priority). Back-edge fuel checks cost 12–18% of tight loops; moving or batching them gained nothing. Rejected: per-site inline caches (noise), proving calls synchronous (error paths defeat it), frame pooling (1.6–2.0× slower than allocation).
- **Engine frame retention**. JavaScriptCore elides tail-position frames and Bun keeps ~10, so one extra frame per call hid callers from stack-class lookup.

### Persisted code and compile Workers

- **Key stores by content** (2026-10-02). Bundles naming the code store by URL deleted each other's chunks, so warm launches ran cold (FitNotes 125k → 2.6k cold calls once keyed by a `src/` hash).
- **Keep frame identity** (2026-10-01). Persisted bodies misattributed frames, breaking caller-sensitive APIs; persisted code must keep frame-to-method identity.
- **Save late, in idle slices**. Periodic whole-script rewrites stalled YouTube. Save after a method's first call (not its compile), clear pending entries only after storage succeeds, and encode in idle slices (`fb29e91c`: a ~245 ms long task became ~150 ms of slices). Persisting every profiled body added 44.6 MB unused.
- **Drop unclaimed code** (2026-10-03). ES-module records keep every compiled function for the realm's life (+49–65 MB); dropping bodies unclaimed 30 s after the last claim took Mihon 669 → 559 MB. A budget by source length, not heap, ran out after Mihon's second tap.
- **Compile Worker pacing** (2026-10-07). Imports after first frame were too late; unthrottled ones finalized inside taps; three in flight, 250 ms after input, kept taps clear. A Worker must install its message listener before awaiting imports, or it loses the first request.

### Cold interpreter and deoptimization

- **No cheaper first tier** (2026-10-05..13). `?cold=1` never sustained an app flow: cold FitNotes failed launch after 1,026,058 cold calls and 76,130 slow modules; a cold Calculator made 2.8M slow calls without a frame. Feeder took 14.4 s vs 3.15 s compiled on demand, and it needed a second platform build. Review: `research/wasm/cold-slowops-review.md` in Git history.
- **Deopt capture nearly doubles code** (2026-10-05). Instrumenting every activation grew platform C modules 97.45%; capture at all 1,817,225 poll sites 82.67%, cut to 54.40% (~87% of the rest executable code). An epoch-only guard (`de483bf6`) saved bytes but was never CPU-compared; byte savings alone do not show a runtime gain.
- **Count, then time**. Sharing adapters by operand role cut a 75-site probe from 75 modules/86,902 B to 5/9,396 B; per-PC modules, whole-method typing and register copies were counted, never shown cheaper.
- **Yields need the host deadline**. Loops of immediately fulfilled async natives run only microtasks; yields must check the host turn deadline.
- **`MethodInfo` is mutable** (2026-10-05). Host overlays can clear `MethodInfo.codeOff`, so decoded-body caches key on DEX/code-offset identity; eager preparation must skip unreachable PCs, or dormant missing classes raise linkage errors early.

### Process iframes

- **Shared page thread** (2026-10-01..10). microG's 2.2–3.4 s boot landed inside Mihon `lat` taps (253–342 vs 60–62 ms), and Calculator tap variance (61–176 ms) tracked microG's start.
- **Keep zygote overlap** (2026-10-02). In an iframe FitNotes drew at 1735–1802 vs 1596–1611 ms: its zygote no longer overlapped the download.

- **Paired branch previews** (2026-10-20). Only Pages watched non-production branches, and Worker preview URLs were disabled. Worker Builds now runs `worker:preview` for branch builds; the script passes the branch explicitly so its isolated Durable Object namespace and renderer alias agree. Pages preview callbacks forward to that Worker's public endpoint, since a static Pages binding cannot select a namespace created for each branch. Production keeps its direct namespace binding. A deployed WebSocket/HTTP round trip across the two branch URLs returned the expected reply; the production deployment stayed at `3b2c5c9a`. Clean-checkout typechecking, Worker dry-run and Pages compilation passed. The build platform archive is pinned by SHA-256 `089ef6dccc01c7d9da8a08158a87fdcb89b66eefc64249778e1227f6d8aa0d91` in the `webdroid-builds` R2 bucket; a remote download reproduced that digest.

- **Static asset ranges at the edge** (2026-10-20). The first hosted APK smoke failed during font preloading: Cloudflare’s assets binding ignored `Range` and omitted `Content-Length`, so the browser’s `RangeFile` received 200 instead of 206. All platform paths and the certificate archive now run through the Worker. It uses the canonical platform/certificate size indexes, decodes normalized asset paths, and streams only the requested bytes. Split and ordinary assets share the stream slicer; split-asset HEAD requests no longer read their bodies. Remote font/certificate byte ranges and a framework.jar range crossing a 24 MiB part boundary matched the local sources; invalid ranges returned 416. The fix changes hosting, not APK or Android behavior.

## OCI backend migration (2026-10-28)

- **Deployment.** TypeScript/Pulumi provisions a VCN, internet gateway, firewall, subnet and one Always Free VM in the tenancy's home region, `us-sanjose-1`, plus the imported Cloudflare Pages project's backend variable. ARM creation returned `Out of host capacity`; the permitted micro profile succeeded with 1/8 OCPU, 1 GB RAM and a 50-GB boot disk. The inventory guard checks compartments, instances and boot/block volumes before allocating; the existing VM's boot volume must be read through boot-volume attachments because the instance-list response omits its ID. It refuses paid profiles and excess capacity. Linux `tc` bounds egress at 20 Mbit/s; Caddy provides HTTPS on a free sslip.io hostname. No account upgrade or paid resource was used. Shared allowances, idle reclamation, DNS availability and the micro VM's concurrency ceiling remain operational constraints.
- **Code.** Deleted the Worker backend, Durable Object bridge, Wrangler runtime configuration and paired preview deployment paths. Local development and OCI share the Node HTTP/WebSocket adapters, TCP/UDP relays and callback bridge. Production pins checked public DNS answers, including redirects and pooled reconnects, while preserving TCP address fallback. TCP writes and UDP lookup/peer state are bounded. Cloudflare Pages still serves every platform binary, including split-file ranges; it forwards only the backend routes. APK bytecode and application behavior were unchanged.
- **Failures caught.** An Undici 8 dispatcher used with Node's built-in fetch failed with `invalid onRequestStart method`; importing fetch from the same Undici package fixed the mismatch and passed live HTTPS. A Pages Wrangler config with empty bindings would erase Pulumi-managed variables during upload; removing that config makes the upload inherit the project configuration. Selecting only the first checked TCP address lost Node's connection fallback; the relay now supplies all checked addresses through its lookup hook, covered by a failed-first-address test. Hosted launch diagnostics must wait for microG's asynchronously started processes: first-frame snapshots legitimately included `starting` rows, so those snapshots were not counted as complete verification.
- **Verification.** The final `pnpm check` passed in 97 s: typecheck, three unit shards, all Wasm parts including FitNotes sweep, and 7/7 deterministic flows with zero reported gaps. Content differences were Calculator M 0.65%, FitNotes 2.92%, Mihon app 22.58% and onboarding 10.95%; goldens were unchanged. Per-step records had no new missing/error/native requirements, wedged loops or unavailable diagnostics; Komikku retained its expected SQLite warning logs. Five core backend tests cover TCP bytes/shutdown, checked address fallback, UDP framing, origin rejection and private/mixed DNS rejection. Production builds and Pages compilation passed.
- **Live evidence.** Both `knlnks-oci.webdroid.pages.dev` and production `webdroid.pages.dev` passed HTTPS proxying, TCP TLS/ALPN, metadata-address rejection, a WebView WebSocket/HTTP callback and a UDP DNS round trip. Framework.jar bytes crossing a 24-MiB part boundary, core-oj.jar bytes and certificate bytes matched local sources. FitNotes launched from a local APK on both hosted origins, with cross-origin isolation active and complete page/app/microG diagnostics: no observed missing APIs, native requirements, browser errors or failed requests. This is observed-path coverage, not all-APK networking or a load benchmark.
- **Cutover.** Production Pages deployment `ef97b77c` uses `https://webdroid.163.192.4.18.sslip.io`. The former `webdroid-backend` Worker was deleted after verification; the older all-in-one `webdroid` Worker's public/preview endpoints were disabled. Production transport checks passed again after retirement. A subsequent Pulumi preview reported all eight resources unchanged. State, stack configuration and credentials remain outside Git; recovery and manual release instructions are in [deployment.md](deployment.md).
- **One network policy** (2026-10-28, after cutover). `backend/network.ts`'s `localNetwork` and `publicNetwork` replace a `publicOnly` flag that the host, proxy and both relays checked apart. The proxy's `reachable` hook again refuses a private target before any fetch, so the guest sees a refused connection (`x-wd-error: refused`) as it did under the Worker rather than a generic I/O error; the failure kind now also reads a Node fetch failure's `cause`, which had classified every DNS failure as I/O. The TCP relay dials through the policy's lookup instead of resolving ahead, which kept address fallback (the IPv6 path of a dual-stack name was taken live) without pausing the WebSocket. The renderer bridge's WebSocket keeps `ws`'s frame limit, since its replies carry whole response bodies; the relays keep 8 MiB. Infra user data is byte-identical, and a Pulumi preview left all eight resources unchanged. `pnpm check` passed in 124 s (7/7 flows, zero gaps, goldens unchanged); the production bundle and the dev server each passed TCP, UDP DNS, proxy and private-target checks. Redeployed the same day (bundle hash matched on the VM); through production Pages, HTTPS proxying, TCP TLS with ALPN `h2`, metadata-address TCP refusal (4000, `ECONNREFUSED`), foreign-origin 403, a UDP DNS round trip and a WebView socket/callback exchange passed. **Cloudflare's 502/504 page.** Cloudflare answers a Pages Function's subrequest that ends in 502 or 504 with its own HTML error page: the Function's own `fetch` already receives it (its headers were `cf-ray`, `retry-after`, `x-frame-options`, no `x-wd-error`), so no rewrapping, buffering or header copy in the Function recovers the answer, while a 502 the Function makes itself and a forwarded 503 pass. Through Pages a proxy failure therefore lost its kind (the client read every one as `io`), a frame's failure page its `wd-proxy-error` meta, and an upstream 502/504 its final URL. This predated the network policy; the Worker's service binding never crossed that path. `src/shell/pages-hop.ts` now has the Function ask the backend to hold such a status as a 200 with `webdroid-held-status`, which the Function restores; the name has no `x-wd-` prefix, which the proxy would forward upstream. Production deployment `be7b7497` (built fresh, no page asset changed) passed refused/host kinds, upstream 502/504 final URLs, a frame failure page, TCP TLS `h2`, UDP DNS, the WebView bridge, ranged vendor reads and cross-origin isolation.


## GitHub Actions production releases (2026-10-28)

- **Flow.** A serialized `main` workflow validates and builds the frontend and backend, deploys OCI and checks HTTPS health, removes temporary SSH access, then uploads Pages. CI supplies the deployment addresses directly; local releases retain their Pulumi lookup. Strict pinned host keys and batch SSH avoid trust-on-first-use and interactive prompts on disposable runners. Pulumi provisioning/state remain local.
- **Firewall ownership.** Each hosted runner receives only a public IPv4 `/32` TCP/22 rule. OCI ETags prevent overwriting concurrent edits. Cleanup preserves unrelated ingress/egress and runs after failure or cancellation; a lost runner's marked rule is replaced on the next release. One lifecycle test covers stale-rule replacement, conflicting updates and idempotent removal. Forced runner loss can still leave access until cleanup or another release; releases and manual applies must not overlap.
- **Validation.** `actionlint`, ShellCheck, typecheck, four existing backend tests, the firewall lifecycle test, backend/Pages compilation and the frontend production build passed. An isolated build-helper run verified real Zig/AOSP downloads, checksums, extraction, command environment and temporary-directory cleanup. A live OCI add/remove round trip preserved the existing ingress and egress exactly. SSH verified the already trusted host key and both services were active. An isolated deployer check used mocked SSH/SCP and live HTTPS health to verify CI inputs, strict SSH arguments and invalid-origin refusal without restarting production.
- **Setup and limits.** OCI/SSH secrets and the account/project, deployment and firewall variables were configured in GitHub. The current reference platform archive was uploaded to the existing `webdroid-builds` bucket under its SHA-256 (`5e67ba4b94daeb734939f5716bd5e4651ec57346571ab70d4830bee20127f75c`); downloading it with curl reproduced the digest. The build uses that pinned URL/digest. Activation still requires a long-lived `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` repository secret and merging the workflow into `main`; no GitHub-hosted production run has been observed. This change did not alter emulator behavior or publish a new application release.

- **Credential completion** (2026-10-28). Created the account-owned `webdroid-github-actions` token in Helium with only Pages Write permission on the existing Cloudflare account, no expiry and no client-IP restriction (hosted runner addresses vary). Stored it directly as the repository's encrypted `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` Actions secret without printing the value or saving it to a file. Cloudflare's account-token verification reported `active`, and an authenticated read of the `webdroid` Pages project succeeded. The credential blocker above is resolved; merge to `main` and the first hosted release remain outstanding.

- **Main integration** (2026-10-28). Squash-integrated the OCI branch with the newer native-thread/A64 work on `main`, preserving both. The merged tree passed `pnpm check` in 113 s: all seven flows reached their checkpoints with zero reported gaps; goldens were unchanged. Actionlint, ShellCheck, the firewall lifecycle test and backend/Pages compilation also passed. The first GitHub-hosted release remains to be verified after this push.

- **Clean-runner cache** (2026-10-28). The first hosted run (`40824048290`) failed before deployment: zlib source extraction called `mkdtemp` beneath a cache parent that local builds had already created. The downloader now creates that parent itself. An isolated empty-cache run downloaded, checksum-verified and extracted the real release, then reused it without another download; typecheck passed.

- **Hosted asset verification** (2026-10-28). Run `40824290928` successfully deployed `b41061cba` to OCI and Pages (`54a494f2`); live proxy, TCP/TLS/ALPN, UDP DNS and WebView callback checks passed, and OCI ingress/egress exactly matched their pre-run snapshot. A production FitNotes launch had complete, clean process diagnostics but exposed 111 browser 404s for AOT modules: CI had omitted `pnpm aot`, while the production alias still served an older cached `current.json` (a cache-busting request and the deployment URL both returned 404). CI now builds the platform modules before bundling, and the manifest explicitly requires revalidation. The local production build contains every startup module named by its manifest; actionlint and typecheck passed.
